Deity: Indra, for Ṛks 1-8, for pādas 2-4 of Ṛk 9, and for Ṛks 10-15; for the first pāda of Ṛk 9: Indra, Uśanā. **NOTE**

Metre: Triṣṭubh.

### Printed p. 159

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> त्रै॑र्य॒मा मनु॑षो दे॒वता॑ता॒ त्रि रो॑च॒ना दि॒व्या धा॑रयंत।  
> अर्चं॑ति त्वा म॒रुतः॑ पू॒तद॑क्षा॒स्त्वमे॑षा॒मृषि॑रिंद्रासि॒ धीरः॑ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> श्री । अर्यमा । मनुषः । देवताता । श्री । रोचना । दिव्या । धारयंत ।  
> अर्चंति । त्वा । मरुतः । पूत्यदक्षाः । त्वं । एषां । ऋषिः । इंद्र । असि । धीरः ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> मनुषो मनोः संबंधिनि देवताता देवतात् यज्ञे त्री त्रीण्यर्यमार्यमाणि यानि तेजांसि संति । तथा त्री त्रीणि रोचना रोचमानानि वाय्वग्निसूर्यात्मकानि दिव्या दिव्यान्यंतरिक्षे भवानि तानि तेजांसि धारयंत । मरुतो
> धारयंति । पूतदक्षाः शुद्धबला मरुतस्त्वा त्वामर्चंति । स्तुवंति । हे इंद्र धीरो
> धीमांस्त्वमेषां मरुतामृषिरसि । द्रष्टा भवसि ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

In the sacrifice that belongs to Manu there are three splendours (त्री त्रीण्यर्यमार्यमाणि (*trī trīṇyaryamāryamāṇi*)) **NOTE**; and the Maruts uphold three shining lights, divine and in the atmosphere, which have the nature of wind, fire and sun, those splendours. The Maruts, of pure strength, praise you, Indra; you, wise one, are the seer of these Maruts, that is, you become their seer.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- मनुषः (*manuṣaḥ*)—belonging to Manu
- देवताता (*devatātā*)—in the sacrifice
- त्री (*trī*)—three
- अर्यमा (*aryamā*)—splendours (are established)
- दिव्या (*divyā*)—that are in the atmosphere
- त्री रोचना (*trī rocanā*)—three masses of splendour
- धारयंत (*dhārayaṃta*)—the Maruts have upheld
- पूतदक्षाः (*pūtadakṣāḥ*)—having pure strength
- मरुतः (*marutaḥ*)—the Maruts
- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- त्वा (*tvā*)—you
- अर्चंति (*arcaṃti*)—worship
- त्वं (*tvaṃ*)—you
- एषां (*eṣāṃ*)—of these Maruts
- धीरः (*dhīraḥ*)—wise
- ऋषिः असि (*ṛṣiḥ asi*)—are the seer

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

In the sacrifice that belongs to Manu and is meant for worshipping the gods, three splendours are established. The Maruts uphold three masses of splendour, which are in the atmosphere and have the forms of fire, wind and sun. O Indra, the Maruts, whose strength is pure, worship you. You are the wise seer of those Maruts.

### Printed p. 160

##### English translation
In the adoration of the gods by Manu there are three effulgences, and they, (the Maruts), uphold three luminaries in heaven : the Maruts of pure energy worship you, for you, Indra, are their intelligent Rishi.

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अनु॒ यदीं॑ म॒रुतो॑ मंदसा॒नमार्च॒न्निंद्रं॑ पपि॒वांसं॑  ॒॑
> सुतस्य ।  
> आद॑त्त॒ वज्र॑म॒भि यदहिं॒ हन्न॒पो य॒ह्वीर॑सृज॒त्सर्त॒ वा उ॑ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अनु । यत् । ईं । मरुतः । मंदसानं । आर्चन् । इंद्रं । पपिवांसं । सुतस्य ।  
> आ । अदत्त । वज्रं । अभि । यत् । अहिं । अपः । यह्विः । असृजत् । सर्तवै । ऊं  
> इति ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> यद्यदा मरुतो मंदसानं तृप्यंतं सुतस्याभिषुतं सोमं पपिवांसं
> पीतवंतमीमेनमिंद्रमन्वार्चन् अन्वस्तुवन् तदेंद्रो वज्रमादत्त । आददौ ।
> ततो यद्यदाहिं वृत्रमभि हन् अभ्यहन् अभिहतवान् तदा यह्वीर्महतीरपो
> वृत्रेण निरुद्धान्न्युदकानि सर्तवै स्वैरत्वेन सर्तुं गंतुमसृजत् निरोधाच्चामुंचत् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

When the Maruts praised Indra, who was delighted, having drunk the pressed Soma, then Indra took up the thunderbolt. Then, when he slew Ahi, that is Vṛtra, he let the mighty waters, which Vṛtra had held back, go forth to flow freely, and freed them from their confinement.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यत् (*yat*)—when
- मरुतः (*marutaḥ*)—the Maruts
- सुतस्य (*sutasya*)—the pressed Soma
- पपिवांसं (*papivāṃsaṃ*)—who has drunk
- मंदसानं (*maṃdasānaṃ*)—who has attained satisfaction
- ईं इंद्रं (*īṃ iṃdraṃ*)—this very Indra
- अनु आर्चन् (*anu ārcan*)—praised with devotion, then (Indra)
- वज्रं (*vajraṃ*)—the thunderbolt
- आदत्त (*ādatta*)—took in his hand (Indra)
- यत् (*yat*)—when
- अहिं (*ahiṃ*)—Vṛtra the Asura
- अभि हन् (*abhi han*)—destroyed (then)
- यह्वीः (*yahvīḥ*)—abundant
- अपः (*apaḥ*)—waters
- सर्तवै (*sartavai*)—to carry along freely
- असृजत् (*asṛjat*)—released

### Printed p. 161

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

As soon as the Maruts praised with devotion Indra, who had drunk the pressed Soma and was satisfied, Indra took the thunderbolt in his hand. As soon as Indra destroyed the Asura Vṛtra, he released the abundant waters to flow freely.

##### English translation
When the Maruts worship Indra, exulting and drinking of the effused libation, he grasps the thunderbolt wherewith he destroys Ahi, and sets the abundant waters free to flow.

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> उ॒त ब्र॑ह्माणो मरतो मे अ॒स्येंद्रः॒ सोम॑स्य॒ सुसु॑तस्य  
> पेयाः ।  
> तद्धि ह॒व्यं मनु॑षे॒ गा अविं॑द॒दह॒न्नहिं॑ पपि॒वा इंद्रो॑ अस्य ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> उत । ब्रह्माणः । मरुतः । मे । अस्य । इंद्रः । सोमस्य । सुसुतस्य । पेयाः ।  
> तत् । हि । हव्यं । मनुषे । गाः । अविंदत् । अहन् । अहिं । पपिवान् । इंद्रः ।  
> अस्य ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> उतापि च हे ब्रह्माणो बृहंतो हे मरुतो यूयमिंद्रश्च सुसुतस्य
> सुष्वृभिषुतस्य मे मदीयस्यास्य सोमस्येमं सोमं ॥ द्वितीयार्थे षष्ठी ॥
> पेयाः । पिबत ॥ पिबतेराशीर्लिज मद्यमबहुनचनस्यैकवचनं ॥ तद्धि तत्पलु हव्यं
> युष्माभिः पीतं सोमात्मकं हविर्मनुषे मनुष्याय यजमानाय गा धेनूर्वृष्टिलक्षणान्युदकानि ना अविंदत् । वेदयति । यजमानं गा लंभयतीत्यर्थः ।
> किंचास्येमं सोमं पपिवान् पीतवानिंद्रोऽहिं वृत्रमहन् । अवधीत् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Maruts, mighty ones, you and Indra drink this Soma of mine, well pressed; the genitive stands in the sense of the accusative. The oblation drunk by you, consisting of Soma, makes the sacrificer find cows, that is, the waters that take the form of rain; it makes the sacrificer obtain cows. Moreover Indra, having drunk this Soma, slew Ahi, that is Vṛtra.

### Printed p. 162

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- उत (*uta*)—and
- ब्रह्माणः (*brahmāṇaḥ*)—mighty in form
- मरुतः (*marutaḥ*)—O Maruts
- यूयं (*yūyaṃ*)—(you)
- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—and Indra
- सुसुतस्य (*susutasya*)—well pressed
- मे (*me*)—my
- अस्य सोमस्य (*asya somasya*)—this Soma
- पेयाः (*peyāḥ*)—drink
- तद्धि हव्यं (*taddhi havyaṃ*)—that same oblation, drunk by you
- मनुषे (*manuṣe*)—to the sacrificer who performs the sacrifice
- गाः (*gāḥ*)—cows
- अविंदत् (*aviṃdat*)—makes known
- अस्य (*asya*)—this Soma
- पपिवान् (*papivān*)—having drunk
- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra
- अहिं (*ahiṃ*)—Vṛtra
- अहन् (*ahan*)—destroyed

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Maruts, mighty in form, you and Indra drink this well-pressed Soma juice. That same oblation, drunk by you, makes known to the sacrificer who performs the sacrifice the regions where the cows are. Indra, having drunk this Soma, destroyed Vṛtra. **NOTE**

##### English translation
Or, mighty Maruts, and you also, Indra, drink of this my copiously-effused libation : then the offered libation obtains cattle for the offerer, and Indra, drinking of it, kills Ahi.

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आद्रोद॑सी वित॒रं वि ष्क॑भायत्संविव्या॒नश्चि॑द्धि॒यसे॑ मृ॒गं कः॑ ।  
> जिग॑र्ति॒मिंद्रो॑ अप॒जगु॑र्राणः॒ प्रति॑श्व॒संत॒मन॑ दान॒वं ह॑न्॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आत् । रोदसी इति । वितरं । वि । स्कभायत् । संविव्यानः । चित् । भियसे । मृगं।  
> करिति कः ।  
> जिगर्तिं । इंद्रः । अप्पजगुर्राणः । प्रति । श्वसंतं । अव । दानवं । हन्निति हन् । ४ ॥

### Printed p. 163

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> इंद्रः परमैश्वर्ययुक्त आत्सोमपानानंतरं रोदसी द्यावापृथिव्यौ
> वितरमतिशयेन ॥ अमु च छंदसीति वेस्तरपि कृतेऽमुप्रत्ययः ॥ वि ष्कभायत् ।
> व्यस्तभ्यात् । चलनरहिते अकरोत् । संविव्यानश्चित् संवृण्वानः संगच्छमानो
> वेंद्रः ॥ व्ययतेर्वा संवरणकर्मणो वेतेर्वा गतिकर्मणो रूपं । चिदित्येवेत्यस्यार्थे वर्तते । मृगं मृगवत्पलायमानं वृत्रं भयसे भयाय कः ।
> अकार्षित् ॥ करोतेर्लुजु मंत्रे घसह्वरेत्यादिना चेर्लोपः । बहुलं छंदस्यमाजज्योगेऽपीत्यडभावः ॥ इंद्रो जिगर्तिं निरंतमाच्छादयंतं श्वसंतं भयार्थं
> श्वासं कुर्वंतं दानवं दनोः पुत्रं वृत्रमपजर्गुराण आच्छादनाद्विमोचयन
> प्रति प्रतिगत्याव हन् । अवहतवान् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Indra, endowed with supreme lordship, after drinking the Soma made the two worlds, heaven and earth, most firm; he made them unmoving. Enveloping (or: coming together, or going; Sāyaṇa derives the word from a root meaning to cover or from one meaning to go) **NOTE**, he made Vṛtra, who fled like a deer, afraid. Indra, freeing from his covering the Dānava, son of Danu, namely Vṛtra, who was concealing himself, panting to cause fear, advanced against him and slew him.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—the lord Indra
- आत् (*āt*)—as soon as the Soma was drunk
- रोदसी (*rodasī*)—heaven and earth, both
- वितरं (*vitaraṃ*)—exceedingly
- वि ष्कभायत् (*vi ṣkabhāyat*)—established them firmly
- संविव्यानश्चित् (*saṃvivyānaścit*)—while moving about with firm resolve
- मृगं (*mṛgaṃ*)—Vṛtra, who was fleeing like a deer
- भियसे कः (*bhiyase kaḥ*)—made him be overcome with fear
- जिगर्ति० (*jigarti0*)—who tried to cover himself
- श्वसंतं (*śvasaṃtaṃ*)—who was breathing in a hideous way so that others should fear
- दानवं (*dānavaṃ*)—Vṛtra, the son of Danu
- अपजगुराणः (*apajagurāṇaḥ*)—preventing him from hiding
- प्रति (*prati*)—confronting him
- अव हन् (*ava han*)—destroyed

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The lord Indra, as soon as the Soma was drunk, established heaven and earth exceedingly and firmly. While he was moving about with firm resolve to destroy his enemy, he made Vṛtra, who was fleeing like a deer, be overcome with fear. He prevented Vṛtra, the son of Danu, who tried to cover himself and was breathing hideously so that others should fear, from hiding, confronted him and destroyed him.

##### English translation
Thereupon he fixed firmly heaven and earth, and, resolutely advancing filled (Vritra), like a deer, with terror : stripping off his covering, Indra slew the Danava, endeavouring to hide, and panting with affright.

### Printed p. 164

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अध॒ क्रत्वा॑ मघव॒न्नुभ्यं॑ दे॒वा अनु॒ विश्वे॑ अददुः सोम॒पेयं॑ ।  
> यत्सूर्य॑स्य ह॒रितः॒ पतं॑तीः पु॒रः स॒तीरुप॑रा॒ एत॑शे॒ कः ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अध । कत्वा । मघवन् । तुभ्यं । देवाः । अनु । विश्वे । अददुः । सोमपेयं ।  
> यत् । सूर्यस्य । हरितः । पतंतीः । पुरः । सतीः । उपराः । एतशे । करिति कः ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अधापि च हे मघवन् घनवन्निंद्र क्रत्वा क्रतुना त्वदीयेन कर्मणा विश्वे
> सर्वॆ देवा द्योतमाना वह्नादयस्तुभ्यं त्वदर्थं सोमपेयं सोमपानमन्वददुः । आनुपूर्व्यण दत्तवंतः । ततः प्रभृतींद्रो देवेभ्यः सर्वेभ्योऽधिकोऽभवदित्यर्थः । तथा च श्रूयते । स महान्भात्वा देवता अब्रवीदुद्धारं म
> उद्धरतेत्यादि । ऐ. ब्रा. ३-२१ । हे इंद्र यद्यस्त्वं पतंतीरागच्छंतीः पुरः सतीः
> पुरस्ताद्भवंतीः सूर्यस्य संबंधिनीर्हरितो वडना उपरा उपरता मंदगतीरेतश एतशाख्याय ऋषये कः अकार्षिः । एतशो हि स्वश्वपुत्रेण सूर्येण सह
> स्पर्धामकरोदिति यावत् । तथा च निगमांतरे । प्रैतशं सूर्ये पस्पृधानं
> सौवश्यॆ सुष्टिमावदिंद्रः । ऋग्वे १-६१-१५ । इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

And further, O Maghavan, O wealthy Indra, through your own deed all the shining gods, Agni and the others, have granted you in due order the drinking of Soma for your sake; from then on Indra became greater than all the gods. So it is heard: स महान्भात्वा देवता अब्रवीदुद्धारं म उद्धरतेत्यादि । (*sa mahānbhātvā devatā abravīduddhāraṃ ma uddharatetyādi |*) (Ai. Brā. 3-21) **NOTE**. Since you, O Indra, made the tawny horses of Sūrya, which were coming on and were in front, slow of pace for the sake of the seer named Etaśa **NOTE**, that is, Etaśa had competed with Sūrya, the son of his own horse **NOTE**; and another passage says the same: प्रैतशं सूर्ये पस्पृधानं सौवश्यॆ सुष्टिमावदिंद्रः (*praitaśaṃ sūrye paspṛdhānaṃ sauvaśyè suṣṭimāvadiṃdraḥ*) (Ṛgve. 1-61-15) **NOTE**.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- एतशे (*etaśe*)—(O Indra) for the help of the seer named Etaśa
- यत् (*yat*)—by which greatness of yours
- पतंतीः (*pataṃtīḥ*)—that were coming against you
- पुरः सतीः (*puraḥ satīḥ*)—that were spreading out in front
- हरितः (*haritaḥ*)—the horses
- उपराः कः (*uparāḥ kaḥ*)—you made slow of pace
- अध (*adha*)—for that very reason
- मघवन् (*maghavan*)—O wealthy Indra
- क्रत्वा (*kratvā*)—pleased by your surpassing deed
- विश्वे देवाः (*viśve devāḥ*)—all the gods
- तुभ्यं (*tubhyaṃ*)—to you
- सोमपेयं (*somapeyaṃ*)—the drinking of Soma
- अनु आदुदुः (*anu āduduḥ*)—approved (to you first, before all others)

### Printed p. 165

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, by that greatness of yours by which, for the help of the seer named Etaśa, you made the horses that belong to Sūrya, which were coming against you and spreading out in front, slow of pace, for that very reason all the gods, pleased by your surpassing deed, approved the drinking of Soma for you, O wealthy Indra, before all others.

##### English translation
Then, for this exploit, all the gods gave you, Maghavan, in succession, the Soma beverage ; whence you have retarded, for the sake of Etasa, the advancing horses of the sun.

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> नव॒ यद॑स्य नव॒तिं च॑ भो॒गान्ता॒स्यकं वज्रे॑ण म॒घवा॑  
> विवृ॒श्चत् ।  
> अर्चं॒तीद्रं॑ म॒रुतः॑ स॒दस्ये॒ त्रैष्टु॑भेन॒ वच॑सा बाधत॒ द्यां ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> नव । यत् । अस्य । नवतिं । च । भोगान् । साकं । वज्रेण । मघवा । विवृश्चत् ।  
> अर्चंति । इंद्रं । मरुतः ! सधस्थे । त्रैस्तुभेन । वचसा । बाधत । द्यां ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> यद्यदा मघवा धनवानिंद्रः । अस्येत्यनेन शंबर उच्यते । अस्य शंबरस्य
> संबंधीनि नव नवसंख्याकानि नवतिं च नवतिसंख्याकानि च भोगान्सुराणि । भोगानित्यंतोदात्तः पुरवचनः । साकं युगपदेव वज्रेण विवृश्चत् व्यवृश्चत् अभिनत्
> तदा मरुतः सधस्थे सहस्थाने युद्धे ना स्थितमिंद्रं त्रैष्टुभेन त्रिष्टुप्‌छंदस्केन
> वचसा नाग्रूपेण स्तोत्रेणार्चंति । आस्तुवन् । इंद्र एवं स्तुतः सन् द्यां
> मरुद्भिः प्रयुज्यमानेन मंत्रेण दीप्तं शंबराख्यमसुरं बाधत । अबाधत ।
> पिपीडे ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

When the wealthy Indra split with his thunderbolt at one stroke the nine and ninety strongholds of Śambara (the word अस्य (*asya*) here denotes Śambara; भोगान् (*bhogān*) means cities), then the Maruts, in the place of their common abode, that is, in the battle, praised Indra who stood there, with a hymn in the Triṣṭubh metre. Thus praised, Indra oppressed the shining Asura named Śambara by the mantra employed by the Maruts; the verb बाधत (*bādhata*) means he crushed.

### Printed p. 166

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यत् (*yat*)—when
- मघवा (*maghavā*)—the wealthy Indra
- अस्य (*asya*)—of Śambara
- नव नवतिं च (*nava navatiṃ ca*)—ninety-nine in number
- भोगान् (*bhogān*)—the cities
- साकं (*sākaṃ*)—at one time
- वज्रेण (*vajreṇa*)—with his thunderbolt
- विवृश्चत् (*vivṛścat*)—split asunder (then)
- मरुतः (*marutaḥ*)—the Maruts
- सदस्ये (*sadasye*)—who stood in one place
- इंद्रं (*iṃdraṃ*)—Indra
- त्रैष्टुभेन (*traiṣṭubhena*)—composed in the Triṣṭubh metre
- वचसा (*vacasā*)—with a praise
- अर्चंति (*arcaṃti*)—worshipped (Indra, pleased by this worship)
- द्यां (*dyāṃ*)—the shining Śambara
- बाधत (*bādhata*)—harmed

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

As soon as the wealthy Indra split asunder at one time, with his thunderbolt, the ninety-nine cities of Śambara, the Maruts worshipped Indra, who stood in one place, with praises composed in the Triṣṭubh metre. Indra, pleased by the recitation of these very mantras, harmed the shining Śambara.

##### English translation
Whereupon Maghavan has destroyed with his thunderbolt at once his (Shambara's) ninety and nine cities: the Maruts glorifying Indra in a common dwelling with the Trishtubh hymn, he destroyed the bright (city of the Asura).

#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> सखा॒ सख्ये॑ अपच॒त्तूय॑म॒ग्निर॒स्य क्रत्वा॑ महि॒षा त्री श॒तानि॑ ।  
> श्री सा॒कमिंद्रो॒ मनु॑षः॒ सरां॑सि सु॒तं पि॑बद्वृत्र॒हत्या॑य॒  ॑
> सोमं ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सखा । सख्ये । अपचत् । तूयं । अग्निः । अस्य । क्रत्वा । महिषा । त्री । शतानि ।  
> श्री । साकं । इंद्रः । मनुषः । सरांसि । सुतं । पिबत् । वृत्तहत्याय । सोमं॥ ७ ॥

### Printed p. 167

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> सखेंद्रस्य मित्रभूतोऽग्निर्महिषा महिषाणां पशूनां श्री त्रीणि शतानि
> शतसंख्याकानि सख्ये सख्युर्मित्रभूतस्यास्येंद्रस्य क्रत्वा कर्मणा निमित्तभूतेन
> तूयं क्षिप्रमपचत् । पपाच । किंच इंद्रः परमैश्वर्ययुक्तो मनुषो
> मनोः संबंधीनि त्री त्रीणि सरांसि पात्राणि । अत्र सरःशब्देन पूतभृदाधवनीयद्रोणकलशसंज्ञानि पात्राण्युच्यंते । तेषु स्थितं सुतमभिषुतं सोमं साकं
> युगपदेव वृत्रहत्याय वृत्रहननाय पिबत् । अपिबत् । पपौ ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Agni, the friend of Indra, quickly cooked three hundred buffaloes, that is, animals, for the friend, this Indra, by a deed that served as the cause. And Indra, endowed with supreme lordship, drank at one time, for the slaying of Vṛtra, the pressed Soma that stood in the three सरांसि (*sarāṃsi*) of Manu, that is, vessels; here the word सरः (*saraḥ*) is said to stand for the vessels named Pūtabhṛt, Ādhavanīya and Droṇakalaśa **NOTE**.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- सखा (*sakhā*)—Agni, the friend of Indra
- अग्निः (*agniḥ*)—Agni
- सख्यॆ (*sakhyè*)—to his own dear friend
- अस्य (*asya*)—of Indra
- क्रत्वा (*kratvā*)—to help in the deed
- तूयं (*tūyaṃ*)—quickly
- त्रि शतानि (*tri śatāni*)—three hundred in number
- महिषा (*mahiṣā*)—animals in the form of buffaloes
- अपचत् (*apacat*)—cooked
- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra
- मनुषः (*manuṣaḥ*)—that belongs to Manu
- त्रि सरांसि (*tri sarāṃsi*)—that is in three Droṇakalaśa vessels
- सुतं (*sutaṃ*)—that was pressed according to the rule
- सोमं (*somaṃ*)—the Soma juice
- साकं (*sākaṃ*)—at one time
- वृत्रहत्याय (*vṛtrahatyāya*)—to gain strength to kill Vṛtra
- पिबत् (*pibat*)—drank

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Agni, the friend of Indra, cooked three hundred animals in the form of buffaloes to help his dear friend Indra in his enemy-destroying deed. Indra drank at one time the Soma juice offered by Manu, which was in three Droṇakalaśa vessels and had been pressed according to the rule, and gained the strength needed for the slaying of Vṛtra.

##### English translation
To aid (the undertaking of) his friend, Agni, the friend (of Indra), has quickly consumed three hundred buffaloes ; and Indra, for the destruct- ion of Vritra, has at once quaffed three vessels of Sama, offered by Manu. **NOTE**

#### Ṛk 8

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> श्री यच्छ॒ता म॑हि॒षाणा॒मघो॒ मास्त्री सरां॑सि म॒घवा॑  ॒॑
> सो॒म्यापाः॑ ।

### Printed p. 168

> कारं न विश्वे अह्वंत देवा भरमिंद्राय यदहिं जघान ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> श्री । यत् । शता । महिषाणां । अघः । माः । श्री । सरांसि । मघवा । सोम्या ।  
> असाः ।  
> कारं । न । विश्वे । अह्वंत । देव्याः । भरं । इंद्राय । यत् । अहिं । जघान ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र त्वं यद्यदा त्री त्रयाणां शता शतसंख्याकानां महिषाणां
> पशूनां मा मांसान्यघः भक्षितवानसि ॥ घसेरत्तेर्वा रूपं ॥ यदा च मघवा
> धनवांस्त्वं सोम्या सोममयानि त्री त्रीणि सरांसि पात्राण्यपाः पीतवानसि ।
> यद्यदेंद्रोऽहिं वृत्रं जघान हतवान् तदा विश्वे सर्वॆ देवा भरं सोमपानादिना पूर्णमिंद्रायेंद्रं युद्धार्थमह्वंत । आह्वयंत । तत्र दृष्टांतः ।
> कारं न । स्वामिनः कर्मकरमिव तद्वत् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, when you ate the flesh of three hundred buffaloes, that is, animals (the verb अघः (*aghaḥ*) stands for eating), and when you, the wealthy one, drank three vessels made of Soma, and when Indra slew Ahi, that is Vṛtra, then all the gods summoned Indra for battle, since he was filled by the drinking of Soma and the like. The example is कारं न (*kāraṃ na*): as a master calls his servant.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यत् (*yat*)—when (O Indra)
- त्री शता (*trī śatā*)—three hundred in number
- महिषाणां (*mahiṣāṇāṃ*)—of animals
- माः (*māḥ*)—the flesh
- अघः (*aghaḥ*)—you ate
- मघवा (*maghavā*)—you, the wealthy one
- सोम्या (*somyā*)—filled with Soma
- त्री सरांसि (*trī sarāṃsi*)—three Droṇakalaśa vessels
- अपाः (*apāḥ*)—you drank
- यत् (*yat*)—when
- अहिं (*ahiṃ*)—Vṛtra
- जघान (*jaghāna*)—Indra slew
- विश्वे देवाः (*viśve devāḥ*)—all the gods
- भरं (*bharaṃ*)—full (through drinking Soma)
- इंद्राय (*iṃdrāya*)—Indra
- कारं न (*kāraṃ na*)—as masters call a servant
- अह्वंत (*ahvaṃta*)—called for battle

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, when you ate the flesh of three hundred animals in the form of buffaloes, when you, the wealthy one, drank all the Soma that filled the three Droṇakalaśa vessels, and when you, Indra, slew Vṛtra, then all the gods summoned Indra, who was filled by the drinking of Soma, for battle, as masters call a servant.

##### English translation
When you have eaten the flesh of the three hundred buffaloes, when you who are Maghavan have drunk the three vessels of Soma, and had slain Ahi. then all the gods summoned Indra, full of food, as if he had been a servant, to the battle.

### Printed p. 169

#### Ṛk 9

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> उ॒शना॒ यत्स॑ह॒स्यै॒३॒॑रिरया॑तं गृ॒हमिं॑द्र जूजुवा॒नेभि॒रश्वैः॑ ।  
> व॒न्वा॒नो अत्र॑ स॒रथं॑ ययाथ॒ कुत्से॑न दे॒वैरव॑नोर्ह॒  ॑
> शुष्णं ॥ ९ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> उशना । यत् । सहस्यैः । अयातं । गृहं । इंद्र । जूजुवानेभिः । अश्वैः ।  
> वन्वानः । अत्र । सरथं ययाथ । कुत्सेन । देवैः । अवनोः । ह । शुष्णं ॥ ९ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> उशनसः स्तोम उशना यत्सहस्यैरिति मरुत्वतीये सूक्तमुखीया ।
> सूत्रितं च । उशनसः स्तोमेन गरगीर्णमिवात्मानं मन्यमानो यजेतोशना
> यत्सहस्यैरयातं । आ. ९-५ । इति ॥
> यद्यदा हे इंद्र त्वमुशना काव्यश्च सहस्यैरभिभवनशीलैर्जूजुवानेभिर्जूजुवानैर्गच्छद्भिरश्वैः कुत्सस्य गृहमयातं अगच्छतं तदा हे इंद्र त्वमत्रास्मिन्काले वन्वानः शत्रून् हिंसन् कुत्सेन देवैश्च सह सरथं समानमेकं रथं
> ययाथ । जगंथ । किंच शुष्कमेतन्नामकमसुरमवनोर्ह । अहिंसीः खलु ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The Ṛk उशना यत्सहस्यै (*uśanā yatsahasyai*) belongs to the Stoma of Uśanas, and in the Marutvatīya rite it is the opening verse of the sūkta. The sūtra says: उशनसः स्तोमेन गरगीर्णमिवात्मानं मन्यमानो यजेतोशना यत्सहस्यैरयातं । (*uśanasaḥ stomena garagīrṇamivātmānaṃ manyamāno yajetośanā yatsahasyairayātaṃ |*) (Ā. Sū. 9-5): one who thinks of himself as if he had swallowed poison should sacrifice with the Stoma of Uśanas. Sāyaṇa explains: when you, O Indra, and Uśanā Kāvya went to the house of Kutsa with swift horses that overcome their foes, then, O Indra, at that time you went, slaying your enemies, in one and the same chariot with Kutsa and the gods; and you also slew the Asura named Śuṣka **NOTE**.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- यत् (*yat*)—when
- उशना (*uśanā*)—and Uśanas (together)
- सहस्यैः (*sahasyaiḥ*)—that have the power to resist enemies
- जूजुवानेभिः (*jūjuvānebhiḥ*)—that are swift
- अश्वैः (*aśvaiḥ*)—with horses
- गृहं (*gṛhaṃ*)—to the house (of Kutsa)
- आयातं (*āyātaṃ*)—you went
- अत्र (*atra*)—at that time
- वन्वानः (*vanvānaḥ*)—as destroyers of enemies
- कुत्सेन (*kutsena*)—with Kutsa
- देवैः (*devaiḥ*)—and with the other gods
- सरथं (*sarathaṃ*)—seated in one chariot
- ययाथ (*yayātha*)—you went
- शुष्णं (*śuṣṇaṃ*)—the Asura named Śuṣṇa
- अवनोर्ह (*avanorha*)—you also harmed

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, when you and Uśanas, joining together, went to the house of Kutsa with horses that have the power to resist enemies and are swift, you went, destroying your enemies, seated in one chariot with Kutsa and the other gods. And you also harmed the Asura named Śuṣṇa.

### Printed p. 170

##### English translation
When, Indra, you and Ushanas, with vigorous and rapid coursers went to the dwelling of Kutsa, then, destroying his foes, you went in one chariot with Kutsa and the gods, and verily you have slain Sushna.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

When the Stoma of Uśanas is recited, this ninth Ṛk of the sūkta, उशना यत्सहस्यैः (*uśanā yatsahasyaiḥ*), is to be recited as the first Ṛk among the Marutvatīya śastra mantras; this is set out by the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra, उशनसस्तोमेन गरगीर्णमिवात्मानं मन्यमानो यजेतोशना यत्सहस्यैरयातं (*uśanasastomena garagīrṇamivātmānaṃ manyamāno yajetośanā yatsahasyairayātaṃ*) (Ā. Sū. 9-5).

#### Ṛk 10

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> प्रान्यच्च॒क्रम॑वृहः॒ सूर्य॑स्य॒ कुत्सा॑या॒न्यद्वरि॑वो॒ यात॑वेकः।  
> अ॒नासो॒ दस्यू॑रमृणो व॒धेन॒ नि दु॑र्यो॒ण आ॑वृणङ्मृ॒ध्रवा॑चः ॥ १० ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> प्र । अन्यत् । चक्रं । अवृहः । सूर्यस्य । कुत्साय । अन्यत् । वरिवः । यातने ।  
> अकरित्यकः ।  
> अनासः । दस्यून् । अमृणः । वधेन । नि । दुर्योणे । अवृणक् । मृध्रवाचः ॥ १० ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र पूर्वं द्विचक्रस्य सूर्यस्यान्यदेकं चक्रं प्रावृहः । पृथक्
> चकर्थ । अन्यदेकं चक्रं कुत्साय कुत्सस्य वरिवो धनं यातवे प्राप्तुमकः ।
> अकार्षिः । किंच अनास आस्यरहितान् । अस्यशब्देन शब्दोलक्ष्यते । अशब्दान्यूकान्
> दस्युनसुरान् वधेनायुधेन वज्रेणामृणः । अहिंसीः । दुर्योणे संग्रामे
> मृध्रवाचो हिंसितवागिंद्रियानसुरान्नृणक् । नितरां भिन्नवानसि । अमृणो
> न्यवृणगिति द्विरुक्तिरादरार्था ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, of the two-wheeled chariot of Sūrya you formerly took away one wheel and set it apart; the other wheel you made over to Kutsa, that he might attain wealth (वरिवः (*varivaḥ*) is wealth). Further, the Dasyus, the Asuras, are अनासः (*anāsaḥ*), without a mouth; by the word for mouth, sound is meant, so they are soundless and dumb. These you killed with your weapon, the thunderbolt. In the battle (दुर्योणे (*duryoṇe*)) you utterly split the Asuras whose organ of speech is harmed **NOTE**. The doubling of the verbs अमृणः (*amṛṇaḥ*) and न्यवृणगिति (*nyavṛṇagiti*) is for emphasis.

### Printed p. 171

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- सूर्यस्य (*sūryasya*)—(O Indra,) of Sūrya (of the chariot with two wheels)
- अन्यत् चक्रं (*anyat cakraṃ*)—one wheel
- प्र आवृकः (*pra āvṛkaḥ*)—you set apart
- अन्यत् (*anyat*)—the other
- कुत्साय (*kutsāya*)—to Kutsa
- वस्विः (*vasviḥ*)—wealth
- यातवे (*yātave*)—for obtaining
- अकः (*akaḥ*)—you made it a help
- अनासः (*anāsaḥ*)—who were without a mouth and dumb
- दस्यून् (*dasyūn*)—the Asuras
- वधेन (*vadhena*)—with the thunderbolt, the means of killing
- अमृणः (*amṛṇaḥ*)—you harmed
- दुर्योणे (*duryoṇe*)—in battle
- मृध्रवाचः (*mṛdhravācaḥ*)—the Asuras whose speech is harmful
- नि अवृणक् (*ni avṛṇak*)—you have cut through completely

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, of the two wheels of Sūrya's chariot you set one apart; the other you made a help to Kutsa for obtaining wealth. With the thunderbolt, the means of killing, you harmed the Asuras who were without a mouth and dumb. In battle you have cut through completely the Asuras whose speech is harmful.

##### English translation
You have formerly detached one wheel (of the car) of Surya : another you have given to Kutsa wherwith to acquire wealth : with the thunderbolt you have confounded the voiceless Dasyus, you have destroyed in battle the speech-bereft (foes).

#### Ṛk 11

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स्तोमा॑सस्त्वा॒ गौरि॑वीतेरवर्ध॒न्नरं॑धयो वैदथि॒नाय॒ पिपुं॑।  
> आ त्वामृ॒जिश्वा॑ स॒ख्याय॑ चक्रे॒ पच॑न्प॒क्तीरपि॑बः॒ सोम॑मस्य ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> स्तोमासः । त्वा । गौरिऽवीतेः । अवर्धन् । आरंधयः । वैदथिनाय । पिपुं ।  
> आ । त्वां । ऋजिश्वा । सख्याय । चक्रे । पचन् । पक्तिः । अपिबः सोमं । अस्य ॥ ११ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र गौरिवीतेरेतन्नामकस्य मंत्रद्रष्टुर्मम संबंधीनि स्तोमासः
> स्तोत्राणि त्वा त्वामवर्धन् । वर्धयंतु । वैदथिनाय विदथिनः पुत्राय यजिश्व-

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, the praises that belong to me, the seer named Gauriviti, increase you; let them make you grow. You brought the Asura named Pipru under the power of Ṛjiśvan, the son of Vidathin.

### Printed p. 172

> नाम्ने पिप्रमेतन्नामकमसुरनुरंधयः । वशमनयः । हे इंद्र ऋजिश्वनामा
> कश्चिदृषिः सख्याय तव सखित्वाय पक्तिः पक्तन्यानि पुरोडाशादीनि पचन् पाकं
> कुर्वन् त्वामा चक्रे । अभिमुखीचकार । किंच त्वमस्य ऋजिश्वनः संबंधिनं
> सोममपिबः । असाः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, a certain seer named Ṛjiśvan, for the sake of your friendship, cooking the offerings fit for cooking, the cakes (puroḍāśa) and the like, has turned you towards himself. And you drank the Soma of this Ṛjiśvan.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- गौरिवीतेः (*gaurivīteḥ*)—(O Indra,) made by me, who am named Gauriviti
- स्तोमासः (*stomāsaḥ*)—the praises
- त्वा (*tvā*)—you
- अवर्धन् (*avardhan*)—let them make you grow
- वैदथिनाय (*vaidathināya*)—for Ṛjiśvan, the son of Vidathi
- पिपुवं (*pipuvaṃ*)—the Asura named Pipu
- आरंधयः (*āraṃdhayaḥ*)—you brought under his power
- ऋजिश्वा (*ṛjiśvā*)—that same seer
- सख्याय (*sakhyāya*)—to win your friendship
- पक्तिः (*paktiḥ*)—the cakes and the like that are fit for cooking
- पचन् (*pacan*)—cooking
- त्वां (*tvāṃ*)—you
- आ चक्रे (*ā cakre*)—has turned towards himself
- त्वं (*tvaṃ*)—you
- अस्य (*asya*)—of Ṛjiśvan
- सोमं (*somaṃ*)—the Soma
- अपिबः (*apibaḥ*)—drink

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, may the praises made by me, who am named Gauriviti, make you grow. For Ṛjiśvan, the son of Vidathi, you brought the Asura named Pipu under his power. That same Ṛjiśvan, to win your friendship, cooking the cakes and the like that are fit for cooking, has turned you towards himself. Drink his Soma.

##### English translation
May the praises of Gauriviti exalt you ; you have humbled Pipru for the son of Vidathin : Rijishwan preparing dressed viands, has, through your friendship, brought you (to his presence), and you have drunk of his libation.

#### Ṛk 12

**NOTE**

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> नव॑ग्वासः सु॒तसो॑मास॒ इंद्रं॒ दश॑ग्वासो अ॒भ्य॑र्चंत्य॒र्कैः ।  
> गव्यं॑ चिदू॒र्वम॑पि॒धान॑वंतं॒ तं चि॒न्नरः॑ शशमा॒ना अप॑व्रन् ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> नवग्वासः । सुतसोमासः । इंद्रं । दशग्वासः । अभि । आर्चंति । अर्कैः ।  
> गव्यं । चित् । ऊर्वं । अपिधानवंतं । तं । चित् । नरः । शशमानाः । अप । व्रन् ॥

### Printed p. 173

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> दशपेयेऽहनि मरुत्वतीये शस्त्रे नवग्वास इति सूक्तमुखीया । सूत्रितं च ।
> नवग्वासः सुतसोमास इंद्रं सखा ह यत्र सखिभिर्नवग्वैरिति निविद्वानयोराद्ये ।
> आ. ९. ३ । इति ॥
> नवग्वासः । सत्रयागमनुतिष्ठंतो ये नवभिर्मास्यॆ: समाप्य गतास्ते
> नवग्वाः । दशभिर्मास्यॆ: समाप्य ये गतास्ते दशग्वाः । एत उभयविधा अंगिरसः
> सुतसोमासोऽभिषुतसोमाः संत इंद्रमर्कैरर्चनीयैः स्तोत्रैरभ्यर्चंति ।
> अभिष्णुवंति । तथा शशमानाः स्तुवंतो नरो नेतारोऽंगिरसोऽपिधानवंतं वलेनासुरेणाच्छादितवंतं तं चित् बहुषु प्रदेशेषु प्रसिद्धमपि गव्यंचित् गोसंबंधि!
> नमष्यपूर्वं समूहमप व्रन् । अपावृण्वन् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

On the day of the Daśapeya, in the Marutvatīya śastra, the Ṛk नवग्वासः (*navagvāsaḥ*) is the sūktamukhīya, the opening verse of the sūkta. The sūtra says so: नवग्वासः सुतसोमास इंद्रं सखा ह यत्र सखिभिर्नवग्वैरिति निविद्वानयोराद्ये । (*navagvāsaḥ sutasomāsa iṃdraṃ sakhā ha yatra sakhibhirnavagvairiti nividvānayorādye |*) (Ā. Sū. 9-3). Those who perform a sattra sacrifice and go home having completed it in nine months are the navagvas; those who go having completed it in ten months are the daśagvas. These two kinds of Aṅgirases, having pressed the Soma, worship Indra with arkas, that is, with hymns fit for worship; they praise him. Likewise the praising leaders, the Aṅgirases, opened up the herd of cattle, well known in many places, which Vala the Asura had covered **NOTE**.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- नवग्वासः (*navagvāsaḥ*)—those who complete the sacrifice in nine months
- दशग्वासः (*daśagvāsaḥ*)—the sons of Aṅgiras who complete it in ten months
- सुतसोमासः (*sutasomāsaḥ*)—holding the pressed Soma juices
- इंद्रं (*iṃdraṃ*)—Indra
- अर्कैः (*arkaiḥ*)—with hymns
- अभ्यर्चंति (*abhyarcaṃti*)—praise and worship
- शशमानाः (*śaśamānāḥ*)—those who worship Indra
- नरः (*naraḥ*)—the Aṅgirases, who are also leaders
- ऊर्वं (*ūrvaṃ*)—in the form of a mass
- गव्यं चित् (*gavyaṃ cit*)—that was filled with cattle
- अपिधानवंतं (*apidhānavaṃtaṃ*)—that was covered (by Vala)
- तं (*taṃ*)—the cave
- अप व्रन् (*apa vran*)—became able to open

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The sons of Aṅgiras, known as navagvas and daśagvas, ready to press the Soma juice and offer it to Indra, worship him with praises. The Aṅgirases, who praise Indra and are his leaders, became able to open the cave that was filled with a mass of cattle and covered by Vala.

##### English translation
The observers of the nine month's celebration, those of the ten months, pouring out libations, worship Indra with hymns : the leaders (of rites), glorifying him have set open the cave (concealing the cattle).

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

At the time of the Soma-drinking of the sacrifice called Daśapeya, when the Marutvatīya śastra mantras are recited, this Ṛk नवग्वासः (*navagvāsaḥ*) is to be recited as the opening Ṛk of the sūkta; this is set out by the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra, in the aphorism

### Printed p. 174

नवग्वासः सुतसोमास इंद्रं सखा ह यत्र सखिभिर्नवगैरिति निविद्दानयोराद्ये (*navagvāsaḥ sutasomāsa iṃdraṃ sakhā ha yatra sakhibhirnavagairiti nividdānayorādye*) (Ā. Sū. 9-3).

Among the Aṅgirasa seers it is the custom to call those who finish a sattra sacrifice and attain success in nine months navagva, and those who finish it in ten months daśagva. We have already explained the meaning of the words navagva and daśagva in Ṛ. Saṃ., Part 6, p. 24.

#### Ṛk 13

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> क॒थो नु ते॒ परि॑ चराणि वि॒द्वान्वी॒या॑ मघव॒न्या च॒कर्थ॑ ।  
> या चो॒ नु नव्या॑ कृ॒णवः॑ शविष्ठ॒ प्रेदु॒ ता ते॑ वि॒दथे॑षु  
> ब्रवाम ॥ १३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> कथो इति । नु । ते । परि । चराणि । विद्वान् । वीर्या । मघवन् । या । चकर्थ ।  
> या । चो इति । नु । नव्या । कृणवः । शविष्ठ । प्र । इत् । ऊं इति । ता । ते ।  
> विदथेषु । ब्रवाम ॥ १३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> परिक्रीनाम्मेकाहे मरुत्वतीयनिष्केवल्ययोः सूक्तमुखीये कथो नु त
> इत्याद्ये द्वे ऋचौ । सूत्रितं च । कथो नु ते परि चराणि विद्वानिति द्वे ।
> आ. ९-५. । इति ॥
> हे मघवन् धनवन्निंद्र त्वं या यानि वीर्या वीर्याणि चकर्थ चकृषे
> अहं तानि वीर्याणि विद्वान् जानन् ते तुभ्यं कथो नु कथं नु परि चराणि ।
> परिचर्यां करवाणि । हे शविष्ठ बलवत्तमेंद्र त्वं या चो यानि चैव नव्या
> नव्यानि नूतनानि वीर्याणि नु क्षिप्रं कृणवः कुर्याः ते तद्वियानि ता तानि
> वीर्याणि विदथेषु यज्ञेषु प्रेदु ब्रवाम । प्रैव वदाम ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

In the one-day rite called Parikrī, in the Marutvatīya and the Niṣkevalya, the first two Ṛks, कथो नु त (*katho nu ta*), are the sūktamukhīya. The sūtra says so: कथो नु ते परि चराणि विद्वानिति द्वे (*katho nu te pari carāṇi vidvāniti dve*) (Ā. Sū. 9-5). O wealthy Indra, knowing those heroic deeds which you have done, how shall I serve you, that is, perform service to you? O strongest Indra, whatever new heroic deeds you quickly do, those deeds of yours we shall proclaim at the sacrifices.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- मघवन् (*maghavan*)—O wealthy Indra
- या (*yā*)—which
- वीर्या (*vīryā*)—heroic deeds
- चकर्थ (*cakartha*)—you have accomplished, those
- विद्वान् (*vidvān*)—knowing
- ते (*te*)—to you

### Printed p. 175

- कथो नु (*katho nu*)—in what way
- परि चराणि (*pari carāṇi*)—can I offer worship
- शविष्ठ (*śaviṣṭha*)—O Indra, endowed with the greatest strength
- या चो (*yā co*)—whichever
- नव्या (*navyā*)—new heroic deeds
- नु (*nu*)—quickly
- कृणवः (*kṛṇavaḥ*)—you do
- ते (*te*)—of you, such
- तानि (*tāni*)—all those heroic deeds
- विदथेषु (*vidatheṣu*)—at the sacrifices
- प्रेदु ब्रवाम (*predu bravāma*)—we praise and proclaim

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O wealthy Indra, knowing fully the nature of the heroic deeds that you have accomplished, in what way can I offer worship to you? O Indra, endowed with the greatest strength, whichever new heroic deeds you do, all those heroic deeds of yours I shall praise and proclaim at the sacrifices.

##### English translation
Although, knowing the heroic acts which, Maghavan, you ahve performed, how may I adequately offer you adoration ? most mighty Indra, we ever celebrate at sacred rites the recent exploits which you have achieved.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

In the one-day rite called Parikrī, in the Marutvatīya and Niṣkevalya śastra mantras, this Ṛk कथो नु ते (*katho nu te*) and the next Ṛk are to be recited as the opening mantras; this is set out by the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra, कथो नु ते परि चराणि विद्वानिति द्वे (*katho nu te pari carāṇi vidvāniti dve*) (Ā. Sū. 9-5).

#### Ṛk 14

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ए॒ता विश्वा॑ चकृ॒वा इं॑द्र॒ भूर्यप॑रीतो ज॒नुषा॑ वी॒र्ये॑ण।  
> या चि॒न्नु व॑ज्रिन्कृ॒णवो॑ दधृ॒ष्टान्न ते॑व॒र्ता तवि॑ष्या आस्ति॒  ॑
> तस्याः ॥ १४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> एता । विश्वा । चकृऽवान् । इंद्र । भूरि । अपरिऽइतः । जनुषा । वीर्येण ॥  
> या । चित् । नु । वज्रिन् । कृणवः । दधृष्णान् । न । ते । वर्ता । तविष्याः । अस्ति ।  
> तस्याः ॥ १४ ॥

### Printed p. 176

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र अपरीतः शत्रुभिरपरिगतस्त्वं जनुषा जननसिद्धेनात्मीयेन वीर्येण
> बलेन भूरि भूरीणि बहून्येतैतानि प्रत्यक्षेणोपलभ्यमानानि विश्वा विश्वानि व्याप्तानि
> भुवनजातानि चकृवान् । कृतवानसि । हे वज्रिन् वज्रवन्निंद्र दधृष्टान् सपश्नानर्षयंस्त्वं नु क्षिप्रं या चिद्यानि च विश्वानि कृणवः कुर्याः ते त्वदीयस्य तस्यास्तविष्यास्तस्य बलस्य वर्ता निवारयिता न कोऽप्यस्ति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, unencompassed by your foes, by your innate strength you have made all these many things, which are perceived plainly, all that is pervasive and born in the worlds. O wielder of the thunderbolt, Indra, who bring your rivals low, whatever you quickly do, there is no one at all who can check that strength of yours.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- अपरीतः (*aparītaḥ*)—without opposition from anyone
- जनुषा (*januṣā*)—by your innate
- वीर्येण (*vīryeṇa*)—strength
- भूरि (*bhūri*)—abundant
- एता (*etā*)—plainly perceived
- विश्वा (*viśvā*)—the whole universe
- चकृवान् (*cakṛvān*)—you have made
- वज्रिन् (*vajrin*)—O Indra, who hold the thunderbolt
- दधृष्टान् (*dadhṛṣṭān*)—you who destroy your enemies
- नु (*nu*)—quickly
- या चित् (*yā cit*)—whatever deeds
- कृणवः (*kṛṇavaḥ*)—you do
- ते (*te*)—of you, such
- तस्याः तविष्याः (*tasyāḥ taviṣyāḥ*)—to that very power
- वर्ता (*vartā*)—an obstructer
- न अस्ति (*na asti*)—there is none

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, without anyone's opposition, by your innate strength alone you have made the whole universe, abundant and plainly perceived by all. O Indra, who hold the thunderbolt, you who destroy your enemies, whatever deeds you quickly do, there is no one who can obstruct that very power of yours.

##### English translation
Unmatched (by any), you have done, Indra, all these many ( deeds ) by your innate energy : wielder of the thunderbolt, whatever you, the humbler of (foes), have undertaken, there is no one the arrester of this your prowess.

#### Ṛk 15

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> इंद्र॒ ब्रह्म॑ क्रि॒यमा॑णा जुषस्व॒ या ते॑ शविष्ठ॒ नव्या॒ अक॑र्म ।  
> वस्त्रे॑व भ॒द्रा सुकृ॑ता वसू॒यू रथं॒ न धीरः॒ स्वपा॑ अतक्षं ॥

### Printed p. 177

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> इंद्र । ब्रह्म । क्रियमाणा । जुषस्व । या । ते । शविष्ठ । नव्याः । अकर्म ।  
> वस्त्रा इव । भद्रा । सुकृता । वसुयुः । रथं । न । धीरः । सुअपाः । अतक्षं ॥१५॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे शविष्ठ बलवत्तम शूरतम वेंद्र ते तुभ्यं या यानि स्तोत्राणि नव्या
> नूतना अद्यतना वयमकर्म अकुर्म हे इंद्र त्वं क्रियमाणास्माभिः क्रियमाणानि ब्रह्म ब्रह्माणि तानि स्तोत्राणि जुषस्व । सेवस्व । धीरो धीमान् स्वपाः
> शोभनकर्मा वसूयुर्धनकामोऽहं वस्त्रेव वस्त्राणीव भद्रा भद्राणि भजनीयानि
> सुकृता सुष्टु कृतानि स्तोत्राणि रथं न रथमिवातक्षु । अकरवं । स्तोत्राण्युपसंहारवद्ग्राह्याणीत्यभिप्रायेण वस्त्रनिदर्शनमुक्तं । रथदृष्टांतस्त्वागमनसाधनत्वप्रतिपादनाय । स्तोत्राणि स्वीकृत्य मह्यं धनं प्रयच्छेत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa takes the verse in this way. O Śaviṣṭha, most mighty and most heroic Indra, whatever new, present-day hymns we have made for you, those hymns, being made by us, you should accept and enjoy. "I, the wise one, of good works and desirous of wealth, have fashioned the hymns as one fashions a chariot": the hymns are thus likened to garments that are good and to be worn, and to a chariot. The comparison with garments is stated with the intention that the hymns should be accepted like something taken up and held in the hand; the example of the chariot is for showing that the hymns are a means of bringing Indra here. The meaning is: accept the hymns and give wealth to me. **NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- शविष्ठ (*śaviṣṭha*)—O most mighty one
- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- ते (*te*)—praising you
- नव्याः (*navyāḥ*)—new
- या (*yā*)—which hymns
- अकर्म (*akarma*)—we shall make (such)
- क्रियमाणा (*kriyamāṇā*)—made by us
- ब्रह्म (*brahma*)—the hymns
- जुषस्व (*juṣasva*)—accept and enjoy
- धीरः (*dhīraḥ*)—possessed of discriminating knowledge
- स्वपाः (*svapāḥ*)—possessed of excellent works
- वसूयुः (*vasūyuḥ*)—I, desirous of wealth
- वस्त्रेव (*vastreva*)—as one weaves garments
- रथं न (*rathaṃ na*)—as one builds a chariot
- भद्रा (*bhadrā*)—auspicious
- सुकृता (*sukṛtā*)—and most excellent hymns
- अतक्षं (*atakṣaṃ*)—I have fashioned

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O most mighty Indra, whatever new hymns we shall make in praise of you, accept and enjoy all those hymns of ours. I, who have discriminating knowledge, who am possessed of excellent works and desirous of wealth, have fashioned auspicious hymns, as one weaves garments and builds a chariot.

##### English translation
Most mighty Indra, be pleased to accept the prayers which we are about to offer, and the present praises which we repeat : firm, doing pious acts, and desirous of wealth, have I fabricated acceptable and pious works like (rich) garments, and like a chariot.

### Printed p. 178

## Sūkta 30 (Maṇḍala 5)

The thirtieth sūkta.

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> क्व स्य वीर इति पंचदशर्चं षोडशं सूक्तं । अत्रानुक्रमणिका । क्व स्य
> बभ्रुर्ऋणंचयोऽप्यत्र राजा स्तुत इति । बभ्रुर्ऋषिः । अनुक्तत्वात्रिष्टुप् । इंद्रो
> देवता । ऋणंचयनामा राजापि क्वचित्सूयते । अतः सोऽपि देवता । तीव्रसोमाख्य एकाहे मरुत्वतीयशस्त्र इदं सूक्तं निविद्वानं । सूत्रितं च । तीव्रसोमेनान्नाद्यकामः क्व स्य वीरः । अ. ९-७ । इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The sūkta beginning क्व स्य वीर (*kva sya vīra*) has fifteen Ṛks and is the sixteenth sūkta. Sāyaṇa cites the Anukramaṇikā: क्व स्य बभ्रुर्ऋणंचयोऽप्यत्र राजा स्तुत इति (*kva sya babhrurṛṇaṃcayo 'pyatra rājā stuta iti*). The seer is Babhru. Since no metre is stated, it is Triṣṭubh. The deity is Indra. A king named Ṛṇañcaya is also praised somewhere (in this sūkta), so he too is a deity. In the Tīvrasoma ekāha, in the Marutvatīya śastra, this sūkta is the nivid-sūkta. It is also prescribed in the sūtra: तीव्रसोमेनान्नाद्यकामः क्व स्य वीरः (*tīvrasomenānnādyakāmaḥ kva sya vīraḥ*) (Ā. Sū. 9-7).

Translation (Anuvāda): This sūkta, beginning क्व स्य वीरः (*kva sya vīraḥ*), is the sixteenth sūkta in the second Anuvāka. It contains fifteen Ṛks. In the Anukramaṇikā it is said: क्वंस्य बभ्रुर्ऋणंचयोऽप्यत्र राजा स्तुतः (*kvaṃsya babhrurṛṇaṃcayo 'pyatra rājā stutaḥ*). The seer of this sūkta is one named Babhru; Indra is the deity; Triṣṭubh is the metre. In this sūkta there is praise of a king named Ṛṇañcaya; therefore that king Ṛṇañcaya may also be called a deity of this sūkta. In the sacrifice called Tīvrasoma, performed for one day only, this sūkta is to be recited as the nivid-sūkta among the mantras of the Marutvatīya śastra; this is explained by the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra, in the sūtra तीव्र सोमेनान्नाद्यकामः क्व स्य वीरः (*tīvra somenānnādyakāmaḥ kva sya vīraḥ*) (Ā. Sū. 9-7).

Sūkta 30.

Maṇḍala 5, Anuvāka 2, Sūkta 30.

Aṣṭaka 4, Adhyāya 1, Varga 26, 27, 28.

Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 15.

Seer: Babhru Ātreya.

Deity: Indra and Ṛṇañcaya.

Metre: Triṣṭubh.

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> क्व१॒ं॑ स्य वी॒रः को अ॑पश्य॒दिंद्रं॑ सु॒खर॑थ॒मीय॑मानं॒ हरि॑भ्यां।  
> यो रा॒या व॒ज्री सु॒तसो॑ममि॒च्छन्तदोको॒ गंता॑ ॒ ॒
> पुरुहूत ऊती ॥ १ ॥

### Printed p. 179

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> क्व । स्यः । वीरः । कः । अपश्यत् । इंद्रं । सुखरथं । ईयमानं । हरिभ्यां ।  
> यः । राया । वज्री । सुत्यसोमं । इच्छन् । तत् ॥ ओकः । गंता । पुरुहूतः ।  
> ऊती ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> वज्री वज्रवान् पुरुहूतो बहुभिराहूतो य इंद्रो राया देयेन
> धनेन सह सुतसोममिच्छन् अभिषुतसोमं यजमानमन्विच्छन् ऊती ऊतैः
> रक्षायै तत्तस्य यजमानस्याको गृहं गंता प्राप्तो भवति वीरो विक्रांतः स्य स
> इंद्रः क्व कुत्र विद्यते । सुखरथं शोभनाक्षद्वारो रथो यस्य स सुखरथः । सुष्टु
> खनति लिखति भूमिमिति ना सुखं । तादृग्रथं । सुहितं खेभ्यः खं पुनः खनतेरिति
> यास्कः । नि. ३-१३ । हरिभ्यां स्ववाहनाभ्यामश्वाभ्यामीयमानं गच्छंतमिंद्रं
> कोऽपश्यत् । कश्च ददृशे ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Indra, the wielder of the thunderbolt and invoked by many, who desires the one who has pressed the Soma and, together with wealth to be given, goes to the house of that sacrificer for his protection: where is that hero Indra? Sāyaṇa glosses *vīra* as valiant, and *kva* as "where". *Sukharatha* is one whose chariot has a good axle-hole; the word *sukha* (good, comfortable) is explained as that which digs or scratches the ground well, such a chariot; and Sāyaṇa cites Yāska (Ni. 3-13) for *sukha* as what is beneficial to the senses, *kha* being derived from the root *khan*. *Harī* are his own two horses; who saw Indra going with these two, and who has seen him?

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वज्री (*vajrī*)—holder of the thunderbolt
- पुरूहूतः (*purūhūtaḥ*)—invoked by many
- यः (*yaḥ*)—which Indra
- सुतसोममिच्छन् (*sutasomamicchan*)—seeking the sacrificer who holds the pressed Soma
- राया (*rāyā*)—with wealth
- ऊती (*ūtī*)—for his protection
- तत् (*tat*)—of that sacrificer
- ओकः (*okaḥ*)—to the house
- गंता (*gaṃtā*)—who will go (such)
- वीरः (*vīraḥ*)—the hero
- स्यः (*syaḥ*)—that Indra
- क्व (*kva*)—where is he?
- सुखरथं (*sukharathaṃ*)—having a comfortable chariot
- हरिभ्यां (*haribhyāṃ*)—with the horses
- ईयमानं (*īyamānaṃ*)—who is going
- इंद्रं (*iṃdraṃ*)—Indra
- कः (*kaḥ*)—who
- अपश्यत् (*apaśyat*)—has seen?

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Where is that hero Indra, the holder of the thunderbolt, invoked by many, who seeks out the sacrificer who has pressed the Soma and holds it ready to offer him, and goes with wealth to the house of that sacrificer to give it to him? Who has seen Indra, who has a comfortable chariot and is going with his horses?

##### English translation
Where is that hero ? who has seen Indra seated in his easy chariot, travelling with his horses, the thunderer, the invoked of many, who, desirous of the libation, is proceeding with riches to the habitation (of his worshipper) for his preservation ?

### Printed p. 180

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

In this Ṛk the seer, besides describing Indra's qualities, asks "Where is Indra? Who has seen that Indra?", and in the next Ṛk he himself gives a kind of answer to these questions.

ओकः (*okaḥ*)—For this word Yāska says: ओक इति निवासनामोच्यते । (*oka iti nivāsanāmocyate |*) (Ni. 3-3). He has given for the word *oka* the meaning "dwelling-place", that is, the house one lives in.

वीरः (*vīraḥ*)—For the word *vīra* Yāska has given the etymology: वीरो वीरयत्यमित्रान् । वेतेर्वा स्याद्गतिकर्मणो वीरयतेर्वा (*vīro vīrayatyamitrān | vetervā syādgatikarmaṇo vīrayatervā*) (Ni. 1-7). That is, one who strikes the enemies in battle, routs them and makes them scatter is called a hero. Or, if the word *vīra* is thought to be formed from the root *vī*, meaning "to go", the meaning may be one who goes, that is, one who goes forward in battle. Or, if it is formed from the root *vīr*, the word *vīra* would mean one who has mighty strength. Thus he has explained three kinds of meaning.

सुखरथं (*sukharathaṃ*)—शोभानाक्षद्वारः रथो यस्य स सुखरथः । सुष्टु खनति लिखति भूमिमिति वा सुखं तादृग्रथं । (*śobhānākṣadvāraḥ ratho yasya sa sukharathaḥ | suṣṭu khanati likhati bhūmimiti vā sukhaṃ tādṛgrathaṃ |*). One having a comfortable chariot: since a soft seat is made ready in Indra's chariot, it is comfortable to sit in. Or the word *sukha* is so called because (the wheel of the chariot) goes marking the ground well. Or, for the word *sukha* Yāska has given the etymology सुखं कस्मात् सुहितं खेभ्यः । खं पुनः खनतेः (*sukhaṃ kasmāt suhitaṃ khebhyaḥ | khaṃ punaḥ khanateḥ*) (Ni. 3-13). The word *kha*, formed from the root *khan* meaning "to make into" **NOTE**, gives the meaning of being beneficial to the senses.

हरिभ्यां (*haribhyāṃ*)—स्ववाहनाभ्यां अश्विभ्यां (*svavāhanābhyāṃ aśvibhyāṃ*). Indra has two horses; they are called Harī. Explaining the vehicles of the various deities, Yāska says:
हरी इंद्रस्य । रोहितोऽग्नेः । हरित आदित्यस्य । रासभावश्विनोः । अजाः पूष्णः । पृषत्यो मरुतां । अरुण्यो गाव उषसः । श्यावाः सवितुः । विश्वरूपा बृहस्पतेः । नियुतो वायोः ॥ (*harī iṃdrasya | rohito 'gneḥ | harita ādityasya | rāsabhāvaśvinoḥ | ajāḥ pūṣṇaḥ | pṛṣatyo marutāṃ | aruṇyo gāva uṣasaḥ | śyāvāḥ savituḥ | viśvarūpā bṛhaspateḥ | niyuto vāyoḥ ‖*) (Ni. 2-28).
For Indra the vehicle is the two horses named Harī; for Agni a red horse; for Āditya green horses; for the Aśvin deities two asses; for the deity called Pūṣan, goats; for the Marut deities, spotted deer; for the deity Uṣas, red cows; for the deity Savitṛ, dusky red horses; for Bṛhaspati, horses of various and manifold forms; for Vāyu, the horse named Niyut. Thus he has described the various kinds of vehicles of the deities.
