#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अवा॑चचक्षं प॒दम॑स्य स॒स्वरु॒ग्रं नि॑धा॒तुरन्वा॑यमि॒च्छन् ।  
> अपृ॑च्छम॒न्या उ॒त ते म॑ आहु॒रिंद्रं॒ नरो॑ बुबुधा॒ना  ॑
> अशेम ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अव । अचचक्षं । पदं । अस्य । सस्वः । उग्रं । निधातुः । अनु । आयं । इच्छन् ।  
> अपृच्छं । अन्यान् । उत । ते । मे । आहुः । इंद्रं । नरः । बुबुधानः । अश्येम ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अस्येंद्रस्य पदं स्थानमवाचचक्षं । अहमवाद्राक्षं । कीदृशं । सस्वोऽंतर्हित मुग्रमुद्दूर्णं। किंच इच्छन्निंद्रमन्विच्छन्नहं;निधातुःस्वं स्थापयितुरस्येंद्रस्य
> पदमन्वायं । अन्वगमं । अन्यानुतान्यान्विदुषोपि मार्ग इंद्रमपृच्छं । पृष्टवानस्मि । पृष्टास्ते मे मह्यमिंद्रमाहुः । किमाहुरित्युच्यते । नरो यज्ञानां
> नेतारो बुबुधाना बुभुत्समाना वयमशेम । इंद्रं प्राप्तवंतः स्मः । यद्यपींद्रो विद्यते तथापि दुर्लभश्चेदनुपगंतव्य इति बुभुत्समानः स्वभावमपृच्छत्
> पृष्टैश्च सुलभ इत्युक्त इंद्रप्राप्त र्थ० सूक्तशेषमपश्यदित्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"I have looked at the place of this Indra": *sasva* is hidden (concealed), *ugra* is raised up, fierce. Desiring Indra, I followed up the place of this Indra, who establishes (his own place). I also asked others, the learned, along the way about Indra. Those who were asked told me about Indra. What did they say? "We, the leaders of sacrifices, desiring to know, have attained Indra." Although Indra exists, if he is hard to attain and not to be approached, the seer, desiring to know, asked about his nature; and when, on being asked, he was told that Indra is easy to obtain, he saw the remainder of the sūkta for the sake of attaining Indra. **NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अस्य (*asya*)—of Indra
- सस्वः (*sasvaḥ*)—hidden
- उग्रं (*ugraṃ*)—and terrible
- पदं (*padaṃ*)—the place
- अवाचचक्षं (*avācacakṣaṃ*)—I have looked down upon
- निधातुः (*nidhātuḥ*)—(the place) of Indra, who is self-established
- इच्छन् (*icchan*)—with desire to see
- अन्वायं (*anvāyaṃ*)—I went to that region
- उत (*uta*)—and
- अन्यान् (*anyān*)—other learned men also
- अपृच्छं (*apṛcchaṃ*)—I asked
- नरः (*naraḥ*)—the leaders of the sacrifice
- बुबुधानाः (*bubudhānāḥ*)—and knowers of Indra's place, we
- अशेम (*aśema*)—let us obtain the nearness of Indra (thus)
- ते (*te*)—they
- मे (*me*)—to me
- इंद्रं (*iṃdraṃ*)—concerning Indra
- आहुः (*āhuḥ*)—said

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

I have looked down upon the hidden and terrible place of Indra. Desiring to approach and behold the place of Indra, who is self-established, I went to that region. And in this matter I asked other learned men. They, the leaders of the sacrifice and knowers of Indra's place, said, "Let us obtain Indra."

### Printed p. 182

##### English translation
I have looked down upon the secret and fearful place of his abiding ; I have repaired, desiring him, (to the place of ) the self-sustainer ; I have inquired (of him) from others : they, the leaders (of rites), the searchers after wisdom, have said to me, let us have recourse to Indra.

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> प्र नु व॒यं सु॒ते या ते॑ कृ॒तानींद्र॒ ब्रवा॑म॒ यानि॑ नो॒  ॑
> जुजोषः ।  
> वेद॒दवि॑द्वाञ्छ॒णव॑च्च वि॒द्वान्वह॑ते॒ऽयं म॒घवा॒ सर्व॑सेनः ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> प्र । नु । वयं । सुते या । ते । कृतानि । इंद्र । ब्रवाम । यानि । नः । जुजोषः ।  
> वेदत् । अविद्वान् । शृणवत् । च । विद्वान् । वहते । अयं । मघवा ।  
> सर्वसेनः ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र ते त्वदीयानि यानि कृतानि कर्माणि संति नु अद्य वयं स्तोतारः
> सुते सोमेऽभिसुते सति तानि कर्माणि प्र ब्रवाम । प्रकर्षण वदाम । त्वमपि
> नोऽस्मदर्थं यानि कर्माणि जुजोषः असेवथाः तानि कर्माण्यविद्वान् इतः
> पूर्वमजानन् जनो वेदत् । जानातु । विद्वान् शृणवच्च । शृणुयाच्च । यद्वा ।
> विद्वान् जानन् पुरुषः शृणवच्च । अजानंतमपि जनं श्रावयेच्च । सर्वसेनः
> सर्वाभिः सेनाभिर्युक्तो मघवा धनवान् विद्वान् जानन्नयमिंद्रो जानतश्च
> शृण्वतश्च तान् जनान् प्रति वहते। अश्वैरुह्यते॥ व्यत्ययेन कर्तृप्रत्ययः॥ प्रापयति॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, whatever deeds of yours have been done, those deeds we, the praisers, now declare forth, when the Soma has been pressed. And whatever deeds you have enjoyed for our sake, those deeds let the man who does not know them, who has not known them before, come to know, and let the one who knows hear them. Or else: let the knowing man hear, and let him make even the one who does not know hear. This Indra, endowed with all armies, wealthy, knowing, carries to those people who know and who hear; that is, he is borne by horses, and the active form is used by inversion (of voice); he brings them (to their goal).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- ते (*te*)—your
- या कृतानि (*yā kṛtāni*)—those heroic deeds that there are
- नु (*nu*)—now
- वयं (*vayaṃ*)—we, the praisers
- सुते (*sute*)—when the Soma has been pressed and made ready for offering
- प्रब्रवाम (*prabravāma*)—we praise
- नः (*naḥ*)—for our help
- यानि (*yāni*)—which

### Printed p. 183

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- जुजोषः (*jujoṣaḥ*)—which you practise, those (deeds)
- अविद्वान् (*avidvān*)—the man who does not know
- वेदत् (*vedat*)—let him know
- विद्वान् (*vidvān*)—he who knows its greatness
- शृणवच्च (*śṛṇavacca*)—let him also declare it so that others may know
- सर्वसेनः (*sarvasenaḥ*)—endowed with all armies
- मघपा (*maghapā*)—wealthy
- अयं (*ayaṃ*)—this Indra
- वहते (*vahate*)—is borne by horses

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, we, the praisers, while holding the Soma pressed for offering to you, praise all your heroic deeds. Whatever deeds you perform for our help, let the people who do not know them come to know them. Let those who know their greatness declare them so that others may know. Indra, endowed with all armies and wealthy, is borne by horses.

##### English translation
When the libation is offered, Indra, we celebrate your exploits ; those (exploits) which you have been pleased (to achieve) for us : let him who is ignorant acquire knowledge (of them); let him who is acquainted(with them) make them known : this Maghavan (is) the lord of hosts.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

The valiant deeds of Indra are many. Some know them, some do not; so that people who do not know them may hear and learn, the seer says, "I praise those valiant deeds of Indra."

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स्थि॒रं मन॑श्च कृषे जा॒त इं॑द्र॒ वेषीदेको॑ यु॒धये॒ भूय॑सश्चित् ।  
> अश्मा॑नं चि॒च्छव॑सा दिद्युतो॒ वि वि॒दो गवा॑मू॒र्वमु॒स्रिया॑णां ॥ ४ ॥

### Printed p. 184

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> स्थिरं । मनः । चकृषे । जातः । इंद्र । वेषि । इत् । एकः । युधये । भूयसः ।  
> चित् ।  
> अश्मानं । चित् । शवसा । दिद्युतः । वि । विदः । गवां । ऊर्वं । उस्रियाणां ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र जात उत्पन्नमात्र एव त्वं स्थिरं चलनरहितं मनश्चित्तं चकृषे ।
> चकर्थ । सर्वान् बलिष्ठान् जयेयमितीदृशं । तथा हे इंद्र एक इदसहाय एव
> त्वं युधये युद्धाय भूयसश्चित् बहुतरान् राक्षसादीनपि वेषि । अनेः ।
> अगमः । किंच अश्मानं चित् गवामावरकं पर्वतमपि शवसा बलेन वि दिद्युतः ।
> व्यभिनः। किंच उस्रियाणां क्षीरमुत्सारयंतीनां गवां धेनूनामूर्वं समूहं
> विदः । अवेदयः । अलंभयः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, as soon as you were born you made your mind steady, immovable, with the resolve "may I conquer all the mighty." Likewise, O Indra, alone and without helper, you went to battle against even many more, Rākṣasas and others; Sāyaṇa glosses वेषि (*veṣi*) with "you went". **NOTE** Further, you split by your strength even the rock, the enclosure of the cows (अश्मानं (*aśmānaṃ*) explained as the mountain that encloses the cows; वि दिद्युतः (*vi didyutaḥ*) as "you broke"). Further, you found, that is, obtained, the herd of the milk-yielding cows, the उस्रियाणां (*usriyāṇāṃ*), which send forth milk.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- जातः (*jātaḥ*)—as soon as you were born
- स्थिरं (*sthiraṃ*)—immovable
- मनः (*manaḥ*)—a mental resolve (for the destruction of enemies)
- चकृषे (*cakṛṣe*)—you have made
- एक इत् (*eka it*)—you alone, without other help
- युधये (*yudhaye*)—for battle
- भूयसश्चित् (*bhūyasaścit*)—even enemies exceedingly numerous
- वेषि (*veṣi*)—you have gone to meet
- आश्मानं चित् (*āśmānaṃ cit*)—even the rocks of the mountain (that confined the cows)
- शवसा (*śavasā*)—by your strength
- वि दिद्युतः (*vi didyutaḥ*)—you have split
- उस्रियाणां (*usriyāṇāṃ*)—milk-giving
- गवां (*gavāṃ*)—of cows
- ऊर्वं (*ūrvaṃ*)—the herd
- विदः (*vidaḥ*)—you obtained

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, as soon as you were born you made a steady resolve for the destruction of your enemies. Without the help of others, you alone went to battle against exceedingly numerous enemies. By your strength you split even the rocks of the mountain that confined the cows. You obtained the herd of milk-giving cows.

##### English translation
As soon as generated, Indra, you have made your mind resolved : you have gone alone to contend against numerous (foes) : you have rent asunder the rock by your strength : you have rescued the herd of milk-yielding cows.

### Printed p. 185

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

अश्मानं चित् शवसा वि दिद्युतः (*aśmānaṃ cit śavasā vi didyutaḥ*)—गवामावरकं पर्वतमपि बलेन व्यभिनः । (*gavāmāvarakaṃ parvatamapi balena vyabhinaḥ |*) Here a matter of ancient history seems to be indicated. In former times the Asuras called Paṇis carried off the cows of the gods and hid them in the cave of a mountain, so that they could be seen by no one. Indra learned this account from the divine bitch Saramā **NOTE**, went there, split the great stone slab that lay across the mouth of the mountain's cave, and released the cows; this is described in many places in the Ṛgveda. In some Ṛks it is said that Agni himself did this deed. It is described here and there that Bṛhaspati or the seer Aṅgiras too had a part in this story.

उस्रियाणां (*usriyāṇāṃ*)—उस्रियेति गोनाम । उत्साविणोऽस्यां भोगाः । उस्रेति च । (*usriyeti gonāma | utsāviṇo 'syāṃ bhogāḥ | usreti ca |*) (Ni. 4-19). *Usriyā* is a name of the cow; it is also said *usrā*. For from this cow enjoyments, or milk and the like (milk, curds, butter, ghee and so on) that are the means of enjoyment, flow forth, that is, are milked. Therefore *usriyāḥ* or *usrāḥ* is the name of cows; thus he has explained the meaning of this word.

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> प॒रो यत्त्वं प॑र॒म आ॒जनि॑ष्ठाः परा॒वति॒ श्रुत्यं॒ नाम॒ बिभ्र॑त् ।  
> अत॑श्चि॒दिंद्रा॑दभयंत दे॒वा विश्वा॑ अ॒पो अ॑जयद्दा॒सप॑त्निः ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> परः । यत् । त्वं । परमः । आजनिष्ठाः । परावति । श्रुत्यं । नाम । बिभ्रत् ।  
> अतः । चित् । इंद्रात् । अभयंत । देवाः । विश्वाः । अपः । अजयत् । दासपत्निः ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> यद्यदा हे इंद्र परः परस्तादुपरिष्टात्स्थितः परम उत्कृष्टतमस्त्वं परावति दूरे श्रुत्यं श्रवणीयं नाम नामधेयं बिभ्रत् धारयन्नाजनिष्ठाः आ समंतादजायथाः । शेषः परोक्षकृतः । अतश्चिदत आरभ्यैव देवा द्योतमाना अग्न्या-

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

When you, O Indra, situated above, the most excellent, were born, bearing a name to be heard of far away (परावति (*parāvati*) glossed as "far off", श्रुत्यं (*śrutyaṃ*) as "worthy to be heard"), the remainder of the verbs is in the sense of the indirect past. From then on, the gods, shining, Agni and the rest,

### Printed p. 186

> दय इंद्रादभयंत । बिभ्युः। विश्वा अपः सर्वाण्युदकानींद्रोऽजयत् । वशीचक्रे।
> केदृशीः । दासपत्निः । दासो वृत्रः पतिः पालयिता यासामपां ता इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

feared Indra. Indra conquered all the waters, that is, brought them under his control. What kind of waters? दासपत्निः (*dāsapatniḥ*): those waters of which the *dāsa*, Vṛtra, is the lord, the protector.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यत् (*yat*)—(O Indra) when
- परः (*paraḥ*)—situated in the highest region
- परमः (*paramaḥ*)—and most excellent
- त्वं (*tvaṃ*)—you
- परावति (*parāvati*)—in a very distant region
- श्रुत्यं (*śrutyaṃ*)—very famous
- नाम (*nāma*)—name
- बिभ्रत् (*bibhrat*)—bearing
- आ जनिष्ठाः (*ā janiṣṭhāḥ*)—you were born
- अतश्चित् (*ataścit*)—immediately thereafter
- देवाः (*devāḥ*)—the gods, Agni and the rest
- इंद्रात् (*iṃdrāt*)—of you, the lord
- अभयंत (*abhayaṃta*)—were afraid
- दासपत्निः (*dāsapatniḥ*)—having Vṛtra himself as husband
- विश्वा अपः (*viśvā apaḥ*)—all the waters
- अजयत् (*ajayat*)—you brought under your control

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, you who are situated in the highest region and are the most excellent, as soon as you were born in a very distant region, possessing a most famous name, the gods, Agni and the rest, were afraid of you, the lord. You brought under your control all the waters, the wives of Vṛtra.

##### English translation
When you have been born most excellent and supreme, bearing a name widely renowned, then the gods have been in dread of Indra, and has subjugated all the waters, the brides of the slave (Vritra).

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

दासपत्निः (*dāsapatniḥ*)—दासो वृत्रः पतिः पालयिता यासामहां ता इति दासपत्निः । (*dāso vṛtraḥ patiḥ pālayitā yāsāmahāṃ tā iti dāsapatniḥ |*) The meaning is: the waters (gathered in the cloud) of which the slave, the demon Vṛtra, is the protector. This word *dāsapatnīḥ* is read also in the Ṛk

दासपत्नीरहिगोपा अतिष्ठन्निरुद्धा आपः पणिनेव गावः । (*dāsapatnīrahigopā atiṣṭhanniruddhā āpaḥ paṇineva gāvaḥ |*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-32-11). There the word is explained: दासपत्नीः दासो विश्वोपक्षपणहेतुर्वत्रः पतिः स्वामी यासामपां ता दासपत्नीः (*dāsapatnīḥ dāso viśvopakṣapaṇaheturvatraḥ patiḥ svāmī yāsāmapāṃ tā dāsapatnīḥ*); that is, Vṛtra, who was causing distress to the whole world, is called *dāsa*; the waters that were under that slave's sway and held back by him, that is, held back in the region of the clouds without pouring down rain, are called *dāsapatnīḥ*. They are the waters of which Vṛtra is the husband or lord. Since Vṛtra is in the form of a cloud, he is said to be the lord of the waters in the cloud; the meaning is that these are the waters under his authority, that is, under his control. Explaining the above Ṛk, Yāska says:

### Printed p. 187

दासपत्नीर्दार्साधिपत्यः । दासो दस्यतेरुपदासयति कर्माणि ॥ (*dāsapatnīrdārsādhipatyaḥ | dāso dasyaterupadāsayati karmāṇi ‖*)

(Ni. 2-17). *Dāsa* means one who works, who lives by a low occupation. Because the waters protect, that is, preside over (अधिष्ठाय पांति (*adhiṣṭhāya pāṃti*)) such workers, they are called अधिपत्न्यः (*adhipatnyaḥ*). Since the worker, wearied by his work, drinks water, rests and is comfortable, these waters are said to give him protection. Therefore the word *dāsapatnīḥ* must be said to mean the waters that refresh the worker. For the word *dāsa* the derivation is दस्यतेः उपदासयति कर्माणि । (*dasyateḥ upadāsayati karmāṇi |*): one who does works such as ploughing is to be called *dāsa*. Besides this, the Āryan people used to call the former inhabitants of this country by the names *dāsa* and *dasyu*. The former inhabitants of this country, uncivilised, rough and dark-skinned, who did not worship the gods, did not perform sacrifices and the like, created obstacles to sacrificial rites, were given to evil practices such as theft, and gave the Āryan people troubles of many kinds and fought with them, were called by the Āryans *dāsa* and *dasyu*. Therefore at that time the names *asura*, *dāsa* and *dasyu* had become current for people of evil conduct. Vṛtra, too, since he held back the rain-water useful to people so that it did not fall on the earth, and caused harm to people, is in many places called by the very name *dāsa*. In designating the waters that were under the sway of this slave, the demon Vṛtra, the words दासपत्निः अपः (*dāsapatniḥ apaḥ*) are used in the present Ṛk also.

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> तुभ्येदे॒ते म॒रुतः॑ सु॒शेवा॒ अर्चं॑त्य॒र्कं सु॒न्वंत्यंधः॑ ।  
> अहि॑मोहा॒नम॒प आ॒ शया॑नं॒ प्र मा॒याभि॑र्मा॒यिनं॑ सक्ष॒दिंद्रः॑ ॥ ६ ॥

### Printed p. 188

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> तुभ्य । इत् । एते । मरुतः । सुशेवाः । अर्चंति । अर्कं । सुन्वंति । अंधः ।  
> अहिं । ओहानं । अपः । आशयानं । प्र । मायाभिः । मायिनं । सक्षत् । इंद्रः ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> एते मरुतः । महद्रवंति वदंतीति मरुतः स्तोतारः । सुशेवाः । शेवमिति
> सुखनामैतत् । शोभनसुखाः संतः । स्तोत्रेण शोभनं सुखमुत्पादयंत
> इत्यर्थः । हे इंद्र तुभ्येत्तुभ्यमेवार्कमर्चनीयं स्तोत्रमर्चंति । स्तुवंति
> कुर्वंतीत्यर्थः । अंधः सोमलक्षणमन्नं सुन्वंति । अभिषवं कुर्वंति । इंद्रः
> परमैश्वर्ययुक्तो मायाभिः स्वकीयाभिः शक्तिभिरहिं वृत्रं प्रसक्षत् । अभ्यभवत् ।
> क्रीदृशं । ओहानं देवान्बाधमानमप उदकान्याशयानमावृत्य शयनं कुर्वंतं
> मायिनं कपटवंतमिति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

These Maruts are the praisers (those who sound forth loudly); सुशेवाः (*suśevāḥ*): *śeva* is a name for happiness, so they, being of good happiness, produce good happiness by their praise. O Indra, for you alone they offer the अर्कं (*arkaṃ*), the hymn to be adored; that is, they praise. They press the अंधः (*aṃdhaḥ*), the food in the form of Soma, that is, they perform the pressing. Indra, possessed of supreme lordship, overcame by his own powers (his *māyā*) the serpent Vṛtra; of what kind? ओहानं (*ohānaṃ*), harassing the gods; आशयानं (*āśayānaṃ*), lying covering the waters; and मायिनं (*māyinaṃ*), deceitful.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- एते मरुतः (*ete marutaḥ*)—(O Indra) these praisers
- सुशेवाः (*suśevāḥ*)—causing excellent happiness
- तुभ्यत् (*tubhyat*)—addressing you alone
- अर्कं (*arkaṃ*)—a hymn
- अर्चंति (*arcaṃti*)—fashion and praise
- अंधः (*aṃdhaḥ*)—the food in the form of Soma
- सुन्वंति (*sunvaṃti*)—press (for you)
- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—the lord Indra
- मायाभिः (*māyābhiḥ*)—by his extraordinary powers
- ओहानं (*ohānaṃ*)—who harasses (the gods)
- अपः (*apaḥ*)—the waters
- आशयानं (*āśayānaṃ*)—who lay covering
- मायिनं (*māyinaṃ*)—and is deceitful
- अहिं (*ahiṃ*)—Vṛtra
- प्र सक्षत् (*pra sakṣat*)—defeated

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, these praisers, causing excellent happiness, fashion a hymn addressed to you alone and praise you. They press for you the food in the form of Soma. The lord Indra, by his extraordinary powers, defeated Vṛtra, who harassed the gods, who lay covering the waters, and who was deceitful.

##### English translation
These devoted Maruts eulogise you with pious praise, and pour out to you the sacrificial food : Indra has overcome by his devices the guileful Ahi, harassing the gods and arresting the waters.

### Printed p. 189

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

मरुतः (*marutaḥ*)—महत् रवंति वदंतीति मरुतः स्तोतारः । (*mahat ravaṃti vadaṃtīti marutaḥ stotāraḥ |*) The commentator explains the parts of the word *marutaḥ* thus: those who sound forth with a great loud voice, that is, who recite hymns of praise loudly, the praisers.

सुशेवाः (*suśevāḥ*)—शेवमिति सुखनामैतत् । शोभनसुखाः संतः । स्तोत्रेण शोभनं सुखमुत्पादयंत इत्यर्थः । (*śevamiti sukhanāmaitat | śobhanasukhāḥ saṃtaḥ | stotreṇa śobhanaṃ sukhamutpādayaṃta ityarthaḥ |*) The word *śeva* is read among the names of happiness (Ni. 3-13). Concerning this word Yāska says:

शेव इति सुखनाम शिष्यतेर्वकारो नामकरणोऽन्तस्थांतरोपलिंगी । विभाषितगुणः शिवमित्यप्यस्य भवति ॥ (*śeva iti sukhanāma śiṣyatervakāro nāmakaraṇo 'ntasthāṃtaropaliṃgī | vibhāṣitaguṇaḥ śivamityapyasya bhavati ‖*)

(Ni. 18-18). **NOTE**

The word *śeva* means happiness. This word is formed from the root *śiṣ*. How: the letter ṣ in the middle of the root *śiṣ* (*śiṣyati*) is counted as a semivowel. The semivowels are the letters ya, ra, la, va; of these semivowels the letter va takes the place of the letter ṣ. This is what is called *nāmakaraṇa*. Therefore, when the ṣ of the root *śiṣ* is dropped, only the letter *śi* remains. When the semivowel va is added to it as an affix, *śiva* results. When the guṇa comes to the letter *śi* and it becomes *śe*, the word *śeva* results. The meaning intended is that the word *śiva* is also in use.

इत् (*it*)—This word in the Ṛgveda, and also in the other Vedas, indicates the meaning of emphasis, *eva*. In some contexts it has no meaning at all and is used to fit the letters required in the metre, that is, as a filler of the line. Yāska says:

अथ ये प्रवृत्तेऽर्थेऽमिताक्षरेषु ग्रंथेषु वाक्यपूरणा आगच्छंति पदपूरणास्ते मिताक्षरेष्वनर्थकाः कमीमिद्विति । (*atha ye pravṛtte 'rthe 'mitākṣareṣu graṃtheṣu vākyapūraṇā āgacchaṃti padapūraṇāste mitākṣareṣvanarthakāḥ kamīmidviti |*)

(Ni. 1-9). That is: the particles *kam*, *īm*, *it*, *u* are used for completing a sentence in works that are in prose, not bound by metre, and for completing a line in works of measured syllables, that is, in metres that contain a fixed number of syllables. In such contexts he says these particles have no definite meaning. Yet according to context various meanings of these words are also found stated. For the word *it* in the present passage the meaning of the word *eva* is the usual one in practice. In the present Ṛk, for the words *tubhyet* (*tubhya* + *it*) the commentator too has explained the meaning as "to you alone".

### Printed p. 190

अर्कं (*arkaṃ*)—अर्चनीयं स्तोत्रं । अथवा अर्चंत्येभिरर्काः मंत्राः । (*arcanīyaṃ stotraṃ | athavā arcaṃtyebhirarkāḥ maṃtrāḥ |*) *Arka* means a hymn of praise. Because they are used for praising, mantras or hymns are called *arka*s. Here *arkam* means a hymn of praise composed of such mantras that are to be adored. Yāska has stated four kinds of meaning for this word:

अर्कः । अर्को देवो भवति यदेनमर्चति । (*arkaḥ | arko devo bhavati yadenamarcati |*)
अर्को मंत्रोभवति यदनेनार्चंति ॥ (*arko maṃtrobhavati yadanenārcaṃti ‖*)
अर्कमन्नं भवत्यर्चति भूतानि । (*arkamannaṃ bhavatyarcati bhūtāni |*)
अर्को वृक्षो भवति संवृत्तः कटुकिम्ना ॥ (*arko vṛkṣo bhavati saṃvṛttaḥ kaṭukimnā ‖*)

(Ni. 5-4). Four kinds of meaning of the word *arka* are stated in this way (Ni. 5-4).

देवः (*devaḥ*)—तावत्सर्व एवार्क इत्युच्यते यदस्मादेनं देवमर्चंति स्तोतारः । (*tāvatsarva evārka ityucyate yadasmādenaṃ devamarcaṃti stotāraḥ |*) *Arka* means a deity. For it is that person or god whom devotees use it to worship; that is, the object worshipped by devotees (the deity) is called *arka*.

अर्को मंत्रो भवति यत् यस्मात् अनेन मंत्रेण अर्चंति स्तोतारः तस्मादर्कः । (*arko maṃtro bhavati yat yasmāt anena maṃtreṇa arcaṃti stotāraḥ tasmādarkaḥ |*) *Arka* is also understood to mean a mantra. For the praisers worship the deity with this (the *arka*, or mantra). Therefore the word *arka* also means a mantra.

अर्कमन्नं भवति—तद्धि अर्चति भूतानि यो हि पूज्यः तस्यान्नमपि संस्कारविशेषेण संस्कृतं पूजायां क्रियते, पूज्यं च सर्वस्यॆवान्नं । (*arkamannaṃ bhavati—taddhi arcati bhūtāni yo hi pūjyaḥ tasyānnamapi saṃskāraviśeṣeṇa saṃskṛtaṃ pūjāyāṃ kriyate, pūjyaṃ ca sarvasyèvānnaṃ |*) *Arka* is also understood to mean food. Since food (nourishment) is necessary to all living beings, everyone respects and desires food with regard; that is, because all honour and wish for it with love and respect, the word *arka* means food.

अर्को वृक्षो भवति य एष प्रसिद्धः । संवृत्तः हि संगतः संव्याप्तः कटुकिम्मा =कटुकभावेन भवति । (*arko vṛkṣo bhavati ya eṣa prasiddhaḥ | saṃvṛttaḥ hi saṃgataḥ saṃvyāptaḥ kaṭukimmā =kaṭukabhāvena bhavati |*) Further, the meaning of a particular tree for the word *arka* is well known. The *arka* tree is called in the Karnataka language the *ekka* plant. Because its sacrificial sticks are used in homa and other rites, and its milk and leaves are customarily used in medicines and the like, the *arka* tree is a thing known to all. Because it is bitter it is called *kaṭukimmā*.

अंधः (*aṃdhaḥ*)—सोमलक्षणवन्नं । (*somalakṣaṇavannaṃ |*) The word *andhaḥ* is read among the twenty-eight names of food (Ni. 3-9). Although this word has the meaning of food, its use in contexts that designate the Soma juice is a special custom in the Ṛgveda. Yāska says:

### Printed p. 191

अंधः । अंध इत्यन्ननामाध्यानीयं भवति । .... तमोऽप्यंध उच्यते नास्मिन् ध्यानं भवति । न दर्शनमंधंतम इत्यभिभाषंते । अयमपीतरो एतस्मादेव । पश्यदक्षण्वान्न विचेतदंधः इत्यपि निगमो भवति ॥ (*aṃdhaḥ | aṃdha ityannanāmādhyānīyaṃ bhavati | .... tamo 'pyaṃdha ucyate nāsmin dhyānaṃ bhavati | na darśanamaṃdhaṃtama ityabhibhāṣaṃte | ayamapītaro etasmādeva | paśyadakṣaṇvānna vicetadaṃdhaḥ ityapi nigamo bhavati ‖*)

(Ni. 5-1). Thus he has stated the etymology of this word. The general meaning of the word *andhas* is food. For all desire this food, that is, pray that it may come to them. The word *andhaḥ* also means darkness. For in darkness it is possible neither to see anything nor to concentrate the mind and reflect. People commonly use the word अंधंतमः (*aṃdhaṃtamaḥ*) in the sense of pitch darkness, deep gloom in which nothing is seen. These two meanings are formed from one and the same word (or root). Yāska has given as an example the Vedic sentence पश्यदक्ष्णान्न विचेतदंधः (*paśyadakṣṇānna vicetadaṃdhaḥ*), which means that one who has eyes sees, the blind cannot see.

मायाभिः (*māyābhiḥ*)—स्वकीयाभिः शक्तिभिः । (*svakīyābhiḥ śaktibhiḥ |*) By his own powers and capacities; the word *māyā* is read among the names of intelligence (Ni. 3-13). And Yāska, in giving the explanation of the Ṛk beginning मूर्धा भुवो (*mūrdhā bhuvo*), says मायां-प्रज्ञां (*māyāṃ-prajñāṃ*) (Ni. 7-27), and in explaining the Ṛk beginning शुक्रं ते अन्यत् (*śukraṃ te anyat*) he explains the words विश्वा हि मायाः (*viśvā hi māyāḥ*) as सर्वाणि प्रज्ञनानि (*sarvāṇi prajñanāni*) (Ni. 12-17). Where this word is used of the Asuras, and in some contexts of Indra, the word *māyā* is used in the senses of crooked means, intrigue and the like.

#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> वि षू मृधो॑ ज॒नुषा॒ दान॒मिन्व॒न्नह॒न्नवा॑ मघवन्त्संचका॒नः।  
> अत्रा॑ दा॒सस्य॒ नमु॑चेः॒ शिरो॒ यदव॑र्तयो॒ मन॑वे गा॒तुमि॒च्छन् ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> वि । सु । मृधः । जनुषा । दानं । इन्वन् । अहन् । गवा । मघवन् । संचकानः ।  
> अत्र । दासस्य । नमुचेः । शिरः । यत् । अवर्तय: । मनवे । गातुं । इच्छन् ॥ ७ ॥

### Printed p. 192

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे सुघवन्थनवन्निंद्र संचकानोऽस्माभिः स्तूयमानस्त्वं ॥ संपूर्वः
> कायतिः शब्दकर्मा । तस्य लडर्थे लिटि रूपु ॥ दानं देवानां बाधकं वृत्रमसुरं
> गवा वज्रेणेन्वन् प्रेरयन् हिंसन् जनुषा जन्मना मृधस्तदनुचरान्राक्षसादीन्
> शत्रुन् सु सुष्टु व्यहन् । विजघंथ । इंद्रस्यासुरा जन्मारभ्य शत्रव इत्यर्थः । हे
> इंद्र त्वमत्रास्मिन्युद्धे मनवे नमुचिनापहृतगोधनाय मह्यं गातुं सुखमिच्छन् अभिलषन् नमुचेरेतन्नामकस्योपक्षपयितुरसुरस्य संबंधि शिरः शीर्षं
> यद्यदावर्तयः अचूर्णयः तदा शत्रुन् विजघंथेति पूर्वेण संबंधः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, praised by us, you, संचकानः (*saṃcakānaḥ*) as the word is explained, impelling and killing with the thunderbolt (here called गवा (*gavā*)) the Dānava, that is, the demon Vṛtra who obstructs the gods, struck down well, by your very birth, the मृधः (*mṛdhaḥ*), his followers, Rākṣasas and other enemies. The meaning is that the Asuras are Indra's enemies from his birth. O Indra, in this battle, desiring ease (*gātu* is glossed as happiness) for Manu, that is, for me, whose wealth of cattle was carried off by Namuci, when you crushed the head of the Asura named Namuci, who torments, then you struck down the enemies: this is the connection with the first part. **NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- मघवन् (*maghavan*)—O Indra, the wealthy
- अत्र (*atra*)—in this battle
- मनवे (*manave*)—to me, who belong to Manu
- गातुं (*gātuṃ*)—happiness
- इच्छन् (*icchan*)—desiring
- नमचीः (*namacīḥ*)—named Namuci
- दासस्य (*dāsasya*)—of the Asura
- शिरः (*śiraḥ*)—the head
- यत् (*yat*)—when
- अवर्तयः (*avartayaḥ*)—you cut off, then
- संचकानः (*saṃcakānaḥ*)—you, praised by us
- दानं (*dānaṃ*)—the Asura who injures the gods
- गवा (*gavā*)—with the thunderbolt
- इन्वन् (*invan*)—injuring
- जनुषा (*januṣā*)—by birth, by natural quality
- मृधः (*mṛdhaḥ*)—the followers of that injurious Asura also
- सु (*su*)—completely
- वि अहन् (*vi ahan*)—you have destroyed

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O wealthy Indra, in this battle, in order to bring happiness to me, who belong to Manu, when you cut off the head of the Asura named Namuci, then, praised by us, injuring with the thunderbolt the Asura who injures the gods, you have completely destroyed also his followers, who are injurious by birth, by natural quality.

##### English translation
Maghavan, you are glorified by us ; assailing with the thunderbolt the antagonist (of the gods), you have slain those who were ever hostile (to you) from your birth : desiring to do good to Manu, you have bruised the head of the slaves Namuchi.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

In this Ṛk and in the next two Ṛks the account of Indra's slaying of the Rākṣasa named Namuci is described. After giving a brief introduction to this Namuci, we shall explain how Indra killed him.

### Printed p. 193

The name Namuci occurs in the Ṛgveda nine times, and besides this the name is found also in the Vājasaneya Saṃhitā, the Taittirīya Brāhmaṇa (Tai. Brā. 1-7-1-6, 7), the Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa (Śa. Brā. 12-7-1-10) and other places. He is spoken of in the Ṛgveda as of Asura nature and as *dāsa* (Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-30-7, 8). In one place (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-53-7) he is described as *māyin*. It is said in the Ṛgveda that when Indra defeated Namuci he had the friendship or support of Namī and Sāyva (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-53-7; 6-20-6). Like other Asuras Namuci too was slain by Indra (Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-14-5; 7-19-5), or was struck down and destroyed (1-53-7). Indra slew the Asuras Vṛtra and Namuci and destroyed a hundred fortresses of the Asuras (Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-19-5). Namuci's head is not cut off as Vṛtra's head is cut off, but he is killed by being churned (crushed) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-30-8; 6-20-6), or by twisting the head (Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-30-7), or by twisting (wringing) his head with the foam of the water (of the sea) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-14-3). In some places it is also said that he cut off the head (Tai. Brā. 1-7-1-6, 7). In one place (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-131-4, 5) Indra sat beside Namuci and drank intoxicating liquor; then Sarasvatī, with the help of the Aśvin deities, healed him. These are all the particulars stated in the Ṛgveda.

Now we shall explain how the Taittirīya Brāhmaṇa of the Yajurveda tells of Indra's slaying of Namuci. नमुचिः । इंद्रेण सह युद्धं न मुंचतीति नमुचिः । (*namuciḥ | iṃdreṇa saha yuddhaṃ na muṃcatīti namuciḥ |*) Some commentators have explained the form of the word *namuci* thus: since he does not leave off fighting with Indra, he is named Namuci. Because this Asura was of great strength, it became very hard for Indra to fight and conquer him. Therefore Indra had to kill him by a stratagem. On this matter there is a story in the Taittirīya Brāhmaṇa:

इंद्रो वृत्रग्ं हत्वा । असुरान् पराभाव्य । नमुचिमासुरं नालभत । तग्ं शच्यागृह्णात् । तौ समलभेतां । सोऽस्मादभिशुनतरोऽभवत् । सोऽब्रवीत् । संधाग्ं संदधावहै । अथ त्वावस्रक्षामि । न मा शुष्केण नार्दॆण हनः । न दिवा न नक्तमिति । स एतमपां फेनमसिंचत् ! न वा एष शुष्को नार्दो व्युष्टासीत् । अनुदितः सूर्यः । न वा एतद्दिवा न नक्तं । तस्यैतस्मिन् लोके । अपां फेनेन शिर उदवर्तयत् । तदेनमन्ववर्तत । मित्रद्रुगिति । स एतानपामार्गानजनयत् । तानजुहोत् । तैर्वै स रक्षाग् स्यपाहत । यदपमार्गहोमो भवति । रक्षसामपहत्यै ॥ (*iṃdro vṛtragṃ hatvā | asurān parābhāvya | namucimāsuraṃ nālabhata | tagṃ śacyāgṛhṇāt | tau samalabhetāṃ | so 'smādabhiśunataro 'bhavat | so 'bravīt | saṃdhāgṃ saṃdadhāvahai | atha tvāvasrakṣāmi | na mā śuṣkeṇa nārdèṇa hanaḥ | na divā na naktamiti | sa etamapāṃ phenamasiṃcat ! na vā eṣa śuṣko nārdo vyuṣṭāsīt | anuditaḥ sūryaḥ | na vā etaddivā na naktaṃ | tasyaitasmin loke | apāṃ phenena śira udavartayat | tadenamanvavartata | mitradrugiti | sa etānapāmārgānajanayat | tānajuhot | tairvai sa rakṣāg syapāhata | yadapamārgahomo bhavati | rakṣasāmapahatyai ‖*)

(Tai. Brā. 1-7-1-6, 7).

### Printed p. 194

After slaying the Asura Vṛtra, Indra slew also other Asuras. But he could not conquer the Asura named Namuci. He was very mighty. He did not yield to any kind of weapons. Therefore Indra, wishing to overcome him by the strength of his own body, began a wrestling fight with him. Yet the more they fought, the more Namuci's strength grew. Indra could not escape from his grip. Then Indra asked Namuci to let him go, and the Asura said to Indra: "O Indra, if I am to let you go, you must do as I say. Now let us make a pact with each other. You may wish again to fight me by some stratagem and kill me. In such a case the weapon you use to kill me must not be wet, nor dry; you must not kill me by day, nor kill me at night. If you agree to these conditions I shall let you go." Indra agreed to this and, freed from his grip, began to think how he should conquer him. After some time Indra thought of a means. While he was walking on the seashore the foam of the sea fell within his sight. This foam is neither wet nor dry. "Therefore I shall make this itself my weapon and kill Namuci. When the night has passed and the time of dawn has begun, before the sun rises it is quite light. That time, since it has no darkness, is not night, and since the sun has not risen, is not day. If at such a time I fight Namuci and kill him with a weapon made of the foam of the water, I shall not have broken my word." Having so resolved, he fought Namuci accordingly and cut off his head with that foam. Yet the Asura did not die, and pursued Indra in order to kill him. "O Indra, betrayer of a friend, stop, stop! Having formerly given your word that you would not kill me, you have now done such a deed of betrayal of a friend. Even now I shall not let you go without killing you," he said. Then Indra, afraid, created a particular tree called *apāmārga* and made homa in the fire with its sacrificial sticks for the increase of his vigour. By that Indra's strength increased and he slew that Namuci. Therefore those who wish to be delivered from the affliction of Rākṣasas should make homa in the fire with *apāmārga* sticks for the increase of vigour. By that homa their strength will increase and they will be able to conquer the Rākṣasas: so the story runs. This name Namuci in the Ṛgveda is in the Ṛks:

नम्मा यदिंद्र सख्या परावति निबर्हयो नमुचिं नाम मायिनं ॥ (*nammā yadiṃdra sakhyā parāvati nibarhayo namuciṃ nāma māyinaṃ ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-53-7)

यः पिप्रुं नमुचिं यो रुधिक्रां तस्मा इंद्रायांधसो जुहोत ॥ (*yaḥ pipruṃ namuciṃ yo rudhikrāṃ tasmā iṃdrāyāṃdhaso juhota ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-14-5)

### Printed p. 195

नीवेशने शततमाविवेषीरहञ्च वृत्रं नमुचिमुताहन् ॥ (*nīveśane śatatamāviveṣīrahañca vṛtraṃ namucimutāhan ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-19-5)

त्वं जघंथ नमुचिं मखस्युं दासं कृण्वान ऋषये विमायं । (*tvaṃ jaghaṃtha namuciṃ makhasyuṃ dāsaṃ kṛṇvāna ṛṣaye vimāyaṃ |*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-23-7)

अत्ता दासस्य नमुचेः शिरो यदवर्तयो मनवे गातुमिच्छन् । (*attā dāsasya namuceḥ śiro yadavartayo manave gātumicchan |*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-30-7)

युजं हि मामकृथा आदिदिंद्र शिरोदासस्य नमुचेर्मथायन् । (*yujaṃ hi māmakṛthā ādidiṃdra śirodāsasya namucermathāyan |*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-30-8)

प्र श्येनो न मदिरमंशुमस्मै शिरो दासस्य नमुचेर्मथायन् । (*pra śyeno na madiramaṃśumasmai śiro dāsasya namucermathāyan |*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-20-6)

अपां फेनेन नमुचेः शिर इंद्रोदवर्तयः । (*apāṃ phenena namuceḥ śira iṃdrodavartayaḥ |*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-14-13) **NOTE**

युवं सुराममश्विना नमुचावासुरे सचा ॥ (*yuvaṃ surāmamaśvinā namucāvāsure sacā ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-131-4)

It is in these Ṛks, and nowhere else.

The main purport: यदा इंद्रः स्तोपासकस्य नमिनाम्मा ऋषे रक्षणार्थं नमुचिनामानमसुरं हतवान् तदा तेन नमुचिना सहैकस्माद्युद्धानंतरमन्यद्युद्धमेवं प्रकारेणानेकानि युद्धानि चकार । तथैकस्मान्नगरादनंतरमन्यन्नगरमेवं प्रकारेण नमचेः सर्वाण्यपि नगराणि हतवानित्यर्थः ॥ (*yadā iṃdraḥ stopāsakasya namināmmā ṛṣe rakṣaṇārthaṃ namucināmānamasuraṃ hatavān tadā tena namucinā sahaikasmādyuddhānaṃtaramanyadyuddhamevaṃ prakāreṇānekāni yuddhāni cakāra | tathaikasmānnagarādanaṃtaramanyannagaramevaṃ prakāreṇa namaceḥ sarvāṇyapi nagarāṇi hatavānityarthaḥ ‖*) Indra, in order to protect the seer named Nami, who praised him, fought many times with the Asura named Namuci, and each time he fought he destroyed his cities one after another, and at last killed him also: so runs the ancient history. It is described in the present Ṛk and in the next two Ṛks.

दासस्य (*dāsasya*)—This word occurs in this sūkta in this Ṛk and in the next two Ṛks. A detailed explanation of this word and of the word *dasyu* we have given in the Special Topics of the ninth Ṛk of this sūkta (pages 198-202).

### Printed p. 196
#### Ṛk 8

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> युजं॒ हि मामकृ॑था॒ आदिदिं॑द्र॒ शिरो॑ दा॒सस्य॒ नमु॑चेर्मथा॒यन् ।  
> अश्मा॑नं चित्स्व॒र्यं१॒॑वर्त॑मानं॒ प्र च॒क्रिये॑व॒ रोद॑सी  ॒॑
> मरुद्भ्यः ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> युजं । हि।मां । अकृथाः । आत् । इत् । इंद्र । शिरः । दासस्य । नमुचेः । मथायन् ।  
> अश्मानं । चित् । स्वर्यं । वर्तमानं । प्र । चक्रियाइव । रोदसी इति ।  
> मरुत्ऽभ्यः ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र त्वं स्वर्यं स्वरेण सहितं वर्तमानं भ्रमंतमश्मानं चित्
> मेघमिव स्थितं दासस्योपक्षपयितुर्नमुचेरसुरस्य शिरो मथायन् चूर्णयन्
> आदिदनंतरमेव मां युजं सखायमकृथा हि । चक्रषे खलु । तदानीं मरुद्भ्यः
> स्त्वत्सहायेभ्यो रोदसी द्यावापृथिव्यौ चक्रियेव चक्रे इव प्रास्तां ॥ उपसर्गश्रुतेर्योग्यक्रियाध्याहारः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa construes the verse thus: "O Indra, you were crushing the head of Namuci the Asura, the slave, that is, the harasser, who, sounding (*svarya*, with sound) and rolling about (revolving), stood like a cloud (*aśmā*); and immediately after that you made me your companion, your friend. For at that time, by the Maruts, your helpers, the two worlds, heaven and earth, were set revolving as if they were two wheels." Because the preposition *pra* is heard in the verse, a verb suited to it must be supplied.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- स्वर्यं (*svaryaṃ*)—the one that is with sound
- वर्तमानं (*vartamānaṃ*)—that wheels about and moves around
- अश्मानं चित् (*aśmānaṃ cit*)—who was like a cloud
- दासस्य (*dāsasya*)—of the harasser
- नमुचेः (*namuceḥ*)—of Namuci
- शिरः (*śiraḥ*)—the head
- मथायन् (*mathāyan*)—while cutting off
- आदित् (*ādit*)—immediately after that
- मां (*māṃ*)—me
- युजं (*yujaṃ*)—as a friend
- अकृथाः हि (*akṛthāḥ hi*)—you made, did you not? (then)
- मरुद्भ्यः (*marudbhyaḥ*)—by the Maruts, your friends
- रोदसी (*rodasī*)—both heaven and earth
- चक्रियेव (*cakriyeva*)—like wheels
- प्र (*pra*) (supply आस्तां (*āstāṃ*))—came to have the form of revolving

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, while cutting off the head of Namuci, the harasser, who was like a cloud, sounding and wheeling about, you immediately made me your friend. At that time, by the Maruts, your friends, heaven and earth both came to revolve like wheels.

### Printed p. 197

##### English translation
Verily you have made me, Indra, your associate when grinding the head of the slave Namuchi like a sounding and rolling cloud : and the heaven and earth (have been caused) by the Maruts (to revolve like a wheel).
#### Ṛk 9

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स्त्रीयो॒ हि दा॒स आयु॑धानि च॒क्रे किं मा॑ करन्नब॒ला अ॑स्य॒  ॑
> सेनाः ।  
> अं॒तर्ह्य ॑दु॒भे अ॑स्य॒ धेने॒ अथोप॒ प्रैद्यु॒धये॒ दस्यु॒मिंद्रः॑ ॥ ९ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> स्त्रीयः । हि । दासः । आयुधानि । चक्रे । किं । मा । करन् । अबलाः । अस्य । सेनाः ।  
> अंतः । हि । अख्यत् । उभे इति । अस्य । धेने इति । अथ । उप । प्र । ऐत् । युधये ।  
> दस्युं । इंद्रः ॥ ९ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> दास उपक्षपयिता नमुचिरसुरः स्त्रीयः स्त्रीरायुधानि युद्धसाधनानि चक्रे
> हि । कृतवान् खलु । इंद्रेण सह योद्दुं प्रतिहितवानित्यर्थः । अस्यासुरस्य संबंधिन्योऽबलाः स्त्रीरूपाः सेना मा मां किं करन् किं कुर्युरिति मन्यमान इंद्रस्तासां मध्येऽस्यासुरस्य धेने प्रीणयित्र्यौ सुरूपे उभे द्वे स्त्रीयावंतरख्यद्धि ।
> गृहमध्ये निदधे खलु । अथानंतरमिंद्रो दस्युं नमुचिं युधये युद्धायोप
> प्रैत् । उपप्रागच्छत् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa explains: the slave, that is, the harasser, the Asura Namuci, made women his weapons, his instruments of war; that is, he set them out to fight with Indra. Thinking, "What can the female hosts of this Asura, weak as they are, do to me?", Indra hid in the midst of them two women of this Asura, two (*dhenū*) who gave him delight and were beautiful, in the middle of the house. Then Indra went forth against the Dasyu, Namuci, for battle; he approached him.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- दासः (*dāsaḥ*)—the harasser, the Asura
- स्त्रीयः (*strīyaḥ*)—women
- आयुधानि (*āyudhāni*)—as his instruments of war
- चक्रे हि (*cakre hi*)—made, did he not?
- अस्य (*asya*)—"of this Asura, belonging to him
- अबलाः (*abalāḥ*)—weak
- सेनाः (*senāḥ*)—the armies of women
- मा (*mā*)—me
- किं करन् (*kiṃ karan*)—what can they do?"

### Printed p. 198
(saying) thus, among them:
- अस्य (*asya*)—for this Asura
- धेने (*dhene*)—who were a delight to him
- उभे (*ubhe*)—the two women
- अंतः (*aṃtaḥ*)—in the inner part of the house
- अख्यत् हि (*akhyat hi*)—placed (Indra) in hiding, did he not?
- अथ (*atha*)—afterwards
- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra
- दस्युं (*dasyuṃ*)—Namuci
- युधये (*yudhaye*)—for battle
- उपप्रैत् (*upaprait*)—confronted

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The harasser, the Asura, made women his instruments of war. Thinking, "What can the armies of these women, belonging to this Asura and weak, do to me?", Indra bound two of them, who were exceedingly dear to that same Asura, in the midst of those women, and hid them in the inner part of the house. Afterwards Indra confronted Namuci for battle.

##### English translation
The slave (Namuchi) made women his weapons : what will his female hosts do unto me ? the two his best beloved, ( Indra ) confined in the inner apartments, and then went forth to combat against the Dasyu.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

दासः (*dāsaḥ*), दस्युं (*dasyuṃ*)—The word *dāsa* and the word *dasyu* are both formed from the root *dasyati* (*das*) and give the meaning "one who exhausts, one who destroys." Yāska has stated the etymology thus:
दासो दस्यतेरुपदासयति कर्माणि (*dāso dasyaterupadāsayati karmāṇi*) (Ni. 2-18) दस्युर्दस्यतेः क्षयार्थादुपदस्यंत्यस्मिन्रसा उप दासयति कर्माणि (*dasyurdasyateḥ kṣayārthādupadasyaṃtyasminrasā upa dāsayati karmāṇi*) (Ni. 7-23).
The meaning suggested is: one who dries up the water that is useful for agriculture and other works, obstructs those works and destroys them. Therefore it has become customary to use the word *dāsa* of Asuras such as Vṛtra, Śuṣṇa, Pipru and Namuci. In the Ṛgveda the word *dāsa* is used not only of Asuras but also of the human enemies, the former inhabitants of this country, who fought again and again with the people of the Āryan race. In many places it is said that these Dāsas, or the dark people, the former inhabitants of this country, lived in groups or in many clans, having built forts here and there.
In the Ṛk
प्रति यदस्य वज्रं बाह्वोर्धुहर्त्वी दस्यून् पुर आयसीर्नि तारीत् । (*prati yadasya vajraṃ bāhvordhuhartvī dasyūn pura āyasīrni tārīt |*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-20-8)
it is said that Indra shattered the cities or forts of these Dasyus, made of iron. And further—

### Printed p. 199
स जातूभर्मा श्रद्दधान ओजः पुरो विभिंदन्नचरद्वि दासीः ॥ (*sa jātūbharmā śraddadhāna ojaḥ puro vibhiṃdannacaradvi dāsīḥ ‖*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-103-3)
इंद्राग्नी नवतिं पुरो दासपत्नीरधूनुतं । (*iṃdrāgnī navatiṃ puro dāsapatnīradhūnutaṃ |*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-12-6)
प्र ते वोचाम वीर्या३या मंदसान आरुजः । पुरो दासीरभीत्य ॥ (*pra te vocāma vīryā3yā maṃdasāna ārujaḥ | puro dāsīrabhītya ‖*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 4-32-10)
In such Ṛks there is mention of the cities and forts of the Dāsa people. There are also descriptions in many places that these Dāsa people were of black colour (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-101-1; 1-130-8; 2-20-7; 4-16-13; 4-47-21; 7-5-3, etc.). That their skin was black is said plainly in the Ṛk
मनवे शासदव्रतान् त्वचं कृष्णामरंधयत् । (*manave śāsadavratān tvacaṃ kṛṣṇāmaraṃdhayat |*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-130-8).
Because the colour of these Dāsa people was black, they are described as noseless (*anāsa*), that is, as having flat noses like the Negro peoples, and as speakers of falsehood (*mṛdhravācaḥ*), in Ṛks such as
अनासो दस्यूरमृणो वधेन नि दुर्योण अवृणज् मृध्रवाचः । (*anāso dasyūramṛṇo vadhena ni duryoṇa avṛṇaj mṛdhravācaḥ |*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-29-10).
It is said in Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-21-5; 1-33-4, 5; 5-16-9; 5-7-10; 5-42-9; 6-14-3; 8-70-10; 10-22-7, 8 and other places that their deity was the phallic deity, that they did not worship the Āryans' deities such as Indra, and that they were not performers of sacrifices and the like (*ayajñānaḥ*). It may be conjectured that the Āryans conquered these black people in wars and the like and made them work as slaves, and that therefore the meanings "slave" (Slave) and "servant" came into use for the word *dāsa* in that order (Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-86-7; 8-56-3; 10-62-10). On these Dāsa people the English scholars Macdonell and Keith have expressed their opinion in this way:
The Rigveda, on the other hand, knows दस्यु (*dasyu*) and दास (*dāsa*), both as aborigines independent of Aryan control and as subjugated slaves ; it is reasonable to reckon the शूद्र (*śūdra*) of the later texts as belonging to the aborigines who had been reduced to subjection by the Aryans. Strictly speaking, the defeated aborigines must have been regarded as slaves, but it is obvious that except on occasions when most of the men were slain, which may have occured quite often, there must have remained too many of them to be used as slaves

### Printed p. 200
of individual owners. The villages of the aborigines must have continued to subsist, but under Aryan lordship and control; there may be this amonnt of truth in Baden Powell's theory, which practically traced all the early cultivating villages in India to Dravidian origin. On the other hand, the term शूद्र (*śūdra*) would also cover the wild hill tribes which lived by hunting and fishing, and many of which would acknowledge the superiority of their Aryan neighbours; it could, in fact, be applied to all beyand the pale of the Aryan State.
( Vedic Index of Names and Subjects, Vol I1 P. 388 )
The women of these Dāsa people are called *dāsī*. Although this name is not current in the Ṛgveda, the word is used in the Atharvaveda (Atha. Saṃ. 5-22-6; 12-3-13; 12-4-9) and in other places (Chā. U. 5-13-2; Bṛ. U. 6-1-10). It is learnt that after the husbands of these women died in battle they worked as servants of the Āryan people, that sometimes the Āryans also kept company with them, and that the sons born of them had no ordinary intercourse with the Āryans. In the Aitareya Brāhmaṇa (Ai. Brā. 2-19) and in the Kauṣītaki Brāhmaṇa (Kau. Brā. 12-3) there is the story of Kavaṣa Ailūṣa, the son of a *dāsī*. Once, when the seers were performing a sacrifice on the bank of the river Sarasvatī, this Kavaṣa Ailūṣa, the son of a *dāsī*, came and sat among those seers; seeing it, the seers said that, since he was the son of a *dāsī*, he was not fit for dealings with the seers, who were Brāhmaṇas, and drove him out from there. Afterwards that Kavaṣa Ailūṣa went to the forest in grief and prayed to the gods; they told him that if he praised the Viśvedevas he would obtain the fitness to become a seer, and he praised the gods accordingly. In the tenth Maṇḍala of the Ṛ. Saṃ. this Kavaṣa Ailūṣa is the seer of the five sūktas 30, 31, 32, 33 and 34. Seeing this, the seers taking part in the sacrifice admitted him into their company: such is the story.
That these Dāsa people were enemies of the Āryans and fought with them, and that they caused obstruction to sacrifices and the like, is described in Ṛks such as Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-158-5; 2-13-8; 4-30-14, 15; 6-20-10; 7-99-5; 10-49-6, 7. Because in many contexts (Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-34-6, 7; 7-6-3; Atha. Saṃ. 5-11-6) the Dāsas and the Paṇis are mentioned together, it may be conjectured that they belonged to one group. That the wealth of these Dāsas was immense is learnt from Ṛks such as Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-176-4; 4-30-13; 7-40-6; 10-69-5; Atha. Saṃ. 7-9-20. It is learnt that among these Dāsa people there were chiefs such as Dhuni, Pipru, Varcin, Śuṣṇa and Śambara, and clans named Kirāta, Kīkaṭa, Caṇḍāla, Parṇaka and Śimyu. What is indicated in the Ṛgveda about some of the chiefs of these Dāsa people is explained in brief—

### Printed p. 201
Śuṣṇa—He is the chief enemy of Kutsa. His name is read about forty times in the Ṛgveda. For Kutsa, or together with Kutsa, Indra kills Śuṣṇa (4-16-12; 5-29-9, etc.). He has strong forts (1-51-11; 4-30-13). In one place it is called a moving fort (8-1-28). Indra destroys the forts of Śuṣṇa and releases the waters (1-51-11); he uproots the race of Śuṣṇa and wins the heavenly waters (*svarvatīḥ*) (8-40-10). Four times Śuṣṇa has the epithet *kuyava*, that is, "one who produces bad grain." In some places he is called simply Kuyava (1-103-8; 1-104-3). The result of the battle of Indra and Śuṣṇa is not only the release of the waters, but may also be the obtaining of cows or of the sun (8-85-17). While fighting with Indra, Śuṣṇa moves about in darkness. He is called the son of snow (hima) and a Dānava (5-32-4). From these descriptions it may be said that Śuṣṇa was a cause of drought.
Śambara—This name is read about twenty times. His name is also read together with Śuṣṇa and Pipru Varcin (1-101-2; 1-103-8; 2-19-6; 6-18-8). When Indra fought with Ahi and Śambara, the Maruts helped Indra (3-47-4). When he shattered Śambara, Indra made the very sky tremble (1-54-4); having searched out Śambara, who lived on a mountain (2-12-11), he hurled him down from the mountain (1-130-7; 6-26-5); Indra hurled down from the great mountain the Dāsa named Śambara, son of Kulitara (4-30-14). Considering himself a minor deity, he struck Śambara and made him fall from the lofty region (7-18-20). Ninety (1-130-7), commonly ninety-nine (2-19-6, etc.) or a hundred (2-14-6, etc.) forts are under his control. In one place the word *śambarāṇi* has the meaning "the forts of Śambara"; Bṛhaspati shattered these and entered the mountain region that was furnished with treasures (2-24-2). It is said that Indra destroys Śambara for Atithigva (1-51-6), for Divodāsa (2-19-6), or for both of them (1-130-7; 4-26-3). Some are of the opinion that Śambara and Divodāsa are one and the same person.
Pipru—He is the enemy of Ṛjiśvan, who was under Indra's protection. This Ṛjiśvan, having offered Soma juice to Indra, obtains his help when he fights against Pipru (5-29-11; 10-99-11). Indra, yoked with Ṛjiśvan (1-101-1; 10-138-3) or for his sake (4-16-13; 6-20-7), defeated Pipru. Many forts are under the control of Pipru, who is endowed with the illusions of Ahi. Indra shatters these (1-51-5; 6-20-7). Indra slew the Dāsa Pipru and others and released the waters (8-32-2). When the sun unyoked his chariot in the middle of the sky, Aryaman saw a warrior who matched Pipru. Indra, joined with Ṛjiśvan, destroyed the strong forts of the Asura named Pipru, the possessor of illusion (10-138-3). Indra handed over Pipru, who had the form of a wild beast, to Ṛjiśvan (4-16-13). Together with Ṛjiśvan

### Printed p. 202
Indra destroyed the very progeny of the dark-coloured people (the black people) (1-111-1). Since he has the two epithets Asura and Dāsa, some are of the opinion that he was a human enemy of Asura nature.
Namuci—His matter has been explained on pages 192-195 of this same part.
Dhuni and Cumuri—Ordinarily these two names occur together. Only once is the name of Cumuri alone found. Once they are also used as a pair (6-21-13). From this it is learnt that the relation between the two was very close. Indra put both of them to sleep (2-15-9; 6-20-13; 7-19-4); the same happened to Cumuri (6-26-6). Indra destroyed them, together with Śambara, Pipru, Śuṣṇa and others, and demolished their forts (6-18-8). For the sake of the seer Dabhīti, who prepared Soma juice for him, these two were destroyed by Indra or were made to fall asleep (10-113-9), and Dabhīti obtained from the gods a reward suited to his devotion (6-26-6). For Dabhīti Indra made thirty thousand Dasyus fall asleep (4-30-21) and for him bound the Dasyus without a rope (2-13-9). Dhuni means "one who roars loudly."
Varcin—His name is mentioned four times together with Śambara. He too is an Asura (7-99-5), but like Śambara he is also called Dāsa (6-47-21). Indra broke the forts of Śambara and destroyed a hundred thousand warriors of Varcin (2-14-6; 4-30-15).
Besides the chiefs mentioned above, Vala, Dabhīka, Rudhikra (2-14-3, 5), Anarśani, Sṛbinda (8-32-2) and Ilībiśa (1-33-12) may also be said to belong to the group of people called Dāsa.
दासः स्त्रीयो आयुधानि चक्रे (*dāsaḥ strīyo āyudhāni cakre*)—Another matter is touched upon in this Ṛk. Namuci, the Dāsa, sent some women, or women soldiers, to fight with Indra. Indra looked at them with contempt, thought that it did not befit his dignity to fight with these women, and put in confinement two young women among them who were most dear to Namuci. When the remaining women fled, Indra set out to fight with Namuci: so it is explained.

### Printed p. 203
#### Ṛk 10

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> समत्र॒ गावो॒ऽभितो॑ऽनवंते॒हेह॑ व॒त्सैर्वियु॑ता॒ यदास॑न्।  
> सं ता इंद्रो॑ असृजदस्य शा॒कैर्यदीं॒ सोमा॑सः॒ सुषु॑ता॒  ॑
> अमंदन् ॥ १० ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सं । अत्र । गावः । अभितः।अनवंत । इहइह । वत्सैः। वियुताः । यत् । आसन् ।  
> सं । ताः । इंद्रः। असृजत् । अस्य । शाकैः । यत् । ईं । सोमासः । सुसुताः ।  
> अमंदन् ॥ १० ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> यद्यदा गावो वत्सैर्वियुता आसन् वियुक्ता अभवन् अत्रास्मिन्कालेऽभितः
> सर्वतो नमुचिनापहृता गाव इहेह इह चेह च सर्वतः समनवंत । अत्यंतमगच्छन् । यद्यदा सुषुता बभ्रुनाम्ना ऋषिणा सुष्ठ्वभिषुताः सोमासः सोमा
> ईमेनमिंद्रममंदन् अमादयन् तदेंद्रः शाकैः शक्तैर्मरुद्भिः सहास्य बभ्रोः
> संबंधिनीर्नमुचिनापहृतास्ता गाः समसृजत् । वत्सैः सह समयोजयत् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa explains: when the cows were separated from their calves, at that time the cows that had been carried off by Namuci wandered on all sides, here and there, everywhere, going about exceedingly. When the Soma juices, well pressed by the seer named Babhru, exhilarated this Indra, then Indra, together with the powerful Maruts, reunited with their calves those cows of this Babhru that Namuci had carried off.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यत् (*yat*)—when
- गावः (*gāvaḥ*)—the cows
- वत्सैः (*vatsaiḥ*)—from their calves
- वियुताः आसन् (*viyutāḥ āsan*)—were separated
- अत्र (*atra*)—at that time
- अभितः (*abhitaḥ*)—all around
- इहेह (*iheha*)—everywhere
- सं अनवंत (*saṃ anavaṃta*)—were wandering about
- यत् (*yat*)—when
- सुषुताः (*suṣutāḥ*)—pressed in the best manner
- सोमाः (*somāḥ*)—the Soma juices
- ईं (*īṃ*)—this Indra
- अमंदन् (*amaṃdan*)—exhilarated (then)
- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra
- शाकैः (*śākaiḥ*)—joined with the exceedingly powerful Maruts
- अस्य (*asya*)—of Babhru
- ताः (*tāḥ*)—those cows
- सं असृजत् (*saṃ asṛjat*)—united with their calves

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

When the cows were separated from their calves, at that time they wandered about everywhere. But as soon as the well-pressed Soma juices had exhilarated Indra, Indra, joined with the exceedingly powerful Maruts, united with their calves the cows of Babhru that had been carried off by Namuci.

### Printed p. 204

##### English translation
When the cows were separated from their calves, they wandered about hither and thither ; but when the well-offered libations had exhilarated him, then Indra, with his vigorous (Maruts), reunited them (with their calves).

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

Once upon a time, when the cows of Babhru, the seer of this sūkta, were grazing in the forest with their calves, the Asura named Namuci separated the cows from the calves and carried them off. The calves, not seeing their cows, wandered about in the forest. Then, when the seer Babhru offered Soma juice to Indra and prayed, Indra, with the help of the Marut deities, freed the cows of the seer Babhru from Namuci and made them join their calves: this ancient history is told here.
#### Ṛk 11

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यदीं॒ सोमा॑ ब॒भ्रुधू॑ता॒ अमं॑द॒न्नरो॑रवीद्वष॒भः  ॑
> सादनेषु ।  
> पु॒रं॒द॒रः प॑पि॒वा इंद्रो॑ अस्य॒ पुन॒र्गवा॑मददादु॒स्रिया॑णां॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यत् । ईं । सोमाः । बभ्रुधूताः । अमंदन् । अरोरवीत् । वृषभः । सदनेषु ।  
> पुरंsdरः । पपिवान् । इंद्रः । अस्य।पुनः । गवां । अददात् । उस्रियाणां ॥ ११ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> यद्यदा बभ्रुधूता बभ्रुणाभिषुताः सोमा ईमेनमिंद्रममंदन्
> अमादयन् तदा वृषभः कामानां वर्षितेंद्रः सदनेषु युद्धेष्टरोरवीत् ।
> अत्यर्थं शब्दमकरोत् । पुरंदरः पुरां दारयितास्येमं सोमं पपिवान्
> पीतवानिंद्रः पुनरुस्रियाणां क्षीरमुत्स्राविणीर्गवां गा अददात् । बभ्रवे
> ददौ ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa explains: when the Soma juices shaken (pressed) by Babhru exhilarated this Indra, then Indra, the showerer of desires, roared in the battles, that is, made an exceeding noise. Indra, the breaker of cities, having drunk this Soma, gave again, that is, gave to Babhru, the cows that flow with milk.

### Printed p. 205

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यत् (*yat*)—when
- बभ्रुधूताः (*babhrudhūtāḥ*)—pressed by Babhru
- सोमाः (*somāḥ*)—the Soma juices
- ईं (*īṃ*)—Indra
- अमंदन् (*amaṃdan*)—exhilarated (then)
- वृषभः (*vṛṣabhaḥ*)—Indra, the showerer of desires
- सदनेषु (*sadaneṣu*)—in the battles
- अरोरवीत् (*aroravīt*)—made a deep sound
- पुरंदरः (*puraṃdaraḥ*)—who splits asunder the cities of enemies
- अस्य (*asya*)—this Soma
- पपिवान् (*papivān*)—who drank
- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra
- पुनः (*punaḥ*)—again
- उस्रियाणां (*usriyāṇāṃ*)—that yield milk
- गवां (*gavāṃ*)—the herd of cows
- अददात् (*adadāt*)—gave to Babhru

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

As soon as the Soma juices pressed by Babhru had exhilarated Indra, Indra, the showerer of desires, made a deep sound in the battles. Indra, who splits asunder the cities of enemies and who drank the Soma, made all the milch cows join Babhru again.

##### English translation
When the libations effused by Babhru had exhilarated him, the showerer (of benefits) shouted aloud in the combats : Indra, the destroyer of cities, quaffing (the Soma), restored to him his milk-yielding cattle.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

What was said in the preceding Ṛk is said again in this Ṛk also.
#### Ṛk 12

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> भ॒द्रमि॒दं रु॒शमा॑ अग्ने अक्र॒न्गवां॑ च॒त्वारि॒ दद॑तः स॒हस्रा॑ ।  
> ऋ॒णं॒च॒यस्य॒ प्रय॑ता म॒घानि॒ प्रत्य॑ग्रभीष्म॒ नृत॑मस्य नृ॒णां ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> भद्रं । इदं । रुशमाः ' अग्ने। अक्रन् । गवां । चत्वरि । ददतः।सहस्रा ।  
> ऋणंचयस्य । प्रयता । मघानि । प्रति । अग्रभीष्म । नृतमस्य । नृणां ॥ १२ ॥

### Printed p. 206

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> रुशम इति कश्चिज्जनपदविशेषः । अत्र रुशमशब्देन तत्रत्या जना उच्यंते ।
> रुशमा ऋणंचयनान्नो राज्ञः किंकरा हे अग्ने भद्रं कल्याणमिदं कर्माक्रन् ।
> अकुर्वन् । कीदृशाः । गवां धेनूनां चत्वारि चतुःसंख्यायुक्तानि सहस्रा सहस्राणि
> ददतो मह्यं प्रयच्छंतः । राज्णा हि दीयमानानि राजपुरुषा एव प्रयच्छंतीत्यर्थः । नृणां नेतृणां मध्यॆ नृतमस्यातिशयेन नेतृतमस्य ऋणंचयस्यैतन्नामकेन राज्णा प्रयता प्रयतानि दत्तानि मघानि गोरूपाणि धनानि वयं
> प्रत्यग्रभीष्म । प्रतिगृहीतवंतः स्मः । पूज्यार्थं बहुवचनं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa explains: Ruśama is the name of a particular country (people); here the word *ruśama* denotes the people of that place. The Ruśamas, servants of the king named Ṛṇañcaya, have done this auspicious, beneficial act, O Agni. Of what kind are they? They give me four thousand cows. The meaning is that gifts that a king bestows are in fact handed over by the king's men. Among leaders, of Ṛṇañcaya, the best of leaders in the highest degree, we have accepted the wealth in the form of cows given by that king of this name. The plural is used in honour.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अग्ने (*agne*)—O Agni
- गवां (*gavāṃ*)—of cows
- चत्वारि सहस्रा (*catvāri sahasrā*)—four thousand in number
- ददतः (*dadataḥ*)—who gave me
- रुशमाः (*ruśamāḥ*)—the people of the Ruśama country
- भद्रं (*bhadraṃ*)—beneficial
- इदं (*idaṃ*)—this act
- अक्रन् (*akran*)—have done
- नृणां (*nṛṇāṃ*)—among all men (among all leaders)
- नृतमस्य (*nṛtamasya*)—of the most excellent
- ऋणंचयस्य (*ṛṇaṃcayasya*)—from the king named Ṛṇañcaya
- प्रयता (*prayatā*)—offered
- मघानि (*maghāni*)—the wealth in the form of cows
- प्रत्यग्रभीष्म (*pratyagrabhīṣma*)—we have accepted

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Agni, the people of the Ruśama country, who have given me four thousand cows, have performed these auspicious acts. We have accepted the wealth in the form of cows offered by the king named Ṛṇañcaya, who is the most excellent of all leaders and himself a leader.

##### English translation
The Rusamas giving me four thousand cows, Agni, have done well : we have accepted the wealth, the donation of the leader of leaders Rinanchaya.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

Ṛṇañcaya was the king of the people called Ruśama. By this king's command his servants, the Ruśamas, gave as a gift many cows to the seer Babhru, the seer of this sūkta. The seer accepted it with gratitude and praised the king's gift. In the fourteenth and fifteenth Ṛks of this same sūkta also there is a description praising this gift; there the seer says that King Ṛṇañcaya gave him four thousand cows.

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> सु॒पेश॑सं॒ माव॑ सृजं॒त्यस्तं॒ गवां॑ स॒हस्रै॑ रु॒शमा॑सो अग्ने ।  
> ती॒व्रा इंद्र॑मममंदुः सु॒तासो॒क्तोर्व्यु॑ष्टौ॒ परि॑तक्क्यायाः ॥ १३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे अग्ने रुशमासो रुशमा ऋणंचयस्य किंकराः सुपेशसं सुरूपमलंकाराच्छादनादिभिः संस्कृतं मा मामस्तं गृहं गवां धेनूनां सहस्रैः सहाव
> सृजंति । प्रापयंति स्म । तदनंतरमेव तीव्रा रसवंतः सुतासो लब्धबहुधनेन
> मयाभिशुताः सोमाः परितक्क्यायाः । तमसा भूतानि परितस्तकति गच्छतीति
> परितक्क्या । तस्या अक्तो रात्रेर्वृष्टौ व्युच्छने सति। उषःकाल इत्यर्थः । इंद्रमनुमंदुः । अमादयन् । बभ्रुर्गवां प्राप्त्यनंतरमविलंबितमेव सोमेनेज
> इत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa explains: O Agni, the Ruśamas, the servants of Ṛṇañcaya, bring to the house (*asta*), together with thousands of cows, me, who am well formed (*supeśasa*), that is, of fair form, adorned with ornaments, clothes and the like. Immediately after that, the keen, that is, juicy, Soma juices pressed by me, who had obtained much wealth, exhilarated Indra at the end (*vyuṣṭi*) of the night (*akti*), that is, at dawn; the night is called *paritakmyā* because it goes around beings with darkness. The meaning is that Babhru, immediately after obtaining the cows, without delay offered sacrifice with Soma. **NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अग्ने (*agne*)—O Agni
- रुशमासः (*ruśamāsaḥ*)—the people of the Ruśama country
- गवां (*gavāṃ*)—of cows
- सहस्रैः (*sahasraiḥ*)—with thousands
- सुपेशसं (*supeśasaṃ*)—adorned with excellent means
- अस्तं (*astaṃ*)—to the house
- मा (*mā*)—to me
- अव सृजंति (*ava sṛjaṃti*)—hand over (afterwards)
- तीव्राः (*tīvrāḥ*)—having keen juice
- सुतासः (*sutāsaḥ*)—and pressed Soma juices
- परितक्क्यायाः (*paritakkyāyāḥ*)—enveloped in darkness
- अक्टोः (*akṭoḥ*)—of the night
- व्युष्टौ (*vyuṣṭau*)—at the end, at dawn
- इंद्रं (*iṃdraṃ*)—Indra
- अममंदुः (*amamaṃduḥ*)—gladdened

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Agni, the people of the Ruśama country have handed over to me a house furnished with thousands of cows and with excellent means. Afterwards the Soma juices, of keen taste and excellently pressed, exhilarated Indra at dawn, the end of the night enveloped in darkness.

### Printed p. 208

##### English translation
The Rusamas, Agni, have presented to me a beautiful abode, with thousands of cattle : the sharp libations have exhilarated Indra upon the breaking up of the (gloom-)investing night.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

In this Ṛk the seer Babhru praises the gift of a house made to him by King Ṛṇañcaya, and the detail is stated that, for the good and prosperity of that king, he offered Soma juice to Indra and prayed.
#### Ṛk 14

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> औच्छ॒त्सा रात्रि॒ परि॑तक्क्या॒ या ऋ॑णंच॒ये राज॑नि रु॒शमा॑नां।  
> अत्यो॒ न वा॒जी र॒घुर॒ज्यमा॑नोः ब॒भ्रुश्च॒त्वार्य॑सनत्स॒हस्रा॑ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> औच्छत् । सा । रात्री । परितक्म्या । या । ऋणंचये । राजनि । रुशमानां ।  
> अत्यः । न । वाजी । रघुः । अज्यमानः । बभ्रुः । चत्वारि । असनत् । सहस्रा ॥ १४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> रुशमानां रुशमनाम्मां जननां राजनि प्रभावृणंचय एतत्संज्ञके तत्समीप एव या रात्रिः परितक्क्या परितो गंत्रि भवति सा रात्रौच्छत् । व्युष्टाभवत् ।
> अत्यः सततगामि वाजी न अश्व इव रघुः शीघ्रगाम्यज्यमानः प्रेर्यमाणो बभ्रुरेतन्नामक ऋषिश्चत्वारि चतुःसंख्याकानि सहस्रा सहस्राणि गोरूपाणि धनान्यसतत् ।
> अलभत ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa explains: near Ṛṇañcaya, the king, the lord of the people named Ruśamas, the night that is *paritakmyā*, that goes about on all sides, dawned; that is, it came to its end. Babhru, the seer of that name, being urged on, like a horse (*vājin*) that is *atya*, a constant goer, and swift (*raghu*), obtained four thousand riches in the form of cattle. **NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- या (*yā*)—the night that
- परितक्क्या (*paritakkyā*)—is enveloped in darkness
- सा रात्रि (*sā rātri*)—that night
- रुशमानां (*ruśamānāṃ*)—of the people of the Ruśama country
- राजनि (*rājani*)—lord
- ऋणंचये (*ṛṇaṃcaye*)—the king named Ṛṇañcaya (as soon as he came into view)
- औच्छत् (*aucchat*)—comes to its end (the appearing of the dawn takes place) (at that time)
- अज्यमानः (*ajyamānaḥ*)—the one who was invited
- अत्यः (*atyaḥ*)—constantly moving
- वाजी न (*vājī na*)—like a horse
- रघुः (*raghuḥ*)—and swift
- बभ्रुः (*babhruḥ*)—the seer named Babhru
- चत्वारि सहस्रा (*catvāri sahasrā*)—four thousand riches in the form of cattle
- आसनत् (*āsanat*)—obtained

### Printed p. 209

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The night that is enveloped in darkness comes to its end, and the dawn appears, as soon as the king named Ṛṇañcaya, the lord of the people of the Ruśama country, came into view. At that time the seer named Babhru, who had been invited, and who was swift like a horse of good breed, obtained four thousand riches in the form of cattle.

##### English translation
The (gloom-)investing night has dispersed with the dawn ( upon the appearance of) Rinanchaya, the Raja of the Rusamas : Babhru being summoned, going like a fleet courser, has received the four thousand (cattle).
#### Ṛk 15

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> चतुः॑सहस्रं॒ गव्य॑स्य प॒श्वः प्रत्य॑ग्रभीष्म रु॒शमे॑ष्वग्ने ।  
> घ॒र्मश्चि॑त्त॒प्तः प्र॒वृजे॒य आसी॑दय॒स्मय॒स्तम्वादा॑म॒ विप्राः॑ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> चतुर्सहस्रं । गव्यस्य । पश्वः । प्रति । अग्रभीष्म । रुशमेषु । अग्ने ।  
> घर्मः । चित् । तप्तः । प्रवृजे । यः । आसीत् । अयस्मयः । तं । ऊं इति ।  
> आदाम । विप्राः ॥ १५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे अग्ने वयं रुशमेष्टेतत्संज्ञकेषु जनेषु गव्यस्य गवात्मकस्य पश्वः पशोः
> पशूनां । जातावेकवचनं । चतुः सहस्रं चत्वारि सहस्राणि प्रत्यग्रभीष्म ! प्रतिगृहीतवंतः स्मः । प्रवृजे प्रवृंजनार्थं तप्तः संतप्तः शोभनवर्णो घर्मश्चिन्महावीर
> इवायस्मयोऽयोमयो हिरण्मयो यः कलश आसीत् अभवत् तमु दोहनार्थं
> कलशं च विप्रा मेधाविनो वयं रुशमेष्वादाम । आदत्तवंतः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa explains: O Agni, we have accepted from the people called Ruśamas four thousand cattle, animals in the form of cows (the singular *paśu* stands for the class). The vessel (*kalaśa*) that was *ayasmaya*, that is, made of iron, of gold, heated for the Pravargya, of fair colour, like the Ghṛma, the Mahāvīra, and a vessel for milking, that too we wise ones have taken from the Ruśamas.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अग्ने (*agne*)—O Agni
- (वयं (*vayaṃ*)—we)
- रुशमेषु (*ruśameṣu*)—from the people of the Ruśama country
- गव्यस्य (*gavyasya*)—in the form of cows
- पश्वः (*paśvaḥ*)—of animals
- चतुः सहस्रं (*catuḥ sahasraṃ*)—four thousand

### Printed p. 210
- प्रत्यग्रभीष्म (*pratyagrabhīṣma*)—we accepted
- प्रवृजे (*pravṛje*)—for the Pravargya
- तप्तः (*taptaḥ*)—heated and golden-coloured
- घर्मश्चित् (*gharmaścit*)—like the Mahāvīra
- अयस्मयः (*ayasmayaḥ*)—golden
- यः (*yaḥ*)—which
- आसीत् (*āsīt*)—was (the vessel)
- तमु (*tamu*)—that too
- विप्राः (*viprāḥ*)—we wise ones
- आदाम (*ādāma*)—have taken

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Agni, we have accepted from the people of the Ruśama country four thousand animals in the form of cows. The golden vessel that was heated for the Pravargya and was of a golden hue like the Mahāvīra, that too we wise ones have taken.

##### English translation
We have accepted, Agni, the four thousand cattle from the Rusamas : and the glowing, the golden ewer prepared for the solemnity, we who are wise have accepted it.
## Sūkta 31 (Maṇḍala 5)

The thirty-first sūkta.

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> इंद्रो रथायेति त्रयोदशर्चं सप्तदशं सूक्तं । अवस्युर्नामात्रेय
> ऋषिः । त्रिष्टुप् छंदः । इंद्रो देवता । उग्रमयातं सं ह यद्वामिति पादयोः
> क्रमेण कुत्सोशनसौ विकल्पेन देवता । इंद्राकुत्सा वहमानेत्यस्या इंद्रः कुत्सश्च ।
> तथा चानुक्रांतं । इंद्रो रथाय सप्तोनावस्युरुग्रमिति कौत्सौ शनसौ ना पादौ
> परैंद्राकौत्सीति ॥ विनियोगो लैंगिकः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The sūkta beginning इंद्रो रथाय (*iṃdro rathāya*) has thirteen Ṛks and is the seventeenth sūkta. Avasyu Ātreya is the seer; the metre is Triṣṭubh; the deity is Indra. In the third and fourth pādas of the Ṛks beginning उग्रमयातं (*ugramayātaṃ*) and सं ह यद्वां (*saṃ ha yadvāṃ*), Kutsa and Uśanas respectively are, optionally, the deities. For the Ṛk इंद्राकुत्सा वहमाना (*iṃdrākutsā vahamānā*) the deities are Indra and Kutsa. So the Anukramaṇikā says: इंद्रो रथाय सप्तोनावस्युरुग्रमिति कौत्सौ शनसौ ना पादौ परैंद्राकौत्सीति ॥ (*iṃdro rathāya saptonāvasyurugramiti kautsau śanasau nā pādau paraiṃdrākautsīti ‖*). The use in ritual (*viniyoga*) is derived from the mark (*laiṅgika*).
Translation: This sūkta, इंद्रो रथाय (*iṃdro rathāya*), is the seventeenth sūkta in the second Anuvāka. It contains thirteen Ṛks. The seer of this sūkta is Avasyu Ātreya; the deity is Indra; the metre is Triṣṭubh. For the third and fourth pādas of the eighth Ṛk of this sūkta, उग्रमयांतं (*ugramayāṃtaṃ*) and सं ह यद्वां (*saṃ ha yadvāṃ*), Indra or Kutsa and Indra or Uśanas respectively are, optionally, the deities. For the ninth Ṛk of this sūkta, इंद्रा कुत्सा वहमाना (*iṃdrā kutsā vahamānā*), both Indra and Kutsa are deities. In the Anukramaṇikā it is said: इंद्रो रथाय सवोनावस्युरुग्रमिति कौत्सौशनसौ ना पादौ परैंद्राकौसी (*iṃdro rathāya savonāvasyurugramiti kautsauśanasau nā pādau paraiṃdrākausī*) **NOTE**. The use in ritual is derived from the mark.

### Printed p. 211
Sūkta 31.

Maṇḍala 5, Anuvāka 2, Sūkta 31.

Aṣṭaka 4, Adhyāya 1, Varga 29, 30, 31.

Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 13.

Seer: Avasyu Ātreya.

Deity: Indra in 1-7, in the second pāda of 8, and in 10-13; in the third pāda of 8, Indra or Kutsa; in the fourth pāda of 8, Indra or Uśanas; in 9, Indra and Kutsa.

Metre: Triṣṭubh.

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> इंद्रो॒ रथा॑य प्र॒वतं॑ कृणोति॒ यम॒ध्यस्था॑न्म॒घवा॑ वाज॒यंतं॑ ।  
> यू॒थेव॑ प॒श्यो व्यु॑नोति गो॒पा अरि॑ष्टो याति प्रथ॒मः  ॑
> सिषासन् ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> इंद्रः । रथाय । प्रवतं । कृणोति । यं । अधिअस्थात् । मघवा । वाजयंतं ।  
> यूथाइव । पश्वः । वि । उनोति । गोपाः । अरिष्टः । याति । प्रथमः । सिसासन् ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> माघवा धनवानिंद्रो वाजयंतमन्नमिच्छंतं यं रथमध्यस्थात् अधितिष्ठति तस्मै रथाय प्रवतं प्रवणमाजिं कृणोति करोति । अपि च गोपा गोपालः
> पश्वो यूथेव पशोः पशूनां यूथानि यथा व्युनोति प्रेरयति तथा शत्रुसैन्यानि प्रेरयति । अरिष्टः शत्रुभिः स्वयमहिंसितः प्रथमो देवानां मुख्य इंद्रः
> सिषासन् शत्रुधनानीच्छन् याति । गच्छति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa explains: Indra, the possessor of wealth, makes a sloping course, a downward-going contest, for that chariot, desirous of food, which he mounts. And as a herdsman (*gopā*) drives the herds of cattle, so he drives the armies of enemies. Unharmed by enemies himself, Indra, the first, the chief of the gods, goes forward, desiring the wealth of the enemies.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- मघवा (*maghavā*)—the wealthy
- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra
- वाजयंतं (*vājayaṃtaṃ*)—that desires food
- यं (*yaṃ*)—which chariot
- अध्यस्थात् (*adhyasthāt*)—he has mounted and sits
- (तस्मै (*tasmai*))रथाय (*rathāya*)—for that chariot
- प्रवतं (*pravataṃ*)—

### Printed p. 212
a downward-moving course, as it were
- कृणोति (*kṛṇoti*)—makes
- गोपाः (*gopāḥ*)—the herdsman
- पश्वः (*paśvaḥ*)—of animals
- यूथेव (*yūtheva*)—as he drives the herds
- व्युनोति (*vyunoti*)—driving (the armies of enemies)
- अरिष्टः (*ariṣṭaḥ*)—unharmed by enemies
- प्रथमः (*prathamaḥ*)—and chief among the gods
- सिषासन् (*siṣāsan*)—desiring the wealth of enemies
- याति (*yāti*)—goes forward

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Indra, the wealthy, makes the chariot which, desirous of food, he has mounted, move downward from the divine world, towards the earth. As a herdsman drives the herds of cattle, he goes forward driving the armies of enemies, unharmed by enemies, the chief among the gods, desiring the wealth of enemies.

##### English translation
Indra, the possessor of opulence, directs downwards the car over which, intended (to receive sacrificial) viands, he presides : he proceeds unimpeded, the first of the gods, driving ( his enemies before him), as a herdsman drives the herds of cattle.
#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आ प्र द्र॑व हरिवो॒ मा वि वे॑नः॒ पिशं॑गराते अ॒भि नः॑ सचस्व ।  
> न॒हि त्वदिं॑द्र॒ वस्यो॑ अ॒न्यदस्त ॑मे॒नांश्चि॒ज्जनि॑वतश्चर्थ ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आ । प्र । द्रव । हरिवः । मा । वि । वेनः । पिशंगराते । अभि । नः । सचस्व ।  
> नहि । त्वत् । इंद्र । वस्यः । अन्यत् । अस्ति । अमेनान् । चित् । जनिवतः । चकर्थः २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे हरिवो हरिवन्निंद्र त्वमास्मानभिमुखं प्र द्रव । प्रकर्षण गच्छ । किंतु
> मा पि वेनः । वेनतिः कांतिकर्मा । आस्मासु विगतकामो मा भूः । किंच हे पिशंगराते बहुरूपधनेंद्र नोऽस्मानभि सचस्व । अभिसेवस्व । अपि च हे इंद्र वस्यो
> वसीयः श्रेयस्करं त्वत्त्वत्तोऽन्यन्नह्यस्ति । अन्यद्वसुजातं नास्ति । अमेनांश्चित् ।
> मेनाशब्दः स्त्रीवाची । अपगतस्त्रीकांश्चित् जनिवतो जायावतश्चर्थ । करोषि ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa explains: O Hariva, Indra, lord of horses, run forward towards us; go forth. But do not be *vena*; the root *venati* has the sense of desire: do not be devoid of desire towards us. And, O Piśaṅgarāte, Indra, who have wealth of many forms, join us; serve us. Moreover, O Indra, there is nothing else that is *vasya*, that is, better, more beneficial, than you; there is nothing else born of wealth. The word *menā* denotes a woman; those who are *amenā*, without a wife, you make *janivat*, that is, possessed of a wife.

### Printed p. 213

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- हरिवः (*harivaḥ*)—O Indra, lord of horses
- आ प्र द्रव (*ā pra drava*)—come to us with regard
- मा वि वेनः (*mā vi venaḥ*)—do not be without regard for us
- पिशंगराते (*piśaṃgarāte*)—O Indra, who have wealth of many kinds
- नः (*naḥ*)—us
- अभि सचस्व (*abhi sacasva*)—serve
- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- त्वत् (*tvat*)—than you
- वस्यः (*vasyaḥ*)—more beneficial
- अन्यत् नहि अस्ति (*anyat nahi asti*)—there is no one else at all
- अमेनांश्चित् (*amenāṃścit*)—one who is without a wife
- जनिवतः (*janivataḥ*)—so that he has a wife
- चकर्थ (*cakartha*)—you make

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, lord of horses, run to us with regard. Do not be without regard for us. O Indra, who have wealth of many kinds, serve us. O Indra, there is no one else at all more beneficial than you. You make one who is without a wife to have a wife.

##### English translation
Hasten to us, lord of horses : be not indifferent to us : distributor of manifold wealth, befriend us ; for there is nothing else that is better, Indra, than you : you have given wives to those who were without women.
#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> उद्यत्सहः॒ सह॑स॒ आज॑निष्ट॒ देदि॑ष्ट॒ इंद्र॑ इंद्रि॒याणि॒ विश्वा॑ ।  
> प्राचो॑दयत्सु॒दुघा॑ व॒व्रे अं॒तर्वि ज्योति॑षा संववृ॒त्वत्तमो॑ऽवः ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> उत् । यत् । सहः । सहसः । आ । अजनिष्ट । देदिष्टे । इंद्रः । इंद्रियाणि । विश्वा ।  
> प्र । अचोदयत् । सुदुघाः । वव्रे । अंतः । वि । ज्योतिषा । संववृत्वत् ।  
> तमः । अवरित्यवः ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> यद्यदा सहः सूर्यसंबंधि तेजः सहस उषःसंबंधिनस्तेजस उदाजनिष्ट
> उपरिष्टात् प्रादुर्बभूव तदेंद्रो विश्वा विश्वानि सर्वाणींद्रियाणि धनानि ।

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa explains: when the might (*sahas*) belonging to the Sun arises (*udājaniṣṭa*), appears above, from the might belonging to Uṣas, then Indra gives all (*viśvā*) the *indriyas*, that is, riches.

### Printed p. 214

> इंद्रियमिति धननामैतत् । देदिष्टे । यजमानेभ्यो दिशति । वव्रे निवारके
> पर्वतेंतर्मध्यॆ बलेन निरुद्धाः सुदुघाः सुष्टु दोग्धिर्गाः प्राचोदयत् ।
> प्रैरयत् । ज्योतिषा तेजसा संववृत्वत् संवरणशीलं तमो व्यवः । निवारितवान् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

इंद्रियमिति धननामैतत् (*iṃdriyamiti dhananāmaitat*): *indriya* is a name for wealth. *Dediṣṭe*: he assigns to the sacrificers. Within the obstructing mountain (*vavre*), he drove out the cows that were well-milking and confined there by force. By light, that is, by his own brilliance, he warded off (*avaḥ*) the darkness that enveloped the world.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यत् (*yat*)—when
- सहः (*sahaḥ*)—the might of the Sun
- सहसः (*sahasaḥ*)—the might of Uṣas
- उत् आजनिष्ट (*ut ājaniṣṭa*)—covers
- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—then Indra
- विश्वा (*viśvā*)—all
- इंद्रियाणि (*iṃdriyāṇi*)—riches (to the sacrificer)
- देदिष्टे (*dediṣṭe*)—gives
- वव्रे (*vavre*)—of the obstructing mountain
- अंतः (*aṃtaḥ*)—in the middle (confined)
- सुदुघाः (*sudughāḥ*)—the cows full of milk
- प्राचोदयत् (*prācodayat*)—made to come out
- संववृत्वत् (*saṃvavṛtvat*)—that enveloped the world
- तमः (*tamaḥ*)—the darkness
- ज्योतिषा (*jyotiṣā*)—by his own brilliance
- वि अवः (*vi avaḥ*)—destroyed

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

As soon as the might of the Sun covers the might of Uṣas, Indra gives all riches to the sacrificer. That same Indra made the cows full of milk, confined in the middle of the obstructing mountain, come out. He destroyed with his own brilliance the darkness that had enveloped the world.

##### English translation
When the light (of the sun) overpowers the light (of the dawn) Indra grants all (sorts of) wealth (to the worshipper) : he has liberated the milch- (cows) from the interior of the obstructing (mountain); he dissipates the enveloping darkness with light.
#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अन॑वस्ते॒ रथ॒मश्वा॑य तक्ष॒न्त्वष्टा॒ वज्रं॑ पुरुहूत द्यु॒मंतं॑।  
> ब्र॒ह्माण॒ इंद्रं॑ म॒हयं॑तो आ॒र्कैरव॑र्षय॒न्नह॑ये॒ हंत॒वा  ॑
> उ ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अनवः । ते । रथं । अश्वाय । तक्षन् । त्वष्टा । वज्रं । पुरुहुत । द्रुमंतं ।  
> ब्रह्माणः । इंद्रं । महयंतः । अर्कैः । अवर्धयन् । अहये । हंतवै । ऊं इति ॥

### Printed p. 215

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे पुरुहूत बहुभिराहूतेंद्र ते त्वदीयं रथमश्वायाश्वाभ्यां संयोगार्हमनवः । अनवो मनुष्याः । ऋभवस्तक्षन् । अतक्षन् । अकुर्वन् । त्वष्टा च
> त्वद्वीयं वज्रं द्युमंतं दीप्तिमंतमकरोत् । उ अपि च महयंत इंद्रं
> पूजयंतो ब्रह्माणोंगिरसः परिवृढा मरुतो वा अहयेऽहिं वृत्रं हंतवै
> हंतुमर्कैः स्तोत्रैरवर्धयन् । वर्धितवंतः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa explains: O Indra, invoked by many, the अनवः (*anavaḥ*), that is, men, namely the Ṛbhus, fashioned (तक्षन् (*takṣan*), "made") your chariot so that it is fit to be yoked to horses; and Tvaṣṭṛ made your thunderbolt radiant. And the ब्रह्माणोंगिरसः (*brahmāṇoṃgirasaḥ*), the Aṅgirases, or else the Maruts, called परिवृढा (*parivṛḍhā*), worshipping Indra, strengthened him with arkas, that is, with hymns of praise, in order to slay Ahi, that is, Vṛtra.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- पुरुहूत (*puruhūta*)—O Indra, invoked by many
- ते (*te*)—your
- रथं (*rathaṃ*)—chariot
- आश्वाय (*āśvāya*)—so as to be fit for yoking with horses
- अनवः (*anavaḥ*)—the Ṛbhus, who are men
- तक्षन्माडिद्दारॆ (*takṣanmāḍiddārè*)—have made
- त्वष्टा (*tvaṣṭā*)—Tvaṣṭṛ
- वज्रं (*vajraṃ*)—your thunderbolt
- द्युमंतं (*dyumaṃtaṃ*)—(has made it) radiant
- उ (*u*)—and
- इंद्रं (*iṃdraṃ*)—Indra
- महयंतः (*mahayaṃtaḥ*)—worshipping
- ब्रह्माणः (*brahmāṇaḥ*)—the venerable Aṅgirases
- अहये (*ahaye*)—of Vṛtrāsura
- हंतवै (*haṃtavai*)—for the destruction
- अर्कॆैः (*arkèaiḥ*)—with hymns
- अवर्धयन् (*avardhayan*)—made (you) grow strong

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)
