- समरः (*samaraḥ*)—accompanied by the Maruts and others
- यः (*yaḥ*)—that same Indra who
- वाजसात् (*vājasāt*)—in the battle fought for food
- स्तुतः (*stutaḥ*)—being praised
- अस्मै जने (*asmai jane*)—for me (named Saṃvaraṇa), on whom it is to be placed
- सुमतिं (*sumatiṃ*)—the favourable mind
- चिकेत (*ciketa*)—he knows

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

For the sake of giving strength to all men, I, who am extremely weak, recite, in the manner to be stated, an abundant and brilliant praise addressed to Indra, who is of great form and mighty. Indra, accompanied by the Maruts and others, being praised in the battle fought for food, knows the favourable mind that is to be placed in me, who am named Saṃvaraṇa.

##### English translation
Feeble as I am, I offer praise to the great and vigorous Indra, for this purpose, (that he may grant) strength to (our) people : he who, associated with the Maruts, shews favour to this person when praised for the sake of sustenance.

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स त्वं न॑ इंद्र धियसा॒नो अ॒र्कैर्हरी॑णां वृष॒न्योक्र॑मश्रेः ।  
> या इ॒त्था म॑घव॒न्ननु॒ जोषं॒ वक्षो॑ अ॒भि प्राय्यः स॑क्षि॒  ॑
> जनान् ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सः । त्वं । नः । इंद्र । धियसानः । अर्कैः । हरीणां । वृषन् । योक्रं । अश्रेः ।  
> याः । इत्था । मघवन् । अनु । जोषं । वक्षः । अभि । प्र । अर्यः । सक्षि । जनान् ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे वृषन् वर्षकेंद्र स प्रसिद्धस्त्वं नोऽस्मानियसानो ध्यायन् अर्कैरर्चनसाधनैः स्तोत्रैर्निमित्तभूतैर्हरीणां रथे नियोज्यानामश्वानां योक्रं

### Printed p. 252

> नियोजनरज्जुमश्रेः । आश्रयसि । याः ॥ लिंगव्यत्ययः ॥ यानर्कान् हे मघवन्
> इत्थेष्ठं जोषं प्रीतिमनु वक्षोऽवहः तैर्निमित्तैर्योक्रमश्रेरिति । तथा कृत्वार्योऽस्मदरीन् जनानभि अभिमुख्येन प्र प्रकृष्टं सक्षि । पराभव ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, showerer, you, the famous one, thinking of us (ध्यायन् (*dhyāyan*)) by means of praises (अर्कै (*arkai*), the instruments of worship) as the cause, fasten (अश्रेः (*aśreḥ*) means "you resort to") the traces (योक्रं (*yokraṃ*), the yoking rope) of the horses (हरीणां (*harīṇāṃ*)) that are to be harnessed to the chariot. याः (*yāḥ*) is a change of gender: O Maghavan, with the praises with which you bring the desired delight (जोषं (*joṣaṃ*), love), for those reasons you fasten the traces. Having done so, overpower strongly, in front, the people who are our enemies (अर्यः (*aryaḥ*), जनान् (*janān*)).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- मघवन् (*maghavan*)—O wealthy one
- वृषवन् (*vṛṣavan*)—O showerer of desires
- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- सः (*saḥ*)—the famous one
- त्वं (*tvaṃ*)—you
- याः (*yāḥ*)—in which of our praises
- इत्या (*ityā*)—in this manner
- जोषं (*joṣaṃ*)—love
- अनु वक्षः (*anu vakṣaḥ*)—you have (such)
- अर्कैः (*arkaiḥ*)—being satisfied by our praises
- नः (*naḥ*)—us alone
- धियसानः (*dhiyasānaḥ*)—thinking (with a mind of favour)
- हरीणां (*harīṇāṃ*)—of the horses
- योक्रं (*yokraṃ*)—the bridle
- अश्रेः (*aśreḥ*)—you take hold of
- अरैः (*araiḥ*)—(for us) the hostile
- जनान् (*janān*)—people
- अभि (*abhi*)—all around
- प्र सक्षि (*pra sakṣi*)—overcome

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O wealthy Indra, showerer of desires, you, famous in the world, take great delight in our praises and, satisfied by them, thinking of us alone with a mind of favour, make your horses ready to come to us and take hold of their bridle. Overcome completely the people who are hostile to us.

##### English translation
You, Indra, medit ting upon us, fasten the traces of yours horses, (incited), showerer (of benefits), by those praises in which you take suitable delight, and therefore overcome (for us) hostile men.

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> न ते त॑ इंद्रा॒भ्य१॒ं॑स्मदृ॒ष्टायु॑क्तासो अब्र॒ह्मता॒ यदस॑न् ।  
> तिष्ठा॒ रथ॒मधि॒ तं व॑ज्रह॒स्ता र॒श्मिं दे॑व यमसे॒ स्वश्वः॑ ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> न । ते। ते । इंद्र । अभि । अस्मत् । ऋष्ट । अयुक्तासः । अब्रह्मता । यत् । असन् !  
> तिष्ठ । रथं । अधि । तं । वज्रहस्त । आ । रश्मिं । देव । यमसे । सुअश्वः ॥ ३ ॥

### Printed p. 253

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे ऋष्ट महन्निंद्र यत् येऽस्थदस्तत्तस्त्वद्भक्तेभ्योऽन्येऽयुक्तासस्त्वयासंयुक्ता आसन् आसन् अभवन् अब्रह्मता । ब्रह्म परिवृढं कर्म ! तद्रहितत्वात् ते
> नरास्ते त्वदीया न भवंति । अतोऽस्थद्यज्ञमभ्यागंतुं तं प्रसिद्धं रथमधि तिष्ठ ।
> हे वज्रहस्तेंद्र देव स्वश्वस्त्वं युं रथमारोढुं रश्मिं प्रग्रहमा यमसे नियमयिसि तमधि तिष्ठ ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: O mighty Indra, those others, different from your devotees, who are not joined with you are without *brahma* (अब्रह्मता (*abrahmatā*)). *Brahma* is the encompassing rite; being devoid of it, those men are not yours. Therefore, in order to come to our sacrifice, ascend that well-known chariot. O thunderbolt-handed Indra, divine one, well-horsed as you are, ascend the chariot whose rein you hold in check, in order to mount it.**NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- ऋष्ट (*ṛṣṭa*)—O one of mighty form
- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- अस्मत् (*asmat*)—those who are different from us
- आयुक्ता सः (*āyuktā saḥ*)—and have no devotion to you
- यत् असन् (*yat asan*)—whoever they are
- अब्रह्मणा (*abrahmaṇā*)—because they do not follow the Vedic path
- ते (*te*)—belonging to you
- न (*na*)—they do not become (therefore)
- वज्रहस्त (*vajrahasta*)—holder of the thunderbolt
- देव (*deva*)—divine Indra
- स्वश्वः (*svaśvaḥ*)—provided with excellent horses
- रश्मिं (*raśmiṃ*)—(of whichever chariot) the rein
- आ यमसेकैयल्लि (*ā yamasekaiyalli*)—you hold in your hand
- तं रथं (*taṃ rathaṃ*)—that same chariot
- अभि (*abhi*)—in order to come (to our sacrifice)
- अधि तिष्ठ (*adhi tiṣṭha*)—ascend and be seated

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra of mighty form, those who are different from us and have no devotion to you, whoever they are, do not belong to you, because they do not follow the Vedic path. Therefore, O Indra, holder of the thunderbolt and divine one, ascend that same chariot, to which you have yoked excellent horses and whose rein you hold in your hand, in order to come to our sacrifice.

##### English translation
Since, mighty Indra, those who, dffering from us, and not united with you through their lack of devotion, are not yours ; therefore, divine holder of the thunderbolt, you are possessed of excellent horses, ascend the car, whose reins you guide, (to come to our sacrifice).

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> पु॒रू यत्त॑ इंद्र॒ संत्यु॒क्था गवे॑ च॒कर्थो॒र्वरा॑सु॒ युध्य॑न् ।  
> त॒त॒क्षे सूर्या॑य चि॒दोक॑सि॒ स्वे वृषा॑ स॒मत्सु॑ दा॒सस्य॒ नाम॑  
> चित् ॥ ४ ॥

### Printed p. 254

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> पुरु । यत् । ते । इंद्र । संति । उक्ता । गवे । चकर्थ । उर्वरासु । युध्यन् ।  
> तत्तक्षे । सूर्याय । चित् । ओकसि । स्वे । वृषा । समत्सु । दासस्य । नाम । चित् ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र ते तव यद्यदा स्वभूतानि पुरु पुरूणि बहून्युक्कोक्कानि शस्त्राणि
> संति तदोर्वरासु सस्योपेतासु भूमिषु निमित्तभूतासु गवे वृष्टुदकाय
> युध्यन् जलप्रतिबंधकान् संहरन् चकर्थ । करोषि भेदनं । कुत्रेति । सूर्याय
> सूर्यस्य स्वे स्वकीय ओकसि स्थाने । वृषा वर्षिता सन् ततक्षे । संपादयसि ।
> समत्सु संग्रामेषु दासस्य वृष्टि प्रतिबंधकर्तुरेतन्नामकस्यासुरस्य नाम चिन्नामापि ततक्षे । नाशयसीत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: O Indra, when your own many (पुरु (*puru*)) praises and recitations, that is, *śastras*, exist, then, fighting for the sake of rain-water (गवे (*gave*)) on fertile lands as the occasion, destroying those who obstruct the waters, you bring about the cleaving open (of the clouds). Where? In the sun's own dwelling-place, the place that is his own. Being the showerer, you accomplish it. In battles you destroy even the name of the Asura called Dāsa, the obstructor of rain; that is the meaning.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- यत् (*yat*)—for which reason
- पुरू (*purū*)—very numerous
- उक्ता (*uktā*)—all the praises that take the form of *śastras*
- ते (*te*)—addressed to you alone
- संति (*saṃti*)—have become (for that reason)
- उर्वरासु (*urvarāsu*)—upon the earth full of plants
- गवे (*gave*)—in order to pour down rain
- युध्यन् (*yudhyan*)—fighting (with the enemies who obstruct the rain)
- चकर्थ (*cakartha*)—you have brought about (their destruction)
- वृषा (*vṛṣā*)—you, the one who pours down rain
- समत्सु (*samatsu*)—when battle comes
- सूराय (*sūrāya*)—of the sun
- स्वी ओकसिचित् (*svī okasicit*)—in his own home (in the atmosphere itself)
- दासस्य (*dāsasya*)—of the Asura
- नामचित् (*nāmacit*)—the very name
- ततक्षे (*tatakṣe*)—you have destroyed

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, praises in the form of *śastras* have been composed in very great numbers and offered to you. All of them are addressed to you alone. For that reason, in order to pour down rain upon the earth full of plants, you have fought with the enemies who obstruct the rain and brought about their destruction. You, the sprinkler of rain, when battle came, destroyed the very name of the Asura in the atmosphere itself, which is the sun's own heaven.

##### English translation
Inasmuch, Indra, as many praises are yours, therefore, combating for the sake of (shedding) waters on fertile (lands), you have effected , the discomfiture of its obstructors); you, who are the showerer (of benefits , have on behalf of the sun, destroyed in his own dwelling, the very name of , the Asura, Dasa, in battle.

### Printed p. 255

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> व॒यं ते त॑ इंद्र॒ ये च॒ नरः॒ शर्धो॑ जज्ञा॒ना या॒ताश्च॒  ॑
> रथाः ।  
> आस्माज्ज॑गम्यादहिशुष्म॒ सत्वा॒ भगो॒ न हव्यः॑ प्रभृ॒थेषु॒ चारुः॑ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> वयं । ते । ते । इंद्र । ये । च । नरः । शर्धः । जज्ञानाः । याताः । च । रथाः ।  
> आ । अस्मान् । जगम्यात् । अहिशुष्म । सत्वा । भगः । न । हव्यः । प्रभृथेषु । चारुः ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र ये च नरः कर्मणां नेतार ऋत्विग्यजमाना वयं स्मः ते वयं
> ते त्वदीयाः । किदृशा वयं । शर्धो बलं जज्ञाना उत्पादयंतः स्तुतिभिर्याताश्च होतुं त्वां प्राप्ताश्च रथा रंहणशीलाश्च । किंच हे अहिशुष्म । अहिरयनात् ।
> सर्वतो व्याप्तबलेंद्र त्वदनुग्रहाद्दव्यः स्तुत्यः प्रतिभटैराह्वातव्यो ना चारुः
> संगंता सत्वा भृत्यादिः प्रभृथेषु संग्रामेषु यज्ञेषु वास्माना जगम्यात् ।
> अगच्छेत् । भगो न भग इव हव्यश्चारुः । भगदेवो यथास्मत्सहाय आगच्छति तथा
> भृत्यादिरागच्छेदित्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: O Indra, we who are the leaders of rites, the priests and the sacrificers, are yours. What are we like? We produce strength (शर्धो (*śardho*) means *bala*) by our praises, we have come to you in order to offer the oblation, and we are swift. Further, O Ahiśuṣma, Indra of all-pervading strength, by your favour let a follower, an attendant and the like (सत्वा (*satvā*)), who is praiseworthy, to be called upon by those who face the adversaries, and agreeable, a companion, come to us in the battles or in the sacrifices (प्रभृथेषु (*prabhṛtheṣu*)). Like Bhaga: as the god Bhaga comes as our helper, so may the attendant and the rest come; that is the meaning.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—O Indra
- शर्धः (*śardhaḥ*)—(your) strength
- जज्ञानाः (*jajñānāḥ*)—those who increase it (by our praises)
- याताश्च (*yātāśca*)—those who approach you
- रथाः (*rathāḥ*)—those who move swiftly to gain your presence
- ये वयं (*ye vayaṃ*)—we who
- नरः (*naraḥ*)—are the leaders of all rites
- ते (*te*)—such as we are
- ते (*te*)—are yours alone
- अहिशुष्म (*ahiśuṣma*)—O Indra, drier up of enemies
- हव्यः (*havyaḥ*)—one of praiseworthy valour
- चारुः (*cāruḥ*)—and trusted
- सत्वा (*satvā*)—follower
- प्रभृथेषु (*prabhṛtheṣu*)—in battles
- भगो न (*bhago na*)—like Bhaga, as a helper
- आस्मान् (*āsmān*)—to our side
- आ जगम्यात् (*ā jagamyāt*)—may he come and join

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, we who increase your strength by our praises, who approach you alone, who strive only for the joy of your presence, and who are the leaders of all rites, such as we are, are indeed yours.

### Printed p. 256

O Indra, drier up of enemies, may a follower of praiseworthy valour and trusted, like Bhaga as a helper, come to our help in battles.

##### English translation
We, Indra, are yours, and yours are they who are the leaders ( of rites), promoters of (your), strength, and willing applicants (to you) : all-powerful Indra, may adherents worthy of commendation, and faithful, come to us, like Bhaga, in battles.

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> प॒पृ॒क्षेण्य॑मिंद्र॒ त्वे ह्योजो॑ नृ॒म्मानि॑ च नृ॒तमा॑नो॒  ॑
> अमर्तः ।  
> स न॒ एनीं॑ वसवानो र॒यिं दाः॒ प्रार्यः स्तु॑षे तुविम॒घस्य॒दानं॑ ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> पपृक्षेण्यं । इंद्र । त्वे इति । हि । ओजः । नृम्मानि । च । नृतमानः । अमर्तः ।  
> सः । नः । एनीं । वसवानः । रयिं । दाः । प्र । अर्यः । स्तुषे । तुविमघस्य ।  
> दानं ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र पपृक्षेण्यं संपर्कार्हं पूज्यं वौजो बलं त्वे हि त्वयि खलु ।
> त्वदधीनमित्यर्थः । नृतमानो नृत्यन् अमर्तोऽमरणधर्मा स त्वं वसवान
> आच्छादयन् स्वतेजसा जगत् नृम्मानि नृमणानि द्रविणानि । वक्षमाणरय्यपेक्षया
> चशब्दः । एनीमेनवर्णां श्वेतवर्णां रयिं धनं दाः । देहि । अहं च अर्य
> ईश्वरस्य तुविमघस्य प्रभूतधनस्य च दानं प्र स्तुषे । सौमि ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: O Indra, your strength (वौजो (*vaujo*)) is worthy of association and to be revered; it rests in you, that is, it depends on you. You, dancing, immortal, subject to no death, covering the world with your own splendour, give wealth (नृम्मानि (*nṛmmāni*) means riches); the word *ca* has regard to the wealth that is to be mentioned. Give the wealth that is tawny, that is, white in colour. And I praise the gift of the lord, the possessor of abundant wealth; that is the meaning.

### Printed p. 257

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- त्वे हि (*tve hi*)—residing in you
- ओजः (*ojaḥ*)—strength
- पपृक्षेण्यं (*papṛkṣeṇyaṃ*)—is exceedingly praiseworthy and radiant
- नृतमानः (*nṛtamānaḥ*)—full of joy
- अमर्तः (*amartaḥ*)—free from the nature of death
- सः (*saḥ*)—you
- वसवानः (*vasavānaḥ*)—covering the whole world with your splendour
- नृम्मानि (*nṛmmāni*)—riches
- एनीं (*enīṃ*)—shining
- रयिं (*rayiṃ*)—wealth
- नः (*naḥ*)—to us
- दाः (*dāḥ*)—give (I also)
- अर्यः (*aryaḥ*)—the lord
- तुविमघस्य (*tuvimaghasya*)—of you who possess abundant wealth
- दानं (*dānaṃ*)—the gift
- प्र स्तुषे (*pra stuṣe*)—I praise

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, the strength that resides in you is exceedingly praiseworthy and radiant. You, who are full of joy and free from the nature of death, covering the whole world with your splendour, give us riches and shining wealth. I also praise the gift of you who are the lord and possess abundant wealth.

##### English translation
Glorious, Indra, is your strength, exulting, immortal, and clothing (the world with light) : give us riches, and brilliant wealth, and I will greatly praise the munificence of the opulent lord.

#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ए॒वा न॑ इंद्रो॒तिभि॑रव पा॒हि गृ॑ण॒तः शू॑र का॒रून् ।  
> उ॒त त्वचं॒ दद॑तो॒ पाज॑सात् पिप्री॒हि मध्वः॒ सुषु॑तस्य॒  ॑
> चारो ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> एव । नः । इंद्र । ऊतिभिः । अव । पाहि । गृणतः । शूर । कारून् ।  
> उत । त्वच । ददतः । वाजसात् । पिप्रीहि । मध्वः । सुसुतस्य । चारोः ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे शूरेंद्र एवैवं नोऽस्मान् गृणतः स्तुवतः कारून् कर्त्र्हनृत्वज
> ऊतिभी रक्षणैरव । पालय । उतापि च वाजसात् संग्रामे त्वचमाच्छादकं रूपं

### Printed p. 258

> ददतः प्रयच्छतः सुषुतस्य सुष्त्वभिषुतस्य चारोर्मनोहरस्य मध्वः सोमस्य
> पिप्रीहि । प्रीणयात्मानं । द्वितीयार्थेषष्ठी । उक्तलक्षणं सोमं पिबेत्यर्थः
> अथवा तृतीयार्थे षष्ठी । उक्तलक्षणेन सोमपानेन प्रीणय ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: O heroic Indra, in this very way protect us, who praise you, the performers of rites and priests, with your protections. And further, in battle (वाजसात् (*vājasāt*)) take the covering form that envelops. Satisfy yourself, give delight to yourself, with the sweet Soma, well pressed and charming. The genitive here stands for the accusative: the meaning is "drink the Soma of the kind described". Or else the genitive stands for the instrumental: delight yourself with the drinking of the Soma of the kind described.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- शूर (*śūra*)—O valiant one
- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- एव (*eva*)—in one and the same way
- गृणतः (*gṛṇataḥ*)—who praise you
- कारून् (*kārūn*)—and perform rites (or praises)
- नः (*naḥ*)—us
- ऊतिभिः (*ūtibhiḥ*)—with your protections
- अव पाहि (*ava pāhi*)—protect
- उत (*uta*)—and
- वाजसात् (*vājasāt*)—in battle
- त्वचं (*tvacaṃ*)—the form
- ददतः (*dadataḥ*)—that produces (for you a form that is a covering)
- सुषुतस्य (*suṣutasya*)—that is pressed out excellently
- चारोः (*cāroḥ*)—and charming
- मध्वः (*madhvaḥ*)—of Soma (by drinking)
- पिप्रीहि (*piprīhi*)—become satisfied

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O valiant Indra, protect us, who praise you in one and the same way and who carry out the rites, with your protections. And in battle become satisfied by drinking the Soma, which produces for you a covering form, which is excellently pressed out and charming.

##### English translation
Hero, Indra, with your protection defend us, praising and worshipping (you', and be propitiated (by drinking) of the well-effused and agree able Soma juice that yields 'a defensive) covering in combat.

#### Ṛk 8

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> उ॒त त्ये मा॑ पौरुकु॒त्स्यस्य॑ सू॒रेस्त्र॒सद॑स्योर्हिर॒णिनो॒ ररा॑णाः ।  
> वहं॑तु मा॒ दश॒ श्येता॑सो अस्य गैरिक्षि॒तस्य॒ क्रतु॑भि॒र्नु स॑श्चे ॥८॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> उत । त्ये । मा । पौरुकुत्स्यस्य । सूरेः । त्रसदस्कोः । हिरणिनः । रराणाः ।  
> वहंतु । मा । दश । श्येतासः । अस्य । गैरिक्षितस्य । क्रतुभिः । नु । सश्चे ॥ ८ ॥

### Printed p. 259

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> त्रसदस्युश्च विदधो ध्वन्यश्चेति त्रयो नृपाः । अश्वान्थनानि गाश्चादुर्यषये
> तद्वदत्यषिः ॥ उतापि च तै ते मा । चतुर्थ्यर्थे द्वितीया । मह्यमित्यर्थः। रराणाः
> रातेर्दानार्थस्य कर्मणि लिटः कानचि रूपं ॥ दत्ता इत्यर्थः । पौरुकुत्स्यस्य पुरुकुत्सापत्यस्य सूरेः प्रेरकस्य हिरणिनो हिरण्यवतोऽस्य त्रसदस्कोरेतन्नामकस्य
> राजर्षेगैरिक्षितस्य गिरिक्षितगोत्रोत्पन्नस्य श्वेतासः शुभ्रवर्णा दशाश्वा मा मां
> वहंतु । प्रापयंतु । अहु च क्रतुभिः कर्मभी रथनियोजनादिभिर्यज्ञादिभिर्वा
> नु क्षिप्रं सष्टे । गच्छेयं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: "Trasadasyu, Vidadha and Dhvanya, these three kings gave horses, wealth and cows to the seer; so the seer says." Further, त्ये (*tye*) means "those", and मा (*mā*) is the accusative in the sense of the dative: to me. रराणाः (*rarāṇāḥ*) means given. May the ten white, bright-coloured horses of this Trasadasyu, the royal seer sprung from the line of Girikṣita, son of Purukutsa, the impeller, possessed of gold, carry me, bring me (to the sacrifice). And with rites, that is, with the harnessing of the chariot and the like, or with sacrifices and the like, may I go quickly; that is the meaning.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- उत (*uta*)—and
- मा (*mā*)—to me
- रराणः (*rarāṇaḥ*)—given
- गैरिक्षितस्य (*gairikṣitasya*)—sprung from the lineage of Girikṣita
- पौरुकुत्सस्य (*paurukutsasya*)—son of Purukutsa
- सूरेः (*sūreḥ*)—composer of praises
- हिरणिनः (*hiraṇinaḥ*)—and wealthy
- अस्य त्रसदस्कोः (*asya trasadaskoḥ*)—belonging to this Trasadasyu
- श्येतासः (*śyetāsaḥ*)—of white colour
- त्ये दश (*tye daśa*)—these ten horses
- मा (*mā*)—me
- वहंतु (*vahaṃtu*)—may they carry (to the place of sacrifice)
- क्रतुभिः (*kratubhiḥ*)—with the rites
- नु (*nu*)—quickly
- सश्चे (*saśce*)—may they bring me together

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

May these ten horses of white colour, belonging to Trasadasyu, sprung from the lineage of Girikṣita, son of Purukutsa, composer of praises and wealthy, carry me to the place of sacrifice. And may they bring us quickly together with the rites.

##### English translation
May those ten bright horses, the gift to me of the pious gold-possessing Trasadasyu, the son of Purukutsa, of the race of Girikshita, convey me (to the sacrifice), and may I proceed quickly with the rites

#### Ṛk 9

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> उ॒त त्वे मा॑ मारु॒ताश्व॑स्य॒ शोणाः॒ क्रत्वा॑मघासो वि॒दथ॑स्य  ॒
> रात् ।  
> स॒हस्रा॑ मे॒ च्यव॑तानो॒ ददा॑न आनू॒कम॒र्यो वपु॑षे॒  ॑
> नार्चत् ॥ ९ ॥

### Printed p. 260

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> उत । त्ये । मा । मारुतऽश्वस्य । शोणाः । क्रत्वामघासः । विदथस्य । रात् ।  
> सहस्रा । मे । च्यवतानः । ददानः । आनूकं । अर्यः । वपुषे । न । आर्चत् ॥ ९ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> उतापि च तै श्वा वक्ष्यमाणा मारुताश्वस्य । मरुत्सदृशवेगाश्ववान्मरुताश्वः । तदपत्यस्य विदथस्यॆतन्नामकस्य राज्ञः शोणाः शोणवर्णाः क्रत्वामघासः
> क्रतुना कर्मणा शीघ्रगमनादिलक्षणेन माहनीया अश्वा रात् दाने विनियुक्ता
> मां वहंतीति शेषः । किंच सहस्रा सहस्राण्यपरिमितानि धनान्यर्यः पूज्यस्य मे ।
> चतुर्थ्यर्थे षष्ठी । पूज्याय मह्यं च्यवतानश्चावयन् ददानः प्रयच्छन् स
> आनूकमाभरणं च वपुषे स्वशरीरालंकारायार्चत् । प्रायच्छत् । नेति चार्थे ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: And those (horses) to be mentioned: the horses of Vidatha, the king so named, son of Mārutāśva, one whose horses have a speed like that of the Maruts; tawny-coloured (शोणाः (*śoṇāḥ*)), made worthy of honour by a rite (क्रतुना (*kratunā*)) marked by swift going and the like, assigned at the gift, carry me; "carry me" is the remainder to be supplied. Further, the king gave countless thousands of riches to me, the venerable one (the genitive stands for the dative), causing them to fall to me as a gift; and he gave ornament for the adornment of my own body. The word *na* is in the sense of "and".**NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- उत (*uta*)—and
- मारुताश्वस्य (*mārutāśvasya*)—of the son of Marutāśva
- विदथस्य (*vidathasya*)—belonging to the king named Vidatha
- शोणाः (*śoṇāḥ*)—golden-coloured
- क्रत्वामघासः (*kratvāmaghāsaḥ*)—those that have gained greatness through rites
- रात् (*rāt*)—(given to me) at the time of the gift
- तै (*tai*)—those horses
- मा (*mā*)—me (may they carry to the sacrifice) (and that same king)
- सहस्रा (*sahasrā*)—countless riches
- अर्यः (*aryaḥ*)—venerable
- मे (*me*)—to me
- च्यवता (*cyavatā*)—wishing to give
- ददानः (*dadānaḥ*)—giving (together with them)
- वपुषे (*vapuṣe*)—for the adornment of the body
- आनुकं (*ānukaṃ*)—and ornament
- अर्चत् (*arcat*)—gave

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

May the horses given to me at the time of the gift by the king named Vidatha, son of Mārutāśva, golden-coloured and having gained greatness through rites, carry me to the sacrifice. The same king, in his affection for me, who am worthy of his respect, gave me countless riches and presented ornaments for the adornment of my body.

##### English translation
Or may those bay, well-actioned horses, the donation of Vidatha, the son of Marutashwa, (convey me) : or (may) the thousands (of treasure) which he was bestowing upon and giving to me, entitled to respect, and the ornaments which he presented (to decorate) the person, (contribute to the ceremony).

### Printed p. 261

#### Ṛk 10

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> उ॒त तै मा॑ ध्व॒न॑स्य॒ जुष्टा॑ लक्ष्म॒ण्य॑स्य सु॒रुचो॒ यता॑नाः ।  
> म॒ह्ना रा॒यः सं॒वर॑णस्य॒ ऋषे॑व्र॒जं न गावः॒ प्रय॑ता॒ ॑ 
> अपि ग्मन् ॥ १० ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> उत । त्ये । मा । ध्वन्यस्य । जुष्टाः । लक्षण्यस्य । सुरुचः । यतानाः ।  
> मह्ना । रायः । संवरणस्य । ऋषेः । व्रजं । न । गावः । प्रयताः । अपि । ग्मन् ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> उतापि च तै ते वक्ष्यमाणा रायो ध्वन्यस्य ध्वन्यनामकस्य लक्षण्यस्य
> लक्ष्मणपुत्रस्य राज्ञः संबंधिनोऽश्वा मा जुष्टा मां प्राप्ताः सुरुचः शोभनदीप्तयो यताना वहमानाय यतमानाः । रायो मह्ना महत्वेन युक्ता
> रायः प्रयता दत्ताः सत्यः संवरणस्य ऋषेरपि ग्मन् । अपिगताः । प्राप्ता मद्गृहमिति शेषः । व्रजं न गावो गोष्ठं गाव इव । यद्वा । नेति चार्थे । गावश्च मम
> व्रजमपिग्मन् । अत्रैंद्रे सूक्ते प्रायेणैंद्रे मरुतो राजां च दानस्तुतॆयः ।
> अनु- २-२२ । इत्युक्तत्वाद्राज्ञां दानस्तुतिरविरुद्धा ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: And those (horses) to be mentioned, belonging to the king Dhvanya, son of Lakṣmaṇa, have come to me, shining beautifully, striving to carry me. The riches endowed with greatness, given, have reached the house of the seer Saṃvaraṇa; "reached my house" is the remainder to be supplied. They are like cows going to a stall. Or else *na* is in the sense of "and": and cows also have reached my stall. In this Aindra sūkta, since it is said, "for the most part in Aindra (hymns) there are praises of gifts by the Maruts and by kings" (Anu. 2-22), a praise of the gifts of kings is not contradictory.**NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- उत (*uta*)—or
- लक्ष्मण्यस्य (*lakṣmaṇyasya*)—of the son of Lakṣmaṇa
- ध्वन्यस्य (*dhvanyasya*)—named Dhvanya, the king who gave
- जुष्टाः (*juṣṭāḥ*)—those that have joined me
- सुरुचः (*surucaḥ*)—of resplendent brilliance
- यतानाः (*yatānāḥ*)—eager to carry me away
- त्ये (*tye*)—those horses
- मा (*mā*)—me (may they carry to the sacrifice) (and)
- गावः (*gāvaḥ*)—cows
- व्रजं न (*vrajaṃ na*)—as they hasten to and reach the stall
- प्रयताः (*prayatāḥ*)—given to me
- मह्वा (*mahvā*)—filled with greatness
- रायः (*rāyaḥ*)—riches
- संवरणस्य ऋषेः (*saṃvaraṇasya ṛṣeḥ*)—to the house of the seer named Saṃvaraṇa
- अपि ग्मन् (*api gman*)—may they go and reach

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

May the horses given by the king named Dhvanya, son of Lakṣmaṇa, that have come into my possession, are radiant and are eager to carry me to the sacrifice, carry me to the sacrifice. As cows hasten to and reach their stalls, may the riches given to me, filled with greatness, go and reach the

### Printed p. 262

house of the seer named Saṃvaraṇa (my house).

##### English translation
Or may the bright and active steeds bestowed upon me by Dhwanya, the son of Lakshmana (bear me) : the riches, endowed with greatness, that have been presented, have passed ( to the dwelling) of the Rishi Samvarana, like cows to their stalls.

## Sūkta 34 (Maṇḍala 5)

The thirty-fourth sūkta.

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अजातशत्रुमिति नवर्चं द्वितीयं सूक्तं प्राजापत्यस्य संवरणस्यार्षमैंद्रं ।
> नवमी त्रिष्टुप् शिष्टास्त्रिष्टुंबतपरिभाषया जगत्यः । अजातशत्रुं नव त्रिष्टुबंतमित्यनुक्रमणिका । विनियोगो लैंगिकः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: The second sūkta, अजातशत्रुमिति (*ajātaśatrumiti*), of nine ṛks, belongs to the seer Saṃvaraṇa Prājāpatya; its deity is Indra. The ninth ṛk is in Triṣṭubh; the rest are Jagatī by the convention on a sūkta that ends in Triṣṭubh.**NOTE** The Anukramaṇikā says: अजातशत्रुं नव (*ajātaśatruṃ nava*) (the sūkta is called nine ṛks ending in Triṣṭubh). The use in ritual is laiṅgika.

Translation of the Anuvāda: This sūkta, Ajātaśatrum, is the second sūkta in the third anuvāka. There are nine ṛks in it. The seer of this sūkta is Saṃvaraṇa, son of Prajāpati; Indra is the deity; the ninth ṛk of this sūkta is in the Triṣṭubh metre and the remaining ṛks are in the Jagatī metre. In the Anukramaṇikā it is said: "Ajātaśatruṃ nava triṣṭubantam". The use in ritual is laiṅgika.

Sūkta 31.**NOTE**

Maṇḍala 5, Anuvāka 3, Sūkta 34.

Aṣṭaka 4, Adhyāya 2, Varga 3, 4.

Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 9.

Seer: Saṃvaraṇa Prājāpatya.

Deity: Indra.

Metre: 1-8, Jagatī, Triṣṭubh.

### Printed p. 263

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अजा॑तशत्रुम॒जरा॒ स्व॑र्व॒त्यनु॑ स्व॒धामि॑ता द॒स्ममी॑यते ।  
> सु॒नोत॑न॒ पच॑त॒ ब्रह्म॑वाहसे पुरुष्टु॒ताय॑ प्रत॒रं द॑धातन ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अजातशत्रुं । अजरा । स्वःवती । अनु । स्वधा । अमिता । दस्मं । ईयते ।  
> सुनोतन । पचत । ब्रह्मवाहसे । पुरुस्तुताय । प्रतरं । दधातन ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अजातशत्रुं । अनुत्पन्नाः शातयितारो यस्य तं । दस्मं शत्रूणामुपक्षपयितारमजराक्षिणा स्वर्वती स्वरणवत्यमितापरिमिता स्वधान्नं हविरन्वियते । अनुप्राप्नोति । तदर्थं हे ऋत्विजः सुनोतन । अभिषुणुत । पचत पुरोडाशादिकं ।
> कस्मै । ब्रह्मवाहसे ब्रह्मणः परिवृढस्य स्तोत्रस्य वाहकाय पुरुष्टुताय बहुभिः
> स्तु तायेंद्राय । प्रतरं प्रकृष्टतरं दधातन । धारयत स्वस्वोचितकर्म ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: *Ajātaśatru* is he whose destroyers are not born. *Dasma* is the one who casts down enemies. Food as oblation (स्वधान्नं (*svadhānnaṃ*)), undecaying, leading to heaven and unlimited, follows him, that is, reaches him. For that purpose, O priests, press out the Soma and cook the cakes and the rest. For whom? For Indra, the bearer of *brahma*, the all-encompassing praise, praised by many. Set in place your respective duties in the best manner.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अजरा (*ajarā*)—undecaying
- स्वर्वती (*svarvatī*)—leading to heaven
- अमिता (*amitā*)—and unlimited
- स्वधा (*svadhā*)—food in the form of oblation
- अजातशत्रुं (*ajātaśatruṃ*)—the one who has no enemy at all
- दस्मं (*dasmaṃ*)—and destroyer of enemies, the god
- अनु ईयते (*anu īyate*)—goes and reaches (O priests)
- सुनोतन (*sunotana*)—press out the Soma juice
- पचत (*pacata*)—cook the cakes and the rest
- ब्रह्मवाहसे (*brahmavāhase*)—who receives and bears the most excellent praises
- पुरुष्टुताय (*puruṣṭutāya*)—and is praised by many, to Indra
- प्रतरं (*prataraṃ*)—in the most excellent manner
- दधातन (*dadhātana*)—perform (your respective) rites

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The food in the form of oblation, undecaying and unlimited, goes and reaches the god who has no enemy and destroys enemies. O priests, press out the Soma juice. Cook the cakes and the rest. Perform your respective rites in the most excellent manner, addressing Indra, who receives and bears the most excellent praises and is praised by many.

### Printed p. 264

##### English translation
The undecaying, heaven-conferring, unlimited (sacrificial) food, goes to the tamer (of enemies), whose adversaries are unborn : therefore (priests), pour out (the libation), dress (the cakes and butter), diligently discharge (your offices) to him who is the accepter of prayer, who is glorified by many.

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आ यः सोमे॑न ज॒ठर॒मपि॑प्र॒तामं॑दत म॒घवा॒ मध्यो॒  ॑
> अंधसः ।  
> यदीं॑ मृ॒गाय॒ हंत॑वे म॒हाव॑धः स॒हस्र॑भृष्टिमु॒शना॑ व॒धं  ॑
> यमत् ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आ । यः । सोमेन । जठरं । आपिप्रत । अमंदत । मघ्ववा । मध्वः । अंधसः ।  
> यत् । ईं । मृगाय । हंतवे । महावधः । सहस्रभृष्टिं । उशना । वधं ।  
> यमत् ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> यो मघवा सोमेन जठरं स्वकीयोदरमा सर्वतोऽपिप्रत अपूरयत्
> मध्वो मधुररसस्यांधसः सोमस्य पानेनामुंदत । अतृप्त्यत् । यद्यदेमयं
> मृगायैतन्नामकायासुराय हंतवे तं हंतुं महावधो महावद्रः सहस्रभृष्टिरपरिमिततेजा उशना कामयमानः शत्रुं यद्वशनसा सह वधं वज्रं
> यमत् उदयच्छत् तदापिप्रत अनुदंत च ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: Maghavan, who filled his own belly all round with Soma, was exhilarated, that is, satisfied, by drinking the sweet-flavoured Soma (अंधसः (*aṃdhasaḥ*)). When he, desiring to kill the Asura named Mṛga, raised the thunderbolt, the great weapon, thousand-edged, of unlimited brilliance, desiring the enemy, or else together with Uśanas, then he filled himself and was delighted.**NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यः (*yaḥ*)—who
- सोमेन (*somena*)—with Soma juice
- जठरं (*jaṭharaṃ*)—his belly
- आ अपिप्रत (*ā apiprata*)—fills completely, such a one
- मघवा (*maghavā*)—Indra
- मध्वा (*madhvā*)—of the sweet
- अंधसः (*aṃdhasaḥ*)—by drinking the Soma juice
- अमंदत (*amaṃdata*)—was satisfied
- यत् (*yat*)—immediately thereafter
- ईं (*īṃ*)—Indra

### Printed p. 265

- मृगाय (*mṛgāya*)—the Asura named Mṛga
- हंतवे (*haṃtave*)—to kill
- उशना (*uśanā*)—desiring
- सहस्रभृष्टिः (*sahasrabhṛṣṭiḥ*)—having thousands of edges
- महावधः (*mahāvadhaḥ*)—and destructive
- वधं (*vadhaṃ*)—the thunderbolt weapon
- यमत् (*yamat*)—raised and held

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Indra, who fills his belly completely with Soma juice, was satisfied by drinking the sweet Soma juice. Immediately thereafter, desiring to kill the Asura named Mṛga, he raised and held in his hand the thunderbolt weapon, which has thousands of edges and is destructive.

##### English translation
Maghavan, who fills his belly with the Soma, is exhilarated (by drinking) of the sweet-savoured beverage, whereupon he has lifted up his destructive thousand-edged weapon, desiring to slay (the Asura) Mriga.

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यो अ॑स्मै घ्रं॒स उ॒त वा॒ य ऊध॑नि॒ सोमं॑ सु॒नोति॒ भव॑ति ॒ ॑
> द्युमा अह ।  
> अपा॑प श॒क्रस्त॑त॒नुष्टि॑मूहति त॒नूशु॑भ्रं म॒घवा॒ यः  ॒॑
> कवासखः ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यः । अस्मै । घ्रंसे । उत । वा । यः । ऊधनि । सोमं । सुनोति । भवति ।  
> द्युमान् । अह ।  
> अपाप । शक्रः । ततनुष्टिं । ऊहति । तनूशुभ्रं । मघवा । यः । कवसखः ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> यो यजमानोऽस्मा इंद्राय फ्रंसे । अहर्नामैतत् । गृह्यंतेऽस्मिन्रसा
> इति फ्रंसः । तस्मिन् । उत वा अथवा यो यजमान ऊधनि । उद्धततरं भवत्यु-

### Printed p. 266

> न्नद्धमिति वोधो रात्रिः । स्नेहप्रदानरसाभ्यामूधसि रात्रौ च समत्वाद्रात्रिरप्यूध
> उच्यते । अहनि रात्रौ च सोमं सुनोति स भवति द्युमान् दीप्तिमान् ॥ अहेति
> विनिग्रहार्थियः । किंच शक्रः शक्तोऽयमिंद्रस्ततनुष्टिं । ततं धर्मसंततिं नुदति
> वष्टि कामयते कामानिति ततनुष्टिः । तं तनूशुभ्रं तनूः शुभ्रा शोभनीयालंकारादिभिर्यस्य तं । तादृशं स्वपोषकमयज्ञानमपोहति । अपरोऽपशब्दः
> पूरणार्थः । मघवा धनवान्यश्च कवासखः कुत्सितपुरुषसहायस्तमपोहतीति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: The sacrificer who, for this Indra, in the *phraṃsa*, which is a name for the day (the juices are taken in it), or else the one who, in the *ūdhan*, the night: what is more swollen and raised is called *ūdhas*; since night is like the udder in giving moisture and sap, night too is called *ūdhas*. He who presses Soma by day and by night becomes radiant. The word *aha* is in the sense of restraint. Further, this capable Indra drives away the *tatanuṣṭi*, that is, one who thrusts aside the continuity of dharma and desires desires. He also drives away such a one, whose body is resplendent with ornaments and the like, who nourishes only himself and does not perform sacrifice; the second *apa* is for completion. And he drives away the wealthy man who is the friend of base men.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यः (*yaḥ*)—the sacrificer who
- अस्मै (*asmai*)—addressing Indra
- फ्रंसे (*phraṃse*)—by day
- उत वा (*uta vā*)—or
- यः (*yaḥ*)—whoever
- ऊधनि (*ūdhani*)—also in the night
- सोमं (*somaṃ*)—the Soma juice (to offer it)
- सुनोति (*sunoti*)—presses (he)
- द्युमान् (*dyumān*)—radiant
- भवति (*bhavati*)—becomes
- शक्रः (*śakraḥ*)—Indra
- ततनुष्टिं (*tatanuṣṭiṃ*)—one who, without devotion to god, is proud only of his offspring
- तनूशुभ्रं (*tanūśubhraṃ*)—and proud only of his own body, one fallen from rites
- अप ऊहति (*apa ūhati*)—rejects
- यः (*yaḥ*)—whoever
- मघवा (*maghavā*)—though wealthy
- कवासखः (*kavāsakhaḥ*)—is a friend of wicked people (he rejects him too)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Those sacrificers who press out and offer Soma juice to Indra by day and by night become radiant. Indra rejects those who, without devotion to god, nurse an unworthy pride in their offspring alone and in their own bodies alone, and those who, though wealthy, are friends of wicked people.

##### English translation
He who pours out the libation to that Indra, by day or by night, undoubtedly becomes illustrious : Shakra disregards the man who is proud of his descendants and vain of his person, and who, though wealthy, is the friend of the base.

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यस्याव॑धीत्पि॒तरं॒ यस्य॑ मा॒तरं॒ यस्य॑ श॒क्रो भ्रात॑रं॒ नात॑  
> ईषते ।  
> वेतीद्व॑स्य॒ प्रय॑ता यतंक॒रो न किल्बि॑षादीषते॒ वस्व॑ आक॒रः ॥

### Printed p. 267

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यस्य । अवधीत् । पितरं । यस्य । मातरं । यस्य । शक्रः । भ्रातरं । न । अतः । ईषते ।  
> वेति । इत् । ऊं इति । अस्य । प्रयता । यतंकरः । न । किल्पिषात् । ईषते ।  
> वस्वः । आकरः ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> शक्रो शक्तोऽयं यस्यायज्वनः पितरमवधीत् हतवान् । यस्य च
> मातरमवधीत् । यस्य च भ्रातरं अतोऽस्मादयज्जनः सकाशान्नेषते । न बिभेति
> न गच्छति वा । किंत्वस्य प्रयता दत्तानि हवींषि वेतीदु । कामयत एव । अयज्ञानं
> शिक्षयित्वा नियोजयतीत्यर्थः । यतंकरो यमनकर्ता वस्वो वसुनो धनस्याकर
> आभिमुख्यकर्ता यः किल्बिषात्पित्रादिवधयुक्तान्नेषते न चलति न बिभेति वा । इंद्रस्यास्तोत्थणां हतरिंद्रो यतीन् सालावृकेभ्यः प्रायच्छत् अंतरिक्षे पौलोमान्पृथिव्यां कालकांज्यानरुन्मुखान्तीन् सालावृकेभ्यः प्रायच्छमित्यादि श्रुतिषु प्रसिद्धा ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: This Indra, the capable one, has slain the father of a certain non-sacrificer, and also his mother and his brother; yet he does not fear this non-sacrificer nor go away from him. Rather he desires the oblations offered by him: having instructed the non-sacrificer, he engages him in the rite; that is the meaning. *Yataṃkara* is the regulator; *ākara* of wealth is the one who brings wealth toward the sacrificer. Indra neither moves away from nor fears the sin consisting in the killing of a father and the like. Passages in the śrutis, such as "Indra gave the ascetics to the wolves; in the atmosphere the Paulomas, on the earth the Kālakāñjas", are well known.**NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- शक्रः (*śakraḥ*)—Indra
- यस्य (*yasya*)—of whichever man
- पितरं (*pitaraṃ*)—the father
- अवधीत् (*avadhīt*)—has slain
- यस्य (*yasya*)—of whom
- मातरं (*mātaraṃ*)—the mother (has slain)
- यस्य (*yasya*)—of whom
- भ्रातरं (*bhrātaraṃ*)—the brothers (has slain)
- अतः (*ataḥ*)—from all these people
- न ईषते (*na īṣate*)—he is not afraid
- अस्य (*asya*)—from such persons
- प्रयता (*prayatā*)—the oblations offered
- वेतीदु (*vetīdu*)—he surely desires and accepts
- यतंकरः (*yataṃkaraḥ*)—who teaches the rules of rites
- वस्वः (*vasvaḥ*)—of wealth
- आकरः (*ākaraḥ*)—the support, Indra
- किल्पिषात् (*kilpiṣāt*)—from the sinful deeds of those who do not follow the Veda
- न ईषते (*na īṣate*)—is not frightened

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Indra is not afraid of a man whose father, mother or brother he has slain for their sinful deeds. Or, if that man offers an oblation in the proper way, he accepts it without rejecting it. Indra, who teaches the rules of rites and is the support of wealth, is not frightened by the sinful deeds of those who do not follow the Veda.

##### English translation
Shakra does not turn away from him whose father, whose mother, whose brother he has slain, but is willing to accept his offerings : the regulator (of acts), the bestower of riches, does not turn away from iniquity.

### Printed p. 268

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> न पं॒चभि॑र्द॒शभि॑ वृष्ट्या॒रभं॒ नासु॑न्वता सचते॒ पुष्य॑ता च॒न ।  
> जि॒नाति॒ वेद॑मु॒या हंति॑ वा॒ धुनि॒रा दे॑व॒युं भ॑जति॒ ॑ ॒
> गोमति व्रजे ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> न । पंचभिः । दश्यभिः । वष्टि । आरभं । न । असुन्वता । सचते । पुष्यता । चन ।  
> जिनाति । वा । इत् । अमुया । हंति । वा । धुनिः । आ । देवयुं । भजति ।  
> गोमति । व्रजे ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अयमिंद्रः पंचभिर्दशभिर्वा सहायैरारभमारंभं शत्रुहननायालंबनं
> न वष्टि । साहाय्यं नापेक्षत इत्यर्थः । यद्वा । अमुमिंद्रं पंचभिर्दशभिर्वा
> यज्ञादन्यैरुषायैरारभमारब्धु मुद्योगं कर्तुं न वष्टि । न कामयते। असुन्वताभिषवमकुर्वता सह पुष्यता चन बंध्वादीनपोषयतापि न सचते। न संगच्छते।
> असुन्वंतमभिषवमकुर्वाणमयष्टारमपुष्कंतं च न प्राप्नोतीत्यर्थः । न केवलमप्राप्तिमात्रं किंतु जिनाति वा । नाशब्दश्चार्थे । जिनाति च : बाधते । इदिति
> पूरणः । हंति नामुयामुमयष्टारु धुनिः कंपकः शत्रूणां । देवयुं
> देवमिंद्रं कामयमानं यजमानं गोमति व्रज आ भजति । योजयतीत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: This Indra does not desire (न वष्टि (*na vaṣṭi*)) to begin an undertaking, a support for slaying enemies, with five or ten helpers; he does not look for help; that is the meaning. Or else: he does not desire, does not wish, to make an effort towards that undertaking by means other than the sacrifice, five or ten. He does not associate even with one who does not press the Soma and does not nourish his dependants; that is, he does not reach one who does not press, does not sacrifice and does not nourish. Not only does he not reach him; he also overcomes him: the word *na* is in the sense of "and": he oppresses and slays that non-sacrificer. The word *it* is for filling out. *Dhuni* is the shaker of enemies. The sacrificer who desires the god Indra he joins to a stall rich in cows; that is the meaning.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra
- आरभं (*ārabhaṃ*)—for an effort that restrains enemies
- पंचभिः (*paṃcabhiḥ*)—with five persons
- दशभिः (*daśabhiḥ*)—or with ten persons
- न वष्टि (*na vaṣṭi*)—he does not look for help
- असुन्वता (*asunvatā*)—with a man who does not press Soma (for the sacrifice)
- पुष्यता चन (*puṣyatā cana*)—and does not nourish his relations and the like
- न सचते (*na sacate*)—he does not associate (not only that)
- जिनाति वा (*jināti vā*)—he oppresses him
- हंति वा (*haṃti vā*)—or slays him
- अमु (*amu*)—such a one fallen from rites
- धुनिः (*dhuniḥ*)—Indra, who terrifies
- देवयुं (*devayuṃ*)—the sacrificer who longs for and worships Indra and the other gods for help
- गोमति (*gomati*)—full of cows
- वज्रे (*vajre*)—together with the stall
- आ भजति (*ā bhajati*)—he joins

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Indra does not look for the help of any company, whether five persons or ten, in his effort to destroy enemies. He does not associate at all with a man who does not press Soma for the sacrifice and does not nourish his relations and the like.

### Printed p. 269

Moreover, he oppresses him, or slays him. Indra, who terrifies such a man fallen from rites, provides stalls full of cows for the sacrificers who worship Indra and the other gods.

##### English translation
He desires not (association in) enterprises with five or with ten ; he associates not with the man who does not present libations ; and cherishes not ( his dependants ) : nay, the terrorier (of foes) punishes him, but he places the devout man in a pasturage stocked with cattle.

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> वि॒त्वक्ष॑णः॒ समृ॑तौ चक्रमास॒जोऽसु॑न्वतो॒ विषु॑णः सुन्व॒तो  ॒
> वृधः ।  
> इंद्रो॒ विश्व॑स्य दमि॒ता वि॒भीष॑णो यथाव॒शं न॑यति॒ दास॒माय्यः॑ ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> वित्वक्षणः । संऋत् । चक्रं आसजः । असुन्वतः । विषुणः । सुन्वतः । वृधः ।  
> इंद्रः । विश्वस्य । दमिता । विभीषणः । यथावशं । नयति । दासं । आर्यः ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> सुमृतौ संग्रामे वित्वक्षणो विशेषेण तनूकर्ता शत्रूणां तदर्थं चक्रमासजो रथचक्रस्यासंजयितासुन्वतोऽयजमानस्य विषुणः पराङ्मुखः सुन्वतो
> यजमानस्य वृधो वर्धयितेंद्रो विश्वस्य दमिता शिक्षयिता विभीषणो भयजनक
> आर्यः स्वामी यथावशं यथेच्छं दासं दासकर्माणं जनं नयति स्ववशं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: Indra, who in battle especially thins out the enemies, and for that purpose sets in motion the wheel of his chariot, turns away from the sacrificer who does not press Soma, the non-sacrificer, and is the increaser of the sacrificer who presses. Indra, the tamer, that is, the instructor, of all, causing fear, the lord, leads at his will the servant, the person who does a servant's work, under his own control.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- समृतौ (*samṛtau*)—in battle
- विश्वक्षणः (*viśvakṣaṇaḥ*)—who makes the strength of enemies decay
- चक्रमासजः (*cakramāsajaḥ*)—and for that purpose makes his chariot wheel run swiftly
- असुन्वतः (*asunvataḥ*)—from the man who does not press

### Printed p. 270

- विषुणः (*viṣuṇaḥ*)—who turns away
- सुन्वतः (*sunvataḥ*)—to the sacrificer who presses Soma for the sacrifice
- वृधः (*vṛdhaḥ*)—the increaser
- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra
- विश्वस्य (*viśvasya*)—of the whole world
- दमिता (*damitā*)—the instructor
- विभीषणः (*vibhīṣaṇaḥ*)—the one who causes fear
- आर्यः (*āryaḥ*)—and lord
- यथावशं (*yathāvaśaṃ*)—as he pleases
- दासु (*dāsu*)—servants
- नयति (*nayati*)—he brings under his own control

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Indra, who makes the strength of enemies decay in battle, and for that purpose makes his chariot wheel run swiftly, who turns away from the man who does not press Soma and increases the sacrificer who presses Soma for the sacrifice, is the instructor of the whole world, the terrifier and the lord, and brings servants under his own control as he pleases.

##### English translation
Thinning (his enemies) in battle, and accelerating the wheels (of his car), he turns away from him who offers no libation, and augments (the prosperity of) the offerer : Indra, the subduer of all, the formidable, the lord, conducts the Dasa at his pleasure.

#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> समीं॑ प॒णेर॑जति॒ भोज॑नं मु॒षे वि दा॒शुषे॑ भजति सू॒नरं॒  ॑
> वसु ।  
> दु॒र्गे च॒न ध्रि॑यते॒ विश्व॒ आ पु॒रु जनो॒ यो अ॑स्य॒ तवि॑षी॒मचु॑क्रुधत् ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सं । ईं । पणेः । अजति । भोजनं । मुषे । वि । दाशुषे । भजति । सूनरं । वसु ।  
> दुःगे । चन । ध्रियते । विश्वः । आ । पुरु । जनः । यः । अस्य । तविषीं ।  
> अचुक्रुधत् ॥ ७ ॥

### Printed p. 271

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ईमयमिंद्रः पणेर्वणिज इव लुब्धकस्यादातुर्भोजनं धनमन्नं वा
> मुषे चोरयितुं समजति । सम्यक् गच्छति । गत्वा चादाय दाशुषे हविर्दात्रे
> यजमानाय वि भजति सूनरु शोभनमनुष्यं वसु धनं । अयष्टुर्धनमाकृष्य
> यष्टे प्रयच्छतीत्यर्थः । न केवलं वणिग्धनप्रदानमात्रं किंतु पुरु पुरुणि दुर्गॆ
> चन दुःखेन गंतव्य आपद्यपि विश्वः सर्वोऽपि दातृजन आ द्रियते । आस्थाप्यते ।
> यो जनोऽस्येंद्रस्य तनिषीं बलमचुक्रुदत् विहिताकरणादिना तस्य पणेरजतीति
> पूर्वशेषः । यद्वा । योऽस्य तनिषीमचुक्रुदत् स सर्वोऽपि जनो दुर्गॆ
> चनाध्रियत इंद्रेण ॥ चनेत्येकपदमध्येतृसंप्रदायप्राप्तं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: This Indra goes, like a trader to a miser who gives nothing, to steal his wealth or food; he goes fully, and having taken it he distributes it to the sacrificer who gives oblations: fine wealth, rich in men. Having drawn away the wealth of the non-sacrificer, he gives it to the sacrificer; that is the meaning. Not only is it a giving of a trader's wealth: even in a danger hard to get through, every giving person is supported. Of the man who has angered the might of Indra by not doing what is prescribed, Indra goes to steal the wealth; this is the remainder to be supplied from before. Or else: every man who has angered his might is not supported by Indra even in a hard place. The word *cana* is a single word, received through the tradition of the reciters.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- ईं (*īṃ*)—this Indra
- पणेः (*paṇeḥ*)—of the miser who gives nothing
- भोजनं (*bhojanaṃ*)—the wealth of food
- मुषे (*muṣe*)—to steal
- सं अजति (*saṃ ajati*)—he strives
- दाशुषे (*dāśuṣe*)—to the sacrificer who offers oblations
- सूनरं (*sūnaraṃ*)—beneficial to men
- वसु (*vasu*)—wealth
- वि भजति (*vi bhajati*)—he provides
- यः जनः (*yaḥ janaḥ*)—whatever man
- अस्य (*asya*)—of Indra
- तविषीं (*taviṣīṃ*)—the might
- अचुक्रुधत् (*acukrudhat*)—provokes by disrespect
- विश्वः (*viśvaḥ*)—all such people
- पुरु (*puru*)—very great
- दुर्गे चन (*durge cana*)—in distress
- आ ध्रियते (*ā dhriyate*)—are placed

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Indra strives to steal the food-wealth of a miser who gives nothing. He provides wealth beneficial to men to the sacrificer who offers oblations. All people who provoke the might of Indra by disrespect have to share in very great distress.

##### English translation
He proceeds to plunder the wealth of the ( avaricious ), and bestows the riches that are prized by man upon the donor (of the libation) : every man is involved in great difficulty who provokes the might of Indra to wrath.

#### Ṛk 8

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> सं यज्जना॑ सु॒धना॑ वि॒श्वश॑र्धसा॒ववे॒दिंद्रो॑ म॒घवा॒ गोषु॑  ॒॑
> शु॒भ्रिषु॑ ।

### Printed p. 272

> युजं ह्यगन्यमकृत प्रवेपन्युदीं गव्यं सृजते सत्वभिर्धुनिः ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सं । यत् । जनौ । सुधन् । विश्वशर्धसौ । अनेत् । इंद्रः । मघवा । गोषु ।  
> शुभ्रिषु ।  
> युजं । हि । अन्यं । अकृत । प्रवेपनी । उत् । ईं । गव्यं । सृजते । सत्वभिः ।  
> धुनिः ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> समवेत्संजानाति यद्यदा जनौ परस्परं प्रतिद्वंद्विनौ सुधनौ शोभनधनौ
> विश्वशर्धसौ व्याप्तबलौ बहूत्साहौ वा मघवा धनवानिंद्रो गोषु शुभ्रिषु
> शोभनासु गोषु निमित्तभूतासु अन्यं यष्टारं युजं सहायमकृत कृतवान्
> प्रवेपनी शत्रूणां प्रवेपनवान् । ईमस्मै गव्यं गोसमूहमुत्सृजते सत्वभिर्मरुद्धिः सह धुनिर्मेघानां कंपयिता । इंद्रो यजमानमयजमानं वा
> यं जानाति गोनिमित्तं ज्ञात्वा यज्जने गोसमूहं प्रयच्छतीत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: Indra discerns when two persons, rivals of each other, rich, of pervading strength or of great zeal, are in contest over shining cows; Maghavan, the wealthy Indra, makes the other, the sacrificer, his ally on account of the cows. *Pravepanī* is the one who makes his enemies tremble. For him, together with the Maruts, he lets loose a herd of cows, he, the shaker of the clouds. Whomever Indra recognises as sacrificer or non-sacrificer, having known him in the matter of the cows, he gives the herd of cows to the sacrificing man; that is the meaning.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- मघवा (*maghavā*)—wealthy
- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra
- यत् (*yat*)—when
- शुभ्रिषु (*śubhriṣu*)—for the sake of the shining
- गोषु (*goṣu*)—cows (who fight)
- सुधना (*sudhanā*)—those who possess wealth
- विश्वशर्धसौ (*viśvaśardhasau*)—and are of exceedingly great strength
- जनौ (*janau*)—among rivals
- सं आवेत् (*saṃ āvet*)—discerns and knows (who is the sacrificer and who is not), then
- प्रवेपनी (*pravepanī*)—causing fear in the enemy
- अन्यं (*anyaṃ*)—to the sacrificer
- युजं (*yujaṃ*)—help
- अकृत (*akṛta*)—he gives
- सत्वभिः (*satvabhiḥ*)—together with the Maruts
- धुनि: (*dhuni:*)—Indra, who makes the clouds move
- ईं (*īṃ*)—to such a sacrificer
- गव्यं (*gavyaṃ*)—a herd of cows
- उत्सृजते (*utsṛjate*)—he creates

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The wealthy Indra fights for the sake of shining cows. As soon as he discerns, among rival persons who are rich and of exceedingly great strength, who is the sacrificer and who is not, he causes fear in the enemy and helps the sacrificer. Indra, who makes the clouds move together with the Maruts, creates a herd of cows for such a sacrificer.

### Printed p. 273

##### English translation
When Indra, the possessor of opulence, discriminates between two men, both wealthy, and exerting themselves (against each other) for the sake of valuable cattle, he takes one of them as his associate, causing (his adversaries) to tremble, and the agitator (of the clouds), together with the Maruts, bestows upon him herds of cattle.

#### Ṛk 9

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स॒ह॒स्र॒सामान्यि॑वेशं गृणीषे॒ शत्रि॑मग्न उप॒मां के॒तुम॒र्यः ।  
> तस्मा॒ आपः॑ सं॒यतः॑ पीपयंत॒ तस्मि॑न् क्ष॒त्रमनु॑वत्त्वे॒षम॑स्तु ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सहस्रसां । आग्निऽवेशं । गृणीषे । शत्रिं । अग्ने । उपमां । केतुं । अर्यः ।  
> तस्मै । आपः । संयतः । पीपयंत । तस्मिन् । क्षत्रं । अमवत् । त्वेषं । अस्तु ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अग्निवेशिसुतं शत्रिमसंख्यातधनप्रदं । आशास्त्र ऋषिरंत्यर्चा प्रजापतिसुतः
> सुधीः ॥ अर्योऽहं सहस्रसामपरिमितधनप्रदमाग्नि वेशिमग्निवेशिसुतं शत्रिमेतन्नामकं राजर्षि० गृणीषे । गृणे । सौमि । हे अग्ने अंगनादिगुणविशिष्टेंद्र ।
> कीदृशं तं । उपमामुपमानभूतं केतुं प्रज्ञापकं । प्रख्यातमित्यर्थः । तस्मा
> आपः संयतः सम्यक् गच्छंत्यः पीपयंत । प्यायंतां । तस्मिन्राज्ञि क्षत्रं धनममवद्बलसहितं त्वेषं दीप्तमस्त्विति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa begins with a verse: Śatri, the son of Agniveśa, is the giver of countless wealth; the seer of the last ṛk is Āśāstra, the wise son of Prajāpati. He then explains: I, Arya, praise (गृणीषे (*gṛṇīṣe*) is used for "I praise") the royal sage named Śatri, the son of Agniveśa, the giver of a thousand, that is, of immeasurable wealth. "O Agni" is addressed to Indra, who has the qualities of Agni and the others.**NOTE** Of what kind is that king? He is a model (उपमां (*upamāṃ*)), a *ketu*, that is, one who makes known; the meaning is "famous". For him may the waters, flowing well together, swell, that is, increase; and in that king may *kṣatra*, that is wealth, accompanied by strength, and brilliant, be.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अग्ने (*agne*)—O Agni
- आर्यः (*āryaḥ*)—I, the venerable one
- उपमां (*upamāṃ*)—a model person (for the sacrificers)
- केतुं (*ketuṃ*)—famous
- सहस्रसां (*sahasrasāṃ*)—the giver of immeasurable wealth
- अग्निवेशिं (*agniveśiṃ*)—the son of Agniveśa
- शत्रिं (*śatriṃ*)—the royal sage named Śatri
- गृणीषे (*gṛṇīṣe*)—I praise
- तस्मै (*tasmai*)—for that king
- आपः (*āpaḥ*)—the waters
- संयतः (*saṃyataḥ*)—gathering together
- पीपयंत (*pīpayaṃta*)—may they satisfy
- तस्मिन् (*tasmin*)—in that king
- क्षत्रं (*kṣatraṃ*)—wealth
- अनवत् (*anavat*)—accompanied by strength
- त्वेषं (*tveṣaṃ*)—and brilliant
- अस्तु (*astu*)—may it gather

### Printed p. 274

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Agni, I, the venerable one, praise the royal sage named Śatri, the son of Agniveśa, a model person for all sacrificers, famous, and the giver of immeasurable wealth. For that king may all the waters, gathering together, give satisfaction. In that king may wealth, accompanied by strength and brilliant, gather.

##### English translation
I, Arya, Agni, praise Satri, the son of Agnivesha, the bestower of thousands, a type for comparison : may the collected waters yield him abundance ; may wealth, and strength, and glory be upon him

## Sūkta 35 (Maṇḍala 5)

The thirty-fifth sūkta.

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> यस्ते साधिष्ठ इत्यष्टर्चं तृतीयं सूक्तं । अंगिरोगोत्रः प्रभूवसुर्नाम
> ऋषिः । अंत्या पंक्तिः । पंक्त्यंतपरिभाषया शिष्टा अनुष्टुभः । इंद्रो देवता ।
> यस्तेऽष्टौ प्रभूवसुरांगिरसः पंक्त्यंतमित्यनुक्रांतं ॥ सूक्तविनियोगो
> लैंगिकः ॥ आभिप्लविकेषूक्ष्येषु तृतीयसवन आद्यस्तृचोऽच्छावाकस्य वैकल्पिकोऽनुरूपः । सूत्रितं च । यस्ते साधिष्ठोऽवसे पुरां भिंदुर्युवा कविः । आ: ७-८ ।
> इति ॥ द्वितीयतृतीययोः स्वरसान्नोश्चाद्यौ तृचावनुरूपौ । यस्ते साधिष्ठोऽवस इति षळनुष्टुभः । आ. ८-५ । इति हि सूत्रितं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: The third sūkta, यस्ते साधिष्ठ (*yaste sādhiṣṭha*), has eight ṛks. The seer is the Aṅgirasa Prabhūvasu. The last ṛk is in Paṅkti; the remaining ones are Anuṣṭubh by the convention on a Paṅkti ending. The deity is Indra. The Anukramaṇikā says: यस्तेऽष्टौ प्रभूवसुरांगिरसः पंक्त्यंतमित्यनुक्रांतं (*yaste 'ṣṭau prabhūvasurāṃgirasaḥ paṃktyaṃtamityanukrāṃtaṃ*). The use of the sūkta in ritual is laiṅgika. In the Ābhiplavika Ukthya sacrifices, at the third pressing, its first ṛk-triad is the optional Anurūpa of the Acchāvāka priest. It is stated in the sūtra: यस्ते साधिष्ठोऽवसे पुरां भिंदुर्युवा कविः (*yaste sādhiṣṭho 'vase purāṃ bhiṃduryuvā kaviḥ*) (Ā. Sū. 7-8). Further, in the second and third Svarasāman, the first two triads are the Anurūpa triads: यस्ते साधिष्ठोऽवस इति षळनुष्टुभः (*yaste sādhiṣṭho 'vasa iti ṣaḷanuṣṭubhaḥ*) (Ā. Sū. 8-5), as is stated in the sūtra.

Translation of the Anuvāda: This sūkta, Yaste sādhiṣṭhaḥ, is the third sūkta in the third anuvāka. There are eight ṛks in it. The seer of this sūkta is Prabhūvasu, born in the family of the seer Aṅgiras; Indra is the deity; the last ṛk of this sūkta is in the Paṅkti metre and the remaining ṛks are in the Anuṣṭubh metre. In the Anukramaṇikā it is said: "Yaste'ṣṭau prabhūvasurāṅgirasaḥ paṅkty-antam". The use in ritual is laiṅgika. In the Ukthya sacrifices connected with the Abhiplava, at the time of the third pressing, the first triad (three ṛks) of this sūkta is to be recited by the priest called Acchāvāka as an optional Anurūpa triad, as is explained by the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra, "Yaste sādhiṣṭho'vase purāṃ bhinduryuvā kaviḥ" (Ā. 7-8). When the second and third Svarasāman mantras are recited, the first and second triads of this sūkta (ṛks 1-3 and 4-6) are to be recited as Anurūpa triads, as is explained by the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra, "Yaste sādhiṣṭho'vasa iti ṣaḷanuṣṭubhaḥ" (Ā. 8-5).

### Printed p. 275

Sūkta 35.

Maṇḍala 5, Anuvāka 3, Sūkta 35.

Aṣṭaka 4, Adhyāya 2, Varga 5, 6.

Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 8.

Seer: Prabhūvasu Āṅgirasa.

Deity: Indra.

Metre: 1-7, Anuṣṭubh; 8, Paṅkti.

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यस्ते॒ साधि॒ष्ठोऽव॑स॒ इंद्र॒ क्रतु॒ष्टमा भ॑र ।  
> अ॒स्मभ्यं॑ चर्षणी॒सहं॒ सस्निं॒ वाजे॑षु दु॒ष्टरं॑ ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यः । ते । साधिष्ठः । अवसे । इंद्र । क्रुतुः । तं । आ । भर ।  
> अस्मभ्यं । चर्षणीसहं । सस्निं । वाजेषु । दुस्तरं ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र ते तव यः साधिष्ठोऽतिशयेन साधकः क्रतुरुक्तलक्षणं कर्म प्रज्ञा
> नास्ति तं क्रतुं नोऽवसेऽस्मद्रक्षणायास्मभ्यमा भर । आहर । कीदृशं तं । चर्षणीसहं सर्वेषां मनुष्याणामभिभवितारं सस्निं संस्कृतं शुद्धं वाजेषु संग्रामेषु
> दुष्टरमन्वैरनभिभाव्यं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: O Indra, whatever act of yours, called *kratu*, is most accomplishing, that is, accomplishes to the highest degree, or the wisdom (*prajñā*)**NOTE**, that *kratu* bring to us for our protection. Of what kind is it? It overpowers all men (चर्षणीसहं (*carṣaṇīsahaṃ*)), it is *sasni*, that is refined and pure, and in battles it is hard to cross, that is, it cannot be overcome by others.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- ते (*te*)—your
- यः (*yaḥ*)—which
- साधिष्ठः (*sādhiṣṭhaḥ*)—most effective in accomplishing
- क्रतुः (*kratuḥ*)—which is the act
- चर्षणीसहं (*carṣaṇīsahaṃ*)—which confronts enemy people
- सस्निं (*sasniṃ*)—which is pure
- वाजेषु (*vājeṣu*)—in battles
- दुष्टरं (*duṣṭaraṃ*)—which cannot be withstood by enemies
- तं (*taṃ*)—that well-known act
- अस्मभ्यं अवसे (*asmabhyaṃ avase*)—for our protection
- आ भर (*ā bhara*)—obtain and perfect it (make it manifest)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, whichever act of yours is most effective in accomplishing, bring for our protection that act which confronts enemy people, is pure, cannot be withstood by enemies in battles, and is well known: obtain it and perfect it.

### Printed p. 276

##### English translation
Perfect for our protection, Indra, that your act which is most effective ; whch is the subduer of men ; holy, and difficult to be encountered in battles.**NOTE**

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> दिं॑द्र ते॒ चत॑स्रो॒ यच्छू॑र॒ संति॑ ति॒स्रः ।  
> यद्वा॒ पंच॑ क्षिती॒नामव॒स्तत्सु न॒ आ भ॑र ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यत् । इंद्र । ते । चतस्रः । यत् । शूर । संति । तिस्रः ।  
> यत् । वा । पंच । क्षितीनां । अवः । तत् । सु । नः । आ । भर ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र ते तव यत् या ऊतयश्चतस्रश्चतुर्सु वर्णेषु संश्रिताः संति
> याश्च हे शूर तिस्रस्त्रिषु लोकेषु वर्तमानाः । यद्वा । नाशब्दश्चार्थे । यद्याश
> पंच क्षितीनां पंचजनसंबंधिन्य ऊतयः संति तत्सर्वमवो रक्षणं सु सुषु
> नोऽस्मभ्यमा भर । आहरेति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: O Indra, those protections of yours that are four, abiding in the four castes, and, O hero, those that are three, existing in the three worlds; or else, the word *nā* is in the sense of "and",**NOTE** and whichever protections there are that belong to the five peoples, all that protection (अवः (*avaḥ*)) bring well (*su*, "excellently") to us.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- ते (*te*)—your
- यत् (*yat*)—which protections
- चतस्रः (*catasraḥ*)—together with the people of the four castes
- संति (*saṃti*)—are
- शूर (*śūra*)—O valiant one
- यत् (*yat*)—which protections
- तिस्रः (*tisraḥ*)—can pervade the three worlds
- यद्वा (*yadvā*)—and moreover
- पंच क्षितीनां (*paṃca kṣitīnāṃ*)—are protective of the five kinds of people, gods, Gandharvas and the rest
- तत् अवः (*tat avaḥ*)—all those acts of protection
- सु (*su*)—exceedingly
- नः (*naḥ*)—for our welfare
- आ भर (*ā bhara*)—obtain

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O valiant Indra, those protections of yours that are together with the people of the four castes, those that can pervade the three worlds, and moreover those that are protective of the five kinds of people, gods, Gandharvas and the rest, all those acts of protection obtain exceedingly for our welfare.

### Printed p. 277

##### English translation
Whatever protections, Indra, may be yours, whether four, or hero, three or those accorded to the five (classes) of men, bestow them freely upon us.

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आ तेवो॒ वरे॑ण्यं॒ वृषं॑तमस्य हूमहे ।  
> वृष॑जूति॒र्हि ज॑ज्जि॒ष आ॒भूभि॑रिंद्र तु॒र्वणिः॑ ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आ । ते । अवः । वरेण्यं । वृषन्सतमस्य । हूमहे ।  
> वृषजूतिः । हि । जज्ञिषे । आभूभिः । इंद्र । तुर्वणिः ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र वृषंतमश्य फलानां वर्षकतमस्य ते तवावो रक्षणं गमनं
> ना वरेण्यं वरणीयमा हूमहे । आह्वयामः । हे इंद्र वृषजूतिर्वर्षणगमनस्तुर्वणिस्तूर्णवनिर्हिंसको ना त्वमाभूमिः सर्वतो भवद्भिव्याप्तैर्मरुद्धिः
> सहावो हि जज्जिषे । उत्पादयसि खलु । तेऽव आह्वयाम इति । यद्वा । वृषगमनः
> सन्यस्माद्यज्ञमागत्य फलं जज्जिषे तस्मात्तेऽवो हूमह इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: O Indra, of you, the one who showers fruits most abundantly, we call upon the desirable, that is, the choice-worthy, protection (*avas*, protection or going).**NOTE** O Indra, you whose going is that of rain (*vṛṣajūti*), swift-conquering and destroying (*turvaṇi*), you indeed produce that protection with the Maruts who pervade on all sides; therefore we call upon your protection. Or else: because you, whose going is like rain, come to the sacrifice and are born for its fruit, therefore we call upon your protection.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- वृषजूतिः (*vṛṣajūtiḥ*)—the distributor of rain
- तुर्वणिः (*turvaṇiḥ*)—and the one who quickly destroys enemies, you
- अभूभिः (*abhūbhiḥ*)—with the all-pervading Maruts
- जज्जिषे हि (*jajjiṣe hi*)—such protections as you produce
- वृषंतमस्य (*vṛṣaṃtamasya*)—of you who rain down fruit
- ते (*te*)—your
- वरेण्यं (*vareṇyaṃ*)—highly esteemed
- अवः (*avaḥ*)—protection
- आ हूमहे (*ā hūmahe*)—we pray for

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, you who distribute rain and quickly destroy enemies, we pray for that highly esteemed protection of you, who rain down fruit, such protections as you produce together with the all-pervading Maruts.

### Printed p. 278

##### English translation
We invoke the desirable protection of you, the most liberal showerer (of benefits); that (protection) which you, the distributor of rain, the quick destroyer (of foes), grant (associated) with the present (Maruts).

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> वृषा॒ ह्यसि॒ राध॑से जज्ञि॒षे वृष्टि॑ ते॒ शवः॑ ।  
> स्वक्ष॑त्रं ते धृ॒षन्मनः॑ सत्रा॒हमिं॑द्र॒ पौंस्यं॑ ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> वृषा । हि । असि । राधसे । जज्जिषे । वृष्टि । ते । शवः ।  
> स्वक्षत्रं । ते । धृषत् । मनः । सत्राहं । इंद्र । पौंस्यं ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र राधसे यजमानानां समृद्धये वृषा ह्यसि । फलस्य वर्षकोऽसि
> खलु । यद्वा । वृषा खल्वसि । राधसे धनार्थं जज्जिषे । उत्पद्यसे । ते शवो बलं
> वृष्णि वर्षितृ । ते मनः स्वक्षत्रं स्वायत्तबलं धृषत् धर्षकं विरोधिनां । हे इंद्र
> ते पौंस्यं पुंस्त्वं सत्राहं संघहंतृ ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: O Indra, for the prosperity of the sacrificers you are a showerer; you are indeed the one who rains down fruit. Or else: you are a showerer, born for wealth. Your strength (*śavas*) is rain-giving. Your mind is *svakṣatra*, with strength dependent on itself, and *dhṛṣat*, overbearing towards adversaries. O Indra, your manhood is *satrāha*, the destroyer of hosts together.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- वृषा (*vṛṣā*)—as the one who showers desires
- असि हि (*asi hi*)—you are
- राधसे (*rādhase*)—for the prosperity in wealth (of the sacrificer)
- जज्जिषे (*jajjiṣe*)—you are born
- ते (*te*)—your
- शवः (*śavaḥ*)—strength
- वृष्टि (*vṛṣṭi*)—pours down the desired objects
- ते (*te*)—your
- स्वक्षत्रं (*svakṣatraṃ*)—of self-possessed strength**NOTE**
- मनः (*manaḥ*)—the mind
- धृषत् (*dhṛṣat*)—is overbearing towards enemies
- पौंस्यं (*pauṃsyaṃ*)—your manly power
- सत्राहं (*satrāhaṃ*)—is such as to destroy (enemies) in a mass

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, you are a showerer of desires. You are born for the prosperity of the sacrificer in wealth. Your strength pours down the desired objects. Your mind, which has self-acquired strength, is overbearing towards enemies. Your manly power is such as to destroy enemies in a mass.

### Printed p. 279

##### English translation
You are the showerer (of benefits) ; you are born to (bestow) riches ; your strength rains (blessings) ; your self-invigorated mind is the restrainer (of adversaries) ; your manhood, Indra, is the destroyer of multitudes.

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> त्वं तमिं॑द्र॒ मर्त्य॑ममित्र॒यंत॑मद्रिनः ।  
> स॒र्व॒र॒था श॑तक्रतो॒ नि या॑हि शवसस्पते ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> त्वं । तं । इंद्र । मर्त्यं । अमित्रयंतं । अद्रिनः ।  
> सर्वऽरथा । शतक्रतो इति शतक्रतो । नि । याहि । शवसः । पते ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र त्वं तममित्रयंतं शत्रुत्वमाचरंतं शत्रुं वेच्छंतं मर्त्यं हे
> अद्रिवो वज्रवन्निंद्र सर्वरथा सर्वत्र व्याप्तेन रथेन ॥ सुपां सुलुगित्याकारः ॥ हे
> शतक्रतो हे शवसस्पते बलस्य पालकेंद्र नि याहि । नितरां गच्छाभिभवितुं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: O Indra, against that mortal who behaves as an enemy, or who desires an enemy; O wielder of the thunderbolt (*adrivaḥ*); with a chariot that pervades everywhere (*sarvarathā*); O Śatakratu; O lord of strength (*śavasaspate*), protector of strength, Indra: go against him (*ni yāhi*), that is, go down upon him in order to overcome him.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अद्रिवः (*adrivaḥ*)—O bearer of the thunderbolt
- शतक्रतो (*śatakrato*)—O doer of many acts
- शवसस्पते (*śavasaspate*)—O protector of strength
- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- सर्वरथा (*sarvarathā*)—with a chariot that pervades everywhere
- त्वं (*tvaṃ*)—you
- अमित्रयंतु (*amitrayaṃtu*)—who behaves like an enemy
- तं मर्त्यं (*taṃ martyaṃ*)—to confront that man
- नि याहि (*ni yāhi*)—set out and oppose him

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, bearer of the thunderbolt, doer of many acts, protector of strength, set out with your chariot that pervades everywhere, to confront men who behave like enemies.

##### English translation
Indra, wielder of the thunderbolt, rider in an all-pervading car, object of many rites, and lord of strength, proceed against the mortal who entertains hostility towards you.

### Printed p. 280

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> त्वामिद्वृ॑त्रहंतम॒ जना॑सो वृ॒क्तब॑र्हिषः ।  
> उ॒ग्रं पू॒र्वीषु॑ पू॒र्व्यं हवं॑ते॒ वाज॑सातये ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> त्वां । इत् । वृत्रहन्ऽतम । जनासः । वृक्तबर्हिषः ।  
> उग्रं । पूर्विषु । पूर्व्यं । हवंते । वाज्यसातये ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे वृत्रहंतमातिशयेन वृत्राणां हंतरिंद्र त्वामित्वामेव जनासो जना
> वृक्तबर्हिष आच्छादितदर्भाः । प्रवृत्तयज्ञा इत्यर्थः । उग्रमुद्गूर्णबलं पूर्वीषु
> बह्वीषु प्रजासु मध्यॆ पूर्व्यं पुरातनं संतं वाजसातये संग्रामाय हवंते ।
> आह्वयंते ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: O Indra, greatest slayer of Vṛtras, you alone do the people whose sacred grass is spread (वृक्तबर्हिषः (*vṛktabarhiṣaḥ*), that is, who have covered the darbha grass; the meaning is that they have set the sacrifice going) call to the battle (वाजसातये (*vājasātaye*), that is, for the fight). You are fierce, of uplifted strength, and ancient among the many peoples.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वृत्रहंतनु (*vṛtrahaṃtanu*)—O Indra, destroyer of Vṛtra
- पूर्वीषु (*pūrvīṣu*)—among many men
- उग्रं (*ugraṃ*)—who are of exceeding strength
- पूर्व्यं (*pūrvyaṃ*)—and ancient
- त्वामित् (*tvāmit*)—you alone
- वृक्तबर्हिषः (*vṛktabarhiṣaḥ*)—who prepare seats of darbha grass to provide you a seat
- जनासः (*janāsaḥ*)—people
- वाजसातये (*vājasātaye*)—for help in battle
- हवंते (*havaṃte*)—call

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, destroyer of Vṛtra, you who are of exceeding strength and ancient among many men, you alone do those men who prepare seats of darbha grass for you and make ready the sacrifice call, desiring help in battle.

##### English translation
Slayer of Vritra, men with clipped sacred grass invoke you, who are fierce and foremost among many, for aid in battle.

### Printed p. 281

#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अ॒स्माक॑मिंद्र दु॒ष्टरं॑ पुरो॒यावा॑नमा॒जिषु॑ ।  
> स॒यावा॑नं॒ धने॑धने वाज॒यंत॑मवा॒ रथं॑ ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अस्माकं । इंद्र । दुस्तरं । पुरःऽयावानं । आजिषु ।  
> स्ययावानं । धनेऽधने। वाजयंतं । अव । रथं ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र अस्माकं रथं रंहणस्वभावं ना पुत्रमव । रक्ष । कीदृशं रथं ।
> दुष्टरं दुःखेन तरणीयमाजिसु संग्रामेषु पुरोयावानं पुरतो मिश्रयितारं सयावानमनुचरैः सह गंतारं धने धने सर्वेषु धनेषु वाजयंतं
> संग्रामं धनं वेच्छंतं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: O Indra, protect our chariot, whose nature is to rush on.**NOTE** Of what kind is the chariot? It is hard to cross, that is, to be got over only with difficulty, in battles; it goes in front (पुरोयावानं (*puroyāvānaṃ*), that is, it mingles in front); it goes together with followers; in every kind of wealth it seeks battle and wealth (वाजयंतं (*vājayaṃtaṃ*)).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- दुष्टरं (*duṣṭaraṃ*)—which others can hardly stop
- आजिषु (*ājiṣu*)—in battles
- पुरोयावानं (*puroyāvānaṃ*)—which moves about in the forefront
- सयावानं (*sayāvānaṃ*)—which moves along with followers
- धनेधने (*dhanedhane*)—again and again, to gather wealth
- वाजयंतं (*vājayaṃtaṃ*)—and which desires battle
- अस्माकं (*asmākaṃ*)—our
- रथं (*rathaṃ*)—chariot
- अव (*ava*)—protect

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, protect our chariot, which others can hardly stop, which moves boldly in the forefront in battles, moves along with followers, and again and again desires battle for the sake of gaining wealth.

##### English translation
Defend, Indra, our chariot, difficult (to be stopped), mingling foremost in combats, followed by attendants. and eagerly striving for repeated spoil.

### Printed p. 282

#### Ṛk 8

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अ॒स्माक॑मिं॒द्रेहि॑ नो॒ रथ॑मवा॒ पुरं॑ध्या ।  
> व॒यं श॑विष्ठ॒ वार्यं॑ दि॒वि श्रवो॑ दधीमहि दि॒वि स्तोमं॑  
> मनामहे ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अस्माकं । इंद्र । आ । इहि । नः । रथं । अव । पुरंs ध्या ।  
> वयं । शविष्ठ । वार्यं । दिवि । श्रवः । दधीमहि । दिवि । स्तोमं । मनामहे ॥८॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र अस्माकमस्मदीय एव सन् एहि । आगच्छ । अगत्य च पुरंध्या शोभनबुद्धा नोऽस्माकु रथमव । रक्ष । वयं च हे शविष्ठ अतिशयेन बलवन् त्वदनुग्रहाद्वार्यं वरणेयं श्रवोऽन्नं कीर्तिं वा दिवि द्योतमाने त्वयि दधीमहि ।
> स्थापयामः । तथा दिवि त्वयि स्तोमं स्तोत्रं मनामहे । करवामह इत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: O Indra, come to us, being truly ours; and having come, protect our chariot with good understanding (पुरंध्या (*puraṃdhyā*)). And we, O Śaviṣṭha, most mighty one, by your favour place the desirable (वार्यं (*vāryaṃ*)) food or fame (श्रवः (*śravaḥ*)) in you who shine; likewise we make (मनामहे (*manāmahe*)) praise in you who shine; that is the meaning.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- अस्माकं (*asmākaṃ*)—to us
- एहि (*ehi*)—be pleased to come
- पुरंध्या (*puraṃdhyā*)—with a favouring understanding
- नः (*naḥ*)—our
- रथं (*rathaṃ*)—chariot
- अव (*ava*)—protect
- शविष्ठ (*śaviṣṭha*)—O Indra, most powerful
- वयं (*vayaṃ*)—we
- वार्यं (*vāryaṃ*)—desired
- श्रवः (*śravaḥ*)—food
- दिवि (*divi*)—in you who shine
- दधीमहि (*dadhīmahi*)—we place
- दिवि (*divi*)—addressed to you who are divine
- स्तोमं (*stomaṃ*)—praise
- मनामहे (*manāmahe*)—we recite and offer

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, be pleased to come to us. Protect our chariot with a favouring understanding. O most powerful Indra, in you who shine we place the most desired**NOTE** food. Addressing you, who are divine, we also recite and offer praise.

### Printed p. 283

##### English translation
Come, Indra, to us ; defend by your providence our car : we contemplate in you who, most mighty one, are divine, all desirable vigour : (to you), who are divine, we offer praise.

## Sūkta 36 (Maṇḍala 5)

The thirty-sixth sūkta.

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> स आ गमदिति षडृचं चतुर्थं सूक्तं ॥ अंगिरसः प्रभूवसुर्ऋषिः । तृतीया
> जगती शिष्टा अनुक्तत्वात् त्रिष्टुभः । इंद्रो देवता । अनुक्रांतं च । स आ गमत्
> षट् तृतीया जगतीति ॥ विनियोगो लैंगिकः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: The fourth sūkta, स आ गमदिति (*sa ā gamaditi*), has six ṛks. The seer is the Aṅgirasa Prabhūvasu. The third ṛk is in Jagatī; the remaining ones are Triṣṭubh, because no other metre is stated. The deity is Indra. The Anukramaṇikā also says: स आ गमत् षट् तृतीया जगतीति (*sa ā gamat ṣaṭ tṛtīyā jagatīti*). The use in ritual is laiṅgika.

Translation of the Anuvāda: This sūkta, Sa ā gamat, is the fourth sūkta in the third anuvāka. There are six ṛks in it. The seer of this sūkta is Prabhūvasu, born in the line of the seer Aṅgiras. Indra is the deity. The third ṛk of this sūkta is in the Jagatī metre and the remaining ṛks are in the Triṣṭubh metre. In the Anukramaṇikā it is said: "Sa ā gamat ṣaṭ tṛtīyā jagatī". The use in ritual is laiṅgika.

Sūkta 36.

Maṇḍala 5, Anuvāka 3, Sūkta 36.

Aṣṭaka 4, Adhyāya 2, Varga 7.

Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 6.

Seer: Prabhūvasu Āṅgirasa.

Deity: Indra.

Metre: 1, 2, 4-6, Triṣṭubh; 3, Jagatī.

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स आ ग॑म॒दिंद्रो॒ यो वसू॑नां॒ चिके॑त॒द्दातुं॒ दाम॑नो  ॒
> रयीणां ।  
> ध॒न्व॒च॒रो न वंस॑गस्तृषा॒णश्च॑कमा॒नः पि॑बतु दु॒ग्धमं॒शुं ॥१॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सः । आ । गमत् । इंद्रः । यः । वसूनां । चिकेतत् । दातुं । दामनः । रयीणां ।  
> धन्वचरः । न । वंसगः । तृषाणः । चकमानः । पिबतु । दुग्धं । अंशुं ॥ १ ॥

### Printed p. 284

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> स इंद्रोऽस्मद्यज्ञं प्रत्या गमत् । आगच्छतु । यो देवो वसूनां धनानां ।
> कर्मणि षष्ठी ॥ वसूनि धनानि चिकेतत् जानाति प्रदातुं । स कीदृशः । दामनो
> दाता दानमना वा रयिणां धनानां । गमने दृष्टांतः । धन्वचरो न धनुषा
> सह संचरन् धानुष्क इव वंसगो वननीयगमनस्तृषाणोऽत्यंतं तृषितश्चकमानः
> कामयमानश्च सन् दुग्धमभिषुतमंशुं सोमं पिबतु ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: May that Indra come to our sacrifice, the god who knows how to give wealth. What kind is he? He is the giver of riches, or one whose mind is set on giving. As an illustration of his coming: like a bowman (धन्वचरो (*dhanvacaro*), one who moves about with a bow), whose going is lovely (वंसगः (*vaṃsagaḥ*)), exceedingly thirsty and desirous, may he drink the pressed (दुग्धं (*dugdhaṃ*)) Soma, the *aṃśu*.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यः (*yaḥ*)—which Indra
- वसूनां (*vasūnāṃ*)—wealth
- दातुं (*dātuṃ*)—to distribute to the sacrificers
- चिकेतत् (*ciketat*)—knows (and)
- रयीणां (*rayīṇāṃ*)—of riches
- दामनः (*dāmanaḥ*)—is the giver
- सः इंद्रः (*saḥ iṃdraḥ*)—that Indra
- धन्वचरो न (*dhanvacaro na*)—like a warrior bearing a bow
- आ गमत् (*ā gamat*)—may he come to our sacrifice
- वंसगः (*vaṃsagaḥ*)—of attractive going
- तृषाणः (*tṛṣāṇaḥ*)—thirsty
- चकमानः (*cakamānaḥ*)—and desirous (of drinking Soma)
- दुग्धं (*dugdhaṃ*)—pressed
- अंशुं (*aṃśuṃ*)—Soma
- पिबतु (*pibatu*)—may he drink

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

May that Indra who knows that wealth is to be distributed to the sacrificers come to our sacrifice like a warrior bearing a bow. Of attractive going, thirsty and desirous of drinking Soma, may he drink the Soma juice.

##### English translation
May that Indra who is the donor of wealth, who knows ( how ) to distribute riches, come (to our sacrifice) marching boldly like a warrior ; and may he, being thirsty, and desirous (of the draught), drink of the effused Soma juice.

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अ ते॒ हनू॑ हरिवः शूर॒ शिप्रे॒ रुह॒त्सोमो॒ न पर्व॑तस्य पृ॒ष्ठे ।  
> अनु॑ त्वा राज॒न्नर्व॑तो॒ न हि॒न्वन् गी॒र्भिर्म॑देम पुरुहूत॒  ॑
> विश्वे ॥ २ ॥

### Printed p. 285

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आ । ते । हनू इति । हरिऽवः । शूर । शिप्रे इति । रुहत् । सोमः । न । पर्वतस्य ।  
> पृष्ठे ।  
> अनु । त्वा । राजन् । अर्वतः । न । हिन्वन् । गीभिः । मदेम । पुरुहूत ।  
> विश्वे ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र हे हरिवो हरिभ्यां तद्वन् शूर ते तव हनू हननसाधने । यद्यप्ययं शिप्रावचनस्तथापि पुनः शिप्राभिधानाद्गौणोऽवगंतव्यः । उक्तरूपे संहते
> वा शिप्रे आ रुहत् आरोहतु प्राप्नोतु सोमोऽस्मद्दत्तः । पर्वतस्य पृष्ठे न
> रुहत् । नारोहति । तत्र न तिष्ठतीत्यर्थः । यद्वा । नशब्दः पुरस्तात्पयुक्तोऽप्यत्रोपमार्थः । पर्वतस्य पृष्ठ उपरिप्रदेशे सोमो न । तत्र यथा विस्रंभेण
> प्रसरत्ति-तद्वत् । हे राजन् राजमानेंद्र त्वा त्वामनु त्वया सह ॥ तृतीयार्थ
> इत्यनोः कर्मप्रवचनीयत्वं ॥ विश्वे वयमर्वतो नाश्वानिव तृणादिभिर्गिर्भिः
> स्तुतिभिर्हिन्वन् हिन्वंतस्त्वां प्रीणयंतः संतो मदेम । हृष्येम ॥ हे पुरुहूत
> बहुप्रकारं बहुभिर्वाहूतेंद्र ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: O Indra, O possessor of bay horses, O hero, these two jaws of yours (हनू (*hanū*)) are the instruments of striking. Although this word has the sense of *śipra*, yet because *śipra* is named again, it is to be understood in a secondary sense.**NOTE** Into the two *śipras*, of the stated form or joined together, may the Soma given by us climb, that is, reach. It does not climb on the back of a mountain, that is, it does not stay there. Or else, the word *na*, although used earlier, has here the sense of comparison: as Soma moves about with confidence on the upper part, the back, of a mountain, so it does here. O shining king Indra, with you (त्वा (*tvā*) अनु (*anu*)) we all, pleasing you with our praises (*girbhiḥ*), as one delights horses (अर्वतो (*arvato*)) with grass and the like, would rejoice, that is, be glad. O Puruhūta, Indra, called in many ways or by many.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- हरिवः (*harivaḥ*)—O one yoked with horses
- शूर (*śūra*)—and O hero, Indra
- सोमः (*somaḥ*)—may Soma
- पर्वतस्य (*parvatasya*)—of a mountain
- पृष्ठे न (*pṛṣṭhe na*)—as one climbs the summit
- हनू (*hanū*)—which are the instrument of striking (enemies)
- ते शिप्रे (*te śipre*)—into your jaws
- आ रुहत् (*ā ruhat*)—climb (and join)
- राजन् (*rājan*)—O lord
- पुरुहूत (*puruhūta*)—and O Indra, called by many
- अर्वतो न (*arvato na*)—as one pleases horses with grass and the like
- गीर्भिः (*gīrbhiḥ*)—with our praises
- हिन्वन् (*hinvan*)—gladdening you
- त्वा अनु (*tvā anu*)—with you
- विश्वे मदेम (*viśve madema*)—we all shall rejoice

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, you who are yoked with horses and are a hero, may Soma climb up and fill your jaws, the instrument of striking enemies, as one climbs the summit of a mountain. O Indra, lord, called by many, gladdening you with our praises as one pleases horses with grass and the like, we all shall rejoice with you.

### Printed p. 286

##### English translation
Hero, lord of horses, may the the Soma ascend to your destructive jaw, as if to the summit of a mountain : may we all, royal Indra, who are invoked of many, give pleasure to you with our hymns, as to horses with fodder.

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> च॒क्रं न वृ॒त्तं पु॑रुहूत वेपते॒ मनो॑ भि॒या मे॒ अम॑ते॒रिद॑द्रवः ।  
> रथा॒दधि॑ त्वा जरि॒ता स॑दावृध कु॒विन्नु स्तो॑षन्मघवन्नुरू॒वसुः॑ ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> चक्रं । न । वृत्तं । पुरुहूत । वेपते । मनः । भिया । मे । अमतेः । इत् ।  
> अद्रिवः ।  
> रथात् । अधि । त्वा । जरिता । सदावृध । कुवित् । नु । स्तोषत् । मघवन् ।  
> पुरुर्वसुः ॥ २ ॥

**NOTE**

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे पुरुहूत बहुभिराहूत हे अद्रिवो वज्रवन्निंद्र मे मनोऽमतेरिद्दारिद्रादस्तोतुर्वा सकाशाद्भिया भीत्या वेपते । कंपते । वृत्तं चक्रं न भूमौ
> वर्तमानं चक्रमिव । यस्मादेवं तस्माद्रथादध्युपरि स्थितं त्वा त्वां हे सदावृध
> सर्वदा वर्धमान हे मघवन्निंद्र जरिता स्तोता पुरूवसुरहमृषिः कुविद्बहु नु
> क्षिप्रं स्तोषत् । स्तोष्यति । स्तूयात् ॥ अत्र कुविच्छब्दयोगेऽप्याख्यातस्य बहुलग्रहणान्निघातः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: O Puruhūta, called by many, O Adrivas, Indra armed with the thunderbolt, my mind trembles with fear, that is, shakes, from poverty (मनोऽमतेरिद्दारिद्रादस्तोतुर्वा (*mano 'materiddāridrādastoturvā*) is read here as "from poverty, or from one who does not praise"),**NOTE** like a wheel rolling on the ground.
