Because this is so, O ever-growing one (सदावृध (*sadāvṛdha*)), O Maghavan Indra, I, the praiser, the seer Puruvasu, will quickly and abundantly (कुविद्बहु नु क्षिप्रं (*kuvidbahu nu kṣipraṃ*) is his gloss of *kuvit* and *nu*) praise you, who stand above the chariot.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- पुरुहूत (*puruhūta*)—who are called by many
- अद्रिवः (*adrivaḥ*)—and who hold the thunderbolt
- मे (*me*)—my
- मनः (*manaḥ*)—mind
- अमशेः (*amaśeḥ*)—of poverty
- भिया (*bhiyā*)—through fear

### Printed p. 287

- वृत्तं (*vṛttaṃ*)—which is rolling on the ground
- चक्रं न (*cakraṃ na*)—like a wheel
- वेपते (*vepate*)—trembles
- सदावृध (*sadāvṛdha*)—who always grow
- मघवन् (*maghavan*)—and are wealthy, O Indra
- रथादधि (*rathādadhi*)—who stand on the chariot
- त्वां (*tvāṃ*)—you
- जरिता (*jaritā*)—the praiser
- पुरूवसुः (*purūvasuḥ*)—named Puruvasu, I
- कुवित् (*kuvit*)—in many ways
- नु (*nu*)—and quickly
- स्तोषत् (*stoṣat*)—praise

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, called by many and holding the thunderbolt, my mind trembles through fear of poverty like a wheel rolling on the ground. O Indra, who always grow and are wealthy, I, the praiser named Puruvasu, praise you, who stand on the chariot, in many ways and quickly.

##### English translation
Wielder of the thunderbolt, the invoked of many, my mind trembles through dread of poverty like a whirling wheel : Maghavan, ever prosperous, may your worshipper, Puruvasu, praise you promptly and abundantly, seated in your chariot.

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ए॒ष ग्रावे॑व जरि॒ता त॑ इं॒द्रेय॑र्ति॒ वाचं॑ बृ॒हदा॑शुषा॒णः ।  
> प्र स॒व्येन॑ मघव॒न्यंसि॑ रा॒यः प्र द॑क्षि॒णिद्ध॑रिवो॒ मा वि  ॑
> वेनः ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> एषः । गावाइव । जरिता । ते । इंद्र । इयर्ति । वाचं । बृहत् । आशुषाणः ।  
> प्र । सव्येन । मघवन् । यंसि । रायः । प्र । दक्षिणित् । हरिवः । मा । वि ।  
> वेनः ॥ ४ ॥

### Printed p. 288

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> एष जरिता गरिता स्तोता ग्रावेवाभिषवाश्मेव । स यथा रसं जनयति तद्वत्
> हे इंद्र ते तुभ्यं वाचं स्तुतिमियर्ति । प्रेरयतीत्यर्थः । बृहन्महत्प्रभूतं
> फलमाशुषाण आशु संभक्ता सन् हे मघवन् सव्येन हस्तेन रायो धनानि प्र
> यंसि । प्रयच्छसि । दक्षिणिद्दक्षिणेनापि प्र यंसीति शेषः । मा वि वेनः । विगतकामं
> मा कार्षिः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: This praiser, like the pressing stone (ग्रावेव (*grāveva*)), which produces the juice, sends forth (इयर्ति (*iyarti*), that is, urges on) praise to you, O Indra, quickly obtaining great, abundant fruit. O Maghavan, with the left hand you give riches (प्र यंसि (*pra yaṃsi*), that is, you give); the remainder is to be supplied thus: with the right hand also you give. Do not disappoint him (मा वि वेनः (*mā vi venaḥ*)): do not make him bereft of desire.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- एषः जरिता (*eṣaḥ jaritā*)—this praiser
- बृहत् (*bṛhat*)—abundant
- आशुषाणः (*āśuṣāṇaḥ*)—looking for fruit
- ग्रावेव (*grāveva*)—as the stone that presses Soma produces the juice
- ते (*te*)—which praises you
- वाचं (*vācaṃ*)—praise
- इयति (*iyati*)—produces
- मघवन् (*maghavan*)—O wealthy Indra
- सव्येन (*savyena*)—with the left hand
- रायः (*rāyaḥ*)—riches
- प्र यंसि (*pra yaṃsi*)—you give
- हरिवः (*harivaḥ*)—O Indra, yoked with horses
- दक्षिणित् (*dakṣiṇit*)—with the right hand also
- प्र (*pra*)—give (generously)
- मा वि वेनः (*mā vi venaḥ*)—do not hold back from giving

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, this praiser, looking for abundant fruit, brings forth praise of you, as the stone that presses Soma brings out the juice. O wealthy Indra, you give riches with the left hand; in the same way, O Indra yoked with horses, give with the right hand also. Give generously; do not hold back from giving.

##### English translation
This your adorer, Indra, like the stone (that express the Soma juice), urges praise to you, participating in the great (reward): you bestow, Maghavan, riches with your left hand, you bestow them, lord of horses, with the right : be not reluctant.

### Printed p. 289

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> वृषा॑ त्वा॒ वृष॑णं वर्धतु॒ द्यौर्वृषा॒ वृष॑भ्यां वहसे॒ हरि॑भ्यां।  
> स नो॒ वृषा॒ वृष॑रथः सुशिप्र॒ वृष॑क्रतो॒ वृषा॑ वज्रि॒न्भरे॑ धाः ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> वृषा । त्वा ॥ वृषणं । वर्धतु । द्यौः । वृषा । वृषभ्यां । वहसे । हरिभ्यां ।  
> सर् । नः । वृषा । वृषरथः । सुशिप्र । वृषक्रतो इति वृषक्रतो । वृषा । वज्रिन् ।  
> भरे । धाः ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र त्वा त्वां वृषा वर्षित्री द्यौर्द्योतयित्रि स्तुतिः ॥ द्योततेर्दीव्यतीर्वा स्तुत्यर्थस्येदं रूपं ॥ वृषणं कामानां वर्षकं त्वां वर्धतु ।
> वर्धयतु । वृषा त्वं वृषभ्यां सेक्तृभ्यां हरिभ्यां वहसे । यज्ञं प्रत्युह्यसे ।
> सुशिप्र शोभनहनो वृषक्रतो वर्षणकर्मन् हे वज्रिन् वज्रयुक्तेंद्र स त्वं
> वृषा वृषरथश्च सन् नोऽस्मान् भरे संग्रामे भरणवति यज्ञे वा धाः । धारय ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: O Indra, may praise, which rains and illumines (द्यौः (*dyauḥ*), a form of the root meaning "to shine" or "to play", used in the sense of praise), increase you, the showerer of desires. You, the showerer, are borne to the sacrifice by the two showering (that is, sprinkling) bay horses. O Suśipra, of fine jaws, O you of showering deeds, O wielder of the thunderbolt, Indra, being such a showerer with a showering chariot, uphold (धाः (*dhāḥ*), that is, support) us in battle, or in the sacrifice that bears us.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वृषा (*vṛṣā*)—this praise, which gives satisfaction (O Indra)
- द्यौः (*dyauḥ*)—and which shines
- वृषणं (*vṛṣaṇaṃ*)—who rain down desires
- त्वा (*tvā*)—you
- वर्धतु (*vardhatu*)—may it make grow
- वृषा (*vṛṣā*)—you who rain down desires
- वृषभ्यां (*vṛṣabhyāṃ*)—which have the power to sprinkle
- हरिभ्यां (*haribhyāṃ*)—by the horses
- वहसे (*vahase*)—are borne
- सुशिप्र (*suśipra*)—who have fine cheeks
- वृषक्रतो (*vṛṣakrato*)—who have deeds that grant desires
- वज्रिन् (*vajrin*)—O Indra, who hold the thunderbolt
- वृषा (*vṛṣā*)—who have the power to sprinkle
- वृषरथः (*vṛṣarathaḥ*)—and have a chariot that grants desires
- सः (*saḥ*)—you
- नः (*naḥ*)—us
- भरे (*bhare*)—in battle
- धाः (*dhāḥ*)—uphold (protect)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, may this praise, which gives satisfaction and shines, make you, the rainer of desires, grow. You, the rainer of desires, are borne by horses that have the power to sprinkle. O Indra, who have fine cheeks, deeds that grant desires, and who hold the thunderbolt, you who have the power to sprinkle and a chariot that grants desires, protect us in battle.

### Printed p. 290

##### English translation
May the effective eulogium melt you, the showerer (of benefits): you, the showerer, are borne (to the sacrifice) by vigorous steeds: sender of rain, you of the handsome chin, wielder of the thunderbolt, showerer whose car showers down (blessings), you defend us in battle.

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यो रोहि॑तौ वा॒जिन्॑ वा॒जिनी॑वान्त्रि॒भिः श॒तैः सच॑माना॒वदि॑ष्ट।  
> यूने॒ सम॑स्मै क्षि॒तयो॑ नमंतां श्रु॒तर॑थाय मरुतो  ॒
> दुवोया ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यः । रोहितौ । वाजिनौ । वाजिनीऽवान् । त्रिभिः । शतैः । सचमानौ । अदिष्ट ।  
> यूने । सं । अस्मै । क्षितयः । नमंतां । श्रुतरथाय । मरुतः । दुवःऽया ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> इयं शत्रुतरथस्य राजेण दानस्तुतिः । यो राजा वाजिनीवानन्नवान्
> श्रुतरथो रोहितौ रोहितवर्णौ वाजिनौ वेजनवंतावश्वा त्रिभिः शतैर्गवां
> धनानां ना उक्तसंख्याभिः सचमानौ संगच्छमानावदिष्ट ॥ वदातेर्दिशतीर्वा
> लुजेदं रूपं ॥ यूने सर्वत्र मिश्रयित्रे नित्यतरुणाय वास्मै श्रुतरथाय
> क्षितयः सर्वाः प्रजा दुवोया ॥ तृतीयैकवचनस्य याजादेशः ॥ परिचयया
> सं नमंतां । प्रणता भवंतु । सेवंतामित्यर्थः । हे मरुत इतींद्रसहायभूतानां मरुतां संबोधनं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: This is a praise of the gift of King Śrutaratha. The king who is rich in food, Śrutaratha, gave (अदिष्ट (*adiṣṭa*)) two red (रोहितौ (*rohitau*)) swift horses (वाजिनौ (*vājinau*)), joined with three hundreds, of the stated number, of cows, that is, of wealth.**NOTE** To him, the ever-youthful (यूने (*yūne*): one who mixes with all everywhere, or who is always young), to this Śrutaratha, may all peoples (क्षितयः (*kṣitayaḥ*)), in service (दुवोया (*duvoyā*), that is, in attendance), bow together, that is, be submissive and serve. "O Maruts" is an address to the Maruts, the helpers of Indra.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- मरुतः (*marutaḥ*)—O Maruts
- वाजिनीवान् (*vājinīvān*)—who is rich in food
- यः (*yaḥ*)—the king named Śrutaratha who
- रोहितौ (*rohitau*)—of golden colour
- वाजिनौ (*vājinau*)—swift
- त्रिभिः शतैः (*tribhiḥ śataiḥ*)—with three hundred cows
- सचमानौ (*sacamānau*)—joined: those two horses
- आदिष्ट (*ādiṣṭa*)—gave

### Printed p. 291

- यूने (*yūne*)—to the youthful
- अस्मै श्रुतंथाय (*asmai śrutaṃthāya*)—to this Śrutaratha
- क्षितयः (*kṣitayaḥ*)—all peoples
- दुवोया (*duvoyā*)—may they offer service
- सं नमंतां (*saṃ namaṃtāṃ*)—may they bow with loyalty to the king

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Maruts, to this youthful Śrutaratha, rich in food, who gave two golden-coloured, swift horses and three hundred cows, may all the peoples offer service and bow with loyalty to the king.

##### English translation
Maruts, may all men bow in obedience to that youthful Shrutaratha, the possessor of abundance, who has bestowed (upon us) two bay steeds accompanied by three hundred (cattle).

## Sūkta 37 (Maṇḍala 5)

The thirty-seventh sūkta.

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> सं भानुनेति पंचर्चं पंचमं सूक्तमत्रेरार्षं त्रैष्टुभमैंद्रं । सं
> भानुना पंचात्रिरित्यनुक्रमणिका ॥ विनियोगो लैंगिकः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: The fifth sūkta, सं भानुनेति (*saṃ bhānuneti*), has five ṛks; its seer is Atri, its metre is Triṣṭubh and its deity is Indra. The Anukramaṇikā says: सं भानुना पंचात्रिरित्यनुक्रमणिका (*saṃ bhānunā paṃcātrirityanukramaṇikā*). The use in ritual is laiṅgika.

Translation of the Anuvāda: This sūkta, Saṃ bhānunā, is the fifth sūkta in the third anuvāka. There are five ṛks in it. The seer of this sūkta is Atri; Indra is the deity; the metre is Triṣṭubh. In the Anukramaṇikā it is said: "Saṃ bhānunā pañcātriḥ". The use in ritual is laiṅgika.

Sūkta 37.

Maṇḍala 5, Anuvāka 3, Sūkta 37.

Aṣṭaka 4, Adhyāya 2, Varga 8.

Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 5.

Seer: Atri.

Deity: Indra.

Metre: Triṣṭubh.

### Printed p. 292

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> सं भा॒नुना॑ यतते॒ सूर्य॑स्या॒जुह्वा॑नो घृ॒तपृ॑ष्ठः॒ स्वंचाः॑।  
> तस्मा॒ अमृ॑ध्रा उ॒षसो॒ व्यु॑च्छा॒न्य इंद्रा॑य सु॒नवा॒मेत्याह॑ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सं । भानुना । यतते । सूर्यस्य । आजुह्वानः । धृतपृष्ठः । सुअंचाः ।  
> तस्मै । अमृध्राः । उषसः । वि । उच्छान् । यः । इंद्राय । सुनवाम । इति । अह॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> सूरस्य भानुना तेजसा सहाजुह्वानः सर्वत्र हूयमानः ॥ कर्मणि कर्तृप्रत्ययः । धृतपृष्ठः प्रदीप्तज्वालो धृतयुक्त पृष्ठप्रदेशो वा स्वंचाः स्वंचनोऽग्निः सं यतते । सम्यक् प्रयत्नं करोति। उदयकाले ह्यग्नयः प्रज्वाल्यंते ।
> तस्मात्कारणाद्यो यजमान इंद्राय सुनवामेत्याह ब्रूतेऽध्वर्युं प्रति तस्मै
> यजमानायामृध्रा अहिंसिता उषसो व्युच्छान् । व्युच्छंतु । उदयकालेऽग्निः
> प्रज्वालितस्तस्मात्सोमयागं कुर्म इति यो ब्रूते तस्यॆ सुदिनानि भवंतीत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: Together with the radiance (bhānu), that is, the brilliance, of the sun, Agni, who is invoked everywhere (the agent-suffix is used here in the passive sense), who has blazing flames or whose back is joined with ghee (*dhṛtapṛṣṭha*), and who is of good motion (*svañcāḥ*), makes a proper effort, for fires are kindled at the time of sunrise. For that reason, the sacrificer who says to the adhvaryu, "Let us press Soma for Indra" — for that sacrificer may the unharmed dawns shine forth. The meaning is that good days come to him who says, "At sunrise the fire is kindled; therefore we perform the Soma sacrifice."

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- धृतपृष्ठः (*dhṛtapṛṣṭhaḥ*)—having blazing flames
- स्वंचाः (*svaṃcāḥ*)—of beautiful motion
- आजुह्वानः (*ājuhvānaḥ*)—he who is invoked everywhere, Agni
- सूर्यस्य (*sūryasya*)—of the sun
- भानुना (*bhānunā*)—with the radiance
- संयतते (*saṃyatate*)—joins and becomes one (or: vies with the splendour of the sun and shines)
- यः (*yaḥ*)—which sacrificer
- इंद्राय (*iṃdrāya*)—for Indra
- सुनवाम (*sunavāma*)—let us press Soma (for offering to Indra)
- इति (*iti*)—thus
- आह (*āha*)—says
- तस्मै (*tasmai*)—for that sacrificer
- अमृध्राः (*amṛdhrāḥ*)—unharmed
- उषसः (*uṣasaḥ*)—the dawns
- व्युच्छान् (*vyucchān*)—may they appear and shine

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Agni, who has blazing flames, who is of good motion and who is invoked everywhere, vies by his own radiance with the splendour of the sun. For the sacrificer who, urging Indra, says, "Let us press Soma for offering to Indra," may the unharmed dawns appear and shine.

### Printed p. 293

##### English translation
The piously worshipped (Agni), when invoked, glistening with the oblation, vies with the splendour of the sun : may the dawns rise innoxious to him who says, let us offer oblations to Indra.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

धृतपृष्ठः (*dhṛtapṛṣṭhaḥ*)—प्रदीप्तज्वालो धृतयुक्तपृष्ठप्रदेशो वा (*pradīptajvālo dhṛtayuktapṛṣṭhapradeśo vā*). Because ghee is offered into the fire, the meaning is that Agni shines with sharp and brilliant flames. It is customary to praise the Agni that blazes through oblations and the like by the word dhṛtapṛṣṭha.

स्वंचाः (*svaṃcāḥ*)—On this word Yāska has given the explanation: स्पंचाः सु अंचनः । आजुह्वानो धृतपृष्ठः स्पंचाः इत्यपि निगमो भवति (*spaṃcāḥ su aṃcanaḥ | ājuhvāno dhṛtapṛṣṭhaḥ spaṃcāḥ ityapi nigamo bhavati*) (Ni. 5-7). Since añcana means motion, svañcāḥ means one who has beautiful motion. If it be asked whether Agni has motion: the fire is churned in the araṇis, and from that place is brought to the fire-pit called Āhavanīya on the northern altar and established there. This rite is called Āgnyānayana or Agnipraṇayana. It is performed with injunction and with mantras. It is the most important of the auxiliary rites at the beginning of the sacrifice. To indicate this, he is here praised as svañcāḥ, one of beautiful motion.

सूर्यस्य भानुना आजुह्वानः सं यतते (*sūryasya bhānunā ājuhvānaḥ saṃ yatate*)—In the morning, when the sun rises, the priests kindle the fire and make it blaze. Since that fire, blazing and burning, shines very brightly, the idea is suggested that Agni, invoked together with the radiance of the sun that is just rising, shines like the radiance of the sun.

यः इंद्राय सुनवाम इति आह तस्मै अमृध्रा उषसः व्युच्छान् (*yaḥ iṃdrāya sunavāma iti āha tasmai amṛdhrā uṣasaḥ vyucchān*)—Whatever sacrificer expresses the wish that in the sacrifice performed in the morning Soma juice be pressed and offered for Indra, in his case may the Dawn deities be pleased, and may that day of the sacrifice turn out for the sacrificer a sudina, that is, a good day that brings about the favour of Indra and Agni. This intention is expressed here.

### Printed p. 294

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> समि॑द्दाग्निर्वनवस्त्री॒र्णब॑र्हियु॒क्तग्रा॑वा सु॒तसो॑मो  
> जराते ।  
> ग्रावा॑णो॒ यस्ये॑षि॒रं वदं॒त्यय॑दध्व॒युर्ह॒विषाव॒ सिंधुं॑॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> समिद्ध्व अग्निः । वनवत् । स्त्रीर्णबर्हिः । युक्तग्रावा । सुतसोमः । जराते ।  
> ग्रावाणः । यस्य । इषिरं । वदंति । आयत् । अध्वर्युः । हविषा । अव । सिंधुं ॥२॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> समिद्दाग्निः प्रदीप्ताग्निः स्तीर्णबर्हिः प्रस्तृतदर्भोऽयं यजमानो
> वनवत् । संभजते । युक्तग्रावा नियुक्ताभिषवपाषाणः सुतसोमोऽभिषुतसोमो
> जराते । जरति । स्तौति । यस्याध्वर्योर्ग्रावाणोऽभिषवसाधना इषिरमेषणीयं
> गमनशीलं वा शब्दं वदंति सोऽध्वर्युर्हविषा सहानायत् सिंधुं । पन्नेजनीरवगच्छति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: He whose fire is kindled (*samiddhāgni*) and whose sacred grass is spread out (*stīrṇabarhiḥ*), this sacrificer, worships (*vanavat*, he serves). He who has the pressing stones fitted in place (*yuktagrāvā*) and whose Soma is pressed (*sutasoma*) praises (*jarāte* means "he praises"). The adhvaryu, whose pressing stones, the instruments of pressing, utter a desirable or moving sound (*iṣira*, that which is to be wished for, or that which has motion), that adhvaryu goes down with the oblation to the stream (*sindhu*), that is, to the waters called pannejanī.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- समिद्दाग्नि (*samiddāgni*)—he who has a kindled fire
- स्तीर्णबर्हिः (*stīrṇabarhiḥ*)—the sacrificer who has sacred grass spread widely for a seat
- वनवत् (*vanavat*)—offers worship
- युक्तग्रावा (*yuktagrāvā*)—he who holds the stone for pressing out the juice
- सुतसोमः (*sutasomaḥ*)—the sacrificer (or priest) who has pressed Soma
- जराते (*jarāte*)—praises
- यस्य (*yasya*)—of which adhvaryu
- ग्रावाणः (*grāvāṇaḥ*)—the stones
- इषिरं (*iṣiraṃ*)—a moving sound
- वदंति (*vadaṃti*)—indicate
- हविषा (*haviṣā*)—with the oblation
- सिंधुं (*siṃdhuṃ*)—to the river
- अपायत् (*apāyat*)—descends (for the preliminary rite) **NOTE**

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The sacrificer who has a kindled fire and sacred grass spread widely for the seat offers worship. The sacrificer, or the priest, who holds the stone for pressing out the juice and has the pressed Soma praises. The adhvaryu whose pressing stones indicate a moving sound goes down with the oblation to the river for the preliminary rite.

### Printed p. 295

##### English translation
He whose fire is kindled, whose sacred grass is strewn, offers worship : he whose stone is uplifted, whose Soma juice is effused, offers praise : the priest, of whom the stones utter the sounds of bruising, descends with the oblation (for previous ablution) to the stream.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

समिद्दाग्निः स्तीर्णबर्हिषः वनवत् युक्तग्रावा सुतसोमः (*samiddāgniḥ stīrṇabarhiṣaḥ vanavat yuktagrāvā sutasomaḥ*)—All these qualifying words describe the preparations that the sacrificer who performs the sacrifice has made for it. That is, the sacrificer, after sunrise in the morning, in order to perform the sacrifice, together with the priests and according to the rules performs the auxiliary rites such as the churning of the fire and the Agnipraṇayana, establishes the fire on the northern altar and kindles it with fuel and other materials so that he may offer oblations in it: this meaning is suggested by the word समिद्दाग्निः (*samiddāgniḥ*). Then the deities, invoked by Agni (or by the Hotṛ) and coming to the place of sacrifice, need a place to sit; for this he spreads darbha grass cut level, layer upon layer, that is, one layer upon another and a third upon that, in three layers. It is the custom to lay three layers in this way so that the seat is soft for those who sit. The idea that the sacrificer has prepared such a seat for the deities is expressed by the word स्तीर्णबर्हिः (*stīrṇabarhiḥ*). After this the priests, the adhvaryu and others, take stones in their hands, crush the Soma creepers and press out the juice. That the stones are made ready for this pressing of Soma, and that Soma is pressed out with them, is understood from the words यक्तग्रावा (*yaktagrāvā*) and सुतसोमः (*sutasomaḥ*). All these, that is, the kindling of the fire, the invocation of the deities, the spreading of the seat and the pressing of Soma, are preparations to be made at the start of the sacrifice as auxiliary rites. The matter that the sacrificer has made them and is ready to perform the sacrifice is indicated in the first half of this Ṛk.

In the second half the beginning of the sacrifice is described in detail: the word जराते (*jarāte*) shows that the sacrificer or the Hotṛ has begun to praise the deities; ग्रावाणः यस्य इषिरं नदंति (*grāvāṇaḥ yasya iṣiraṃ nadaṃti*) shows that the stones that crush the Soma, busy in the work of pressing, are pressing out the Soma juice with an audible sound; and the sentence अध्वर्युः हविषा सिंधुं अव आयत् (*adhvaryuḥ haviṣā siṃdhuṃ ava āyat*) describes that the adhvaryu and others are engaged in their respective duties.

### Printed p. 296

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> व॒धूरि॒यं पति॑मि॒च्छंत्ये॑ति॒ य ईं॒ वहा॑ते॒ महि॑षीमिषि॒रां ।  
> आस्य॑ श्रवस्या॒द्रथ॒ आ च॑ घोषात्पु॒रू स॒हस्रा॒ परि॑ वर्तयाते ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> वधूः । इयं । पतिं । इच्छंती । एति । यः । ईं । वहाते । महिषीं । इषिरां ।  
> आ । अस्य । श्रवस्यात् । रथः । आ । च । घोषात् । पुरु । सहस्रा । परि । वर्तयाते ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> इयं वधूरिंद्रस्य पत्नि पतिमिच्छंती स्वप्रियं यज्ञगमनाय प्रवृत्तमिच्छंत्येति । अनुगच्छति । य इंद्र ईमेनां महिषीं वहाते वहतीषिरां गमनवतीं अस्येंद्रस्य रथ आ आर्वागस्मदभिमुखं श्रवस्यात् । अन्नमिच्छति । आ च
> घोषात् । आघुष्यति । शब्दयति । पूर्वत्यधिकं सहस्रा सहस्राणि धनानि परि परितो
> वर्तयाते । वर्तयति । प्रापयति । श्रवस्यादा च घुष्यादिति वा ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: This bride, the wife of Indra, desiring her husband, that is, wishing for her beloved who has set out for the sacrifice, follows him. The Indra who brings this chief queen (*mahiṣī*), who is *iṣirā*, that is, endowed with motion: of this Indra the chariot comes hither, facing us. It desires food (*śravasyāt*), it sounds loudly (*ghoṣāt*, it cries out, makes a noise), and it rolls around, that is, brings all around, more than before, thousands of riches. Or else the two verbs are to be taken as meaning "it desires food and it would sound."

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यः (*yaḥ*)—which Indra
- इषिरां (*iṣirāṃ*)—his companion
- ईं महिषीं (*īṃ mahiṣīṃ*)—his chief queen
- वहते (*vahate*)—brings along (his wife)
- इयं वधूः (*iyaṃ vadhūḥ*)—this wife
- पतिं (*patiṃ*)—her husband Indra
- इच्छंती (*icchaṃtī*)—wishing that he should go toward the sacrifice
- एति (*eti*)—follows him and goes
- अस्य (*asya*)—of this same Indra
- रथः (*rathaḥ*)—the chariot
- आ (*ā*)—all around
- श्रवस्यात् (*śravasyāt*)—wishes food (for the sacrificer)
- आ च घोषात् (*ā ca ghoṣāt*)—makes sound all around
- पुरु (*puru*)—exceedingly
- सहस्रा (*sahasrā*)—immeasurably
- परि (*pari*)—all around
- वर्तयते (*vartayate*)—provides (the wealth)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The wife of the Indra who brings his companion, his chief queen, wishing that her husband Indra should go toward the sacrifice, follows him. The chariot of that same Indra wishes food for the sacrificer all around and makes sound all around. It provides all around wealth that is exceedingly abundant and immeasurable.

### Printed p. 297

##### English translation
The bride (of Indra), devoted to her lord, attends, him (to the rite), who thus brings (with him) his accompanying queen : may his car convey to us ample food; may it sound loudly ; may it scatter around many thousands (of riches).

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

In this Ṛk there is a description of Indra's wife following Indra, sitting with Indra in the chariot, and coming to the place of sacrifice. Ordinarily, in sacrifices and the like, it is the custom to invite Indra alone for the drinking of Soma. But in this Ṛk the meaning is implied that Indra and his wife Indrāṇī come in a chariot that is able to give food and the like, that makes a sound by the turning of its wheels as it moves, and that gives wealth and the like without number to those who worship Indra.

महिषी (*mahiṣī*)—The word mahiṣa is read among the names for "great" (Ni. 2-13) and indicates the sense "large, of great form". Therefore the word mahiṣī can be given the senses "worshipful, excellent".

वधूः (*vadhūḥ*)—The word vadhū is read among the names of rivers (Ni. 2-24). Ordinarily the word vadhū is customarily used in the sense of a woman who has a young husband and is married. **NOTE**

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> न स राजा॑ व्यथते॒ यस्मि॒न्निंद्र॑स्ती॒व्रं सोमं॒ पिब॑ति॒ गोस॑खायं ।  
> आ स॑त्त्व॒नैरज॑ति॒ हंति॑ वृ॒त्रं क्षेति॑ क्षि॒तीः सु॒भगो॒ नाम॒ पुष्य॑न् ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> न । सः । राजा । व्यथते । यस्मिन् । इंद्रः । तीव्रं । सोमं । पिबति । गोऽसखायं ।  
> आ । सत्त्वनैः । अजति । हंति । न तं । क्षेति । क्षितीः । सुभगो । नाम । पुष्यन् ॥ ८ ॥

**NOTE**

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> आ । सत्वनैः । अजति । हंति । वृत्रं । क्षेति । क्षितीः । सुभगः । नाम । पुष्यन् ॥ ४ ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The block gives only a word-by-word line of the second half of the Ṛk, with the number 4, and no explanation by Sāyaṇa. **NOTE**

### Printed p. 298

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> स राजा न व्यथते । व्यथितो न भवति । यस्मिन्राजनि तदीये यज्ञ इंद्रस्तीव्रं मदजनकं सोमं पिबति गोसखायं । विकारे प्रकृतिशब्दः क्षीरादिमिश्रणवंतं । स राजा सत्वनैः सत्वभिरनुचरैरा सर्वतोजति । गच्छति । हुति च वृत्रं पापं
> वैरिणं वा । क्षेति गच्छति क्षितीः प्रजाः । अथवा क्षितीर्निवासान्निवसते । सुभगः
> शोभनसुखो नाम नामकं धनमिंद्रस्य स्तोत्रं वा पुष्यन् पोषयन् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: That king is not afflicted; he does not become troubled, in whose realm, that is, in whose sacrifice, Indra drinks the sharp, intoxicating Soma. The word *gosakhāyam* is a case of using the name of the original substance for its product: it means Soma mixed with milk and the like. That king, with his *satvans*, that is, with his attendants, goes about everywhere. He also destroys Vṛtra, that is, sin, or else the enemy. He dwells among the *kṣitīḥ*, the subjects; or else *kṣitīḥ* are dwellings, and he dwells in dwellings. *Subhaga* means one of fair happiness; *nāma* means the name, that is, wealth, or a hymn of praise of Indra; *puṣyan* means nourishing it. **NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यस्मिन् (*yasmin*)—in whose sacrifice, that of which king
- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra
- तीव्रं (*tīvraṃ*)—intoxicating
- गोसखायं (*gosakhāyaṃ*)—mixed with milk
- सोमं (*somaṃ*)—the Soma juice
- पिबति (*pibati*)—drinks
- स राजा (*sa rājā*)—that king
- न व्यथते (*na vyathate*)—does not become afflicted
- सत्वनैः (*satvanaiḥ*)—with his attendants
- आ (*ā*)—all around (fearlessly)
- अजति (*ajati*)—can move about
- वृत्रं (*vṛtraṃ*)—the enemy
- हुति (*huti*)—destroys
- सुभगः (*subhagaḥ*)—enjoying excellent happiness
- नाम (*nāma*)—the name of Indra
- पुष्यन् (*puṣyan*)—praising
- क्षितीः (*kṣitīḥ*)—his subjects
- क्षेति (*kṣeti*)—makes them dwell happily

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The king in whose sacrifice Indra drinks the intoxicating Soma juice mixed with milk is not afflicted. With his attendants he can move about everywhere without fear of enemies. He destroys his enemies. And while he himself enjoys excellent happiness, praising the divine names of Indra, he also carries on the government in such a way that his subjects live well.

##### English translation
That prince suffers no evil in whose realm Indra drinks the sharp Soma juice mixed with milk : attended by faithful (followers), he moves (in all directions) : he destroys his enemy, he protects his subjects : enjoying prosperity, he cherishes the name of (Indra).

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

गोसखायं (*gosakhāyaṃ*)—क्षीरादिमिश्रणवंतं (*kṣīrādimiśraṇavaṃtaṃ*). Here the word *go* is used to denote milk. The meaning is Soma juice mixed with milk. There is also the practice of purifying the Soma juice and drinking it just so, without mixing anything with it; such Soma is called *tīvra*. And in order to make the Soma juice tasty, substances such as milk, curds and flour are mixed with it. These

### Printed p. 299

substances are called *āśira* substances. Soma juice mixed with milk is called *gavāśira*, Soma juice mixed with curds *dadhyāśira*, and Soma juice mixed with flour *yavāśira*. In the present Ṛk it is stated that Indra drinks the Soma juice mixed with milk, called *gavāśira*.

The fruits that come to the sacrificer who performs the sacrifice when Indra is satisfied by the drinking of Soma are described in this Ṛk. The king in whose sacrifice Indra, invited and arrived, drinks the Soma juice offered by the priests so as to be satisfied, suffers no kind of sorrow. Many attendants, servants and others, resort to him. He overcomes his enemies in battle, enjoys many kinds of happiness, and protects his subjects so that they live happily. Thus the greatness of Indra's Soma-drinking is described in this Ṛk.

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> पुष्प्या॒त् क्षेमे॑ अ॒भि योगे॑ भवात्यु॒भे वृत्॑ संय॒ुती सं  ॑
> जयाति ।  
> प्रि॒यः सूर्ये॑ प्रि॒यो अ॒ग्ना भ॑वाति॒ य इंद्रा॑य सु॒तसो॑मो॒ ददा॑शत् ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> पुष्यात् । क्षेमे । अभि । योगे । भवाति । उभे इति । वृतौ । संयती इति संयती । सं । जयाति ।  
> प्रियः । सूर्ये । प्रियः । अग्ना । भवाति । यः । इंद्राय । सुतसोमः ।  
> ददाशत् ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> पुष्यात् पोषयेद्बंध्वादीन् । क्षेमे प्राप्तस्य धनस्य रक्षणे योगेऽलब्धस्य
> प्राप्ता चाभि भवाति । भवति । अभीत्यनर्थको धात्वर्थानुवादी वा । प्रभुर्भवती-

### Printed p. 300

> त्यर्थः । तथोभे वृत् वर्तमाने संयती नियते अहोरात्रे सं जयाति ।
> सम्यक् जयति । ते सुखकरे स्यातामित्यर्थः । किंच स प्रियो भवति सूर्ये तथाग्नाग्नौ प्रियो भवति य इंद्राय सुतसोमो ददाशत् सोमं ददाति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: *Puṣyāt* means that he would nourish his kinsmen and others. *Kṣeme* means in the protection of wealth already obtained; *yoge* means in the acquiring of what has not been obtained; he becomes (*abhi bhavāti* means *bhavati*; the word *abhi* is either without meaning or repeats the sense of the root) the master of both; this is the meaning, that he becomes lord. Likewise he conquers well the two, day and night, which are present and regulated; the meaning is that they would be happy for him. Further, he who gives Soma to Indra, that is, offers the pressed Soma, becomes dear in the sun and likewise dear in Agni.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यः (*yaḥ*)—whoever
- सुतसोमः (*sutasomaḥ*)—pressing out Soma juice
- इंद्राय (*iṃdrāya*)—for Indra
- ददाशत् (*dadāśat*)—offers it (he)
- पुष्यात् (*puṣyāt*)—nourishes (his kinsmen and friends)
- क्षेमे (*kṣeme*)—in the protection of wealth
- योगे (*yoge*)—in the obtaining of new wealth
- अभि भवाति (*abhi bhavāti*)—becomes capable
- उभे वृता (*ubhe vṛtā*)—in the past, present and future (always) **NOTE**
- संयुती (*saṃyutī*)—in day and night of regulated course
- सं जयाति (*saṃ jayāti*)—wins complete victory
- सूर्ये (*sūrye*)—to the sun
- प्रियः भवति (*priyaḥ bhavati*)—becomes dear
- अग्ना (*agnā*)—to Agni
- प्रियः (*priyaḥ*)—becomes dear

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Whoever presses out Soma juice and offers it to Indra nourishes his kinsmen and friends. He becomes capable of protecting the wealth he has and of obtaining new wealth. In the past, the future and the present, and in the days and nights of regulated course, he wins complete victory. He becomes dear to the sun and to Agni.

##### English translation
He cherishes (his kin) ; he (reigns) in welfare and prosperity ; he is victorious in present and continuous ( time ) : dear is he to the sun, dear to Agni, who, with prepared libation, offers it to Indra.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

As said in the previous Ṛk, in this Ṛk too the great nature of Indra's Soma-drinking and the fruits that come from it are described. But here no particular person is indicated by the word king. It can apply to any man who performs a sacrifice.

### Printed p. 301

## Sūkta 38 (Maṇḍala 5)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> उरोष्ट इति पंचर्चं षष्ठं सूक्तमत्रेरार्षमानुष्टुभमैंद्रं । उरोरानुष्टुभमित्यनुक्रांतं ॥ विनियोगो लैंगिकः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: The sixth sūkta, उरोष्ट इति (*uroṣṭa iti*), has five ṛks; its seer is Atri, its metre is Anuṣṭubh and its deity is Indra. The Anukramaṇikā says: उरोरानुष्टुभमित्यनुक्रांतं (*urorānuṣṭubhamityanukrāṃtaṃ*). The use in ritual is laiṅgika.

Translation of the Anuvāda: This sūkta, उरोष्टे (*uroṣṭe*), is the sixth sūkta in the third anuvāka. There are five ṛks in it. The seer of this sūkta is Atri; Indra is the deity; the metre is Anuṣṭubh. In the Anukramaṇikā it is said: उरोरानुष्टुभं (*urorānuṣṭubhaṃ*). The use in ritual is laiṅgika.

Sūkta: Khila. **NOTE**

Maṇḍala 5, Anuvāka 3, Sūkta 38.

Aṣṭaka 4, Adhyāya 2, Varga 9.

Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 5.

Seer: Atri.

Deity: Indra.

Metre: Anuṣṭubh.

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> उ॒रोष्ट॑ इंद्र॒ राध॑सो वि॒भ्वी रा॒तिः श॑तक्रतो ।  
> अधा॑ नो विश्वचर्षणे द्यु॒म्ना सु॑क्षत्र मंहय ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> उरोः । ते । इंद्र । राधसः । विभ्वीरातिः । शतक्रतो इति शतक्रतो ।  
> अध । नः । विश्वचर्षणे । द्युम्ना । सुक्षत्र । मंहय ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र शतक्रतो बहुकर्मन् ते तवोरोः प्रभूतस्य राधसो धनस्य
> रातिर्दानं विभ्वी महती । अधातः कारणात् नोऽस्मभ्यं हे विश्वचर्षणे सर्वॆस्य

### Printed p. 302

> द्रष्टः सुक्षत्र शोभनधनेंद्र । क्षत्रमिति धननाम । द्युम्ना द्युम्नानि धनानि मंहय ।
> प्रयच्छ ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: O Indra of a hundred acts, that is, of many deeds, the gift of your abundant wealth is great. For that reason, to us, O all-seeing one, O Indra of excellent wealth (*kṣatra* is a name for wealth), bestow riches (*dyumna* means wealth); *mamhaya* means give.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- शतक्रतो (*śatakrato*)—O doer of many deeds
- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- ते (*te*)—your
- उरोः (*uroḥ*)—abundant
- राधसः (*rādhasaḥ*)—of wealth
- रातिः (*rātiḥ*)—the gift
- विभ्वी (*vibhvī*)—exceedingly great
- अध (*adha*)—therefore
- विश्वचर्षषे (*viśvacarṣaṣe*)—all-beholder
- सुकृत्र (*sukṛtra*)—possessor of excellent wealth, O Indra
- नः (*naḥ*)—to us
- द्युम्ना (*dyumnā*)—the wealth of riches
- मंहय (*maṃhaya*)—bestow

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, doer of many deeds, the gift of your abundant wealth is of great glory. Therefore, O Indra, all-beholder and possessor of excellent wealth, bestow wealth and riches upon us.

##### English translation
Indra of many exploits, liberal is the distribution of your abundant riches: therefore, all-beholder, possessor of excellent wealth, bestow upon us opulence.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

शतक्रतो (*śatakrato*)—बहुकर्मन् (*bahukarman*). One who has done many deeds; that is, one who has done many daring deeds that cannot be done by anyone else, such as the slaying of Vṛtra. Such is the intention. Here the word *śata* must be understood as "many". The word *kratu* means deed, a daring act. A daring act that can be done by the use of strength, valour and courage is called *kratu*, and because Indra has done many such deeds, the calling of Indra also by the word *śatakratu* is specially in use in the Ṛgveda.

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यदी॑मिंद्र श्र॒वाय्य॒मिषं॑ शविष्ठ दधि॒षे ।  
> प॒प्र॒थे दी॑र्घ॒श्रुत्त॑मं॒ हिर॑ण्यवर्ण दु॒ष्टरं॑ ॥ २ ॥

### Printed p. 303

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यत् । ईं । इंद्र । श्रवाय्यं । इषं । शविष्ठ । दधिषे ।  
> पप्रथे । दीर्घश्रुत्तमं । हिरण्यवर्ण । दुस्तरं ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र शविष्ठ अतिशयेन बलवन् यदिषमन्नं श्रवाय्यं श्रवणीयमीं
> सर्वतो दधिषे धारयसि ददासि ना ॥ इषशब्दो नपुंसकलिंगोऽप्यस्ति ॥ हे हिरण्यवर्णेंद्र तदन्नं पप्रथे । प्रथते । कथं । दीर्घश्रुत्तममतिदूरश्रवणीयतमं ।
> सर्वत्र कीर्त्यमित्यर्थः । दुष्टरमनभिभाव्यमुक्तलक्षणं प्रथते ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: O Indra, O most mighty, the food (*iṣa*) that is *śravāyya*, that is, worthy of being heard, you hold on all sides and give. The word *iṣa* also occurs in the neuter gender. **NOTE** O golden-hued Indra, that food spreads. How? Most widely heard, that is, to be celebrated everywhere. It spreads as unconquerable, having the stated character.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- शविष्ठ (*śaviṣṭha*)—most mighty
- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- यत् (*yat*)—even if
- श्रवाय्यं (*śravāyyaṃ*)—exceedingly well known
- इषं (*iṣaṃ*)—food
- ईं (*īṃ*)—everywhere
- दधिषे (*dadhiṣe*)—you hold
- हिरण्यवर्ण (*hiraṇyavarṇa*)—O Indra of golden-coloured form
- दुष्टरं (*duṣṭaraṃ*)—that to obtain that food is very difficult
- दीर्घश्रुत्थमं (*dīrghaśrutthamaṃ*)—so as to be heard everywhere
- पप्रथे (*paprathe*)—it is renowned

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O most mighty Indra, it is known to everyone that you hold food that is very well known and abundant. Yet, O golden-hued Indra, it is so renowned as to be heard everywhere that obtaining that food is a very laborious task.

##### English translation
Although, most mighty Indra, you possess well-known abundance (of food), yet golden-hued (deity) it is most notoriously reported as difficult (to be procured).

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

ईं (*īṃ*)—The word *īm* is well known as a particle (*nipāta*). On this word Yāska says: अथ ये प्रवृत्तेऽर्थेऽमिताक्षरेषु ग्रंथेषु वाक्यपूरणा आगच्छंति पदपूरणास्ते मिताक्षरेष्वनर्थकाः कमीमिद्विति ॥ (*atha ye pravṛtte 'rthe 'mitākṣareṣu graṃtheṣu vākyapūraṇā āgacchaṃti padapūraṇāste mitākṣareṣvanarthakāḥ kamīmidviti ‖*) (Ni. 1-9)

### Printed p. 304

That is, the particles *kam*, *ī*, *it*, *u* have no definite meaning of their own. It is said that these words are used in prose works for completing the sentence, and in metrical Ṛks and the like for completing the pāda. The pādas of metres (a pāda is a part of the metre, generally a quarter) must have a fixed number of syllables. In some cases, when a pāda is short by one syllable, one of these particles is used to fill out the pāda; therefore it is said that these particles are used for completing the words. Besides this, another kind of meaning may also result. Here the word *īm* has the sense "everywhere, on all sides". In some places the sense of the word *eva* is also given, and so on.

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> शुष्मा॑सो॒ ये ते॑ अद्रिवो मे॒हना॑ केत॒सापः॑ ।  
> उ॒भा दे॒वाव॒भिष्ट॑ये दि॒वश्च॒ ग्मश्च॑ राजथः ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> शुष्मासः । ये । ते । अद्रिवः । मेहना । केतसापः ।  
> उभा । देवौ । अभिष्टये । दिवः । च। गृः । च । राजथः ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे अद्रिवो वज्रवन्निंद्र ते त्वदीया ये शुष्मासो बलभूता मरुतः
> संति । कीदृशास्ते । मेहना मंहनीयाः केतसापः प्रज्ञापकं कर्मांतरिक्षं वा
> स्पृशंतः । उभौ देवौ त्वं च मरुत्समूहश्चेत्सु भौ युवां दिवश्च द्युलोकात्
> ग्मश्च भूमेः सकाशादभिष्टयेऽभितो गमनाय राजथः । ईशाथे ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: O Indra, wielder of the thunderbolt, those *śuṣmas* of yours, that is, the Maruts who are your strength, are such as are *mehanā*, worthy of praise, and *ketasāpaḥ*, touching the rite that makes known, or touching the sky. Both gods, you and the host of the Maruts **NOTE**, rule from heaven and from earth, for going on all sides; *rājathaḥ* means "you two rule".

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अद्रिवः (*adrivaḥ*)—O Indra, bearer of the thunderbolt
- मेहना (*mehanā*)—worthy of worship
- केतसापः (*ketasāpaḥ*)—having rites that make known
- ते (*te*)—to you
- शुष्मासः (*śuṣmāsaḥ*)—givers of strength
- ये (*ye*)—whichever Maruts there are (they)
- उभा देवौ (*ubhā devau*)—you and the host of the Maruts, who are of divine nature
- दिवश्च (*divaśca*)—from the world of heaven
- ग्मश्च (*gmaśca*)—and from the earth
- अभिष्टये (*abhiṣṭaye*)—to come to this place of sacrifice **NOTE**
- राजथः (*rājathaḥ*)—you shine as lords

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, bearer of the thunderbolt, the Maruts who are worthy of worship, who have rites that make known and who give you strength, and you together with them, you who are of divine nature, being in the highest station, shine as lords of all heaven and earth.

### Printed p. 305

##### English translation
Wielder of the thunderbolt, yours are the strong (Maruts), who are adorable, and whose exploits are renowned : both divinities, you and they) rule at pleasure over heaven and earth.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

अद्रिवः (*adrivaḥ*)—The word *adri* is stated among the names of the thunderbolt, among the names of the cloud (Ni. 2.2) and among the names of the mountain. *Adri* means the stone called *grāvā* that crushes the Soma plant.

मेहना (*mehanā*)—On this word Yāska stated the meaning "*mehanā* is *maṃhanīya*", that is, worthy of worship, and in support of it gave as an example the Ṛk beginning यदिंद्र चित्र (*yadiṃdra citra*).

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> उ॒तो नो॑ अ॒स्य कस्य॑ चि॒द्दक्ष॑स्य॒ तव॑ वृत्रहन् ।  
> अ॒स्मभ्यं॑ नृ॒म्लमा भ॑रा॒स्मभ्यं॑ नृमणस्यसे ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> उतो इति । नः । अस्य । कस्य । चित् । दक्षस्य । तव । वृत्तहन् ।  
> अस्मभ्यं । नृम्णं । आ । भर । अस्मभ्यं । नृमनस्यसे ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> उतो अपि च हे वृत्रहन्निंद्र नोऽस्मभ्यं कस्य चिदनिर्देश्यस्य दक्षस्य प्रवृद्धस्य
> दातुर्वास्य तव स्वभूतेभ्योऽस्मभ्यं । पुनःश्रुतिरादरार्था । नृण्नं नृमणु धनमा
> भर । आहर । हे इंद्र यतस्त्वमस्मभ्यं नृमणस्यसे धनमिच्छसि ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: And also, O Vṛtra-slayer Indra, to us, who are your own, bring (*ā bhara* means "fetch") the wealth (*nṛmṇa* is wealth) of some unspecified person who is *dakṣa*, that is, grown great, or a giver; the repetition of "to us" is for emphasis. O Indra, since you desire wealth for us (*nṛmaṇasyase* means "you wish wealth").

### Printed p. 306

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- उतो (*uto*)—moreover
- वृत्रहन् (*vṛtrahan*)—O Indra, slayer of Vṛtra
- अस्य तव (*asya tava*)—belonging to you
- अस्मभ्यं (*asmabhyaṃ*)—to us
- कस्य चित् दक्षस्य (*kasya cit dakṣasya*)—of someone abounding in wealth
- नृण्मुं (*nṛṇmuṃ*)—the wealth
- आ भर (*ā bhara*)—bring and give (O Indra)
- अस्मभ्यं (*asmabhyaṃ*)—to us
- नृमणस्यसे (*nṛmaṇasyase*)—you wish that wealth be obtained

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, slayer of Vṛtra, bring to us, who belong to you, the wealth of someone abounding in wealth. You certainly wish that wealth be obtained by us.

##### English translation
Slayer of Vritra, bring to us, your (worshippers), the wealth of any powerful (man) whatsoever, for you are disposed to enrich us.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

नृम्मं (*nṛmmaṃ*)—The word *nṛmṇa* is read among the names of strength (Ni. 3-9) and among the names of wealth (Ni. 3-9).

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> नू त॑ अ॒भिर॒भिष्टि॑भि॒स्तन॒ शर्म॑ञ्घातक्रतो ।  
> इंद्र॒ स्याम॑ सुगो॒पाः शूर॒ स्याम॑ सुगो॒पाः ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> नु । ते । अभिः । अभिष्टिभिः । तव । शर्मन् । शतक्रतो अतिशत्यक्रतो ।  
> इंद्र ॥ स्याम । सुगोपाः । शूर । स्याम । सुगोपाः ॥ ५ ॥

### Printed p. 307

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे शतक्रतो इंद्र ते तवाभिरभिष्टिभिरभिगमनैर्नु शीघ्रं समृद्धा भवेमेति
> शेषः । हे इंद्र तव शर्मन् शर्मणि सुखी सति सुगोपाः स्याम ! भवेम । हे
> शूर तव शर्मणि सुगोपाः शोभनरक्षणाः स्याम । भवेम । पुनरुक्तिरादरार्था ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: O Indra of a hundred acts, by your approaches (*abhiṣṭi* means going towards), may we quickly become prosperous; the verb "may we become" is what is to be supplied. O Indra, being happy in your shelter (*śarman*), may we be well protected, may we be. O hero, in your shelter may we be well protected, that is, well guarded; may we be. The repetition is for emphasis.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- शतक्रतो (*śatakrato*)—O Indra
- ते (*te*)—made for you
- अभिः अभिष्टिभिः (*abhiḥ abhiṣṭibhiḥ*)—by these our praises
- नु (*nu*)—quickly (attain satisfaction) **NOTE**
- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- तव (*tava*)—to you
- शर्मन् (*śarman*)—when there is happiness
- सुगोपाः (*sugopāḥ*)—having excellent protection
- स्याम (*syāma*)—may we be
- शूर (*śūra*)—O valiant Indra
- सुगोपाः स्याम (*sugopāḥ syāma*)—may we obtain your excellent protection

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, be quickly satisfied by these our praises made for you. O heroic Indra, as a result of your satisfaction may we obtain your excellent protection.

##### English translation
May we, Shatakratu, speedily (partake of) your felicity through these our prayers: may we be well secured, Indra, (by you): may we be carefully protected, hero, by you.

## Sūkta 39 (Maṇḍala 5)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> यदिंद्र चित्रेति पंचर्चं सप्तमं सूक्तमत्रेरार्षमैंद्रं । अंत्या पंक्तिः
> शिष्टाः पंक्त्यंतपरिभाषयानुष्टुभः । यदिंद्र पंक्त्यंतमित्यनुक्रमणिका ॥ अभिप्लविकेषूक्ष्येषु तृतीयसवन आद्यस्तृचोऽच्छावाकस्य वैकल्पिकः स्तोत्रियः ।
> सूश्रितं च । यदिंद्र चित्र मेहना यस्ते साधिष्ठोऽवसे । आ. ७-८ । इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: The seventh sūkta, यदिंद्र चित्रेति (*yadiṃdra citreti*), has five ṛks; its seer is Atri and its deity is Indra. The last Ṛk is in the Paṅkti metre; the remaining ones are in Anuṣṭubh by the convention that the rest take the metre of a sūkta that ends in a Paṅkti. The Anukramaṇikā says: यदिंद्र पंक्त्यंतमित्यनुक्रमणिका (*yadiṃdra paṃktyaṃtamityanukramaṇikā*). Use in ritual: in the Ukthya days connected with the Abhiplava, at the third pressing, the first tṛca is the optional Stotriya of the Acchāvāka. And the sūtra: "Yad indra citra mehanā yas te sādhiṣṭho 'vase" (Ā. Sū. 7-8). **NOTE**

Translation of the Anuvāda: This sūkta, यदिंद्र चित्र (*yadiṃdra citra*), is the seventh sūkta in the third anuvāka. There are five ṛks in it. The seer of this sūkta is Atri; Indra is the deity; the

### Printed p. 308

last Ṛk of this sūkta is in the Paṅkti metre; the remaining Ṛks are in the Anuṣṭubh metre. In the Anukramaṇikā it is said: यदिंद्र पंक्त्यंतं (*yadiṃdra paṃktyaṃtaṃ*). In the Ukthya sacrifices connected with the Abhiplava, at the time of the third pressing, the first three Ṛks of this sūkta are to be chosen by the priest called Acchāvāka as optional Stotriya mantras; this is explained by the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta-sūtra यदिंद्र चित्र मेहना यस्ते साधिष्टोऽवसे (*yadiṃdra citra mehanā yaste sādhiṣṭo 'vase*) (A. 7-8). **NOTE**

Sūkta 39.

Maṇḍala 5, Anuvāka 3, Sūkta 39.

Aṣṭaka 4, Adhyāya 2, Varga 10.

Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 5.

Seer: Atri.

Deity: Indra.

Metre: Ṛks 1-4 Anuṣṭubh; Ṛk 5 Paṅkti.

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यदिं॑द्र चित्र मे॒हनास्ति॒ त्वादा॑तमद्रिवः ।  
> राध॒स्तन्नो॑ विदद्वस उभयाह॒स्त्या भ॑र ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यत् । इंद्र । चित्र । मेहना । अस्ति । त्वादातं । अद्विन्वः ।  
> राधः । तत् । नः । विदद्वसो इति विदत्स्‌वसो । उभयाहस्ति । आ । भर ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र हे चित्र जायनीय हे आद्रिवो वज्रवन्निंद्र यन्मेहना मंहनीयं त्वादातं त्वया दातव्यं राधो धनमस्ति तद्धनं हे विदद्वसो लब्धधनेंद्र
> नोऽस्मभ्यमुभयाहस्तुभाभ्यां हस्ताभ्यामा भर । आहर । अत्र यदिंद्र चित्र
> चायनीय मंहनीयं धनमस्तीत्यादिनिरुक्तं द्रष्टव्यं ॥ नि. ४-४ ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: O Indra, O wonderful one (*citra*, worthy of regard) **NOTE**, O Indra with the thunderbolt: whatever wealth (*rādhas*) there is that is *mehanā*, worthy of praise, and *tvādāta*, to be given by you, that wealth, O Vidadvasu, O Indra who has obtained wealth, bring (*ā bhara* means "fetch") to us with both hands. Here the Nirukta beginning धनमस्तीत्यादिनिरुक्तं (*dhanamastītyādiniruktaṃ*) is to be seen (Ni. 4-4).

### Printed p. 309

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- चित्र (*citra*)—having an attractive form
- अद्रिवः (*adrivaḥ*)—bearer of the thunderbolt
- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- मेहना (*mehanā*)—worthy of worship
- त्वादातं (*tvādātaṃ*)—to be given by you
- यत् राधः (*yat rādhaḥ*)—whatever wealth
- अस्ति (*asti*)—there is
- विदद्वसो (*vidadvaso*)—O Indra, lord of wealth
- तत् (*tat*)—that wealth
- नः (*naḥ*)—to us
- उभया हस्ति (*ubhayā hasti*)—with both hands
- आ भर (*ā bhara*)—provide

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, of attractive form, bearer of the thunderbolt and lord of wealth, whatever wealth there is that is worthy of worship and to be given by you, fill both your hands with that wealth and give it to us.

##### English translation
Wonderful Indra, wielder of the thunderbolt, since precious treasure is to be distributed by you, bestow it, possessor of riches, with both hands, upon us.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

मेहना (*mehanā*)—In order to explain the meaning of this word Yāska took the present Ṛk itself and gave an etymology for it: यदिंद्र चित्रं चायनीयं मंहनीयं धनमस्ति । यन्म इह नास्तीति वा त्रीणि मध्यमानि पदानि त्वया नस्तद्दातव्यं । अद्रिवन् । अद्रिरादृणात्येतेन । अपि वात्तेः स्यात् । ते सोमाद इति ह विज्ञायते । राध इति धननाम राध्ववंत्यनेन । तन्नस्त्वं वित्तधनोभाभ्यां हस्ताभ्यामाहर । उभौ समुच्चौ भवतः ॥ (*yadiṃdra citraṃ cāyanīyaṃ maṃhanīyaṃ dhanamasti | yanma iha nāstīti vā trīṇi madhyamāni padāni tvayā nastaddātavyaṃ | adrivan | adrirādṛṇātyetena | api vātteḥ syāt | te somāda iti ha vijñāyate | rādha iti dhananāma rādhvavaṃtyanena | tannastvaṃ vittadhanobhābhyāṃ hastābhyāmāhara | ubhau samuccau bhavataḥ ‖*) (Ni. 4-3).

Yāska has explained the component meaning of the word *mehanā* in this way: the word *mehanā* is made up of three separate words, *me* + *iha* + *na*. That is, in the sentence of five words *yat me iha na asti*, the word *mehanā* is formed from the middle words *me iha na*. The meaning of the sentence is "that which is not with me". The man who prays to Indra for wealth prays to Indra to give it for the very reason that the wealth is not with him. According to the rule that people ask from another that thing which they do not have, the word *mehanā* is taken to mean wealth.

### Printed p. 310

O Indra, whatever wonderful and excellent wealth you have, and whatever wealth I do not have, such wealth, O Indra bearer of the thunderbolt, bring by filling both your hands, and give it to me: this is the main intention.

The word *adri* is formed from the root आदृणाति (*ādṛṇāti*), which has the sense "to split". Because Indra splits mountains and the like, or enemies, with his thunderbolt weapon, the thunderbolt of Indra is called *adri*. Indra, who holds that thunderbolt in his hand, is addressed here by the vocative word आद्रिवः (*ādrivaḥ*). Or else the word *adri* may be formed from the root *ad*, "to eat". The stones that crush the Soma plant are called *adri*. Since they crush the Soma plant as if chewing and eating it, the stone is also called *adri*. In the sentence ते सोमादः (*te somādaḥ*) this same intention is suggested.

The word *rādhas* is formed from the root राध्नवंति (*rādhnavaṃti*), which has the sense "to pacify, to please", and indicates the meaning "wealth". For with this wealth others can be pacified or pleased, and so the meaning "*rādhas*, wealth" is given to such a thing. "O Indra, in order to give us such wealth, fill both your hands and bring it": thus Indra is entreated here.

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यन्मन्य॑से॒ वरे॑ण्य॒मिंद्र॑ द्यु॒क्षं तदा भ॑र ।  
> वि॒द्याम॒ तस्य॑ ते व॒यमकू॑पारस्य दा॒वने॑ ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यत् । मन्यसे । वरेण्यं । इंद्र । द्युक्षं । तत् । आ । भर ।  
> विद्याम । तस्य । ते । वयं । अकूपारस्य । दावने ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र यत् द्युक्षमन्नं वरेण्यं वरणीयं मन्यसे तत् द्युक्षमा
> भरास्मभ्यं । ते तव संबंधिनो वयं तस्य तादृशस्योक्तलक्षणस्याकूपारस्य ।
> अकुत्सितः पारोऽंतो यस्य तादृशस्यान्नस्य दावने दाने विद्याम । स्याम ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: O Indra, whatever *dyukṣa*, food that you consider *vareṇya*, desirable, that *dyukṣa* bring to us. We, who are yours, in the giving of such food of the stated character, *akūpāra*, that is, of which the shore or end is not bad, may we be (*vidyāma* means "may we be").

### Printed p. 311

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- यत् (*yat*)—which
- द्युक्षं (*dyukṣaṃ*)—food
- वरेण्यं (*vareṇyaṃ*)—as esteemed (excellent)
- मन्यसे (*manyase*)—you consider
- तत् (*tat*)—that food
- आ भर (*ā bhara*)—fill up and give to us
- ते (*te*)—belonging to you
- वयिं (*vayiṃ*)—we
- तस्य (*tasya*)—of such
- अकूपारस्य (*akūpārasya*)—of the food without limit
- दावने (*dāvane*)—fit for the giving
- विद्याम (*vidyāma*)—we shall be

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, whatever food you consider most esteemed, fill it up and give it to us. We, who belong to you, shall be fit to receive the gift of such food, which is unstinted and without limit.

##### English translation
Whatever food you consider desirable, Indra, bestow it: let us be to you (in the relation of your) gift of unlimited sustenance.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

दावने (*dāvane*), अकूपारस्य (*akūpārasya*)—to explain the meaning of these words Yāska has given an etymology for this Ṛk:

दावने । अकूपारस्य । विद्याम तस्य ते वयमकूपारस्य दावने । विद्याम तस्य ते वयमकुपरणस्य दानस्य ॥ आदित्योऽप्यकूपार उच्यतेऽकूपारो भवति दूरपारः ॥ समुद्रोऽप्यकूपार उच्यतेऽकूपारो भवति महा पारः ॥ कच्छपोऽप्यकूपार उच्यतेऽकूपारो न कूपमृच्छतीति ॥ कच्छपः कच्छं पाति कच्छेन पातीति वा कच्छेन पिबतीति वा ॥ कच्छः खच्छः खच्छदः । अयमपीतरो नदीकच्छ एतस्मादेव । कमु=कं तेन छाद्यते ॥ (*dāvane | akūpārasya | vidyāma tasya te vayamakūpārasya dāvane | vidyāma tasya te vayamakuparaṇasya dānasya ‖ ādityo 'pyakūpāra ucyate 'kūpāro bhavati dūrapāraḥ ‖ samudro 'pyakūpāra ucyate 'kūpāro bhavati mahā pāraḥ ‖ kacchapo 'pyakūpāra ucyate 'kūpāro na kūpamṛcchatīti ‖ kacchapaḥ kacchaṃ pāti kacchena pātīti vā kacchena pibatīti vā ‖ kacchaḥ khacchaḥ khacchadaḥ | ayamapītaro nadīkaccha etasmādeva | kamu=kaṃ tena chādyate ‖*)

Here the word दावने (*dāvane*) means दानस्य (*dānasya*), for giving (to us). The word अकूपारस्य (*akūpārasya*) has several meanings. The statement of the Nirukta authors is that अकूपारस्य (*akūpārasya*) means अकुपरणस्य (*akuparaṇasya*). What, then, is अकुपरणस्य (*akuparaṇasya*)? कु (*ku*) means contemptible, little; परण (*paraṇa*) means पूरण (*pūraṇa*), fullness, being filled; कुपरण (*kuparaṇa*) means that which is only little; अकुपरण (*akuparaṇa*) means that which is not little, that is, more, immense, endless. The word अकूपारस्य (*akūpārasya*) has the same meaning. In what contexts this meaning is used the Nirukta authors themselves explain thus:

### Printed p. 312

आदित्योऽप्यकूपार उच्यतेऽकूपारो भवति दूरपारः । (*ādityo 'pyakūpāra ucyate 'kūpāro bhavati dūrapāraḥ |*) The word अकूपार (*akūpāra*) is also taken to mean Āditya, that is, Sūrya. How? Since the sun travels from the time of rising to the time of setting over so great a distance that it cannot be measured, Āditya too is called अकूपार (*akūpāra*).

समुद्रोऽप्यकूपार उच्यतेऽकूपारो भवति महापारः । (*samudro 'pyakūpāra ucyate 'kūpāro bhavati mahāpāraḥ |*) The ocean also is called अकूपार (*akūpāra*). For it spreads over a great distance; its end is not seen near at hand. Or, if पार (*pāra*) is taken to mean bank, shore, then, as the Nirukta authors say, महापारः (*mahāpāraḥ*): the shore of the ocean is endless, extremely long. Because it has such a boundless shore, the ocean too is called अकूपार (*akūpāra*).

कच्छपोऽप्यकूपार उच्यते न कूपमृच्छति । (*kacchapo 'pyakūpāra ucyate na kūpamṛcchati |*) The tortoise also is called अकूपार (*akūpāra*). For, living in a well with little water, it has no room for much movement and stays in a small place. Here the word अकूपार (*akūpāra*) must be divided as आ+कूप+अर (*ā+kūpa+ara*) and so interpreted.

कच्छपः कच्छं पाति कच्छेन पातीति वा कच्छेन पिबतीति वा । कच्छः खच्छः खच्छदः । (*kacchapaḥ kacchaṃ pāti kacchena pātīti vā kacchena pibatīti vā | kacchaḥ khacchaḥ khacchadaḥ |*) Here the word कच्छप (*kacchapa*) has been used to denote the tortoise. Incidentally Yāska has given an etymology for this word कच्छप (*kacchapa*) also:

(1) कच्छ (*kaccha*) means the tortoise's face. कच्छं पातीति कच्छपः (*kacchaṃ pātīti kacchapaḥ*): because it specially protects its face or head, the tortoise is called कच्छप (*kacchapa*). In this sense the root पाति (*pāti*) is the root पा रक्षणे (*pā rakṣaṇe*), protect.

(2) Or कच्छेन पातीति वा कच्छपः (*kacchena pātīti vā kacchapaḥ*): in this phrase the root पाति (*pāti*) has the sense of the root पा पाने (*pā pāne*), drink. Because the tortoise drinks with its mouth (कच्छेन (*kacchena*)), it is called कच्छप (*kacchapa*).

(3) Or कच्छ (*kaccha*) means खच्छ (*khaccha*); the two words have one meaning. क (*ka*) means water, ख (*kha*) means sky, space. छादयतीति छः (*chādayatīti chaḥ*): छ (*cha*) means a covering, a cloak, a shell, and so on. Therefore कच्छप (*kacchapa*) means that which, living in the water itself, hides itself in the water, that is, plunges into the water and so protects itself; or, if the word खच्छप (*khacchapa*) is taken to mean covering, shell, then, because in time of danger it draws its head into its shell and so protects itself, the tortoise is called खच्छप (*khacchapa*) or कच्छप (*kacchapa*). **NOTE**

### Printed p. 313

(4) अयमपीतरो नदीकच्छ एतस्मादेव । कमुदकं तेन छाद्यते ॥ (*ayamapītaro nadīkaccha etasmādeva | kamudakaṃ tena chādyate ‖*) कच्छ (*kaccha*) is also the name of a river bank. Here क (*ka*) means water. च्छ (*ccha*) means छाद्यतॆ (*chādyatè*), is covered. Because the river bank is covered with water, it is called कच्छ (*kaccha*).

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यत्ते॑ दि॒त्सु प्र॒राध्यं॒ मनो॒ अस्ति॑ श्रु॒तं बृ॒हत् ।  
> तेन॑ दृ॒ळ्ता चि॑दद्रिव॒ आ वाजं॑ दर्षि सा॒तये॑ ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यत् । ते । दित्सु । प्रराध्यं। मनः । अस्ति । श्रुतं । बृहत् ।  
> तेन । दृळ्हा । चित् । अद्रिवः । आ । वाजं । दर्षि । सातये ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> वाजपेयेऽतिरिक्तोक्के यत्ते दित्त्वत्येषा । सूत्रितं च । यत्ते दित्तु
> प्रराध्यं त्वामिच्छवसस्पते । आ- ९-९ । इति ॥
> हे इंद्र ते तव यद्दित्सु दानेच्छु प्रराध्यं प्रकर्षण स्तुत्यं श्रुतं विश्रुतं
> बृहन्महन्मनोऽस्ति तेन मनसा हे अद्रिवो वज्रवन्निंद्र दृळ्हा चिद्दृढमपि वाजमन्नमा दर्षि । आदरयसि सातयेऽस्मत्संभजनाय लाभाय ना ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says that at the Vājapeya, in the supplementary utterance (atirikta-ukta), this Ṛk is used; **NOTE** and the sūtra says: the Ṛk beginning त्वामिच्छवसस्पते (*tvāmicchavasaspate*) (Ā. 9-9). He then explains: O Indra, that mind of yours which is desirous of giving (दित्सु (*ditsu*)), most highly praiseworthy (प्रराध्यं (*prarādhyaṃ*), praised in the highest degree), renowned (श्रुतं (*śrutaṃ*)), great (बृहत् (*bṛhat*)) — with that mind, O wielder of the thunderbolt (अद्रिवः (*adrivaḥ*), Indra who has the vajra), you bring (आ दर्षि (*ā darṣi*), you cause to be honoured) even the firm (दृळ्हा चित् (*dṛḷhā cit*), firm indeed) vāja, food, for सातये (*sātaye*), that is, for our sharing in it, for gain. **NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (O Indra) ते (*te*)—your
- दित्सु (*ditsu*)—having the intention of giving
- प्रराध्यं (*prarādhyaṃ*)—to be praised exceedingly
- श्रुतं (*śrutaṃ*)—renowned
- बृहत् (*bṛhat*)—of great nature
- यत् (*yat*)—which
- मनः (*manaḥ*)—mind
- अस्ति (*asti*)—there is
- तेन (*tena*)—with that mind
- अद्रिवः (*adrivaḥ*)—O Indra, bearer of the thunderbolt
- दृळ्ता चित् (*dṛḷtā cit*)—firm and nourishing
- वाजं (*vājaṃ*)—food
- सातये (*sātaye*)—for giving to us
- आ दर्षि (*ā darṣi*)—prepare and make ready

### Printed p. 314

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, bearer of the thunderbolt, with that mind of yours which has the resolve to give, which is to be praised exceedingly, renowned and of great nature, make ready that firm and nourishing food, so as to give it to us.

##### English translation
Since your bountiful and praiseworthy will is famous and vastl therefore, thunderer, you have got ready for bestowing upon us substantia, food. **NOTE**

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

It is settled by the Āśvalāyana-śrauta-sūtra, in the sūtra यत्ते दित्सु प्रराध्यं त्वामिच्छवसस्पते (*yatte ditsu prarādhyaṃ tvāmicchavasaspate*) (Ā. 9-9), that in the atirikta-ukta connected with the Vājapeya this Ṛk beginning यत्ते दित्सु (*yatte ditsu*) is to be recited.

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> मंहि॑ष्ठं वो म॒घोनां॒ राजा॑नं चर्षणी॒नां ।  
> • इंद्र॒मुप॒ प्रश॑स्तये पू॒र्विभि॑र्जुजुषे॒ गिरः॑ ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> मुंहिष्ठं । वः । मघोनां । राजानं । चर्षणीनां ।  
> इंद्रं । उप । प्रशस्तये। पूर्विभिः । जुजुषे । गिरः ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> मघोनां हविर्लक्षणधनवतां वो युष्माकं मंहिष्ठमतिशयेन पूज्यं
> चर्षणीनां मनुष्याहां राजानमिंद्रमुपोपेत्य प्रशस्तये प्रशंसितुं पूर्वीभिः
> पुरातनीभिर्वाग्निर्गिरः स्तोतारो जुजुषे । सेवंते ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: मघोनां (*maghonāṃ*) means of those who have wealth in the form of oblations; वः (*vaḥ*) means of you. मंहिष्ठं (*maṃhiṣṭhaṃ*) means worthy of worship in the highest degree. चर्षणीनां (*carṣaṇīnāṃ*) means of men. Approaching Indra, the king of men, the praisers (गिरः (*giraḥ*)) serve him (जुजुषे (*jujuṣe*), सेवंते (*sevaṃte*)) with ancient words, in order to praise him (प्रशस्तये (*praśastaye*), प्रशंसितुं (*praśaṃsituṃ*)).

### Printed p. 315

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- मघोनां (*maghonāṃ*)—of you who have wealth in the form of oblations
- वः (*vaḥ*)—among you
- मंहिष्ठं (*maṃhiṣṭhaṃ*)—most worthy of worship
- चर्षणीनां (*carṣaṇīnāṃ*)—of all men
- राजानं (*rājānaṃ*)—and lord
- इंद्रं (*iṃdraṃ*)—Indra
- उप (*upa*)—approach
- प्रशस्तये (*praśastaye*)—in order to praise him
- पूर्विभिः (*pūrvibhiḥ*)—with ancient praises
- गिरः (*giraḥ*)—the praisers
- जुजुषे (*jujuṣe*)—serve (praise)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The praisers approach Indra, who among you that have wealth in the form of oblations is most worthy of worship and is the lord of all men, praise him with ancient praises, and serve him.

##### English translation
With ancient (hymns) the pious approach Indra, to laud him who is most worthy of the beverage (the offering) of you who are opulent ; ( him ) who is the king of men.

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आस्मा॒ इत्याव्यं॒ वच॑ उ॒क्षमिंद्रा॑य॒ शंस्यं॑ ।  
> तस्मा॑ उ॒ ब्रह्म॑वाहसे॒ गिरो॑ वर्धं॒त्यत्र॑यो॒ गिरः॑ शुंभं॒त्यत्र॑यः ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अस्मै । इत् । काव्यं । वचः । उक्धं । इंद्राय । शंस्यं ।  
> तस्मैः । ऊं इति । ब्रह्मवाहसे । गिरः । वर्धंति । अत्रयः । गिरः । शुंभंति ।  
> अत्रयः ॥ ५ ॥

### Printed p. 316

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अस्मा इदस्मा एनेंद्राय काव्यं कवेः स्तोतुः संबंधि । यद्वा । कु शब्दे ।
> शब्दनीयं वचो वाग्रूपमुक्थं शस्त्रं शंस्यं शंसनीयं । यस्मादेवं तस्मात्तस्मा
> उ तस्मा इंद्रायैव ब्रह्मवाहसे परिवृढस्य स्तोत्रस्य वाहकाय गिरः स्तुतीरत्रयोऽत्रिगोत्रा वर्धंति । वर्धयंति । अत्रयो गिरः शुंभंति । दीपयंति । पुन-

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: अस्मा इत् (*asmā it*) means to this Indra alone; काव्यं (*kāvyaṃ*) means belonging to the poet, the praiser. Or else (यद्वा (*yadvā*)): from the root कु (*ku*) in the sense of sound, it means that which is to be sounded. वचः (*vacaḥ*) is speech, *uktha* is the śastra, शंस्यं (*śaṃsyaṃ*) is to be recited. Since this is so, therefore to him, to Indra alone, who is ब्रह्मवाहसे (*brahmavāhase*), the bearer of the all-embracing (परिवृढस्य (*parivṛḍhasya*)) hymn, the Atris, those of Atri's family, increase (वर्धंति (*vardhaṃti*), cause to grow) their praises; the Atris adorn (शुंभंति (*śuṃbhaṃti*), make shine) their praises. The repetition **NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अस्मा इत् इंद्राय (*asmā it iṃdrāya*)—for this Indra alone
- काव्यं (*kāvyaṃ*)—of the nature of poetry
- वचः (*vacaḥ*)—in the form of speech
- उक्तं (*uktaṃ*)—the śastra mantra
- शंस्यं (*śaṃsyaṃ*)—to be recited
- ब्रह्मवाहसे (*brahmavāhase*)—who accepts praise
- तस्मा उ (*tasmā u*)—for that very Indra
- गिरः (*giraḥ*)—praises
- अत्रयः (*atrayaḥ*)—the seers born in the family of Atri
- वधंति (*vadhaṃti*)—increase
- अत्रयः (*atrayaḥ*)—those same sons of Atri
- गिरः (*giraḥ*)—praises
- शुंभंति (*śuṃbhaṃti*)—make shine (recite)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The śastra mantra, poetic and in the form of speech, is to be recited for this Indra alone. For that Indra, who accepts praise, the seers born in the family of Atri increase the praises. Those same sons of Atri make the praises shine (recite them).

##### English translation
To that Indra is the poetical and articulate prayer to be recited: to him the conveyers of pious praise, the sons of Atri, raise their hymns: the sons of Arti illumine their hymns.

## Sūkta 40 (Maṇḍala 5)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> आ याह्यद्रिभिरिति नवर्चमष्टमं सूक्तं । अत्रेयमनुक्रमणिका । आ याहि
> नव त्र्युष्टिगाद्यंत्यानुष्टुम्मध्ये च सा सौरी तदादीतिहासः स्तुतिकर्मत्वादत्रिर्देवतैवेति । कृत्सस्य ऋषिश्चान्यस्मात् । अनु. १२-२ । इति परिभाषयात्रिर्यषिः ।

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says that the eighth sūkta, beginning आ याह्यद्रिभिरिति (*ā yāhyadribhiriti*), has nine Ṛks. The Anukramaṇikā says: its seer is Atri, of the Atri family; the metre of the nine Ṛks is Triṣṭubh, with the first and last Anuṣṭubh and, in the middle, one more, that is, the Sūrya Ṛk; the history that begins with it, and since the sūkta is a praise (stuti-karman), the deity is Atri. **NOTE** By the rule cited (Anu. 12-2) Atri is the seer.

### Printed p. 317

> आद्यास्तिस्र उष्णिहः पंचमीनवम्यावनुष्टुभौ शिष्टाश्चतस्रस्त्रिष्टुभः । अत्रेः स्तूयमानत्वात् षष्ठ्याद्या अत्रिदेवताका अत्यार्षेयाश्च पंचमी सूर्यदेवत्या
> शिष्टा ऐंद्रः ॥ दशरात्रे तृतीयेहनि प्रउगशस्त्र आद्यस्तृच ऐंद्रः पंचमः ।
> सूत्रितं च । आ याह्र्यद्रिभिः सुतं सजूर्विश्वेभिर्देवेभिः । आ. ७-१० । इति ॥ महाव्रतेऽप्यौषिहतृचाशीतावयं तृचः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa continues with the Anukramaṇikā: the first three Ṛks are Uṣṇih, the fifth and the ninth are Anuṣṭubh, and the remaining four are Triṣṭubh. Because Atri is praised in them, the Ṛks from the sixth onward have Atri as their deity and are Ātreya; the fifth has Sūrya as its deity, and the rest are Aindra (addressed to Indra). **NOTE** Then he gives the ritual use: on the third day of the Daśarātra, in the Prauga-śastra, the first tṛca is the fifth, Aindra. The sūtra says: the Ṛk beginning आ याह्र्यद्रिभिः (*ā yāhryadribhiḥ*) (Ā. 7-10). At the Mahāvrata also the tṛca, **NOTE**

Anuvāda: This sūkta आ याह्यद्रिभिः (*ā yāhyadribhiḥ*) is the eighth sūkta in the third anuvāka. It has nine Ṛks. In the Anukramaṇikā it is said: आ याहि नव त्रुष्टिगाद्यंत्यानुष्टुम्मध्यॆ च सा सौरी तदादीतिहासः स्तुतिकर्मत्वादत्रिर्देवतैव (*ā yāhi nava truṣṭigādyaṃtyānuṣṭummadhyè ca sā saurī tadādītihāsaḥ stutikarmatvādatrirdevataiva*). Atri is the seer of this sūkta; the first three Ṛks of this sūkta are in the Uṣṇik metre; the fifth and the ninth Ṛks are in the Anuṣṭup metre; the remaining four Ṛks are in the Triṣṭup metre. In the four Ṛks from the sixth to the ninth of this sūkta there is praise of the seer Atri; therefore Atri is the deity of those four Ṛks, and the seer of those Ṛks also is Atri himself. The fifth Ṛk has Sūrya as its deity. In the sacrifice called Daśarātra, on the third day, at the time of reciting the Prauga-śastra mantra, the first three Ṛks of this sūkta are to be recited as the fifth tṛca (tṛca means three Ṛks), relating to Indra; this is explained by the sūtra आ याह्यद्रिभिः सुतं सजूर्विश्वेभिर्देवेभिः (*ā yāhyadribhiḥ sutaṃ sajūrviśvebhirdevebhiḥ*) (Ā. 7-10) of the Āśvalāyana-śrauta-sūtra. In the sacrifice called Mahāvrata also, the first three Ṛks of this sūkta are employed for mantras in the Uṣṇik metre.

- Sūkta 40
- Maṇḍala 5, Anuvāka 3, Sūkta 40
- Aṣṭaka 4, Adhyāya 2, Varga 12, 13
- Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 9
- Glory (mahimā): आत्म (*ātma*)
- Deity: Ṛks 1-4 इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*); 5 सूर्यः (*sūryaḥ*); 6-9 अत्रिः (*atriḥ*)
- Metre: Ṛks 1-3 उष्णिक् (*uṣṇik*); 4, 6-8 त्रिष्टुप् (*triṣṭup*); 5-9 अनुष्टुप् (*anuṣṭup*) **NOTE**

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आ या॒ह्यद्रि॑भिः सु॒तं सोमं॑ सोमपते पिब ।  
> वृष॑न्निंद्र॒ वृष॑भिर्वृत्रहन्तम ॥ १ ॥

### Printed p. 318

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आ । याहि । अद्रिभिः । सुतं । सोमं । सोमपते । पिब ।  
> वृषन् । इंद्र । वृषभिः । वृत्रहन्तम ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र आ याहि । अस्मद्यज्ञमागच्छ ; आगत्य च हे सोमपते सोमस्य
> स्वामिन्निंद्र अद्रिभिग्रावभिः सुतनुभिषुतं सोमं पिब । हे वृषन् फलस्य वर्षयितर्वृत्रहंतमातिशयेन शत्रुणां हंतृतम वृषभिर्वर्षकैर्मरुद्भिः सहा याहि
> पिब च ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: O Indra, come (आ याहि (*ā yāhi*)): come to our sacrifice; and having come, O lord of Soma (सोमपते (*somapate*), master of Soma), drink the Soma pressed with the stones (अद्रिभिः (*adribhiḥ*), the pressing stones). O showerer (वृषन् (*vṛṣan*), one who rains down the fruit), O foremost slayer of Vṛtra (वृत्रहंतम (*vṛtrahaṃtama*), one who is exceedingly the destroyer of enemies), come and drink together with the showerers (वृषभिः (*vṛṣabhiḥ*)), the Maruts, who rain.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- आ याहि (*ā yāhi*)—(to our sacrifice) deign to come
- सोमपते (*somapate*)—O Indra, lord of Soma
- अद्रिभिः (*adribhiḥ*)—(crushed) with stones
- सुतुं (*sutuṃ*)—pressed
- सोमं (*somaṃ*)—the Soma juice
- पिब (*piba*)—drink
- वृषन् (*vṛṣan*)—bestower of what is wished
- वृत्रहंतम (*vṛtrahaṃtama*)—O Indra, who destroy Vṛtra completely
- वृषभिः (*vṛṣabhiḥ*)—with the Maruts, who shower what is desired (deign to come to the sacrifice)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, bestower of what is wished, who destroy Vṛtra completely, you, as lord of Soma, drink the Soma juice that has been crushed and pressed with stones.

##### English translation
Come, Indra, (to our sacrifice) ; drink, lord of the Soma, of the juice expressed by the stones : showerer (of benefits). utter destroyer of Vritra, (come) with the showering (Maruts).

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> वृषा॒ ग्रावा॒ वृषा॒ मदो॒ वृषा॒ सोमो॑ अ॒यं सु॒तः ।  
> वृष॑न्निंद्र॒ वृष॑भिवृत्रहस्तम ॥ २ ॥

### Printed p. 319

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> वृषा । ग्रावा । वृषा । मदः । वृषा । सोमः । अयं । सुतः ।  
> वृषन् । इंद्र । वृषभिः । वृत्रहन्तम ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ग्रावाभिषवसाधनः पाषाणो वृषा वर्षकः सोमरसस्य फलस्य ना । मदः
> सोमपानेन जनितो वृषा वर्षकः । अयं सुतोऽभिषुतः सोमो वृषा । तं
> सोमं वृषभिः सह पिब ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: ग्रावा (*grāvā*) is the stone, the instrument of pressing; it is वृषा (*vṛṣā*), the showerer, of the Soma juice and of the fruit. **NOTE** The exhilaration (मदः (*madaḥ*)) produced by drinking Soma is वृषा (*vṛṣā*), a showerer. This pressed Soma is वृषा (*vṛṣā*). Drink that Soma together with the showerers (वृषभिः (*vṛṣabhiḥ*)).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- ग्रावा (*grāvā*)—the stone that presses the Soma
- वृषा (*vṛṣā*)—that which showers desires
- मदः (*madaḥ*)—the joy arising from drinking Soma
- वृषा (*vṛṣā*)—that which bestows what is wished
- अयं (*ayaṃ*)—here present
- सुतः (*sutaḥ*)—pressed
- सोमः (*somaḥ*)—the Soma
- वृषा (*vṛṣā*)—that which showers desires
- वृषन् (*vṛṣan*)—bestower of what is wished
- वृत्रहंतनु (*vṛtrahaṃtanu*)—and destroyer of Vṛtra completely
- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- वृषभिः (*vṛṣabhiḥ*)—with the Maruts (drink)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The stone that presses the Soma is a showerer of desires. The joy arising from drinking Soma is a bestower of what is wished. The Soma that has been pressed and made ready here is something that gives what is desired. May Indra, the bestower of what is wished and complete destroyer of Vṛtra, drink it together with the Maruts.

##### English translation
The stone is the showerer, the inebriation is the showerer, this effused Soma is the showerer; showerer (of benefits), Indra, utter destroyer of Vritra, come with the showering (Maruts).

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> वृषा॑ त्वा॒ वृष॑णं हुवे॒ वज्रन्॑ इ॒त्राभि॑रू॒तिभिः॑ ।  
> वृष॑न्निंद्र॒ वृष॑भि र्वृत्रहस्तम ॥ ३ ॥

### Printed p. 320

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> वृषा । त्वा । वृषणं । हुवे । वज्रन् । चित्राभिः । ऊतिभिः ।  
> वृषन् । इंद्र । वृषभिः । वृत्रहन्तम ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे वज्रिन् वज्रवन्निंद वृषा सोमरसस्य सेक्ताहं वृषणं त्वां हुवे ।
> आह्वयामि । किमर्थं । चित्राभिश्चायनीयाभिरूतिभी रक्षाभिर्निमित्तभूताभिः ।
> शिष्टं गतं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: O wielder of the thunderbolt, Indra, I, who am वृषा (*vṛṣā*), the pourer of the Soma juice, call (हुवे (*huve*), invoke) you, the showerer. For what purpose? With wonderful (चित्राभिः (*citrābhiḥ*)) protections (ऊतिभिः (*ūtibhiḥ*)) as the occasion. The rest has already been explained.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वज्रिन् (*vajrin*)—bearer of the thunderbolt
- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- वृषा (*vṛṣā*)—I, who press the Soma juice
- वृषणं (*vṛṣaṇaṃ*)—who shower desires
- त्वा (*tvā*)—you
- चित्राभिः (*citrābhiḥ*)—of many kinds
- ऊतिभिः (*ūtibhiḥ*)—for the sake of protections
- हुवे (*huve*)—I call
- वृषन् (*vṛṣan*)—bestower of what is wished
- वृत्रहंतम (*vṛtrahaṃtama*)—O Indra, who destroy Vṛtra completely
- वृषभिः (*vṛṣabhiḥ*)—with the Maruts (deign to drink the Soma)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, who hold the thunderbolt, I, who press the Soma juice, call you, the shower of desires, for the sake of protections of many kinds. O Indra, bestower of what is wished and destroyer of Vṛtra, deign to drink the Soma together with the Maruts, who bestow what is wished.

##### English translation
Effusing the libation, I invoke you, the showerer (of benefits), for your marvellous protections : showerer (of benefits), Indra, utter destroyer of Vritra, come with the showering (Maruts).

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ऋ॒जी॒षी व॒ज्रे वृ॑ष॒भस्तु॑रा॒षाट्च्यु॒ष्मी राजा॑ वृत्र॒हा सो॑म॒सावा॑ ।

### Printed p. 321

> युक्त्वा हरिभ्यामुप यासदर्वाज्माध्यंदिने सवने मत्सदिंद्रः ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> ऋजीषी । वज्री । वृषभः । तुराषाट् । शुष्मी । राजा । वृत्रहा । सोमपावा ।  
> युक्त्वा । हरिभ्यां । उप । यासत् । अर्वाज् । माध्यंदिने । सवने । मत्सत् ।  
> इंद्रः . ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ऋजीषी वज्रीत्येषा ब्राह्मणाच्छंसिनः शस्त्रयाज्या । ऋजीषी वज्री
> वृषभस्तुराषाळिति याज्या ! आ. ५. १६. । इति हि सूत्रितं ॥
> ऋजीषी । सवनद्वयेऽभिषुतस्य गतसारस्य सोमरसस्य तृतीयसवन
> आप्यायाभिसुतो योऽस्ति स ऋजीषः सोमः सोऽस्त्यास्तीत्यजीषी । वज्री
> वज्रवान् वृषभो वर्षिता तुराषाट् तुराणां त्वरमाणानां शत्रूणां सोढा
> शुष्मी बलवान् राजेश्वरः सर्वस्य वृत्रहा वृत्रस्य हंता सोमपावा सोमरसस
> पाता एवं महानुभाव इंद्रो हरिभ्यां युक्त्वा रथं योजयित्वोप यासत् ।
> उपगच्छतु । अर्वाजिस्मदभिमुखनागच्छतु । माध्यंदिने सवने मत्सत् माड्यतु
> सोमेनेंद्रः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says that this Ṛk ऋजीषी वज्री (*ṛjīṣī vajrī*) is the śastra-yājyā of the Brāhmaṇācchaṃsin. For the sūtra says: ऋजीषी वज्री वृषभस्तुराषाळिति याज्या (*ṛjīṣī vajrī vṛṣabhasturāṣāḷiti yājyā*) (Ā. 5. 16.). Then he explains ऋजीषी (*ṛjīṣī*): of the Soma juice pressed at the two savanas and its essence gone, that which is pressed again at the third savana is ऋजीषः (*ṛjīṣaḥ*) Soma; he who has it is ऋजीषी (*ṛjīṣī*). वज्री (*vajrī*) is the possessor of the vajra; वृषभः (*vṛṣabhaḥ*) is the rainer; तुराषाट् (*turāṣāṭ*) is the overcomer of the hastening enemies; शुष्मी (*śuṣmī*) is the mighty; राजा (*rājā*) is the lord of all; वृत्रहा (*vṛtrahā*) is the slayer of Vṛtra; सोमपावा (*somapāvā*) is the drinker of the Soma juice. May such a mighty Indra, having yoked his chariot to his two bay horses, come near (उप यासत् (*upa yāsat*)), come toward us (अर्वाज् (*arvāj*)), and be exhilarated by Soma at the midday savana (मत्सत् (*matsat*)).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- ऋजीषी (*ṛjīṣī*)—who accepts the Soma that is ṛjīṣa
- वज्री (*vajrī*)—who holds the thunderbolt
- वृषभः (*vṛṣabhaḥ*)—who causes the shedding of rain
- तुराषाट् (*turāṣāṭ*)—who has the power to face enemies
- शुष्मी (*śuṣmī*)—the mighty
- राजा (*rājā*)—the lord
- वृत्रहा (*vṛtrahā*)—the slayer of Vṛtra
- सोमपावा (*somapāvā*)—and the drinker of the Soma juice
- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra
- अर्वाज् (*arvāj*)—toward us
- उष्य यासत् (*uṣya yāsat*)—may he deign to come
- माध्यंदिने सवने (*mādhyaṃdine savane*)—at the time of the midday savana
- मत्सत् (*matsat*)—may he be delighted (by drinking Soma)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

May Indra, who accepts the Soma that is ṛjīṣa, holds the thunderbolt, causes the shedding of rain, has the power to face enemies, is mighty, lord, slayer of Vṛtra and drinker of the Soma juice, deign to come toward us. May he be delighted by drinking Soma at the time of the midday savana.

### Printed p. 322

##### English translation
May the accepter of the spiritless libation, the wielder of the thunderbolt, the showerer ( of benefits ), the overcomer of quick-(flying foes), the mighty, the monarch, the slayer of Vritra, the drinker of the Soma, having harnessed his horses, come down (to us) : may Indra be exhilarated at the mid-day sacrifice.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

The Brāhmaṇācchaṃsin priest is to recite this Ṛk ऋजीषी वज्री (*ṛjīṣī vajrī*) as the śastra-yājyā mantra; this is explained by the sūtra ऋजीषी वज्री वृषभस्तुराषाळिति याज्या (*ṛjīṣī vajrī vṛṣabhasturāṣāḷiti yājyā*) (Ā. 5-16) of the Āśvalāyana-śrauta-sūtra.

यजीषी (*yajīṣī*) **NOTE**—Yāska has given this etymology of the word:

ऋजिषी सोमो यत्सोमस्य पूयमानस्यातिरिच्यते तदृजीषमपार्जितं भवति तेनर्जिषी सोमः। अथाप्यैंद्रो निगमो भवति ऋजिषी वज्रे इति॥ (*ṛjiṣī somo yatsomasya pūyamānasyātiricyate tadṛjīṣamapārjitaṃ bhavati tenarjiṣī somaḥ| athāpyaiṃdro nigamo bhavati ṛjiṣī vajre iti‖*)

The sapless matter, like husk, which remains after the Soma plants have been crushed with stones and the juice taken out, is called ऋजीष (*ṛjīṣa*). This ऋजीष (*ṛjīṣa*) is thrown away as not wanted. In some cases such ऋजीष (*ṛjīṣa*) is soaked in water and a little more juice is pressed out of it. That Soma juice is called ऋजीषः सोमः (*ṛjīṣaḥ somaḥ*). At the third savana flour and other mixing substances (āśira) are mixed with such ṛjīṣa Soma and offered to Indra. Because Indra drinks this ṛjīṣa Soma, it has become customary to call Indra by the word ऋजीषी (*ṛjīṣī*) itself. Besides this, it is well known that Indra's horses, called Hari, eat the husk of these Soma plants called ṛjīṣa. On this matter Yāska has quoted, as an example, the Vedic sentence बद्धां ते हरीधाना उप ऋजीषं जिघ्रतां (*baddhāṃ te harīdhānā upa ṛjīṣaṃ jighratāṃ*). The meaning of the above sentence is: O Indra, may your horses eat the grain and smell this ṛjīṣa.

What is ṛjīṣa? What is the reason that it has this name? What is its use in the sacrifice? We shall examine closely here what is said on these matters in the Aitareya Brāhmaṇa and the Taittirīya Saṃhitā:

सोमो वै राजामुष्मिंल्लोक आसीत्तं देवाश्च ऋषयश्चाभ्यध्यायन् कथमयमस्मात्सोमो राजागच्छेदिति तेऽब्रुवंश्चंदांसि यूयं न (*somo vai rājāmuṣmiṃlloka āsīttaṃ devāśca ṛṣayaścābhyadhyāyan kathamayamasmātsomo rājāgacchediti te 'bruvaṃścaṃdāṃsi yūyaṃ na*)

### Printed p. 323

इमं सोमं राजानमाहरेति तथेति ते सुपर्णा भूत्वोदपतन् (*imaṃ somaṃ rājānamāhareti tatheti te suparṇā bhūtvodapatan*) (Ai. Brā. 13-25)

In former times the Soma-rāja (the Soma plant; it is the custom to call the Soma plant out of respect Soma-rāja), that is, the Soma plant, was in the heavenly world. It was not in this world. Knowing this, the gods and the seers considered in what way this Soma could be brought here. Then, having deliberated on this matter, they understood that the metres had the power to bring the Soma from heaven to the earth, and they addressed the metres in prayer: "O metres, for us, that is, for use in our sacrifice, you must bring to the earth the Soma that is in heaven." Accepting their prayer, the metres took the forms of certain birds well known in the world and flew off to the heavenly world. First the Jagatī metre took the form of a bird and flew toward the heavenly world. But before it had gone a little way it became tired, could not go as far as heaven, and turned back. Then the Triṣṭup metre said that it would bring the Soma and leapt into the sky. But before it had gone a little beyond half the way it was worn out with fatigue and turned back. Thus the gods saw that the Jagatī and Triṣṭup metres had come back, unable to bring the Soma, and going to the Gāyatrī metre they prayed: "O Gāyatrī, you must bring the Soma from heaven for us." Gāyatrī agreed, "So be it," took the form of a śyena (falcon), flew to heaven, frightened the guardians of the Soma there, Sthāna, Bhrāja and others who were guarding the Soma, carried off the Soma and brought it to the earth. **NOTE** Because she brought the Soma plant, Gāyatrī obtained the most excellent place among the metres.
