सा यद्दक्षिणेन पदा समग्रभ्वात्तत्प्रातःसवनमभवत्तद्गायत्री स्वमायतनम कुरुत तस्मात्तत्समृद्धतमं मन्यंते सर्वेषां सवनानामग्रियो मुख्यो भवति श्रेष्ठतामश्नुते य एवं वेदाथ यत्सव्येन पदा समगृभ्वात्तन्माध्यंदिनं सवनमभवत्तद्विस्रंसत तद्विप्रस्तं नान्वाप्पोत्तूर्वं सवनं ते देवाः प्राजिज्ञासंत तस्मिंस्त्रिष्टुभं छंदसामदधुरिंद्रं देवतानां तेन तत्समावद्वीर्यमभवत्तूर्वेण सवनेनोभाभ्यां सवनाभ्यां समावद्वीर्याभ्यां समावज्जामीभ्यां राध्योति य एवं वेदाथ यन्नुखेन समगृभ्वात्तत्सृतीयसवनमभवत् । (*sā yaddakṣiṇena padā samagrabhvāttatprātaḥsavanamabhavattadgāyatrī svamāyatanama kuruta tasmāttatsamṛddhatamaṃ manyaṃte sarveṣāṃ savanānāmagriyo mukhyo bhavati śreṣṭhatāmaśnute ya evaṃ vedātha yatsavyena padā samagṛbhvāttanmādhyaṃdinaṃ savanamabhavattadvisraṃsata tadviprastaṃ nānvāppottūrvaṃ savanaṃ te devāḥ prājijñāsaṃta tasmiṃstriṣṭubhaṃ chaṃdasāmadadhuriṃdraṃ devatānāṃ tena tatsamāvadvīryamabhavattūrveṇa savanenobhābhyāṃ savanābhyāṃ samāvadvīryābhyāṃ samāvajjāmībhyāṃ rādhyoti ya evaṃ vedātha yannukhena samagṛbhvāttatsṛtīyasavanamabhavat |*) (Ai. Brā. 13-26)

When bringing the Soma plants, the śyena bird carried them gripping them with both its feet and with its beak. The Soma (or the Soma plant) that the bird held with its right foot and brought became the morning savana. Gāyatrī made it, that is, the morning savana, her own place.

### Printed p. 324

Therefore, among all the savanas the morning savana is the best, in the opinion of the sacrificers. In that morning-savana rite the application of the mantras and the rest is also special. Therefore a man who knows this matter well is regarded as the best and chief among his equals. The portion of Soma that Gāyatrī held with her left foot and brought became famous as the midday savana. While carrying the Soma plant with the left foot, since the left foot did not have as much strength as the right foot, the plants kept slipping a little from the foot now and then. For this reason this midday savana did not become equal to the morning savana in strength and capacity. Seeing this, the gods deliberated and established the Triṣṭup metre and Indra, the best among the gods, for the metres used in the midday-savana rite. Through the association of Indra, the best among the gods, and of the Triṣṭup metre dear to him, the midday savana obtained a place equal to the morning savana in strength, capacity and excellence. The man who knows this equality of the morning and midday savanas, and their strength, capacity and excellence, obtains prosperity in wealth and the rest, and well-being, and lives happily. Then the portion of Soma that Gāyatrī, in the form of the śyena, bit with her beak and brought became famous as the third savana.

तस्य पतंती रसमधयत्तद्वीतरसं नान्वाप्पोत्पूर्वे सवने ते देवाः प्राजिज्ञासंत तत्पशुष्पपश्यंस्तद्यदाशिरमवनयंत्याज्येन पशुना चरंति तेन तत्समावद्वीर्यमभवत्पूर्वाभ्यां सवनाभ्यां सर्वैः सवनैः समावद्वीर्यैः समावज्जा मिभी राध्योति य एवं वेद ॥ (*tasya pataṃtī rasamadhayattadvītarasaṃ nānvāppotpūrve savane te devāḥ prājijñāsaṃta tatpaśuṣpapaśyaṃstadyadāśiramavanayaṃtyājyena paśunā caraṃti tena tatsamāvadvīryamabhavatpūrvābhyāṃ savanābhyāṃ sarvaiḥ savanaiḥ samāvadvīryaiḥ samāvajjā mibhī rādhyoti ya evaṃ veda ‖*) (I. Bra. 13-17) **NOTE**

While Gāyatrī (the śyena) was coming, holding the Soma plant in her beak, she sipped the juice that was in the plant little by little. Therefore, that is, because the juice in the Soma plants brought in the beak was not abundant but scanty, this third savana did not become equal in strength, capacity and the rest to the morning and midday savanas. Seeing this, the gods deliberated and found that the means suited to it lay in animals. From animals come milk, and from it the oblation materials such as ājya (ghee). If milk is mixed with the Soma juice that is scanty in the plants, that juice becomes powerful. If homas and the rest are performed with ājya and with the limbs of animals, the savana rite becomes complete. Therefore those who know the sacrificial rite, to the ṛjīṣa used in the third savana (the husks of the Soma plant that remain after the juice has been taken out in the morning and midday savanas are called ṛjīṣa) add āśira substances such as milk, and by performing homas with the ājya obtained from animals and with the heart and the other

### Printed p. 325

parts of animals, understand that the third savana rite becomes equal to the morning and midday savanas in strength, capacity and the rest, and act like them. This matter is stated in another śākhā thus:

ब्रह्मवादिनो वदंति कस्मात्सत्याद्दायत्री कनिष्ठा छंदसाग्ं सती यज्ञमुखं परीयायेति यदेवादः सोममाहरत्तस्माद्यज्ञमुखं पर्येत्तस्मा त्तेजस्विनीतमा पद्भ्यां द्वे सवने समगृह्णान्मुखेनैकं यन्मुखेन समगृह्णात्तदधयत्तस्माद्वे सवने शुक्रवती प्रातस्सवनं च माध्यंदिनं च तस्मात्तृतीयसवन ऋजीषमभिषुण्वंति धीतमिव हि मन्यंते आशिरमवनयति सशुक्रत्वाय ॥ (*brahmavādino vadaṃti kasmātsatyāddāyatrī kaniṣṭhā chaṃdasāgṃ satī yajñamukhaṃ parīyāyeti yadevādaḥ somamāharattasmādyajñamukhaṃ paryettasmā ttejasvinītamā padbhyāṃ dve savane samagṛhṇānmukhenaikaṃ yanmukhena samagṛhṇāttadadhayattasmādve savane śukravatī prātassavanaṃ ca mādhyaṃdinaṃ ca tasmāttṛtīyasavana ṛjīṣamabhiṣuṇvaṃti dhītamiva hi manyaṃte āśiramavanayati saśukratvāya ‖*) (Śrī. Saṃ. 6-1-6-4) **NOTE**

The same view is stated in the Taittirīya Saṃhitā. The man who knows this matter, that is, that the three savanas, the morning savana, the midday savana and the third savana, have obtained places equal in strength, capacity and excellence, lives happily, endowed with every prosperity.

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यत्त्वा॑ सूर्य॒ स्व॑र्भानु॒स्तम॒सावि॑ध्यदासु॒रः ।  
> अक्षे॑त्रवि॒द्यथा॑ मु॒ग्धो भुव॑नान्यदीधयुः ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यत् । त्वा । सूर्य । स्वःभानुः । तमसा । अविध्यत् । आसुरः ।  
> अक्षेत्रवित् । यथा । मुग्धः । भुवनानि । अदीधयुः ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> इदमादिचतुर्भिर्मंत्रैरत्रीणां कर्म कीर्त्यते । हे सूर्य प्रेरक देव
> त्वा त्वां यद्यदासुरोऽसुरस्य प्राणापहर्तुरसुर्या वा पुत्रः स्वर्भानुः स्वर्भानुसंज्ञकस्तमसा मायानिमितेनाविध्यत् । आवृणोदित्यर्थः । तदा भुवनानि सर्वाण्यदीधयुः । दृश्यंते । यथा तत्रत्यः सर्वो जनोऽक्षेत्रवित् स्वस्वस्थानमजानन्
> मुग्धो मूढो भवति तथा ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says that with this and the three following mantras (four in all) the deed of the Atris is proclaimed. O Sūrya, impelling god, when स्वर्भानुः (*svarbhānuḥ*), the Āsura, that is, the son of an asura, a taker of life, or else the son of Asurī, who bears the name Svarbhānu, pierced you (अविध्यत् (*avidhyat*), that is, covered you) with darkness produced by magic (māyā), then all the worlds (भुवनानि (*bhuvanāni*)) are seen (अदीधयुः (*adīdhayuḥ*), they appear), just as all the people there, not knowing their own places (अक्षेत्रवित् (*akṣetravit*)), become bewildered (मुग्धः (*mugdhaḥ*), that is, stupefied).

### Printed p. 326

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- सूर्य (*sūrya*)—O god Sūrya
- त्वा (*tvā*)—you
- यत् (*yat*)—when
- आसुरः (*āsuraḥ*)—the son of an asura
- स्वर्भानुः (*svarbhānuḥ*)—Svarbhānu
- तमसा (*tamasā*)—with darkness (created by his magic)
- अविध्यत् (*avidhyat*)—covered (then)
- भुवनानि (*bhuvanāni*)—all the worlds
- यथा (*yathā*)—as
- अक्षेत्रवित् (*akṣetravit*)—as if all the people there had no knowledge of their own dwellings
- मुग्धः (*mugdhaḥ*)—and had become bewildered (so it happened)
- अधीधयुः (*adhīdhayuḥ*)—appeared

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Sūrya, when Svarbhānu, the son of an asura, covered you with the darkness created by his magic, all the worlds were terrified. All the inhabitants there, not knowing their own dwellings, became as if bewildered.

##### English translation
When, Surya, the son of the Asura Swarbhanu, overspread you with darkness, the worlds were beheld like one bewildered, knowing not his place.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

सभानुः (*sabhānuḥ*) **NOTE**—he is a person of asura nature. With his magic he hides Sūrya with tamas (darkness). In the present sūkta his name occurs in the 5th, 6th, 8th and 9th Ṛks. These four Ṛks tell how Indra fought against his magic, and how Atri, cooperating with Indra in this matter, re-established the eye (the light) of Sūrya in the sky. In the Brāhmaṇas also this asura is mentioned in many places. In the Purāṇas and elsewhere he is called Rāhu.

Svarbhānu is described in the Saṃhitā, the Brāhmaṇas and the rest everywhere as an extremely powerful asura. In worldly usage it is he who is famous as Rāhu. In the Saṃhitā the name Rāhu is not current for him. But since all the nature described in the अनुरकोश (*anurakośa*) **NOTE**, तमस्तु राहुस्स्वर्भानुः सैंहिकेयो विधंतुदः (*tamastu rāhussvarbhānuḥ saiṃhikeyo vidhaṃtudaḥ*), is found in him, it may be said that the asura who is later famous in the Purāṇas and elsewhere as Rāhu is this Svarbhānu. But it must also be understood that in the Purāṇas and elsewhere Rāhu means only the head of the asura. When Sāyaṇa explains the Ṛk यत्त्वा सूर्य स्वर्भानुस्तमसाविध्यदासुरः (*yattvā sūrya svarbhānustamasāvidhyadāsuraḥ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-40-5), he makes it known, with आसुरः असुरस्य प्राणापहर्तुरसुरा वा पुत्रः । स्वर्भानुः स्वर्भानुसंज्ञकः (*āsuraḥ asurasya prāṇāpaharturasurā vā putraḥ | svarbhānuḥ svarbhānusaṃjñakaḥ*), that he is an asura person, the son of an asura and bearing the name Svarbhānu. The meaning "merely the face of an asura" is not given.

### Printed p. 327

In the Ṛk-saṃhitā the matter of Svarbhānu is described only in the present context. That too is a context that shows the power of the seer Atri.

- यत्त्वा सूर्य स्वर्भानुः .... (*yattvā sūrya svarbhānuḥ ....*) (5-40-5)
- स्वर्भानोरध यदिंद्र माया .... (*svarbhānoradha yadiṃdra māyā ....*) (5-40-6)
- अत्रिः सूर्यस्य दिवि चक्षुराधा स्वर्भानोः .... (*atriḥ sūryasya divi cakṣurādhā svarbhānoḥ ....*) (5-40-8)
- यं वै सूर्यं स्वर्भानुः .... (*yaṃ vai sūryaṃ svarbhānuḥ ....*) (5-40-9)

In these four mantras he is mentioned incidentally, at the time of praising Atri. The meaning of these mantras is as follows.

1. (5) O Sūra, when Svarbhānu, the son of an asura, covered you, pervading you with his pitch darkness, the whole world, seeing neither you nor your place, was completely bewildered.

(6) O Indra, at the time when you were engaged in the effort to destroy the pitch darkness spread around Sūra, the seer Atri, with the help of his fourth mantra, found Sūra, who was hidden in the darkness.

(8) Afterwards Atri destroyed the power of Svarbhānu, which was of the nature of darkness, and established Sūra in the sky for the benefit of the world.

(9) The Ātreyas afterwards found Sūra, who had been covered by Svarbhānu. This release of Sūra from bondage was possible for no one other than the Atris.

Thus the power of Svarbhānu is described. That is, it is understood that he possessed an asura power that hid Sūrya, the eye of the world, and created an obstruction to the business of the world. Pointing out this very matter, the Bṛhaddevatā says:

स्वर्भानुदृष्टं सूर्यस्य अपहत्य ततोऽत्रयः । सप्तविंशतिभिः सूक्तैरबोधीत्यग्निमस्तुवन् ॥ (*svarbhānudṛṣṭaṃ sūryasya apahatya tato 'trayaḥ | saptaviṃśatibhiḥ sūktairabodhītyagnimastuvan ‖*) (Bṛ. De. 5-12)

It is said that the Atris freed from darkness Sūrya, who had been seized by Svarbhānu, and afterwards, after the light of Sūra, praised Agni. This same matter of Svarbhānu is told in the form of a story in the Kauṣītakī Brāhmaṇa, which explains it as follows. The asura called Svarbhānu covered Sūrya with darkness. At the prayer of the gods the Atris agreed to free Sūrya from this darkness. Then they performed a rite of three days' extent, joined with the saptadaśa-stoma, preceding the viṣuvat. Then the darkness of the asura was destroyed only in the front part; in the rear part it remained. Afterwards, above the viṣuvat, they performed this same rite of three days again. Then the darkness that was in the rear part also was destroyed forever. Therefore those who perform this rite according to the injunction become able to destroy the darkness of both worlds. The mantra by whose power this darkness is destroyed is called the svara-sāman. It was with the help of this svara-sāman that the Atris became able to destroy the power of Svarbhānu. (Kau. Brā. 24.3) **NOTE**

### Printed p. 328

The same matter is told somewhat differently in the Taittirīya Saṃhitā:

स्वर्भानुरासुरस्सूरं तमसाविध्यत्तस्यै देवाः प्रायश्चित्ति वैच्छन्तस्य यत्प्रथमं तमोपाघन्ता कृष्णा विरभवद्यद्वितीयग् सा फल्गुनी यत्तृतीयग् सा वलक्ष्मी यदध्यस्थादपाकृंतन्ता निर्वशा समभवत्ते देवा अब्रुवन्तेवपशुर्वा अयग्ं समभूत्कस्मा इममा लप्स्यामहा इत्यथ वै तहल्यल्पा पृथिव्यासीत् ॥ (*svarbhānurāsurassūraṃ tamasāvidhyattasyai devāḥ prāyaścitti vaicchantasya yatprathamaṃ tamopāghantā kṛṣṇā virabhavadyadvitīyag sā phalgunī yattṛtīyag sā valakṣmī yadadhyasthādapākṛṃtantā nirvaśā samabhavatte devā abruvantevapaśurvā ayagṃ samabhūtkasmā imamā lapsyāmahā ityatha vai tahalyalpā pṛthivyāsīt ‖*) (Kṛ. Saṃ. 2-1-2-2) **NOTE**

The asura called Svarbhānu covered Sūra with darkness. Then the gods desired the release of Sūra. In that effort they cut off a small part of the darkness. It turned into a cloud of black colour. The second part became of bright colour. The third part became of white colour. When the upper region of the darkness was cut off, it turned into a barren one. Then the gods saw it and fell into doubt: "Here a divine animal has come into being; to whom should this animal be offered?" Then, since the earth was extremely small and plants and the rest had not yet been produced, they offered it to the Ādityas, it is described. Thus, although the context of the story is the same, there are differences in the account. Although this same situation is described in the Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa, the account there is quite different. How? Thus:

स्वभानुर्हवा असुराः । सूर्यं तमसा विव्याध । स तमसा विद्धो न व्यरोचत तस्य सोमारुद्रावेवैतत्तमोऽपाहताग्ं स एषोऽपहतपाप्मा तपति ॥ ( (*svabhānurhavā asurāḥ | sūryaṃ tamasā vivyādha | sa tamasā viddho na vyarocata tasya somārudrāvevaitattamo 'pāhatāgṃ sa eṣo 'pahatapāpmā tapati ‖ (*) (Śa. Brā. 5-3-2-2)

In this passage it is said that when Sūrya was covered by the darkness of Svarbhānu, Soma and Rudra freed him from the darkness. Thus the Kauṣītakī Brāhmaṇa says that the release of Sūrya came about through the power of the seer Atri **NOTE**, the Taittirīya Saṃhitā says that it was brought about by the effort of the gods alone, and the Śatapatha says that the release of Sūrya came about through the power of Soma and Rudra. Thus, in the account of the manner of the release of Sūrya

### Printed p. 329

Though there are differences, there is one and the same view concerning the power of Svarbhānu and the obstruction to the course of the world that was caused by him. Whether the hiding of Sūrya seized by Rāhu, which the Purāṇas and the like and astronomy always describe as an eclipse, is the very same eclipse that the Ṛk-saṃhitā and the other texts speak of when Sūrya is seized by Svarbhānu, is a matter of debate among scholars. In order to discuss this point, one must first examine what the nature of Rāhu is in the worldly works, and whether Rāhu possesses all the features that are described in Svarbhānu. In the Purāṇas and the like, Svarbhānu and Rāhu are both said to be sons of Kaśyapa. But one might have to say that the persons are different.

स्वर्गानुरश्वोऽश्वपतिवृषपर्नाजकस्तथा (*svargānuraśvo 'śvapativṛṣaparnājakastathā*)
(Ma. Bhā. Ādi. Pa. 66-24)

Here it is understood that the asura called Svarbhānu was born of the sage Kaśyapa in his wife called Danu, and that Rāhu is another name of his. Moreover,

सिंहिका सुषुवे पुत्रं राहु चंद्रार्कमर्दनं (*siṃhikā suṣuve putraṃ rāhu caṃdrārkamardanaṃ*)
(Ādi-parva 66-31)

Here it is said that Rāhu is the son of Siṃhikā and the one who swallows the moon and the sun. In the same way, in several other places it is said that Rāhu and Svarbhānu are not different persons but one and the same person. Moreover, in the Bṛhaddevatā also,

प्राजापत्यो मरीचिर्हि मारीचः कश्यपो मुनिः । तस्य देव्योऽभवञ्चाया दाक्षायण्यस्रयोदश ॥ अदितिर्दितीर्दनुः काला दनायुः सिंहिका मुनिः । (*prājāpatyo marīcirhi mārīcaḥ kaśyapo muniḥ | tasya devyo 'bhavañcāyā dākṣāyaṇyasrayodaśa ‖ aditirditīrdanuḥ kālā danāyuḥ siṃhikā muniḥ |*)
(Bṛ. De. 5-143, 4)

Prajāpati had a son called Marīci, and Marīci had a son called Kaśyapa. Kaśyapa had thirteen divine wives, the daughters of Dakṣa: Aditi, Diti, Danu, Kālā, Danāyu, Siṃhikā and so on. Thus there is no great difference between what is set out in the Vedic passages and what is set out in the Purāṇas and the like. Therefore, to answer the question whether one can say that the state of Sūrya seized by Rāhu, which is described as the solar eclipse, is the very thing that is described in the Vedic passages as being seized by Svarbhānu, one will have to investigate on the basis of certain Vedic passages. In the present context, certain passages must be examined in order to learn whether the matter of the solar eclipse is stated clearly and whether there is an exact account of it.

### Printed p. 330

सा मा सत्योक्तिः परि पातु विश्वतो द्यावा च यत्र ततनन्नहानि च । विश्वमन्नन्नि विशते यदेजति विश्वहापो विश्वाहोदेति सूर्यः ॥ (*sā mā satyoktiḥ pari pātu viśvato dyāvā ca yatra tatanannahāni ca | viśvamannanni viśate yadejati viśvahāpo viśvāhodeti sūryaḥ ‖*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-37-2)

Some scholars (Ludwig) have held, on the basis of this mantra, which gives the meaning that by which truth the expanse of heaven and earth comes about, and by which truth the revolution of night and day comes about, may that truth protect me; by the power of that truth all the business of the world goes on; and Sūrya too keeps up his daily movement of revolution, that Sūrya revolves around the earth, that when an obstruction by the moon occurs it is a solar eclipse, and that this moon is the one described as Svarbhānu. But this meaning does not give the purport of the mantra above correctly. Moreover, there is also the possibility of saying that there are passages in the Ṛk-saṃhitā which support the above view. For example:

अनाधृष्टानि धृषितो व्यास्यन्निधीरदेवा अमृणदयास्यः । मासेव सूरो वसु पुर‍्यमा ददे गृणानः शत्रुरशृणाद्विरुक्मता ॥ (*anādhṛṣṭāni dhṛṣito vyāsyannidhīradevā amṛṇadayāsyaḥ | māseva sūro vasu puryamā dade gṛṇānaḥ śatruraśṛṇādvirukmatā ‖*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-138-4)

In this mantra they have taken the word मासेव (*māseva*) to mean the moon, and have shown the purport that, just as Sūrya is covered and seized by the moon, so may the wealth of the asuras be seized by Indra.
(Journal of American oriental Society. Vol. 13. p. LXII)
(Journal of American oriental Society. Vol. 13. p. LXII)

But this meaning is plainly wrong. In this mantra there is no room at all for the meaning moon. The purport of this mantra is "in the way that Sūrya takes up the moisture of the earth, in that way." Therefore this mantra does not help the above view. In the same way they have given wrong meanings to many other mantras and criticised them. But to cite all of them would be out of place here. In the present context there is no support whatever for the idea that the revolution of Sūrya is obstructed by the moon. Svarbhānu must be understood to mean Rāhu; Rāhu is the cause of the eclipse of both Sūrya and the moon. रहति गृहीत्वा त्यजति चंद्रार्कौ (*rahati gṛhītvā tyajati caṃdrārkau*) (रह त्यागे (*raha tyāge*)): because he first seizes the moon and Sūrya and afterwards releases them, he is called Rāhu. स्वः आकाशे विपरीतलक्षणया भाति (*svaḥ ākāśe viparītalakṣaṇayā bhāti*): because he shines in a way different from both Sūrya and the moon, he is called Svarbhānu. The present sūkta praises the fact that only the fourth mantra of the great sage Atri was able to release the eclipse brought about by such a Svarbhānu.

### Printed p. 331

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स्व॑र्भानो॒रथ॒ यदिं॑द्र मा॒या अ॒वो दि॒वो वर्त॑माना अ॒वाह॑न्।  
> गू॒ळ्लं सूर्यं॒ तम॒साप॑व्रतेन तु॒रीये॑ण॒ ब्रह्म॑णाविंद॒दत्रिः॑ ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> स्वःभानोः। आध । यत् । इंद्र । मायाः । अवः । दिवः। वर्तमानाः । अवअहन् ।  
> गूळं । सूर्यं । तमसा । अस्यव्रतेन । तुरीयेण । ब्रह्मणा । अविंदत् । अत्रिः ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अधाथ जगन्मौड्यानंतरं स्वर्भानोरसुरस्य यत्, या मायाः संति ।
> कीदृश्यस्ताः । दिवो द्योतमानादादित्याववोवस्ताद्वर्तमानाः। तदुपरितिरोधानासामर्थ्यादिति भावः । हे इंद्र ताः सर्वा अवाहन् । अवहंसि । अथात्रेरेव
> परोक्षवादः । तमसांधकारेणापव्रतेनापगतकर्मणा गूळं सूर्यं । अंधकारस्यावरणरूपत्वादपव्रतत्वं । तथाविधु तुरीयेण ब्रह्मणा ग्राव्णो ब्रह्मेत्यनेनात्रिरविंदत् , लब्धवान् आवरणापगमोपायं निवारणं शुद्धं ना सूर्यमिति ।
> पूर्वमंत्रापेक्षयास्य तुरीयत्वं । एकैकं मायांशमेकैकेन मंत्रेणापनोद्य
> चतुर्थेन मंत्रेण निलीनं तमोऽप्यनुददित्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa explains that, after the bewildering of the world, whatever illusions the asura Svarbhānu has are spread out below the shining sun, because they were able to obstruct it; O Indra, you destroyed all of them. The statement then turns to Atri himself, in indirect speech: Atri found the sun, hidden by the darkness that is अपव्रतेन (*apavratena*), that is, one that has put a stop to the sun's action (darkness is of the nature of a covering, hence it is called "deprived of action"), by the fourth prayer; he obtained the means by which the covering is removed, namely the prayer that begins with the word of the pressing-stone. It is called the fourth in relation to the preceding mantras: having driven off one portion of the illusion with each mantra, with the fourth mantra he drove away even the darkness that had lurked in hiding.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अध (*adha*)—afterwards
- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- दिवः (*divaḥ*)—of the shining Sun
- अवः (*avaḥ*)—in the region below
- वर्तमानाः (*vartamānāḥ*)—(hiding the Sun above) lying completely covering
- स्वर्भानोः (*svarbhānoḥ*)—of Svarbhānu
- यत् मायाः (*yat māyāḥ*)—those illusory forms of the nature of darkness that there are
- अवाहन् (*avāhan*)—you destroyed
- अपव्रतेन (*apavratena*)—that obstructs (the Sun's) action
- तमसा (*tamasā*)—by pitch darkness
- गूळ्हं (*gūḷhaṃ*)—concealed
- सूर‍्यं (*sūryaṃ*)—Sūrya
- अत्रिः (*atriḥ*)—the sage Atri
- तुरियेण (*turiyeṇa*) ब्रह्मणा (*brahmaṇā*)—by the recitation of the fourth mantra
- अविंदत् (*aviṃdat*)—obtained

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, as soon as you destroyed the illusory powers, of the nature of darkness, of Svarbhānu, which lay in the region below the shining Sun, hiding the Sun above and covering him completely, the sage Atri obtained, by reciting the fourth mantra, the Sūrya who was concealed by the pitch darkness that obstructs action.

### Printed p. 332

##### English translation
When Indra, you were dissipating those illusions of Swarbhanu which were spread below the sun, then Atri, by his fourth sacred prayer, discovered the sun concealed by the darkness impeding his functions.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

तुरियेण ब्रह्मणा (*turiyeṇa brahmaṇā*)—by the fourth hymn of praise or by the recitation of a mantra. In this sūkta the matter of Svarbhānu, and how he hid the radiance of Sūrya so that it did not shine, is stated. Indra, with the help of the sage Atri, destroyed this asura and made Sūrya, who had been covered by darkness, shine again. This matter is also set out in detail in the 5th, 6th, 7th and 8th Ṛks of this sūkta. Therefore तुरियेण ब्रह्मणा (*turiyeṇa brahmaṇā*), "by the fourth mantra", means that here the sage Atri made the radiance of Sūrya shine again by the fourth (of the 5th, 6th, 7th and 8th Ṛks), the 8th Ṛk of this sūkta, ग्राज्ञो ब्रह्मा (*grājño brahmā*) **NOTE**. This is the view stated here.

#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> मा मामि॒मं तव॒ संत॑मत्र इर॒स्या द्रु॒ग्धो भि॒यसा॒ नि  ॑
> गारीत् ।  
> त्वं मि॒त्रो अ॑सि स॒त्यरा॑धा॒स्तौ मे॒हाव॑तं॒ वरु॑णश्च॒ राजा॑ ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> मा । मां । इमं । तव । संतं । अत्ते । इरस्या । द्रुग्धः । भियसा । नि । गारीत् ।  
> त्वं । मित्रः । असि । सत्यराधाः । शौ । मा । इह । अवतं । वरुणः । च । राजा ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> इदं सूर्यवाक्यं । हे अत्रे मामिममीदृगवस्थं मां तव संतं तन
> स्वभूतमिरस्यान्नेच्छया द्रुग्धो द्रोग्धासुरो भियसा भयजनकेन तमसांध-

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This is the speech of Sūrya. He says: O Atri, let the deceiver, the asura, who is moved by desire for food (इरस्या (*irasyā*) here means by wish for food), not swallow me, who am in such a state and who belong to you, who am your own, with fearful darkness.

### Printed p. 333

> कारेण मा नि गारीत् । मा गिरतु । किंच हे मित्र त्वं मित्रोऽसि । प्रमितेः
> सकाशात्माता भवसि । सत्यराधाः सत्यधनश्च । तौ राजा वरुणश्च त्वं च तौ युवां मा
> मामिहावतं । रक्षतं । यद्वा । अत्रिरेव मित्र उच्यते । वरुणश्च युवां ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The sentence continues: let him not swallow me with the darkness that causes fear. And further: O Mitra, you are Mitra, for you become the one who saves from harm; you are also one whose wealth is truth. You and the royal Varuṇa, you two, protect me here. Or else, Atri himself is called Mitra, and Varuṇa is the other; you two protect me.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अत्रे (*atre*)—O Atri
- तव संतं (*tava saṃtaṃ*)—who belong to you
- इमं मां (*imaṃ māṃ*)—me, who am covered by darkness
- इरस्या (*irasyā*)—who is moved by desire for food
- द्रुग्धः (*drugdhaḥ*)—the deceiver, the asura
- भियसा (*bhiyasā*)—by the fearful pitch darkness
- मा नि गारीत् (*mā ni gārīt*)—let him not swallow
- त्वं (*tvaṃ*)—you
- सत्यराधाः (*satyarādhāḥ*)—whose wealth is truth
- मित्रः असि (*mitraḥ asi*)—you are of the nature of Mitra
- राजा (*rājā*)—the lord
- वरुणश्च (*varuṇaśca*)—and you are also Varuṇa
- ता (*tā*)—you two
- मा (*mā*)—me
- इह (*iha*)—in this situation
- अनतं (*anataṃ*)—protect

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Atri, let the deceiver, the asura, who is moved by desire for food, not swallow me, who belong to you and am covered by darkness, with fearful pitch darkness. You are of the nature of Mitra, whose wealth is truth. You together with Varuṇa, the lord, protect me in this situation. (This is the speech of Sūrya.)

##### English translation
[Surya speaks] : Let not the violator, Atri through hunger, swallow with fearful (darkness) me who am yours ; you are Mitra, whose wealth is truth : you and the royal Varuna both protect me.

#### Ṛk 8

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> गाव्णो॑ ब्र॒ह्मा यु॑युजा॒नः स॑प॒र्यन् की॒रिणा॑ दे॒वान्नम॑सोप॒शिक्ष॑न् ।

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> ग्राव्णः । ब्रह्मा । ययुजानः । सपर्यन् । कीरिणा । देवान् । नमसा । उप  
> शिक्षन् ।  
> अत्रिः । सूर्यस्य । दिवि । चक्षुः । आ । अधात् । स्वभानोः । अप । मायाः । अफृक्षत् ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ब्रह्मा ब्राह्मणोऽत्रिर्मामिहावतमित्युपशिक्षन् ग्रावोऽभिषवसाधनाय
> युयुजानो युंजन् । इद्रार्थं सोममभिषुण्णन्नित्यर्थः । तथा कीरिणा ।
> कीर्यते विक्षिष्यत इति कीरि स्तोत्रं । तेन देवान् सपर्यन् पूजयन्नमसा ।
> उक्तैः साधनैः सूर्यस्य सर्वप्रेरकस्य चक्षु: सर्वस्य व्यापकं मंडलं दिव्यंतरिक्ष
> आधात् । निस्तमस्कं कृतवानित्यर्थः । तदेव स्पष्टयति । स्पर्धानोरेतन्नामकस्यासुरस
> मायाः । स्वाश्रयमया मोहयंती परांस्तथा कुर्वती मायेत्युच्यते । तादृशीर्मायाः । तुरीयेण ब्रह्मणेत्युक्तं । तदेतदुक्तं यत्तुरीयं ब्रह्म ।
> तेनात्रिसहाय इंद्रोऽपाघुक्तः । अपजुगोप । न्यवारयदित्यर्थः । अथवा
> तृतीयपाद एवं व्याख्येयः । सूर्यस्य दिवि पूर्वमावृते प्रकाशे तदपनोद्य
> स्वकीयं चक्षुराधात् । निवारणं तेजःसंस्त्यायं दृष्टवानित्यर्थः । स्पर्धानुमायया सूर्यस्यावृतिर्हारिद्रविके समाम्नाता । स्वर्भानुश्चासुरः सूर्यं तमसाविध्यत्तस्मै देवाः प्रायश्चित्तमैच्छन् तस्य यत्पथमं तमोऽपाघन् सा कृष्णाविरभवत्, यद्वितीयं सा फाल्गुनी यत्तृतीयं सा वलक्ष्मी यदध्यस्थादपाकृंतन्नित्यादि॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa explains that Atri, the Brahmin, saying "protect me here," engaged the pressing-stones for pressing, that is, pressed Soma for Indra; and with a hymn (कीर्यते विक्षिष्यत इति कीरि स्तोत्रं (*kīryate vikṣiṣyata iti kīri stotraṃ*), "what is scattered abroad is a kīri, a hymn") he worshipped the gods, with homage. By the means so stated he placed the eye of Sūrya, the impeller of all, that is, his disc, which pervades everything, in the sky: he made it free from darkness. He then makes this plain: the illusions of the asura named Svarbhānu are called illusion because they bewilder, by means of their own power, and so make others the same; it is such illusions that the fourth prayer drove away, as was said by "the fourth prayer". Thus Indra, with Atri's help, warded them off, that is, he held them back. Or else the third quarter may be explained thus: in the sky of Sūrya, where the light had before been covered, having removed that covering, he set his own eye; that is, he saw the removal of the obstruction and the mass of radiance. The covering of Sūrya by the illusion of Svarbhānu is recorded in the हारिद्रविके (*hāridravike*): the asura Svarbhānu pierced Sūrya with darkness; the gods sought an atonement for him; the first darkness that they struck away became Kṛṣṇāvi, the second became Phālgunī, the third became Valakṣmī, and what remained they cut away, and so on. **NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- ब्रह्मा (*brahmā*)—the knower of Brahman
- अत्रिः (*atriḥ*)—Atri
- ग्राव्यः (*grāvyaḥ*)—the Soma-pressing stones
- युयुजानः (*yuyujānaḥ*)—joining together (and pressing Soma for Indra)
- कीरिणा (*kīriṇā*)—with a hymn
- देवान् (*devān*)—the gods
- सपर्यन् (*saparyan*)—worshipping
- नमसा (*namasā*)—with food in the form of an oblation
- उपशिक्षन् (*upaśikṣan*)—adorning
- सूरस्य (*sūrasya*)—of Sūrya
- चक्षुः (*cakṣuḥ*)—the eye of disc form
- दिवि (*divi*)—in the sky
- आधात् (*ādhāt*)—he set
- स्वर्भानोः (*svarbhānoḥ*)—of Svarbhānu
- मायाः (*māyāḥ*)—the powers that cause bewilderment
- अपाघुक्षत् (*apāghukṣat*)—he warded off

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The Brahman-knowing Atri, pressing Soma for Indra with the pressing-stones, worshipping the gods with hymns, adorning them with food in the form of oblation, Sūrya's

### Printed p. 335

disc-shaped eye (the radiance) in the sky; he removed the delusion-causing power of Svarbhānu.

##### English translation
Then the Brahman, 'Atri), applying the stones together, propitiating the gods with praise, and adoring them with reverence, placed the eye of Surya in the sky : he dispersed the delusions of Swarbhanu.

#### Ṛk 9

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यं वै सूर्यं॒ स्व॑र्भानु॒स्तम॒सा वि॑द्यदासु॒रः ।  
> अत्र॑य॒स्तमन्व॑विंद न्न॒ह्य१॒॑गन्ने अश॑क्नुवन् ॥ ९ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यं । वै । सूर्यं । स्वःभानुः । तमसा । अविध्यत् । असुरः ।  
> अत्रयः । तं । अनु । अविंदन् । नहि । अन्ये । अशक्नुवन् ॥ ९ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अत्रिकृतं सामर्थ्यमनुवदति यं वै सूर्यमिति । निगतव्याख्येषा। अत्रयस्तं
> सूर्यमन्वविंदन् । इंद्रार्थं सोमयागदेवतास्तुतिनमस्कारैरनुक्रमेणेषदीपत्तमो विरुध्य लब्धवंत इत्यर्थः । अन्वे नह्यशक्नु वन् । न लब्धवंतः खलु ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says that this Ṛk restates what Atri accomplished: यं वै सूर्यं (*yaṃ vai sūryaṃ*). Its explanation is already given. The Atris found that Sūrya afterwards: for Indra, in the Soma sacrifice, by praise of the deities and by homage, one after another, they resisted the thick darkness **NOTE** and obtained him. Others were not able; they did not obtain him.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यं वै सूर्यं (*yaṃ vai sūryaṃ*)—which Sūrya
- आसुरः (*āsuraḥ*)—the son of an asura
- स्वर्भानुः (*svarbhānuḥ*)—Svarbhānu
- तमसा (*tamasā*)—with pitch darkness
- अविध्यत् (*avidhyat*)—covered and hid
- तं (*taṃ*)—that Sūrya
- अत्रयः (*atrayaḥ*)—the sages born in the family of Atri
- अन्वविंदन् (*anvaviṃdan*)—obtained
- अन्ये (*anye*)—others
- नहि अशक्नु वन् (*nahi aśaknu van*)—were not able to do this work

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The sages born in the family of Atri obtained that Sūrya whom Svarbhānu, the son of an asura, had covered and hidden with pitch darkness. No others were able to do this great deed.

### Printed p. 336

##### English translation
The sun, whom the Asura Swarbhanu, had enveloped with darkness, the sons of Atri subsequently recovered : no others were able (to effect his release).

## Sūkta 41 (Maṇḍala 5)

Deities: the Viśvedevas. Ṛks: 21.

From this sūkta कोनु वां (*konu vāṃ*) onwards, the next eleven sūktas are addressed to the Viśvedevas. Who are the Viśvedevas? What is their nature? It is necessary to examine such matters here.

को नु वां वैश्वदेवानि एकादश पराण्यतः (*ko nu vāṃ vaiśvadevāni ekādaśa parāṇyataḥ*)
(Bṛ. De. 5-36)

This statement of the Bṛhaddevatā, that beginning with the sūkta that starts with the mantra को नु वां मित्रावरुणौ (*ko nu vāṃ mitrāvaruṇau*) a total of eleven sūktas are sūktas praising the Viśvedevas, points to the matters praised in the eleven sūktas of the Ṛk-saṃhitā from the 41st sūkta of the fifth maṇḍala up to the 51st. This the Sarvānukramaṇī states as वैश्वदेवं वै तत् (*vaiśvadevaṃ vai tat*).
Macdonell Brihad Dwata. English Translation. p. 175.

It is not easy to state a precise view on the question what the Viśvedevas are. There are differences of opinion on whether the word means the assembly of all the deities, or a separate group of deities that indicates a definite number. But in the Ṛk-saṃhitā there is also a context in which, under the name of the groups of Viśvedevas, all the deities are clearly indicated.

अविमा ये देवा स्तन त्रिष्वा रोचने दिवः । (*avimā ye devā stana triṣvā rocane divaḥ |*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-185-5)

Speaking of the very deities who are all indicated in this mantra as those of the three worlds, the Bṛhaddevatā says:

### Printed p. 337

बृहस्पतिप्रचोदिता विश्वेदेवगणास्त्रयः । जग्मुस्त्रितस्य तं यज्ञं भागांश्च जगृहुः सह ॥ (*bṛhaspatipracoditā viśvedevagaṇāstrayaḥ | jagmustritasya taṃ yajñaṃ bhāgāṃśca jagṛhuḥ saha ‖*)
(Bṛ. Je. 1-136) **NOTE**

All the deities, divided into three groups in the three worlds, Pṛthivī and the others, impelled by Bṛhaspati, came to the sacrifice of Trita and took part in it. Thus the word viśvadeva is explained as all the deities. The Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa also explains this same view:

वैश्वदेवश्चतुर्थमहर्भवति । विश्वे वै सर्वॆ देवाः सर्वेषां देवानामास्मै वैश्वदेवा ग्रहा भवंति वैश्वदेव्यः परोरुचः सर्वं वैश्वदेवमसदिति ॥ (*vaiśvadevaścaturthamaharbhavati | viśve vai sarvè devāḥ sarveṣāṃ devānāmāsmai vaiśvadevā grahā bhavaṃti vaiśvadevyaḥ parorucaḥ sarvaṃ vaiśvadevamasaditi ‖*)
(Śa. Brā. 13-7-1-6)

The fourth day is that of the Vaiśvadevas. By Viśvedevas is meant all the deities. The aim is that on the fourth day their complete favour should be obtained. The Soma cups are the Vaiśvadeva cups. The groups called परोरुक् (*paroruk*) also serve to win the favour of all the deities. Therefore these cups must satisfy all the deities. And likewise:

इति ह स्माह सत्यकामो जाबालो विश्वे वै सर्वॆ देवास्तदेनान्यथादेवतं प्रीणातीति (*iti ha smāha satyakāmo jābālo viśve vai sarvè devāstadenānyathādevataṃ prīṇātīti*)
(Ṭa. Brā. 13-5-2-1) **NOTE**

By Viśvedevas is meant all the deities. It is therefore stated plainly in the Śatapatha that Satyakāma Jābāla proclaimed that by this rite, which has the Viśvedevas as its deity, individual satisfaction is obtained for all the deities. Thus, although there is in some Vedic passages the view that the word applies to all the deities, in other places there is room for an alternative sense. For example:

स नैन व्यभवत्स विशमसृजत यान्येतानि देवजातानि गणश आख्यायंते वसवो रुद्रा आदित्या विश्वे देवा मरुत इति । (*sa naina vyabhavatsa viśamasṛjata yānyetāni devajātāni gaṇaśa ākhyāyaṃte vasavo rudrā ādityā viśve devā maruta iti |*)
(Bṛ. U. 1-4-12)

Prajāpati, engaged in the work of creation, had not yet attained fullness of creation. Then he created the subjects and the deities who are indicated in the form of groups. They are the Vasus, the Rudras, the Ādityas and the Viśvedevas, and also the Maruts. Of these groups, the Vasus show a group of eight in number, the Rudras a group of eleven (11), the Ādityas a group of twelve (12), and the Maruts a group of seven. Having explained thus, Śaṅkarācārya in his commentary has given this meaning for the word Viśvedeva.

### Printed p. 338

विश्वेदेवास्त्रयोदश विश्वाया अपत्यानि । सर्वे वा देवाः ॥ (*viśvedevāstrayodaśa viśvāyā apatyāni | sarve vā devāḥ ‖*)
(Bṛ. U. Śaṃ. Bhā. 1-4-12)

He has said that there is an alternative meaning: one meaning is the group of thirteen deities who are the sons of Viśvā, or else the other meaning is all the deities. It is not easy to say who this Viśvā is and how the status of her sons came to the deities. In the Saṃhitās and the rest she is not a very famous person. Only in the Bṛhaddevatā is her name stated, in one place.

अदितिर्दितिदः नुः काला दनायुः सिंहिका मुनिः । क्रोधा विश्वा वरिष्ठा च सुरभिर्विनता तथा । कद्रूश्चैवेति दुहित्वाः कश्यपाय ददौ स च ॥ (*aditirditidaḥ nuḥ kālā danāyuḥ siṃhikā muniḥ | krodhā viśvā variṣṭhā ca surabhirvinatā tathā | kadrūścaiveti duhitvāḥ kaśyapāya dadau sa ca ‖*)
(Bṛ. De. 5-144)

Kaśyapa, the son of Marīci, had thirteen divine wives. They were: Aditi, Diti, Danu, Kālā, Danāyu, Siṃhikā, Muni, Krodhā, Viśvā, Variṣṭhā, Surabhi, Vinatā, Kadru. They were the daughters of Dakṣa. Dakṣa gave these thirteen maidens to Kaśyapa and married them to him.

तासु देवासुराश्चैव गंधर्वोरगराक्षसाः (*tāsu devāsurāścaiva gaṃdharvoragarākṣasāḥ*)
(Bṛ. De. 25-145) **NOTE**

Of these daughters of Dakṣa were born gods, asuras, gandharvas, serpents, rākṣasas and others. Giving such details as that twelve sons were born to Aditi, it describes the order of birth of Agastya and Vasiṣṭha. In that very context:

सर्वत्र पुष्करं तत्र विश्वे देवा आधारयन् (*sarvatra puṣkaraṃ tatra viśve devā ādhārayan*)
(Bṛ. De. 5-125)

It says that the Viśvedevas bore Vasiṣṭha, who was lying in a pushkara (lotus flower). Although it is said that Viśvā was a wife of Kaśyapa, it is not stated that the Viśvedevas were her sons. But the bearing of Vasiṣṭha, who lay in a pushkara, is also described in the Ṛk-saṃhitā.

उतासि मैत्रावरुणो वसिष्ठोर्वश्या ब्रह्मन्मनसोऽधि जातः । द्रप्सं स्कन्नं ब्रह्मणा दैन्वेन विश्वे देवाः पुष्करे त्वाददंत ॥ (*utāsi maitrāvaruṇo vasiṣṭhorvaśyā brahmanmanaso 'dhi jātaḥ | drapsaṃ skannaṃ brahmaṇā dainvena viśve devāḥ puṣkare tvādadaṃta ‖*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-23.)

O Vasiṣṭha, you are the one born of Mitra and Varuṇa in Urvaśī. It says that the Viśvedevas bore you, who lay in a pushkara. Sāyaṇa has not said here who is meant by विश्वेदेवाः (*viśvedevāḥ*). The author of the Nirukta, giving the purport of this very passage,

### Printed p. 339

सर्वे देवाः श्वाधारयंत (*sarve devāḥ śvādhārayaṃta*)
(Ni. 5-14)

has taken विश्वे देवाः (*viśve devāḥ*) to mean all the deities as well. Only in the Matsya Purāṇa is it said, in one place, that the Viśvedevas are the sons of Viśvā, and that they are ten persons, namely Kratu, Dakṣa, Vasu, Satya, Kālakāma, Muni, Kuraja, Manuja, Vīja and Rocamāna. It praises them as worthy of worship in offerings to gods and ancestors. (Matsya. 203). The Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa says that they received a curse from Viśvāmitra. (Mārkaṇḍeya 7). But for the present we cannot settle the meaning of Vedic passages on the basis of the Purāṇas. In the Vedic passages the praise of the Viśvedevas is of three kinds. The Bṛhaddevatā states it thus:

वैश्वदेवानि सूक्तानि त्रिविधानि भवंति तु । सूर्यसंस्तवसंयुक्तं विश्वलिंगं पृथक्स्तुति ॥ (*vaiśvadevāni sūktāni trividhāni bhavaṃti tu | sūryasaṃstavasaṃyuktaṃ viśvaliṃgaṃ pṛthakstuti ‖*)
(Pṛ. Pe. 3-32) **NOTE**

The sūktas that praise and pray to the Viśvedevas are of three kinds. Those sūktas that are combined with praises of Sūrya belong to the first class. Those sūktas that indicate and praise the general characteristics of all the deities belong to the second class. And again, praising an individual deity and extolling the individual qualities of each deity (पृथक् स्तुति (*pṛthak stuti*)) belongs to the third class.

पृथक् स्तुतीति यत्प्रोक्तं तद्विद्याद्बहुदैवतं । विश्वलिंगं तु तद्यत्र विश्वैः स्वैः कर्मजैर्गुणैः ॥ (*pṛthak stutīti yatproktaṃ tadvidyādbahudaivataṃ | viśvaliṃgaṃ tu tadyatra viśvaiḥ svaiḥ karmajairguṇaiḥ ‖*)
(Bṛ. Ve. 3-43) **NOTE**

Of the three kinds of praises explained in the verse above, (1) सूर‍्यसंस्तवसंयुक्त (*sūryasaṃstavasaṃyukta*) (2) विश्वलिंग (*viśvaliṃga*) (3) पृथक् स्तुति (*pṛthak stuti*), the पृथक् स्तुति (*pṛthak stuti*) contains the individual praise of many deities. The stating of the general characteristics of the deities is the विश्वलिंग (*viśvaliṃga*). Stating this same point in another place, it explains what विश्वलिंग (*viśvaliṃga*) is.

यषिभिर्देवताः सर्वा विश्वाभिः स्तुतिभिः स्तुताः । संज्ञा तु विश्वमित्येषा सर्वानाप्ता निपातिता ॥ (*yaṣibhirdevatāḥ sarvā viśvābhiḥ stutibhiḥ stutāḥ | saṃjñā tu viśvamityeṣā sarvānāptā nipātitā ‖*)
(Bṛ. De. 2-134)

### Printed p. 340

All the deities are praised by the seers through praises that are combined and that describe the qualities common to all. The designation विश्व (*viśva*) is used in the sense of सर्वावाप्ति (*sarvāvāpti*). सर्वावाप्ति (*sarvāvāpti*) means containing everything within itself. According to this designation, विश्वे देवाः (*viśve devāḥ*) means that which includes all the deities. This is what is spoken of above by the designation विश्वलिंग (*viśvaliṃga*). In explaining the designation विश्वलिंग (*viśvaliṃga*), the author of the Nirukta has stated the view of Śākapūṇi, that only those sūktas praising the deities that are indicated by the word विश्व (*viśva*) are Vaiśvadeva sūktas. (Ni. 12-40)

विश्वैः स्वैः कर्मजैर्गुणैः (*viśvaiḥ svaiḥ karmajairguṇaiḥ*) (3-43): it is said that the qualities common to the whole world are born of the individual actions of each deity. In this sense there is no great difference between the sūktas of the two groups, विश्वालिंग (*viśvāliṃga*) and पृथक् स्तुति (*pṛthak stuti*). How? Explaining what is meant by स्वकर्मज (*svakarmaja*), it indicates thus what the world-wide qualities born of one's own actions are.

स्तूयंते देवता ह्यासु कर्मभिः स्वैः पृथक् पृथक् । स्तुताः कर्मगुणैः स्वैः स्वैर्देवता यत्र तत्र तु ॥ पृथक्कर्मस्तुतिर्नाम वैश्वदेवं तदेव तु । (*stūyaṃte devatā hyāsu karmabhiḥ svaiḥ pṛthak pṛthak | stutāḥ karmaguṇaiḥ svaiḥ svairdevatā yatra tatra tu ‖ pṛthakkarmastutirnāma vaiśvadevaṃ tadeva tu |*)
(Bṛ. De. 6-69,70)

Wherever the deities are praised by the description of their respective individual qualities and by their respective individual deeds, there is room for पृथक्कर्मस्तुति (*pṛthakkarmastuti*) (praise of individual deeds). Such praise is Vaiśvadevika.

Thirdly, if one examines what is the nature of the Vaiśvadeva praise described by the statement सूर‍्यसंस्तवसंयुक्तं (*sūryasaṃstavasaṃyuktaṃ*), its nature is stated thus.

विश्वानुद्दिश्य यद्देवान् स्तौति सूर्यमनेकधा । देवानेनाभिसंस्तौति तं प्राहुः सूर्यसंस्तवं ॥ (*viśvānuddiśya yaddevān stauti sūryamanekadhā | devānenābhisaṃstauti taṃ prāhuḥ sūryasaṃstavaṃ ‖*)
(Bṛ. Je. 3-43) **NOTE**

The mantra that, addressing the Viśvedevas, praises Sūrya greatly, and yet is mainly in praise of the deities, is called by the name सूरसंस्तव (*sūrasaṃstava*). But there is a lack of coverage for this rule in one or two places. For example:

न तु भागस्य सूक्तादौ सूक्तेष्टेवौषसेषु वा । न सावित्रे ह्वयामीति न सूर्यायां क्रत्वा मखे ॥ (*na tu bhāgasya sūktādau sūkteṣṭevauṣaseṣu vā | na sāvitre hvayāmīti na sūryāyāṃ kratvā makhe ‖*)
(Bṛ. De. 3-45)

### Printed p. 341

In the Ṛk-saṃhitā, many deities are indicated in the sūkta 7-41, the only sūkta addressed to Bhaga. But it cannot be a Vaiśvadeva sūkta. Already in the first mantra of this sūkta, along with Savitṛ, various deities such as Indra and Agni are praised and prayed to. But that too is not Vaiśvadeva. The same rule applies to the 10.85th sūkta of the Ṛ. Saṃ. It is regarded merely as a Sūrya sūkta. Although these mantras have many deities, they are not counted as Vaiśvadeva when they are used in sacrificial rites. Likewise,

ह्वयाम्यग्निं प्रथमं स्वस्तये ह्वयामि मित्रावरुणाविहावसे । ह्वयामि रात्रीं जगतो निवेशनीं ह्वयामि देवं सवितारमूतये ॥ (*hvayāmyagniṃ prathamaṃ svastaye hvayāmi mitrāvaruṇāvihāvase | hvayāmi rātrīṃ jagato niveśanīṃ hvayāmi devaṃ savitāramūtaye ‖*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-35-1)

in such places there are mantras that could be put in the group called Vaiśvadeva combined with praise of Sūrya, सूर‍्यसंस्तवसंयुक्त (*sūryasaṃstavasaṃyukta*), and there is praise of other deities along with Savitṛ. But this too is not Vaiśvadeva. Thus many sūktas that appear as if they could belong to the third class do not fall under this rule.

While examining the injunctions and prohibitions in this way, one must also know who the deities are that are incidentally indicated in the Vaiśvadeva sūktas.

देवताविदवेक्षेत प्रयोगे सर्वकर्मणां (*devatāvidavekṣeta prayoge sarvakarmaṇāṃ*)
(Bṛ. Ve. 8-124) **NOTE**

In order to gain skill in the performance of all rites, one must know fully these natures of the deities, both individual and collective. Who those deities are:

सप्तर्षयो वसवश्चापि देवा अथर्वाणो भृगवः सोमसूर्याः । पथ्या स्वस्ति रोदसी चोक्तमंत्रे कुहूर्गुज्जूरदितिर्धेनुरघ्न्या ॥ (*saptarṣayo vasavaścāpi devā atharvāṇo bhṛgavaḥ somasūryāḥ | pathyā svasti rodasī coktamaṃtre kuhūrgujjūraditirdhenuraghnyā ‖*)
(Bṛ. De. 8-125)

The seven sages, the Vasus, the gods, the Atharvans, the Bhṛgus, Soma, Sūrya, Sūryā, Pathyā Svasti, the Heaven and Earth of the said mantras, Kuhū, Gungū, Aditi, Dhenu and Aghnyā.

असुनीतिरिळा चाप्ता विधातानुमतिर्ह या । अंगिरोभिः सहैताः स्युरुक्तमंत्राश्च देवताः ॥ ( (*asunītiriḷā cāptā vidhātānumatirha yā | aṃgirobhiḥ sahaitāḥ syuruktamaṃtrāśca devatāḥ ‖ (*)
(Bṛ. Pe. 8-12) **NOTE**

Asunīti, Iḷā, the Āptyas, Vidhātṛ, Anumati together with the Aṅgirases: all these are reckoned as deities of the said mantras. (The deities indicated here are rarely praised. All of these are praised in the Vaiśvadeva sūktas.)

### Printed p. 342

वैश्वानरो हि सुपर्णो विवस्वान् प्रजापतिदौर्यः सुधन्वा नगोह्यः । अपांनपादर‍्यमा वातजूतिरिळस्पतिश्चापि रथस्पतिश्च ॥ ( (*vaiśvānaro hi suparṇo vivasvān prajāpatidauryaḥ sudhanvā nagohyaḥ | apāṃnapādaryamā vātajūtiriḷaspatiścāpi rathaspatiśca ‖ (*)
(Bṛ. De. 8-12)

Vaiśvānara, Suparṇa, Vivasvān, Prajāpati, Dyauḥ, Sudhanvan, Nagohya, Apāṃ Napāt, Aryaman, Vātajūti, Iḷaspati and Rathaspati.

ऋभवः पर्जन्यः पर्वता ग्नाश्च दक्षो भगो देवपत्नीर्दिशश्च । आदित्या रुद्राः पितरोऽथ साध्या निपातिनो वैश्वदेवेषु सर्वॆ ॥ (*ṛbhavaḥ parjanyaḥ parvatā gnāśca dakṣo bhago devapatnīrdiśaśca | ādityā rudrāḥ pitaro 'tha sādhyā nipātino vaiśvadeveṣu sarvè ‖*)
(Bṛ. De. 8-128)

The Ṛbhus, Parjanya, the mountains, the deities called Gnā, Dakṣa, Bhaga, the wives of the gods, the deities of the directions, the Ādityas, the Rudras, the Fathers and the Sādhyas: all these are deities who are incidentally indicated and prayed to in the Vaiśvadeva sūkta.

In sum, in answer to the question what the Vaiśvadeva sūktas are, the following sentence showing their general mark is cited.

अस्तु शौषड्वैश्वदेवं तृतीयं वैश्वदेवं स्याद्भहुदेवतेषु । (*astu śauṣaḍvaiśvadevaṃ tṛtīyaṃ vaiśvadevaṃ syādbhahudevateṣu |*)
(Bṛ. Je. 4-7) **NOTE**

The general rule is that wherever, as seen in sūktas such as अस्य वामस्य पलितस्य (*asya vāmasya palitasya*) (1-164), there is praise addressed to many deities, they are to be determined as Vaiśvadeva sūktas. But the over-extension and the non-coverage of this rule have been explained in the sentences above. There are many such Vaiśvadeva sūktas in the Ṛk-saṃhitā. Of these, some are separate sūktas, and some are mantras included in sūktas addressed to other deities. They are as follows:

1-3-7 to 9; 1-14; 1-23-10 to 12; 1-89; 1-90; 1-105 to 107; 1-122; 1-139-1; 11; 1-164.1 to 41; 1-186; 2.29; 2.31; 2-41-13 to 15; 3-20-1 to 5; 3.54 to 57; 4.255; 5.26.9; 4.41; 5.42.1 to 10, 12 to 18: 5-43 to 45; 5.46.1 to 6; 5-47 to 51; 6-21.9, 11; 6-49 to 52; 7-34.1 to 15, 18 to 25; 7-35 to 37; 7-39; 7-40; 7.42; 7.43; 7.50-3; 8-25-10 to 12: 8-27 to 30; 8.54-3, 4; 8-58-2, 3; 8.69-11; 8-83; 10-31; 10-32; 10-33-1; 10-35; 10-36; 10-56; 10.57; 10-61; 10-62.7; 10.63-1 to 14, 17; 10-64.66; 10-92; 10-93; 10.100; 10-109; 10-114; 10-126; 10.128; 10.137; 10-141; 10-155.5; 10-157; 10-165; 10-181,

### Printed p. 343

Viśvedevas or Agni: 9.67-27;
Viśvedevas or Aṅgirases: 10.62-1 to 6;
Viśvedevas or Indra: 1-121;
Viśvedevas or the priests: 8-58-1; 10-101;
Viśvedevas or the sacrificial posts: 3-8-8:

The names of the sages who received the vision of these sūktas and praised and prayed to the Viśvedevas are given in the following sentences.

मेधातिथिरगस्यस्तु बृहदुक्को मनुर्गयः । ऋजिश्वा वसुकर्णश्च शार्यातो गोतमो लुशः ॥ स्वस्तात्रेयः परुच्छेपः कक्षीवान् गाथिनौर्वशौ । नाभाकश्चैव निर्दिष्टो दुवस्युर्ममतासुतः ॥ विहव्यः कश्यप ऋषिरवत्सारश्च नाम यः । वामदेवो मधुच्छंदाः पार्थो दक्षसुतादितिः ॥ जुहूर्ग्नत्समदश्चर्षिर्देवाः सप्तर्षयश्च ये । यमोऽग्निस्तापसः कुत्सः कुसीदी चित्र एव च ॥ बंधुप्रभृतयश्चैव चत्वारो भ्रातरः पृथक् । विष्णुश्च नेजमेषश्च नाम्मा संवननश्च यः ॥ एते तु सर्व एवास्य विश्वैः स्वैः कर्मजैर्गुणैः । समस्तॆरथ च व्यस्थैः पृथक्कूक्तेषु तुष्टुवुः ॥ (*medhātithiragasyastu bṛhadukko manurgayaḥ | ṛjiśvā vasukarṇaśca śāryāto gotamo luśaḥ ‖ svastātreyaḥ parucchepaḥ kakṣīvān gāthinaurvaśau | nābhākaścaiva nirdiṣṭo duvasyurmamatāsutaḥ ‖ vihavyaḥ kaśyapa ṛṣiravatsāraśca nāma yaḥ | vāmadevo madhucchaṃdāḥ pārtho dakṣasutāditiḥ ‖ juhūrgnatsamadaścarṣirdevāḥ saptarṣayaśca ye | yamo 'gnistāpasaḥ kutsaḥ kusīdī citra eva ca ‖ baṃdhuprabhṛtayaścaiva catvāro bhrātaraḥ pṛthak | viṣṇuśca nejameṣaśca nāmmā saṃvananaśca yaḥ ‖ ete tu sarva evāsya viśvaiḥ svaiḥ karmajairguṇaiḥ | samastèratha ca vyasthaiḥ pṛthakkūkteṣu tuṣṭuvuḥ ‖*)
(Bṛ. De. 3-55 to 60).

Medhātithi, Agastya, Bṛhaduktha, Manu, Gaya, Ṛjiśvan, Vasukarṇa, Śāryāta, Gotama, Luśa, Svastyātreya, Parucchepa, Kakṣīvān, Viśvāmitra the son of Gāthin, Vasiṣṭha the son of Urvaśī, Nābhāka, Duvasya, Dīrghatamas the son of Mamatā, Vihavya, Kaśyapa, Avatsāra, Vāmadeva, Madhucchandas, Pārtha, Aditi the daughter of Dakṣa, Juhū, Gṛtsamada, the seven sages, Yama, Agni, Tāpasa, Kutsa, Kusīdī, Trita **NOTE**, Bandhu and his brothers, Viṣṇu, Nejameṣa and Saṃvanana.

All these sages mentioned above have praised Agni in various ways in separate sūktas.

एते तु सर्व एवास्य विश्वैः स्वैः कर्मजैर्गुणैः । समस्तैरथ चव्यस्तैः पृथक्सूक्तेषु तुष्टुवुः ॥ (*ete tu sarva evāsya viśvaiḥ svaiḥ karmajairguṇaiḥ | samastairatha cavyastaiḥ pṛthaksūkteṣu tuṣṭuvuḥ ‖*)
(Bṛ. De. 3.60)

### Printed p. 344

The description of the world-wide qualities of the god Agni, produced by his manifold deeds, is the description of the Viśvedevas. That is, since all the different deities are only transformations of Agni, the praise of many deities is the praise of the many qualities of Agni. The purport is that all the sages indicated above, in their own individual sūktas, have fully praised only the world-wide qualities of the god Agni, who takes the form of the Viśvedevas. This same view is stated clearly in some other places too. For example:

आग्नेयं सूक्तमैभिर्यद्वैश्वदेवमिहोच्यते । तद्विश्वलिंगं गायत्रं वैश्वदेवेषु शस्यते ॥ (*āgneyaṃ sūktamaibhiryadvaiśvadevamihocyate | tadviśvaliṃgaṃ gāyatraṃ vaiśvadeveṣu śasyate ‖*)
(Bṛ. De. 3-33)

In the mantra ऐभिरग्ने (*aibhiragne*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-14), although Agni is the one addressed, the deity is the Viśvedevas. Because it is composed in the Gāyatrī metre, the mantras of this sūkta are of the nature of विश्वलिंग (*viśvaliṃga*) and also praise world-wide qualities. For that very reason,

यद्यॆभिरिति चैतस्मिन् वैश्वदेवेऽग्निमर्चति (*yadyèbhiriti caitasmin vaiśvadeve 'gnimarcati*)
(3-51)

this same idea is stated again and again. Moreover, explaining the relation of the Agnihotra to the Viśvedevas, the Aitareya Brāhmaṇa says:

यो ना अग्निहोत्रं वैश्वदेवं षोळशकलं पशुषु प्रतिष्ठितं वेद वैश्वदेवेनाग्निहोत्रेण षोळशकलेन पशुषु प्रतिष्ठितेन राध्योति ॥ (*yo nā agnihotraṃ vaiśvadevaṃ ṣoḷaśakalaṃ paśuṣu pratiṣṭhitaṃ veda vaiśvadevenāgnihotreṇa ṣoḷaśakalena paśuṣu pratiṣṭhitena rādhyoti ‖*)
(Ai. Brā. 5-26)

It praises the one who knows that the Agnihotra, which has sixteen parts and is established in cattle, is Vaiśvadeva, and who performs this Agnihotra whose deity is the Viśvedevas: he becomes endowed with wealth such as cattle. And for that very reason,

यस्याग्निहोत्रमदर्शमपौर्णमासमचातुर्मास्यमनाग्रयणमतिथिवर्जितं च । अहुतमवैश्वदेवमविधिना हुतमासप्तमांस्तस्य लोकान् हिनस्ति ॥ (*yasyāgnihotramadarśamapaurṇamāsamacāturmāsyamanāgrayaṇamatithivarjitaṃ ca | ahutamavaiśvadevamavidhinā hutamāsaptamāṃstasya lokān hinasti ‖*)
(Muṇḍaka. 1.2 3)

it praises the pre-eminence of the Vaiśvadeva rite by saying that if the Agnihotra is without rites such as the Paurṇamāsa, the Cāturmāsya, the Āgrayaṇa and the Vaiśvadeva, it causes a loss to a man's future in all seven worlds. All the Viśvedevas are included in Agni; or else the natures of all the other deities are transformations of Agni; and for that very reason there is an identity between the Agnihotra and the Vaiśvadeva rite, an identity-

### Printed p. 345

relationship. This point can be understood from the वैश्वदेवनिविन्मंत्र (*vaiśvadevanivinmaṃtra*). In the same way, in some other mantras too, it is stated plainly that the Viśvedevas are contained in Agni. For example:

त्वमग्ने वरुणो जायसे यत्त्वं मित्रो भवसि यत्समिद्धः । तै विश्वे सहसस्पुत्र देवास्त्व मिंद्रो दाशुषे मर्त्याय ॥ (*tvamagne varuṇo jāyase yattvaṃ mitro bhavasi yatsamiddhaḥ | tai viśve sahasasputra devāstva miṃdro dāśuṣe martyāya ‖*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-3-1)

O Agni, you are Varuṇa, you are Mitra, and in you all the Viśvedevas are contained; by the oblation offered in you they all live. The oblation that is offered to you is what reaches them, and what is offered to all of them is what reaches you; such is the praise. But who are they, and how many are they? This is stated in the वैश्वदेवनिविन्मंत्र (*vaiśvadevanivinmaṃtra*).

श्रीणि शता त्री सहस्राण्यग्निं त्रिंशच्च देवा नव चासपर्यन् । औक्षन् घृतैरस्त्रणन्बर्हिरस्मा आदिद्दोतारं नृसादयंत ॥ (*śrīṇi śatā trī sahasrāṇyagniṃ triṃśacca devā nava cāsaparyan | aukṣan ghṛtairastraṇanbarhirasmā ādiddotāraṃ nṛsādayaṃta ‖*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-9-9).

This is regarded as the Vaiśvadeva nivid mantra. In it the number of the deities is stated. The Bṛhadāraṇyaka explains this same number as the number of the Viśvedevas. Explaining the number of all these deities contained in Agni:

अथ हैनं विदग्धः शाकल्यः पप्रच्छ कति देवा याज्ञवल्कॆति स हैतयैव निविदा प्रतिपेदे यावंतो वैश्वदेवस्य निविदुच्यंते त्रयश्च त्री च शता त्रयश्च श्री च सहस्रेत्योमिति । (*atha hainaṃ vidagdhaḥ śākalyaḥ papraccha kati devā yājñavalkèti sa haitayaiva nividā pratipede yāvaṃto vaiśvadevasya nividucyaṃte trayaśca trī ca śatā trayaśca śrī ca sahasretyomiti |*)
(Bṛ. U. 3-9-1)

it is stated. In the commentary on the mantra Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-9-9 quoted above, Sāyaṇa has cited and explained this Upaniṣad passage. Here there is a conversation between Vidagdha Śākalya and Yājñavalkya on the numbers of the deities.

Vidagdha Śākalya: Yājñavalkya, how many deities are there in all? Yājñavalkya: The number that is indicated in the Viśvedeva nivid mantra is the number of the Viśvedevas. That is, three hundred and three and three thousand and three. In all, three thousand three hundred and six. But this number differs a little from the number stated elsewhere. According to that, 11 x 3 = 33 must be added. That is, in all three thousand three hundred and thirty-nine. This is the number of the Viśvedevas contained in Agni. The worship of these Viśvedevas is the worship of Agni, and the worship of Agni is the worship of the Viśvedevas.

### Printed p. 346

Among such descriptions, to say that the Viśvedevas are contained in Agni is one way; there are also some passages where the Viśvedevas are described as being of the nature of the five peoples. The five peoples, in such contexts, are the gods, men, yakṣas, gandharvas and fathers. This point is explained thus in the section on the Vaiśvadeva śastra.

पांचजन्यं वा एतदुक्थं यद्वैश्वदेवं सर्वेषां वा एतत्पंचजनानामुक्थं देवमनुष्याणां गंधर्वाप्सरसां सर्पाणां च पित्थणां चैतेषां वा एतत्पंचजनानामुक्थं सर्व एनं पंचजना विदुः । (*pāṃcajanyaṃ vā etadukthaṃ yadvaiśvadevaṃ sarveṣāṃ vā etatpaṃcajanānāmukthaṃ devamanuṣyāṇāṃ gaṃdharvāpsarasāṃ sarpāṇāṃ ca pitthaṇāṃ caiteṣāṃ vā etatpaṃcajanānāmukthaṃ sarva enaṃ paṃcajanā viduḥ |*)
(Ai. Brā. 3-21)

The Vaiśvadeva rite is of the five peoples; that is, it concerns the people of five groups, namely gods, men, gandharvas, apsarases and fathers. There is praise of the Vaiśvadeva rite, saying that one who knows this principle is skilled in this rite, and there is description of the Viśvedevas. Other occasions that praise the greatness of this rite will be given later.

Thus, that the Viśvedevas are contained in Agni, who is of the nature of all the deities, is one situation; that all beings, gods, men and the rest, are contained in the Viśvedevas is another situation. Besides these, a third kind of description is also well known in the Vedic passages. This third is that the Viśvedevas are a power-designation showing the distinction of varṇas in the regulation of creation. According to this description, Agni, Indra and the Viśvedevas are the three particular deities who reflect three descriptions. The order of division of the sūktas in the Ṛk-saṃhitā also suggests this. Except for the eighth and ninth maṇḍalas, in all the remaining maṇḍalas there are, at the beginning, sūktas praising Agni, and then those praising Indra. After that, sūktas praising the Viśvedevas generally follow. Later, in the Brāhmaṇas and the rest, identity is shown, in order, between Agni, Indra and the Viśvedevas on the one hand and the three varṇas, Brāhmaṇa, Kṣatriya and Vaiśya, on the other. A few examples make this point clear.

प्रजापतिर्वाव जेष्ठस्स ह्येतेनाग्रेयजत प्रजापतिरकामयत प्र जायेयेति स मुखतस्त्रिवृतं निरमिमीत तमग्निर्देवतान्नसृजत गायत्रीच्छंदो रथतरग्‌ंसाम ब्राह्मणो मनुष्याणामजः पशूनां . . . ॥ (*prajāpatirvāva jeṣṭhassa hyetenāgreyajata prajāpatirakāmayata pra jāyeyeti sa mukhatastrivṛtaṃ niramimīta tamagnirdevatānnasṛjata gāyatrīcchaṃdo rathataragṃsāma brāhmaṇo manuṣyāṇāmajaḥ paśūnāṃ . . . ‖*)
(Tai. Saṃ. 7-1-1-3) **NOTE**

Because he performed the sacrificial rite first of all, Prajāpati is the greatest of all. Then he desired, "May I obtain offspring." Then he brought forth the trivṛt from his mouth. From it, Agni

### Printed p. 347

the deity, the Gāyatrī metre, the Rathantara sāman, among men the Brāhmaṇas and among animals the goat came into being. Because they were born from the mouth, all of them obtained pre-eminence.

उरसो बाहुभ्यां पंचदशं निरमिमीत तमिंद्रो देवतान्नसृज्यत तिष्टुप्छंदो बृहत् साम राजन्यो मनुष्याणामविः पशूनां तस्मात्ते वीर्यावंतो वीर्याद्यसृज्यंत । (*uraso bāhubhyāṃ paṃcadaśaṃ niramimīta tamiṃdro devatānnasṛjyata tiṣṭupchaṃdo bṛhat sāma rājanyo manuṣyāṇāmaviḥ paśūnāṃ tasmātte vīryāvaṃto vīryādyasṛjyaṃta |*)
(Tai. Saṃ. 7-1-1-4, 5)

From the chest and the arms he brought forth the pañcadaśa. From it were created the deity Indra, the Triṣṭubh metre, the Bṛhat sāman, among men the Kṣatriyas and among animals the rams. Because they were born from the strength of the arm, all of them became signs of valour.

मध्यतस्सप्तदशं निरमिमीत तं विश्वेदेवा देवता अन्वसृज्यंत जगतीछंदो वैरूपग्‌ंसाम वैश्यो मनुष्याणां गावः पशूनां तस्मात्त आद्या अन्नधानाद्दस्यृज्यंत तस्माद्भूयाग्‌ंसोऽन्येऽभ्यो भूयिष्ठा हि देवता अन्वसृज्यंत ॥ (*madhyatassaptadaśaṃ niramimīta taṃ viśvedevā devatā anvasṛjyaṃta jagatīchaṃdo vairūpagṃsāma vaiśyo manuṣyāṇāṃ gāvaḥ paśūnāṃ tasmātta ādyā annadhānāddasyṛjyaṃta tasmādbhūyāgṃso 'nye 'bhyo bhūyiṣṭhā hi devatā anvasṛjyaṃta ‖*)
(Kṛ. Saṃ. 7-1-1-5) **NOTE**

Then, from his middle part he brought forth the saptadaśa stoma. From it the Viśvedevas were created as deities. Among metres the Jagatī, among sāmans the Vairūpa, among the people the Vaiśyas, and among animals the cows came into being. Because all these were born from the store of food, they became rich in food and fit for enjoyment. Because they are connected with the Viśvedevas, their number too became very great. In this way the order of origin of Agni, Indra and the Viśvedevas, and the different kinds of their powers, are shown. The same point is explained in the Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa as stated below.

क्षत्रं ना इंद्राग्नी । विशो विश्वेदेवा यत्र वै क्षत्रमुज्जयत्यन्वाभक्ता वै तत्र विड्वद्विश्वान्देवानन्वाभजतां तस्मादेष वैश्वदेवश्चरुर्भवति । (*kṣatraṃ nā iṃdrāgnī | viśo viśvedevā yatra vai kṣatramujjayatyanvābhaktā vai tatra viḍvadviśvāndevānanvābhajatāṃ tasmādeṣa vaiśvadevaścarurbhavati |*)
(Śa. Brā. 2-4-3-6)

Indra and Agni are of the nature of the kṣatra. The Viśvedevas are of the nature of the viś (the common people). Wherever the Kṣatriyas win victory, the Vaiśyas too receive a share there. For this very reason it is said that the Viśvedevas also take part in the victory of Indra and Agni. It can be said that this division of the varṇas exists in a certain way by nature. For Agni, bringing the gods to the sacrifice in the form of the sacrificial fire, carrying the oblation to the gods and building the relation between gods and men, stands in the form of the priest (purohita). Sharp radiance and brilliance are also marks of the Brāhmaṇa. Indra, who always fights with the Asuras and the like and wins victory, possesses

### Printed p. 348

the natural kṣatra power. Just as the rest of the people, other than these, are called Vaiśyas, so in this division, in which the gods other than Indra and Agni belong to the group of the Vaiśyas, it is understood that the Viśvedevas are different from Indra and Agni, and that they do not have the radiance or the valour that those two have. According to this division their shares in the three savanas are fixed. This order of division can be seen in the Saṃhitā and Brāhmaṇa passages.

प्रजापतिर्वै यज्ञं छंदासि देवेभ्यो भागधेयानि व्यभजत्स गायत्रीमेवाग्नये वसुभ्यः प्रातःसवनेऽभजत्रिष्टुभमिंद्राय रुद्रॆभ्यो मध्यंदिने जगतीं विश्वेभ्यो देवेभ्य आदित्येभ्यस्तृतीयसवने............॥ (*prajāpatirvai yajñaṃ chaṃdāsi devebhyo bhāgadheyāni vyabhajatsa gāyatrīmevāgnaye vasubhyaḥ prātaḥsavane 'bhajatriṣṭubhamiṃdrāya rudrèbhyo madhyaṃdine jagatīṃ viśvebhyo devebhya ādityebhyastṛtīyasavane............‖*)
(I. Bā. 3-15) **NOTE**

Prajāpati divided the shares of the sacrifice and the metres among the gods. In the morning savana he gave the Gāyatrī to Agni and the Vasus; in the midday savana the Triṣṭubh to Indra and the Rudras; in the third savana the Jagatī to the Viśvedevas and the Ādityas. This same passage is somewhat different in the Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa. It is as follows.

अथ प्रातः । आग्नेयः पुरोडाशो भवत्येंद्रग्‌ंसांनाय्यं प्रातःसवन मेवास्याग्नेयः पुरोडाश आग्नेयग्‌हि प्रातःसवनमथैंद्रग्‌ंसां. नाय्कं माध्यंदिनमेवास्य तत्सवनमैंद्रग्‌हि माध्यंदिनग्‌ंसवनं । (*atha prātaḥ | āgneyaḥ puroḍāśo bhavatyeṃdragṃsāṃnāyyaṃ prātaḥsavana mevāsyāgneyaḥ puroḍāśa āgneyaghi prātaḥsavanamathaiṃdragṃsāṃ. nāykaṃ mādhyaṃdinamevāsya tatsavanamaiṃdraghi mādhyaṃdinagṃsavanaṃ |*)
(Śa. Brā. 2-4-2-12)
(Sa. 83, 3-4-2-1) **NOTE**

In the morning an Āgneya puroḍāśa and the Aindra sāṃnāyya are offered; the morning savana is pleasing to Agni. In the same way the midday savana is pleasing to Indra.

अथ यत्पूर्वेद्युः । ऐंद्राग्नेन यजतेऽमावास्यायां तृतीयसवनमेवास्य तद्वैश्वदेवं वै तृतीयसवनमिंद्राग्नी वै विश्वदेवाः ॥ (*atha yatpūrvedyuḥ | aiṃdrāgnena yajate 'māvāsyāyāṃ tṛtīyasavanamevāsya tadvaiśvadevaṃ vai tṛtīyasavanamiṃdrāgnī vai viśvadevāḥ ‖*)
(Sa. Barā. 2-4-13) **NOTE**

The puroḍāśa connected with Indra and Agni that is offered on the new-moon day is the third savana, that of the Viśvedevas. In this passage, which describes Indra and Agni as the Viśvedevas, the identity of Indra and Agni with the Viśvedevas is shown.

Besides this, a historical episode showing that the Viśvedevas have pre-eminence in the third savana is described in the Aitareya Brāhmaṇa in the following way.

### Printed p. 349

देवा वै यज्ञमतन्वत तांस्तन्वानानसुरा अभ्यायन् यज्ञवेशसमेषां करिष्याम इति.... .... .... .... .... .... .... .... ते वै पुरस्तादपहता असुराः पश्चात्परीत्य प्राविशंस्ते देवाः प्रतिबुध्य विश्वान्देवानात्मानं पश्चात्तृतीयसवने पत्याहंस्ते विश्वैरेव देवैरात्मभिः पश्चात्तृतीयसवनेऽसुररक्षांस्यपाघ्नत तथैवृतदद्यजमाना विश्वैरेव देवैरात्मभिः पश्चात्तृतीयसवनेऽसुररक्षांस्यपघ्नते तस्माद्वैश्वदेवं तृतीयसवनं ॥ (*devā vai yajñamatanvata tāṃstanvānānasurā abhyāyan yajñaveśasameṣāṃ kariṣyāma iti.... .... .... .... .... .... .... .... te vai purastādapahatā asurāḥ paścātparītya prāviśaṃste devāḥ pratibudhya viśvāndevānātmānaṃ paścāttṛtīyasavane patyāhaṃste viśvaireva devairātmabhiḥ paścāttṛtīyasavane 'surarakṣāṃsyapāghnata tathaivṛtadadyajamānā viśvaireva devairātmabhiḥ paścāttṛtīyasavane 'surarakṣāṃsyapaghnate tasmādvaiśvadevaṃ tṛtīyasavanaṃ ‖*)
(I. Prā. 6-4) **NOTE**

When the gods tried to perform the sacrifice, the Asuras entered, thinking, "We shall create an obstacle to it", and tried to do so. Then, in the morning savana, with the help of Mitra and Varuṇa, they drove away the Asuras who had entered from the south. Indra became the guardian of the middle part, and Indra and Agni together became the guardians of the northern quarter. Agni became the guardian of the front. The Asuras, who had no opening to enter from the front, began to enter from behind. Then the gods appointed the Viśvedevas there as guardians in the third savana. With their help they drove away the Asuras who were entering from behind. For this very reason the Viśvedevas have pre-eminence in the third savana. One who knows this truth becomes able to drive away the Asuras.

Thus, while in the Brāhmaṇas and elsewhere the order is Agni, Indra, the Viśvedevas, in the Upaniṣads the order is the Vasus, Rudras, Ādityas and Viśvedevas. It is as follows.

ब्रह्मवादिनो वदंति यद्वसूनां प्रातःसवनं रुद्राणां माध्यंदिनं सवनमादित्यानां च विश्वेषां च देवानां तृतीयसवनं । (*brahmavādino vadaṃti yadvasūnāṃ prātaḥsavanaṃ rudrāṇāṃ mādhyaṃdinaṃ savanamādityānāṃ ca viśveṣāṃ ca devānāṃ tṛtīyasavanaṃ |*)
(Bhā. U. 2-24-1) **NOTE**

The knowers of Brahman have said that the morning savana is placed with the Vasus, the midday savana with the Rudras, and the third savana with the Ādityas and the Viśvedevas.

अथ जुहोति नम आदित्येभ्यश्च विश्वेभ्यश्च देवेभ्यो दिविक्षिद्द्यो लोकक्षिद्द्यो लोकं मे यजमानाय विंदत । (*atha juhoti nama ādityebhyaśca viśvebhyaśca devebhyo divikṣiddyo lokakṣiddyo lokaṃ me yajamānāya viṃdata |*)
(Bhā. U. 2-24-15) **NOTE**

तस्मा आदित्याश्च विश्वे च देवास्त्रीयसवनं संप्रयच्छंतेष ह वै यज्ञस्य मात्रां वेद य एवं वेद य एवं वेद ॥ (*tasmā ādityāśca viśve ca devāstrīyasavanaṃ saṃprayacchaṃteṣa ha vai yajñasya mātrāṃ veda ya evaṃ veda ya evaṃ veda ‖*)
(Bhā. U. 2-24-16) **NOTE**

### Printed p. 350

Then: homage to the Ādityas, homage to the Viśvedevas, homage to these gods who dwell in the atmosphere and on the earth. "Prepare a world for me, the sacrificer, to dwell in", when he prays thus, the Ādityas and the Viśvedevas favour him with the third savana.

In the time of the rites in the form of the pṛṣṭha ṣaḍaha too, the Aitareya Brāhmaṇa describes that, in the same order, Agni, Indra and the Viśvedevas have pre-eminence on the first, the second and the third day.

(1) अग्निर्वै देवता प्रथममहर्वहति त्रिवृत्स्तोमो रथंतरं साम गायत्रीच्छंदः । (*agnirvai devatā prathamamaharvahati trivṛtstomo rathaṃtaraṃ sāma gāyatrīcchaṃdaḥ |*)
(Ai. Brā. 4-29)

(2) इंद्रो वै देवता द्वितीयमहर्वहति पंचदशस्तोमो बृहत्साम त्रिष्टुप्छंदः । (*iṃdro vai devatā dvitīyamaharvahati paṃcadaśastomo bṛhatsāma triṣṭupchaṃdaḥ |*)
(Ai. Brā. 4-31)

(3) विश्वे वै देवा देवतास्त्रीयमहर्वहंति सप्तदशः स्तोमो वैरूपं साम जगतीछंदः । (*viśve vai devā devatāstrīyamaharvahaṃti saptadaśaḥ stomo vairūpaṃ sāma jagatīchaṃdaḥ |*)
(I. Prā. 5-1) **NOTE**

In the pṛṣṭha ṣaḍahas, on the first day the deity Agni is joined with the trivṛt stoma, the Rathantara sāman and the Gāyatrī metre. On the second day the deity Indra is joined with the pañcadaśa stoma, the Bṛhat sāman and the Triṣṭubh metre. On the third day the Viśvedevas are joined with the saptadaśa stoma, the Vairūpa sāman and the Jagatī metre. Thus the third place is assigned to the Viśvedevas. In this way the views expressed about the position and rank of the Viśvedevas have been given. But because these same Viśvedevas reflect various principles, they are described in many ways in many places. Only a few of these may be taken here as examples.

इमे वै प्राणाः । मनोज्ञाता मनोयुजो दक्षक्रतवो वागेवाग्निः प्राणोदानौ मित्रावरुणौ चक्षुरादित्यः श्रोत्रं विश्वेदेवाः । (*ime vai prāṇāḥ | manojñātā manoyujo dakṣakratavo vāgevāgniḥ prāṇodānau mitrāvaruṇau cakṣurādityaḥ śrotraṃ viśvedevāḥ |*)
(Śa. Brā. 3-2-2-13)

These life-breaths, endowed with consciousness, are born of the mind. Agni is speech. Mitra and Varuṇa are the in-breath and the out-breath. Āditya is the principle of the eye. The Viśvedevas are the principle of the organ of hearing.

### Printed p. 351

वैश्वदेवं ना अंतरिक्षं तद्यदेनेनेमाः प्रजाः प्राणत्यश्चोदानत्यश्चांतरिक्ष मनुचरंति तेन वैश्वदेवं वषट्कृते जुहोति ॥ (*vaiśvadevaṃ nā aṃtarikṣaṃ tadyadenenemāḥ prajāḥ prāṇatyaścodānatyaścāṃtarikṣa manucaraṃti tena vaiśvadevaṃ vaṣaṭkṛte juhoti ‖*)
(Śa. Brā. 3-8-2-32)

Because it is the means for the movement of sound, which belongs to the organ of hearing, and because all beings move about in the atmosphere itself through the in-breath and the out-breath, the Viśvedevas, who are the presiding deities of all these, are the ones who reflect the principle of the atmosphere.

एतस्मै वै गृह्णाति य एष तपति विश्वभ्यो ह्येना देवेभ्यो गृह्णाति रश्मयो हस्य विश्वे देवास्तस्मादाह हविष्मां अस्तु सूर इति ॥ (*etasmai vai gṛhṇāti ya eṣa tapati viśvabhyo hyenā devebhyo gṛhṇāti raśmayo hasya viśve devāstasmādāha haviṣmāṃ astu sūra iti ‖*)
(Śa. Brā. 3-9-2-12)

All the oblations that are offered to Sūrya are as if offered to the Viśvedevas. How? All the rays of the sun are forms of the principle of the Viśvedevas. Therefore the sun's rays, which are the objects of movement, and the atmosphere, which is the means of movement, are both emblems of the Viśvedevas. For this very reason they are also described as the makers of the quarters.

विश्वे देवा वैश्वानरा दिशः कृत्वा तास्वेतामाशिषमाशासत । ( (*viśve devā vaiśvānarā diśaḥ kṛtvā tāsvetāmāśiṣamāśāsata | (*)
(Śa. Brā. 6-5-2-6)

The Viśvedevas made all the quarters and blessed them. Because they are the presiding deities of the quarters and of the atmosphere that pervades them, they are described as the regulators of the seasons and the rest, and accordingly as the producers of rain.

ऋतवो वै विश्वेदेवास्तदेनां विश्वैर्देवैर्यतुभिः संविधानः । (*ṛtavo vai viśvedevāstadenāṃ viśvairdevairyatubhiḥ saṃvidhānaḥ |*)
(Śa. Brā. 7-1-143)

विश्वे च वै देवा मरुतश्च वर्षस्येशत ऊर्जस्वती.........। (*viśve ca vai devā marutaśca varṣasyeśata ūrjasvatī.........|*)
(Śa. Brā. 7-2-2-10)

By such passages their all-pervasiveness and exceeding power are praised. Because of such exceeding power, in praise of it,

विश्वे वै देवा देवानां यशस्वितमा यश एनास्मिन्धाति ॥ (*viśve vai devā devānāṃ yaśasvitamā yaśa enāsmindhāti ‖*)
(Śa. Brā. 13-1-2-8)

there are such eulogies as: among all the gods the Viśvedevas are the most glorious. Thus, in some places showing identity with Indra and Agni, and in some

### Printed p. 352

places describing an individual form, they are praised in many ways. If all these descriptions are examined, it can be said that, on the whole, the Viśvedevas are deities who reflect all the powers in the universe, or that the very form of Brahman, which contains all principles, is the Viśvedeva deity. How? Thus:

तद्वै तत्पश्यन्नृषिरभ्यनूनाद । भूतं भविष्यत्प्रस्तौमि महद्ब्रह्मैकमक्षरं बहु ब्रह्मैकमक्षरमित्येतद्धेवाक्षरग्ं सर्वे देवाः सर्वाणि भूतान्यभिसंपद्यंते तदेतद्ब्रह्म च क्षत्रं चाग्निरेव ब्रह्मेंद्रः क्षत्रमिंद्राग्नी वै विश्वे देवा विडु विश्वेदेवास्तदेतद्ब्रह्म क्षेत्रं विट् ॥ (*tadvai tatpaśyannṛṣirabhyanūnāda | bhūtaṃ bhaviṣyatprastaumi mahadbrahmaikamakṣaraṃ bahu brahmaikamakṣaramityetaddhevākṣaragṃ sarve devāḥ sarvāṇi bhūtānyabhisaṃpadyaṃte tadetadbrahma ca kṣatraṃ cāgnireva brahmeṃdraḥ kṣatramiṃdrāgnī vai viśve devā viḍu viśvedevāstadetadbrahma kṣetraṃ viṭ ‖*)
(Śa. Jā. 10-4-19) **NOTE**

This is the utterance of a seer who had the vision of the great principle. I praise the Brahman principle, which was in the past and will be in the future, which is great, imperishable and of many forms. All the gods and all beings are absorbed into this principle itself. This is the form of brahma, this is the form of kṣatra. Agni is the exponent of the power of brahma. Indra is the exponent of the power of kṣatra. The Viśvedevas are Indra and Agni; but the Viśvedevas are also the exponents of the power of the viś (the common people). Therefore this principle, which contains everything, is the source of all these powers, called brahma, kṣatra and viś.

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> को नु वामिति विंशत्यचं नवमं सूक्तं भौमस्यात्रेरार्षं । षोडशीसप्तदश्यावतिजगत्यौ विंश्येकपदा विराट् शिष्टाः सप्तदश त्रिष्टुभः । विश्वे देवा देवता ।
> तथा चानुक्रातं । को नु विंशतिर्वैश्वदेवं वै तत् षोळश्याद्यतिजगत्यावंतैकपदेति ॥ विनियोगो लैंगिकः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says that the sūkta beginning को नु वां (*ko nu vāṃ*) has twenty Ṛks and is the ninth sūkta; its seer is Atri of the Bhauma line. The sixteenth and seventeenth Ṛks are in the Atijagatī metre, the twentieth is an Ekapadā Virāṭ, and the remaining seventeen are Triṣṭubh. The deity is the Viśvedevas. He supports this with the Anukramaṇī: को नु विंशतिर्वैश्वदेवं वै तत् षोळश्याद्यतिजगत्यावंतैकपदेति ॥ (*ko nu viṃśatirvaiśvadevaṃ vai tat ṣoḷaśyādyatijagatyāvaṃtaikapadeti ‖*). The use (viniyoga) is लैंगिकः (*laiṃgikaḥ*), that is, according to the indication of the Ṛks themselves.

The Anuvāda: This sūkta beginning "Ko nu vām" is the ninth sūkta in the third anuvāka. It has twenty Ṛks. Atri is the seer of this sūkta; the Viśvedevas are the deity; the sixteenth and seventeenth Ṛks of this sūkta are in the Atijagatī metre; the twentieth

### Printed p. 353

Ṛk is in the metre called Ekapadā Virāṭ; the remaining Ṛks are in the Triṣṭubh metre. In the Anukramaṇī it is said नु विंशतिर्वृश्वदेवं वै तत् षोळश्याद्यतिजगत्यावंतैकपदा (*nu viṃśatirvṛśvadevaṃ vai tat ṣoḷaśyādyatijagatyāvaṃtaikapadā*). The use is Laiṅgika (by indication).

Sūkta—41
Maṇḍala—5. Anuvāka—3. Sūkta—41.
Aṣṭaka—4. Adhyāya—2. Varga—13.
Number of Ṛks in the sūkta—20.
Seer—Atri.
Deity—the Viśvedevas.
Metre—1–15 and 18–19 Triṣṭubh; 16–17 Atijagatī; 20 Ekapadā.

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> को नु वां॑ मित्रावरुणावृता॒यन्दि॒वो वा॑ म॒हः पार्थि॑वस्य वा॒ दे ।  
> ऋ॒तस्य॑ वा॒ सद॑सि॒ त्रासी॑थां नो यज्ञाय॒ते वा॑ पशु॒षो न  ॑
> वाजान् ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> कः । नु । वां । मित्रावरुणौ । ऋत्ययन् । दिवः । वा । महः । पार्थिवस्य । वा ।  
> दे ।  
> ऋतस्य । वा । सदसि । त्रासीथां । नः । यज्ञयते । वा । पशुसः । न । नाजान् ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे मित्रावरुणौ देवौ नां युवां को नु कः खलु यजमान ऋतायन्यज्ञमिच्छन् शक्नुयादिति शेषः । तस्मात्कृतं मय्यॆव । दिवः सदसि द्युलोकसंबंधिनि
> स्थाने महो महतः पार्थिवस्य सदस्यृतस्योत्पादकस्यांतरिक्षस्य सदसि । तद्धेतुत्वात्ताच्छब्धं । त्रिषु स्थानेषु नोऽस्मान् त्रासीथां । रक्षतं । वाशब्दत्वार्थे । किंच

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa addresses the two deities Mitra and Varuṇa: which sacrificer, desiring the sacrifice (ऋतायन् (*ṛtāyan*)), could (so he supplies the missing verb) perform it for you? **NOTE** He then glosses दिवः सदसि (*divaḥ sadasi*) as "in the seat, the place belonging to the heaven-world", महो (*maho*) as "of the great", and takes पार्थिवस्य (*pārthivasya*) as the seat of the atmosphere, which gives rise to ṛta, the word being used of it because it is its cause. He explains त्रासीथां (*trāsīthāṃ*) as "protect": protect us in these three places; the word वा (*vā*) is used in the sense of ... **NOTE** Then the block goes on with "moreover" (किंच (*kiṃca*)).

### Printed p. 354

> यज्ञायते दे ना हनिर्दात्रे च मह्यं पशुषो न वाजान् पशुषु सीदतो
> वाजानन्नानि क्षीरदध्यादीनीव । तानि यथा प्रयच्छथः तद्वदवतमिति । यद्वा ।
> नशब्दश्चार्थे । पशुषः पशून्वाजांश्च दत्तमित्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The block continues the gloss: for the one who desires to sacrifice to you and for the giver of the oblation, and for me (यज्ञायते दे ना हनिर्दात्रे च मह्यं (*yajñāyate de nā hanirdātre ca mahyaṃ*)) **NOTE**, give पशुषो न वाजान् (*paśuṣo na vājān*): the foods that dwell in animals, such as milk and curds. As you bestow them, so protect. Or else the word न (*na*) is in the sense of "and": give both animals and foods (पशुषः (*paśuṣaḥ*), वाजांश्च (*vājāṃśca*)).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- मित्रावरुणौ (*mitrāvaruṇau*)—O Mitra and Varuṇa
- नां (*nāṃ*)—with reference to you
- को नु (*ko nu*)—which sacrificer indeed
- ऋतायन् (*ṛtāyan*)—to perform the sacrifice (is he able?)
- दिवः वा सदसि (*divaḥ vā sadasi*)—either from the place of the heaven-world
- महः (*mahaḥ*)—great
- पार्थिवस्य वा (*pārthivasya vā*)—or from the place of the earth
- ऋतस्य वा (*ṛtasya vā*)—or from the place of the atmosphere
- नः (*naḥ*)—us
- त्रासीथां (*trāsīthāṃ*)—protect
- यज्ञायते (*yajñāyate*)—to one who wishes to worship (you) with sacrifice
- दे वा (*de vā*)—and to one who offers the oblation
- पशुष (*paśuṣa*)—animals
- नाजान् (*nājān*)—foods (provide)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Mitra and Varuṇa, what sacrificer, with reference to you, is able to perform a sacrifice that is pleasing to you? Whether you are in the place of the heaven-world, in the great place of the earth, or in the region of the atmosphere, protect us from there. Provide animals and foods to the one who wishes to worship you through the sacrifice and to the one who offers the oblation.

##### English translation
Who, Mitra, and Varuna, desiring to sacrifice to you, (is able to do so) ? do you, whether (abiding) in the region of heaven, or of the vast earth, or of the firmament, protect us, and give to the donor (of the oblation), and to the sacrificer, cattle and food.

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ते नो॑ मि॒त्रो वरु॑णो आर्य॒मायुरिंद्र॑ ऋभु॒क्षा ॒॑ 
> नुरुतो जुषंत ।

### Printed p. 355

> नमोभिर्वा ये दधते सुवृक्तिं स्तोमं रुद्राय मीळुषे  
> सजोषाः ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> ते । नः । मित्रः । वरुणः । अर्यमा । आयुः । इंद्रः । ऋभुक्षाः । मरुतः जुषंत ।  
> नमःभिः । वा । ये । दधते । सुवृक्तिं । स्तोमं । रुद्राय । मीळुषे ।  
> सृजोषाः ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ते मित्रादयो देवा नोऽस्माकं सुवृक्तिं स्तोमं शोभनपापादिवर्जनवत्सोत्रं नमोभिर्वा हविर्भिश्च जुषंत । सेवंतां । ये दधते धारयंत्युक्तलक्षणं
> स्तोत्रं । देवा द्विविधाः स्तोत्रभाजो हविर्भाजश्च । अत्र स्तोत्रभाज उच्यंते ।
> आयुः सततगतिर्वायुरुच्यते । अथ मरुत एव वियुज्य विशेष्यंते । रुद्राय
> माळ्हुषे ॥ तृतीयार्थे चतुर्थी ॥ रुद्रेण सह सजोषाः सजोषसः सह प्रीयमाणाः । तेषां पुत्रत्वादिति भावः । केचन रुद्राय सजोषा इत्येतन्मित्रादिसर्वदेवताविशेषणमिति कथयंति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Those deities, Mitra and the others, are to accept our well-made hymn (सुवृक्तिं स्तोमं (*suvṛktiṃ stomaṃ*), a hymn of praise free from evil and the like) with praises or with oblations; जुषंत (*juṣaṃta*) means "let them enjoy". They are those who hold (दधते (*dadhate*)) the hymn so described. Deities are of two kinds, those who share in praise and those who share in oblations; here those who share in praise are meant. आयुः (*āyuḥ*), the ever-moving, is said to be Vāyu. Then the Maruts are separated off and specified. रुद्राय माळ्हुषे (*rudrāya māḷhuṣe*): the dative is used in the sense of the instrumental, so "together with Rudra, the showerer". सजोषाः (*sajoṣāḥ*) means "pleased together"; the idea is that they are his sons. Some say that "रुद्राय सजोषा (*rudrāya sajoṣā*)" is an epithet of all the deities, Mitra and the rest.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- ये (*ye*)—which deities
- सुवृक्तिं (*suvṛktiṃ*)—well made
- स्तोमं (*stomaṃ*)—the hymn
- दधते (*dadhate*)—those who accept and hold, they also (such sharers in praise); (or) ये (*ye*)—those who
- मीळ्हुषे (*mīḷhuṣe*)—the giver of what is desired
- रुद्राय (*rudrāya*)—together with Rudra
