- सजोषाः (*sajoṣāḥ*)—those who with equal affection together receive the oblation (sharers in oblations); ते (*te*)—all those deities, that is,
- मित्रः (*mitraḥ*)—Mitra
- वरुणः (*varuṇaḥ*)—Varuṇa
- अरमा (*aramā*)—Aryaman
- आयुः (*āyuḥ*)—Vāyu
- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra
- ऋभुक्षाः (*ṛbhukṣāḥ*)—Ṛbhukṣan
- मरुतः (*marutaḥ*)—the Maruts
- नः (*naḥ*)—our
- नमोभिः (*namobhiḥ*)—with praises
- जुषंत (*juṣaṃta*)—let them be pleased, enjoying

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Those deities who have accepted the well-made praises and are famed as sharers in praise, and those who, joined in equal affection with Rudra, the giver of what is desired, receive the oblation together and are renowned as sharers in oblations, that is, Mitra, Varuṇa, Aryaman, Vāyu, Indra, Ṛbhukṣan and the Maruts, may all of them enjoy our praises and be satisfied.

### Printed p. 356

##### English translation
May those divinities, Mitra, Varuna, Aryaman, Ayu, Indra, Ribhukshin, the Maruts who accept pious praise, be pleased by our adorations, partaking of the gratification afforded to Rudra, the showerer (of benefits).

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आ वां॒ येष्ठा॑श्विना हु॒वध्यै॒ वात॑स्य॒ पत्म॒न्रथ्य॑स्य पु॒ष्टौ ।  
> उ॒त वा॑ दि॒वो असु॑राय॒ मन्म॒ प्रांधां॑सीव॒ यज्य॑वे भरध्वं॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आ । वां । येष्णा । अश्विना । हुवध्यै । वातस्य । पत्मन् । रथ्यस्य । पुष्टौ ।  
> उत । वा । दिवः । असुराय । मन्म । प्र । अंधांसिइव । यज्यवे । भरध्वं ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अनयाश्विनौ रुद्रश्चोच्यंते । हे अश्विनाश्विनौ येष्ठा कामानां यंतृतमौ
> नां युवां वातस्य पत्मन् । धर्मसाम्याद्दर्मिग्रहणं । वायुसमानगतेरश्वस्य
> नमने प्राप्तौ रथ्यस्य रथस्य पुष्टौ च निमित्ते सति हुवध्यै । हुवे । आह्वयामि ।
> आह्वातुं प्रभवामि । अथवा वायुवद्युवयोः शीघ्रगमनसाधनरथस्य पोषे च
> निमित्ते सति । उत ना किंचेत्यर्थः । दिवो द्योतमानाय ॥ चतुर्थ्यर्थे षष्ठी ॥
> असुराय प्राणापहर्त्रॆ रुद्राय । यद्वा । द्युलोकसंबंधिनेऽसुराय प्राणदात्रे
> सूर्याय वायवे वा यज्यवे यागसाधकाय मन्म मननीयं स्तोत्रं प्र
> भरध्वं । संपादयत हे ऋत्विजः । अंधांसीवान्नानि हविर्लक्षणानि हवींषि स्तोत्रं
> चेत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

By this verse the Aśvins and Rudra are addressed. O Aśvins, best controllers of desires: you, at the course of the wind (वातस्य पत्मन् (*vātasya patman*)), the possessor being taken by likeness of property, when a horse whose going equals the wind's is reached **NOTE**, and for the nourishment of the chariot (रथ्यस्य रथस्य पुष्टौ (*rathyasya rathasya puṣṭau*)), I call (हुवध्यै (*huvadhyai*): I call; I am able to call). Or else: for the nourishing of your chariot, the means of swift going like the wind. उत वा (*uta vā*) means "and moreover". दिवः (*divaḥ*) means "to the shining one", the genitive being used in the sense of the dative; असुराय (*asurāya*), to Rudra, the taker of life. Or else: to the Asura belonging to the heaven-world, the giver of life, that is, to Sūrya or to Vāyu. यज्ञवे (*yajñave*): to the accomplisher of the sacrifice. O priests, bring forth (भरध्वं (*bharadhvaṃ*): provide) a hymn worthy of reflection (मन्म (*manma*)), and as foods (अंधांसीव (*aṃdhāṃsīva*)) the oblations; that is, both oblations and hymn.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- येष्ठा (*yeṣṭhā*)—those who restrain desires
- अश्विना (*aśvinā*)—O Aśvin deities
- वां (*vāṃ*)—you
- वातस्य (*vātasya*)—of the wind
- पत्मन् (*patman*)—swift like the going
- रथ्यस्य (*rathyasya*)—of the chariot
- पुष्पा (*puṣpā*)—for the increase of its speed
- हुवध्यै (*huvadhyai*)—I pray and call
- उत ना (*uta nā*)—moreover (O priests)

### Printed p. 357

- दिवः (*divaḥ*)—the shining one
- यज्ञवे (*yajñave*)—the accomplisher of the sacrifice
- असुराय (*asurāya*)—Rudra, the taker of life, with reference to whom
- अंधांसीव (*aṃdhāṃsīva*)—as one procures foods in the form of oblations
- मन्म (*manma*)—the hymn worthy of reflection
- प्र भरध्वं (*pra bharadhvaṃ*)—procure

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Aśvin deities, restrainers of desire, we pray to you that your chariot, swift like the going of the wind, may attain increase of its speed towards our sacrifice. O priests, as you procure foods in the form of oblations to offer to Rudra, the shining, the accomplisher of the sacrifice and the taker of life, so procure (recite) a hymn worthy of reflection.

##### English translation
I invoke you, Aswins, the restrainers (of desire), for the acceleration
of your chariot with the swiftness of the wind: (priests) offer praise and
oblations to the celestial destroyer of life, to the accomplisher of the
sacrifice.

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> प्र स॒क्षणो॑ दि॒व्यः कण्व॑होता त्रि॒तो दि॒वः स॒जोषा॒ वातो॑  ॒
> अग्निः ।  
> पू॒षा भगः॑ प्रभृ॒थे वि॒श्वभो॑जा आ॒जिं न ज॑ग्मुरा॒श्व॑श्वतमाः ॥४॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> प्र । सक्षणः । दिव्यः । कण्वहोता । त्रितः । दिवः । सजोषाः । वातः । अग्निः ।  
> पूषा । भगः । प्रभृथे । विश्वभोजाः । आजिं । न । जग्मुः । अश्वश्वंतमाः ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> आत्र लिंगोक्तदेवताः । प्र जग्मुरिति संबंधः । सक्षणो यज्ञं सेवमानः
> शत्रुणां सोढा वा दिव्यो दिवि भवः कण्वहोता । कण्णा ऋषयो मेधाविनो ना

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The deities here are those named by their marks (liṅga). The construction is प्र जग्मुः (*pra jagmuḥ*), "they go forth". सक्षणः (*sakṣaṇaḥ*) is one who attends the sacrifice or one who overcomes enemies; दिव्यः (*divyaḥ*) is one born in heaven; कण्वहोता (*kaṇvahotā*): the Kaṇvas are seers, the wise,

### Printed p. 358

> होतार आह्वातारो यस्य स तथोक्तः । त्रितस्त्रिषु क्षित्यादिस्थानेषु तायमानो
> दिवः सूर्येण सह सजोषाः समानगतिः समानप्रीतिर्वा वात उक्तलक्षणको
> नायुरग्निः पूषा पोषक एतन्नामा भगश्च प्रभृथे प्रभरणवत्यस्मिन्यज्ञ उक्ताः
> सर्वदेवा विश्वभोजा विश्वरक्षकाः कृत्सात्तारो वा आश्वश्वतमाः प्रकृष्टगमनाश्ववतां
> श्रेष्ठाः संत आजिं न संग्राममिव प्र जग्मुः । प्रगच्छंति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

who are the hotṛs, the callers, of that one; so he is described. त्रितः (*tritaḥ*) is the one spread out in the three places beginning with the earth; दिवः सजोषाः (*divaḥ sajoṣāḥ*), of the same course or the same pleasure with the sun (Sūrya); वातः (*vātaḥ*) is Vāyu as already characterised; Agni; Pūṣan, the nourisher of that name; and Bhaga. In this sacrifice, which has the bringing forward (प्रभृथे (*prabhṛthe*)), all the deities named, विश्वभोजाः (*viśvabhojāḥ*), protectors of all or devourers of all **NOTE**, the best among those who possess horses of excellent going, go forth (प्र जग्मुः (*pra jagmuḥ*): proceed) as if to battle (आजिं न (*ājiṃ na*)).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- सक्षणः (*sakṣaṇaḥ*)—the one who accepts the share of the sacrifice
- दिव्यः (*divyaḥ*)—the one born in the heaven-world
- कण्वहोता (*kaṇvahotā*)—the one who has the Kaṇva seers themselves as hotṛs
- त्रितः (*tritaḥ*)—Trita
- दिवः (*divaḥ*)—with the sun
- सजोषाः (*sajoṣāḥ*)—having equal affection
- वातः (*vātaḥ*)—Vāyu
- अग्निः (*agniḥ*)—Agni
- पूषा (*pūṣā*)—the god Pūṣan
- भगः (*bhagaḥ*)—Bhaga
- विश्वभोजाः (*viśvabhojāḥ*)—all the deities who protect the universe
- प्रकृते (*prakṛte*)—to this sacrifice
- आश्वश्वतमाः (*āśvaśvatamāḥ*)—the horses that are the best even among swift-going horses
- आजिं न (*ājiṃ na*)—as they run to the field of battle
- प्रजग्मुः (*prajagmuḥ*)—let them graciously come quickly

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The one who accepts the share of the sacrifice, who is born in the heaven-world, and who has the Kaṇva seers as his hotṛs, namely Trita; Vāyu, who has equal affection with the sun; Agni; the god Pūṣan; Bhaga; and all the deities who protect the universe: may they come quickly and graciously to this sacrifice, as the best of the swift-going horses run to the battle.

##### English translation
May the divine accepter of sacrifice, of whom the Kanwas are
the priests, Trita, Vayu, and Agni, concurring in satisfaction with (the
ruler of) heaven, or (Surya and) Pushan, and Bhaga, and they who are the
protectors of the universe, (come quickly) to the sacrifice, as the fleetest
coursers rush to battle.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

कण्वहोता त्रितः (*kaṇvahotā tritaḥ*)

The deities mentioned in the present mantra are all, Sāyaṇa has said in the introduction, deities indicated by marks (liṅgokta). Liṅga means the individual and unique quality of each deity. For example, what are the unique qualities of Agni, Indra, Vāyu and Sūrya? —

### Printed p. 359

व्यवस्येन्मंत्रमाग्नेयं अंगैरग्नेशश्च लक्षितं । हविष्संक्ति प्रधान्यैश्च नामाह्वानैश्च केवलैः ॥ (*vyavasyenmaṃtramāgneyaṃ aṃgairagneśaśca lakṣitaṃ | haviṣsaṃkti pradhānyaiśca nāmāhvānaiśca kevalaiḥ ‖*)
(Bṛ. De. 1-86)

By mantras that indicate the special qualities of Agni **NOTE**, Agni is to be worshipped. One kind is that in which the havis-paṅkti is prominent, or the other kind is that which is to be recited in the form of an invocation. Thus the indication of the limbs of Agni is of two kinds. In the same way,

ऐंद्रस्तु मंत्रो वायवैर्लिंगैरैंद्रॆश्च लक्ष्यते । नामधेयैश्च वज्रस्य बलकृत्या बलेन च ॥ (*aiṃdrastu maṃtro vāyavairliṃgairaiṃdrèśca lakṣyate | nāmadheyaiśca vajrasya balakṛtyā balena ca ‖*)
(Bṛ. Pe. 1-87) **NOTE**

Just as the mantras that indicate the special qualities of Indra describe the nature of Indra, they make known the natures of Vāyu in the same way. The nature of these two becomes known through the mention of the names of the thunderbolt weapon, through usages that suggest prowess, and through descriptions of prowess. And,

सौरस्तु लिंगैः सूरस्य गुणैः सर्वैश्च तैजसैः । नामधेयैश्च चंद्रस्य सूक्तं च भजतेऽत्र यैः ॥ (*saurastu liṃgaiḥ sūrasya guṇaiḥ sarvaiśca taijasaiḥ | nāmadheyaiśca caṃdrasya sūktaṃ ca bhajate 'tra yaiḥ ‖*)
(Bṛ. De. 1-88)

The Sūrya mantras are to be recognised by the description of all the qualities of Sūrya and by the account of the divisions relating to his radiance. Thus the way of recognising the deities indicated by marks has been told. In the same manner, in the present mantra too, it takes no great effort to know the place of deities such as Agni and Pūṣan. But in "कण्वहोता त्रितः (*kaṇvahotā tritaḥ*)" it takes a little effort to understand the nature of Trita. Sāyaṇa has explained त्रिषु क्षित्यादिस्थानेषु तायमानः (सूरैण सह समानगतिः) वातः । वायुः (*triṣu kṣityādisthāneṣu tāyamānaḥ (sūraiṇa saha samānagatiḥ) vātaḥ | vāyuḥ*) as the Vāyu who is in the three worlds beginning with the earth and who has the same course as the sun. Therefore the two, दिवः सजोषाः (*divaḥ sajoṣāḥ*) and कण्वहोता (*kaṇvahotā*), become epithets of वातः (*vātaḥ*). In this view, what is the import of the epithet कण्वहोता (*kaṇvahotā*)? He has explained that too in this way: कण्वा ऋषयो मेधाविनो वा होतार आह्वातारो यस्य सः । (*kaṇvā ṛṣayo medhāvino vā hotāra āhvātāro yasya saḥ |*). The word kaṇva means a seer. This word is also used in the sense of "wise". Sāyaṇa, explaining the meaning of this word **NOTE**,

कण्वशब्दार्थः । कणंति ध्वनंति स्तोत्रादिपाठेनेति कण्वा ऋत्विजः । (*kaṇvaśabdārthaḥ | kaṇaṃti dhvanaṃti stotrādipāṭheneti kaṇvā ṛtvijaḥ |*)
(Sā. Bhā. Hu. Saṃ. 1-14-5) **NOTE**

### Printed p. 360

has said that priests are called Kaṇvas because they worship while making a sound in the form of praise. In the present mantra the import has been explained as Vāyu, who has as his hotṛs the seers endowed with intelligence. Besides, there is also the view that by the words दिवः सजोषाः त्रितः (*divaḥ sajoṣāḥ tritaḥ*) a special deity different from Vāyu is indicated, namely the god named Trita, who has equal affection with Āditya. Therefore, in this context, what precise meaning is possible from the epithet कण्वहोता (*kaṇvahotā*), and for which deity the epithet, if it is applied to him, makes the meaning natural here: these and similar points have to be examined at once.

Some have also given an import referring to Indra, by taking the words प्र सक्षणो दिव्यः (*pra sakṣaṇo divyaḥ*) to mean "the victor of the world of the gods". The scholar Griffith has translated it as "The heavenly victor, he whose priest is Kanva, Trita........." and has explained that "heavenly victor" means Indra. In this context one must understand what the word सक्षणः (*sakṣaṇaḥ*) means.

Sāyaṇa has given सक्षणः यज्ञं सेवमानः; शत्रूणां सोढा ना (*sakṣaṇaḥ yajñaṃ sevamānaḥ; śatrūṇāṃ soḍhā nā*), alternatively: one meaning, "one who attends the sacrifice", or another, "one who has the strength to defeat enemies". The word sakṣaṇa, formed from the root sac (sañj), gives the first meaning. It means "one who is joined together"; here the sense is "one who is joined with the sacrifice, that is, one who attends it". If this meaning is taken, then the epithet sakṣaṇa need not apply to Indra alone. This mark could apply to other deities such as Agni too. Therefore, in the present context, it is natural to take the second meaning. Then the word sakṣaṇa, formed from the root saha, gives the sense "one who has the power to face enemies".

The word sakṣaṇa is not used anywhere else in the whole Ṛk-saṃhitā except in this one place. But the word sakṣiṇi is used in other places. It can only be said that the meaning of the two words is the same. Although in one or two places the word sakṣiṇi is used in the first meaning, its use in the sense of enduring and in the sense of power is the more frequent. For example: सिंचंति हर्मस्य सक्षिणिं (*siṃcaṃti harmasya sakṣiṇiṃ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 9-71-4); परि वृत्राणि सक्षिणिः (*pari vṛtrāṇi sakṣiṇiḥ*) (9.110-1); पृतनासु सक्षिणिं (*pṛtanāsu sakṣiṇiṃ*) (1.111-3). In all such places the import is "one who has the power to win victory".

If, in the present mantra too, one takes the word sakṣaṇa to mean "victorious" and explains it as an epithet referring to Indra, an objection is possible even then. Trita too is famed as a renowned victor. The question why the word sakṣaṇa should not be an epithet of Trita as well is natural. Besides, the epithet कण्वहोता (*kaṇvahotā*) naturally states the quality of Trita.

### Printed p. 361

It does not fit Indra very well. The epithet kaṇvahotā too is not used anywhere else in the Ṛk-saṃhitā except in this one place. Since there are usages such as कण्वसखि (*kaṇvasakhi*) and कण्वतम (*kaṇvatama*), one must examine what they mean in those contexts and the special quality of which deity they make known.

स इदग्निः कण्वतमः कण्वसखार्यः परस्यांतरस्य तरुषः । अग्निः पातु गृणतो अग्निः सूरीनग्निर्ददातु तेषामवो नः ॥ (*sa idagniḥ kaṇvatamaḥ kaṇvasakhāryaḥ parasyāṃtarasya taruṣaḥ | agniḥ pātu gṛṇato agniḥ sūrīnagnirdadātu teṣāmavo naḥ ‖*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-115-5)

In this verse the word kaṇva means "praisers". The epithet कण्वतम (*kaṇvatama*) is used to show the sense that Agni is the best among praisers, and the epithet कण्वसखा (*kaṇvasakhā*) to show the sense that he has the praisers themselves as his friends. These two words make known special qualities of Agni. Besides,

नि यस्य ते ज्रयसानस्याजर धक्षोर्न वाताः परि संत्यच्युताः । आ रण्वासो युयुधयो न सत्वनं त्रितं नशंत प्र शिषंत इष्टये ॥ (*ni yasya te jrayasānasyājara dhakṣorna vātāḥ pari saṃtyacyutāḥ | ā raṇvāso yuyudhayo na satvanaṃ tritaṃ naśaṃta pra śiṣaṃta iṣṭaye ‖*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10.115-4)

in this verse there is a description of Trita, who has the quality of a warrior. It is a description of Agni. This same Agni, who is of the form of Trita, is described in the next mantra after the one quoted above with the epithets kaṇvatama and kaṇvasakhā. Thus, just as in many contexts there is a reference to Agni named Trita, it would not be wrong to say that in the present mantra too Agni is the one indicated. But here room remains for another kind of objection. Since the word Agni itself has been stated in the present mantra, some may also ask whether, when Agni is being indicated, the use of the word Trita again with reference to Agni is unnecessary. But there is no substance in that objection. If it were so, the reference to Pūṣan, who is a transformed form of Agni, being of the form of Sūrya, would also be out of place. Except for Vāta alone, all the other deities praised in the present mantra are deities whose nature is that of light. Among them, the epithet दिवः सजोषाः (*divaḥ sajoṣāḥ*) fits Trita naturally. Therefore, if it is said that it will not be wrong for Agni, who is of the form of light, to be denoted here by the word Trita, then, since the word Trita is used in other places to show the special qualities of Indra and others also, if one asks what the basis is for saying that in the present mantra Agni alone is precisely indicated, two kinds of answer can be given. First, another mantra contained in this same sūkta supports this view. How? Thus:

### Printed p. 362

वृष्णो अस्तोषि भूम्यस्य गर्भं त्रितो नपातमपां सुवृक्ति । गृणीते अग्निरेतरी न शूषैः शोचिष्केशो नि रिणाति वना ॥ (*vṛṣṇo astoṣi bhūmyasya garbhaṃ trito napātamapāṃ suvṛkti | gṛṇīte agniretarī na śūṣaiḥ śociṣkeśo ni riṇāti vanā ‖*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-41-10).

In this verse there is a description of the lightning-fire. This lightning-fire stands as the womb of the cloud. The word Trita is an epithet of this lightning-fire. Therefore it can be said that the word Trita here is an individual mark of Agni. Second, when Indra and others are indicated in other places, the import of this word is of a different kind. Therefore, to understand these contexts one must also notice for which of the remaining deities the word Trita is an epithet.

(1) Yāska, explaining this word in two contexts, has given two derivations of it. First, when giving the import of the mantra Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-105-8, he explained श्रितः तीर्णतमो मेधया बभूव (*śritaḥ tīrṇatamo medhayā babhūva*) **NOTE**: Trita is the most excellent among the wise; because he crossed over ignorance exceedingly by his intelligence, he has this name. Here Trita is the name of a great seer, a disciple of Bṛhaspati. (The story of this seer and his greatness are explained in the Ṛgveda Saṃhitā, part 8, pp. 443-447. That explanation is not relevant here.) (Ni. 4.6)

(2) Second, when explaining the mantra Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-187-1, he has given the import त्रिस्थान इंद्रः (*tristhāna iṃdraḥ*), Indra who stands pervading all three worlds. (Ni. 9 25)

Sāyaṇa's derivation follows, more or less, the derivation of the author of the Nirukta. In about twenty mantras Trita is praised as a great seer. In all the other places he has explained him as pervading the three places, and has said that, according to the context, the word is an epithet of deities such as Vāyu, Varuṇa, Indra and Agni. As we said above, if it is to be stated precisely that the present mantra indicates only the lightning-fire, one must notice the use of this word in the other places. The word Trita is used in forty contexts in the Ṛk-saṃhitā. Among them, in most it is used either together with some deity of the atmosphere, or in identity with a deity. Besides, in some contexts he is described as a deity who has occupied a place beyond the range of sight. In this way the uses of the word Trita in these various contexts can roughly be divided into eight groups. All of them are descriptions of the power of Trita.

(1) Situations in which he is joined with Indra: 16. अनु त्रितस्य युध्यतः शुष्मं (*anu tritasya yudhyataḥ śuṣmaṃ*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-7-24) and so on.

### Printed p. 363

(2) Situation in which, not with Indra but alone, he is the slayer of Vṛtra: 1. (यस्य त्रितो व्योजसा वृत्रं विपर्वमर्दयत् (*yasya trito vyojasā vṛtraṃ viparvamardayat*)) (1-187-1)

(3) Described as having the form of a sacrificial horse: 1. असि त्रितो गुह्येन व्रतेन (*asi trito guhyena vratena*) (1-163-3)

(4) Situations in which he is joined with the Maruts: 4. त्रितं जराय जरतां (*tritaṃ jarāya jaratāṃ*) (2-34-10) and so on.

(5) Situations in which he is described as shown in identity with Agni: 7. यदीमह त्रितो दिवि (*yadīmaha trito divi*) (5-9-5) and so on.

(6) In identity with Varuṇa: 1. त्रितं जूती सपर्यत (*tritaṃ jūtī saparyata*) (8.41-6) and so on.

(7) Described together with Soma: 4. उप त्रितस्य (*upa tritasya*) (9-102-2) and so on.

(8) Described as one who has, in a place beyond the range of sight, an individual form that is beyond comprehension: 6. त्रिताय तद्विभावरी (*tritāya tadvibhāvarī*) (8-47-14) and so on.

Thus there are forty situations in all. Among these, Trita, when described together with Indra, is generally described as fighting with Indra against the demon Vṛtra, or as offering Soma to Indra. When he is connected with the Maruts, he is a helper in matters such as the shining of lightning. With Agni he has identity itself. As to who he is and what his nature is, it has been said that his place and nature are beyond the range of sight.

When one explains the subject of such an indescribable Trita, the opinions of the scholars cannot possibly be of one kind. Therefore some have explained by showing identity with Indra, some with Vāyu, and some with Agni. But the nature of each of the original deities cannot be described precisely. It cannot be said that, through the division called "deities indicated by marks", knowledge of the individual mark of each deity is possible. It is also possible that a single epithet makes known a quality common to many deities. For example, the epithet Trita alone describes the pervasion of the three worlds by deities such as Indra, Agni and Vāyu, and so in some contexts it is a means of stating the precise meaning. But in the present case, which deity's special quality Trita reflects most can be stated only as far as it is possible, on the basis of Vedic passages.

### Printed p. 364

Although some scholars have shown identity between Trita and Indra, Trita and Indra are generally indicated together; so in all the contexts where he is described with Indra, he has to be regarded as a seer. (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1.52.5; 2-11-19, 20; 5-86-1; 8-13-16; 10.8-7; 10-99-6 and so on).

Identity with Vāyu cannot be stated. How? In places such as नि पस्त्यासु त्रितः स्तभूयन् (*ni pastyāsu tritaḥ stabhūyan*) (10.46-6) there is a description of Trita as the friend of the house. Vāyu is never described as the friend of the house; the friend of the house is none other than Agni.

It is described in many contexts that the atmosphere, which is the common abode of Agni, Vāyu and Indra, is the abode of Trita. Therefore it may be said that the view is sound that the lightning-fire, which has qualities common to all of them, is the one indicated by the name Trita. The identity shown, as stated above, between Trita and the horse of Varuṇa also supports this view. The association with the Maruts too becomes clear from this itself. This lightning-fire is one of the forms of the god Agni. Therefore it is the most natural view to show identity between Trita and Agni. Finally, the place beyond the range of sight also only suggests the nature of the lightning-fire.

There is no doubt that the derivation that Sāyaṇa has stated in the present mantra (5-41-4), त्रिषु क्षित्यादिस्थानेषु तायमानः (*triṣu kṣityādisthāneṣu tāyamānaḥ*), makes known these three forms: the earthly fire, the lightning-fire of the atmosphere, and the fire of the form of Āditya in the heaven-world. In the Ṛk-saṃhitā no other matter is described so much as these three conditions of Agni. (तिस्रो यह्वस्य समिधः (*tisro yahvasya samidhaḥ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-2-9).

अग्ने श्री ते वाजिना त्री षधस्था तिस्रस्ते जिह्वा ऋतजात पूर्विः । तिस्र उ ते तन्नो देववातास्ताभिर्नः पाहि गिरो अप्रयुच्छन् ॥ (*agne śrī te vājinā trī ṣadhasthā tisraste jihvā ṛtajāta pūrviḥ | tisra u te tanno devavātāstābhirnaḥ pāhi giro aprayucchan ‖*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-20.-2)

In this way, even when all kinds of powers of Agni, such as his origin, extent, nature and action, are described, this threefold character must invariably be described. (दिवस्परि प्रथमं जज्ञे (*divaspari prathamaṃ jajñe*) 10.45-1 and so on). Apart from the two forms that are well established, the earthly fire and Āditya, what remains is the third,

### Printed p. 365

the lightning-fire. Therefore it appears that one may say freely that this lightning-fire itself is what the word Trita denotes.

In the present mantra too, if one takes the word Trita to mean Agni, and if one is to stress the point that the epithet kaṇvahotā indicates no deity other than Agni, one must again examine the qualities common to Trita and Agni. These qualities must be common only to Trita and Agni, and must not apply to other deities.

Therefore, just as above the details of the description of Trita were divided into eight kinds, the details of the descriptions of Agni too can be divided into the same eight parts.

(1) First, the association and relation of Agni with Indra. Apart from eleven sūktas that praise Agni and Indra together, addressed to both at once, in the other mantras contained in many more sūktas too these two deities are praised and extolled together.

चक्राथे हि सध्र्य १ ज्ञाम भद्रं सध्रीचीना वृत्रहणा उत स्थः । ताविंद्राग्नी सध्वंचा निषद्या वृष्टः सोमस्य वृषणा वृषेथां ॥ (*cakrāthe hi sadhrya 1 jñāma bhadraṃ sadhrīcīnā vṛtrahaṇā uta sthaḥ | tāviṃdrāgnī sadhvaṃcā niṣadyā vṛṣṭaḥ somasya vṛṣaṇā vṛṣethāṃ ‖*) **NOTE**
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1.108.3)

O Indra and Agni, you have made your auspicious names known as always joined together. And you have been together even at the time of the slaying of Vṛtra, as slayers of the demon Vṛtra. Therefore, O Indra and Agni, bestowers of what is desired, you two, joined together and seated on the sacrificial altar, drink the flowing Soma juice of your share. In this way the constant association is praised in all the main activities such as the slaying of Vṛtra and the drinking of Soma. (This matter is described clearly in many mantras such as Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-108-5; 1-109-5; 1-109-8; 3-12-4.)

(2) Second, the very relation that has been shown above between Trita and the horse of Varuṇa is shown between Agni and the horse of Varuṇa. The horse called Dadhikrā, who is praised as being of the form of Āditya, is that same horse.

हंसः शुचिषद्वसुरंतरिक्षसद्दूता वेदिषदतिथिर्दुरोणसत् । नृपद्वरसदृतसद्योमसदब्बा गोजा ऋतजा अद्रिजा ऋतं ॥ (*haṃsaḥ śuciṣadvasuraṃtarikṣasaddūtā vediṣadatithirduroṇasat | nṛpadvarasadṛtasadyomasadabbā gojā ṛtajā adrijā ṛtaṃ ‖*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 4-40-5)

### Printed p. 366

This Agni, named Dadhikrā, is praised as being within the light in the form of a swan, in the atmosphere in the form of Vasu, on the sacrificial altar in the form of the hotṛ, in the house in the form of a guest, as born of the mountain, as born of ṛta, and with many other qualities. He is described in this way in दीदिवांसं नाग्निं (*dīdivāṃsaṃ nāgniṃ*) (4-39.2 ; ) and so on.

(3) In the way Trita is praised as the slayer of Vṛtra (alone, without Indra's company), Agni too is praised in the same way. तमु त्वा वृत्रहंतमं (*tamu tvā vṛtrahaṃtamaṃ*) (1-78-4); स वृत्रहा सनयो विश्ववेदाः (*sa vṛtrahā sanayo viśvavedāḥ*) (3-20-4); अग्निरगामि भारतो वृत्रहा पुरुचेतनः (*agniragāmi bhārato vṛtrahā purucetanaḥ*) (6-16-19): mantras such as these praise the act of Agni in slaying Vṛtra. In sixteen mantras in all Agni is praised as the slayer of Vṛtra. Among these, in seven the matter of his being joined with Indra is stated.

(4) The association between Trita and the Maruts, as it was described above, is established in the same way between Agni and the Maruts; epithets such as मरुद्वधः (*marudvadhaḥ*) (3-13-6) make known this point. It is also said that Agni is the tongue of the Maruts: अग्निजिह्वा (*agnijihvā*) (1-89-7). Here one more special point must be noticed. Just as the epithets kaṇvatama and kaṇvahotā and the like are used in the praises of Agni and Trita, in the same way, when Agni and the Maruts are praised together too, the praisers named Kaṇva are the ones addressed.

सहो षु णो वज्रहस्तैः कण्वासो अग्निं मरुद्भिः (*saho ṣu ṇo vajrahastaiḥ kaṇvāso agniṃ marudbhiḥ*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-7-32)

In this verse the Kaṇvas are entreated to praise Agni joined with the Maruts. Sāyaṇa has said that here the word kaṇva means either praisers or the seers born in the Kaṇva lineage.

(5) Fifth, not only the direct relation between Trita and Agni, but the identity between Trita and Agni is the main point. In this context the question naturally arises whether any main quality that applies to no other deity is well known as showing a common property of Trita and Agni. In answer to this, one may say freely that such a common property is well known. The epithet आप्त्य (*āptya*) is used in the Ṛk-saṃhitā in ten contexts only with reference to Trita. Sāyaṇa, in the introduction to the sūkta Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-105, says that the derivation "Āptya, because he was manifested from the waters (water)" is stated in the Taittirīya Brāhmaṇa too:

### Printed p. 367
त्रितस्य चापां पुत्रत्वं तैत्तीरियाः स्पष्टमामनंति । ततस्ति तोऽजायत । यदद्भ्योऽजायंत । तदास्यानामाप्यत्यं ॥ (*tritasya cāpāṃ putratvaṃ taittīriyāḥ spaṣṭamāmanaṃti | tatasti to 'jāyata | yadadbhyo 'jāyaṃta | tadāsyānāmāpyatyaṃ ‖*) (Tai. Brā. 3-2-8-11)

Sāyaṇa has cited just this Brāhmaṇa sentence. Because he was born from the waters, he became Āpya. But how does the derivation of the form Āptya arise? Sāyaṇa has given the answer elsewhere, that it is Vedic usage. Sentences such as त्रितस्तद्वेदात् (*tritastadvedāt*) and so on are the illustrations (1-105-9 etc.). That Agni too has manifested from the waters is well known, with accounts in many places. O Agni, you are kindled in the firmament, the abode of the waters (अग्ने अपां समिध्यसे दुरोणे (*agne apāṃ samidhyase duroṇe*)). (Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-25-5) When Agni, held by the waters, occupies them, then the streams of water, in the dwelling-place of Āditya, their place of origin, are pervaded by his rays and receive a new refinement. Afterwards Agni, who is in the form of lightning, unites and joins with the waters. (अपामुपस्थे विभृतः (*apāmupasthe vibhṛtaḥ*) Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-144-2).

The hotṛ who knows the sky is stationed in the lap of the waters in the form of lightning (10-46-1). The Bhṛgu seers realised Agni, who stands in the midst of the waters, and established him among men. (10-46-2; 2-4-2). निधीयमानपमगूळ्मप्पु प्र मे देवानां व्रतपा उवाच ॥ (*nidhīyamānapamagūḷmappu pra me devānāṃ vratapā uvāca ‖*) The great seer who knew the secret of the gods told me that Agni is within the waters. (18-32-6)**NOTE**

Thus the account that Agni too is born of the waters, and the account that Trita, being born of the waters, is Āptya, both tell in the same way of an origin from the waters of the firmament; so there is no doubt that both accounts convey the very nature of the lightning-fire.

In the same way the epithet Apāṃ Napāt too conveys the nature of the lightning-fire. How? अपां नपादा ह्यस्थादुपस्थं जिह्मानामूर्ध्वो विद्युत्व वसानः । तस्य ज्येष्ठं महिमानं वहंतीर्हिरण्यवर्णाः परि यंति यह्वीः ॥ (*apāṃ napādā hyasthādupasthaṃ jihmānāmūrdhvo vidyutva vasānaḥ | tasya jyeṣṭhaṃ mahimānaṃ vahaṃtīrhiraṇyavarṇāḥ pari yaṃti yahvīḥ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-35-9)

### Printed p. 368
Clothed in a garment of lightning, the son of the waters stands within the shining waters. In his own form he is golden-hued and exceedingly attractive. The sentences quoted above show the identity of Trita Āptya with the fire named Apāṃnapāt. Both epithets are descriptions of the nature of the lightning-fire that dwells in the firmament. As has been said above, it can be stated with certainty that Trita, the Kaṇva hotṛ indicated in the mantra under discussion, is none other than this lightning-fire.

6) Just as the identity of Trita and Varuṇa has been shown, there are also many sentences showing the identity of Agni and Varuṇa. For example: I have known the face of Agni to be the face of Varuṇa. (अनीकं वरुणस्य मंसि (*anīkaṃ varuṇasya maṃsi*) Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-88-2).

7) युवमेतानि दिवि रोचनान्यग्निश्च सोम सक्रतू अधत्तं । (*yuvametāni divi rocanānyagniśca soma sakratū adhattaṃ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1.93-5)

O Agni and Soma, you two are of like action. These shining star-systems, visible to our eyes, you two together have lifted up in the heaven. In sentences such as these, the relation between Agni and Soma is stated in the same way as the relation between Trita and Soma was shown above.

8) Lastly, just as Trita is described as hidden in a place beyond sight, so Agni too is praised. त्वामग्ने अंगिरसो गुहा हितमन्वदिंदञ्छायाणं वनेवने । (*tvāmagne aṃgiraso guhā hitamanvadiṃdañchāyāṇaṃ vanevane |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 5.10-6) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 51-6)**NOTE**

O Agni, the Aṅgirases found you hidden in a secret place.

बृहंत इद्वानवो भाय्जीकमग्निं सचंत विद्युतो न शुक्राः । गुहेव वृद्धं सदसि स्वे अंतरपार ऊर्वे अमृतं दुहानाः ॥ (*bṛhaṃta idvānavo bhāyjīkamagniṃ sacaṃta vidyuto na śukrāḥ | guheva vṛddhaṃ sadasi sve aṃtarapāra ūrve amṛtaṃ duhānāḥ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-1-14)

Great lights, like the gleams of lightning, served Agni who stood in a secret place. And so on: this is a description of Agni as hidden in a place beyond sight.

Thus, having set out the common qualities of Trita and Agni, it has been shown that the sentences of the Ṛk-saṃhitā themselves are clear proofs for establishing that Trita is none other than the lightning-fire. Other sentences of the Brāhmaṇas and the like, which help to confirm this very point, have also been quoted above.

### Printed p. 369
#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> प्र वो॑ र॒यिं यु॒क्ताश्वं॑ भरध्वं रा॒य एषेऽव॑से दधीत॒  
> धीः ।  
> सु॒शेव॒ एवै॑रौशि॒जस्य॒ होता॒ ये व॒ एवा॑ मरुतस्तु॒राणां॑ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> प्र । वः । रयिं । युक्त अश्वं । भरध्वं । रायः । एषे । अवसे । दधीत । धीः ।  
> सुशेवः । एवैः । औशिजस्य । होता । ये । वः । एवाः । मरुतः । तुराणां ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे मरुतो वो यूयं रयिं धनं युक्ताश्वमश्वसहितमश्वयोग्यं
> पुत्रं वा प्र भरध्वं । संपादयत । रायो धनानि गवाश्वादिलक्षणान्येषे प्राप्तं ।
> अवसे । अव इत्यन्ननाम । अन्नाय प्राप्तानां धनानां रक्षणाय वा धीः स्तोता
> दधीत । धारयति स्तुतिं । स चौशिजस्य कक्षीवतो होतात्रिरेनैर्गंतवैः कामैर्गमनसाधनैरश्वैर्वा सुशेवः । शेवमिति सुखनाम । सुसुखो भवतु । हे मरुतस्तुराणां त्वरमाणानां वो युष्माकं य एवाः कामा अश्वा ना संति तैरिति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Maruts, you bring wealth that is yoked with horses, that is, wealth accompanied by horses, or a son fit for horses: provide it. The riches, that is, wealth in the form of cattle, horses and the like, have been obtained. अव (*ava*) is a name for food; so for food, or for the protection of the wealth that has been obtained, the praiser holds, that is, offers, his praise. And may the hotṛ Atri, of Auśija Kakṣīvat, be well happy (शेवमिति सुखनाम (*śevamiti sukhanāma*), "śeva" is a name for happiness), through the objects of desire that are means of going, or through horses. Maruts, who are swift, he says, the desires or horses of you swift ones are the means.**NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वः (*vaḥ*)—you
- रयिं (*rayiṃ*)—wealth
- युक्ताश्वं (*yuktāśvaṃ*)—so that it is accompanied by horses
- प्र भरध्वं (*pra bharadhvaṃ*)—procure and give
- रायः (*rāyaḥ*)—(new) riches
- एषे (*eṣe*)—for obtaining
- अवसे (*avase*)—for protecting (what one has)
- धीः (*dhīḥ*)—the praise (that praises you)
- दधीत (*dadhīta*)—composes and recites
- मरुतः (*marutaḥ*)—O Maruts
- तुराणां (*turāṇāṃ*)—swift-going
- वः (*vaḥ*)—your
- ये एवाः (*ye evāḥ*)—whatever swift-moving horses there are
- एवैः (*evaiḥ*)—with those same horses
- औशिजस्य (*auśijasya*)—of Kakṣīvat
- होता (*hotā*)—the hotṛ, Atri
- सुशेवः (*suśevaḥ*)—may he obtain excellent happiness

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Maruts, procure for us wealth accompanied by horses. Both for obtaining new riches and for preserving what we have, the praiser composes and recites praise that extols you. O Maruts, may the hotṛ Atri, of Kakṣīvat, obtain excellent happiness together with the horses that belong to you, the swift-going, and that move swiftly.

### Printed p. 370

##### English translation
Maruts, you bring wealth comprising horses: to acquire and preserve riches the wise man offers you praise: may the ministrant priest, (Atri), of the son of Ushij (Kakshivat), be made happy by those swift-going (horses) which are the swift.going (horses), Maruts, of you who are rapid in motion.
#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> प्र वो॑ वा॒युं र॑थ॒युजं॑ कृणुध्वं॒ प्र दे॒वं विप्रं॑  ॒॒॑
> पनि॒तार॑म॒र्कैः ।  
> इ॒षु॒ध्यव॑ ऋत॒सापः॒ पुरं॑धी॒र्वस्वी॑र्नो॒ अत्र॒ पत्नी॒रा धि॒ये  ॑
> धुः ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> प्र । वः । वायुं । रथ्ययुजं । कृणुध्वं । प्र । देवं । विप्रं । पनितारं । अर्कैः।  
> इषुध्यवः । ऋत्यसापः । पुरंधीः । वस्वीः । नः । अत्र । पत्निः । आ । धिये। धुरिति धुः॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> प्र वो वायुमिति षष्ठी वायव्ये पशौ हविषो याज्या । प्र वो वायुं
> रथयुजं कृणुद्धमुत त्वामदिते महि । आ. ३-८ । इति सूत्रितं ॥
> हे मदीया ऋत्विजो वो यूयं वायुं वायुदेवं रथयुजं
> यज्ञगमनाय रथसंबद्धं कृणुध्वं । कुरुध्वं । प्र देवं द्योतमानं विप्रं
> विशेषेण कामानां पूरकं विप्रवत्पूज्यं वा पनितारं ॥ कर्मणि कर्तृप्रत्ययः ॥
> स्तुतमित्यर्थः । फलप्रदातारं वा अर्कैरर्चनसाधनैर्मंत्रैः प्र कृणुध्वं । स्तुत ।
> किंचेषुध्यवो गंत्र्य ऋतसापो यज्ञस्पृशः पुरंधीः पुरंध्यः स्त्रीरूपा रूपवत्यो

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The sixth Ṛk is the yājyā for the oblation in the animal sacrifice to Vāyu; it is prescribed by the sūtra प्र वो वायुं रथयुजं कृणुद्धमुत त्वामदिते महि (*pra vo vāyuṃ rathayujaṃ kṛṇuddhamuta tvāmadite mahi*) (Ā. 3-8). O priests who are mine, make Vāyu, the god, yoked to the chariot for going to the sacrifice. Praise with hymns, that is, with the mantras that are means of worship, the shining one, the vipra, who is the special fulfiller of desires or is worshipful like a vipra, the one who is to be praised, or the giver of fruit. Moreover, the goers, those who touch the sacrifice, the women of fair form, or the excellent, praiseworthy wives, the wives of the gods,

### Printed p. 371

> वा वस्वीः प्रशंस्याः पत्नीर्देवपत्न्योऽत्रास्मिन्यज्ञे नोऽस्मदीयाय धीये
> कर्मणे तन्निस्सत्तय आ धुः । आगतवत्यः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

in this sacrifice, for our rite, to accomplish it,**NOTE** may come: so he says, आगतवत्यः (*āgatavatyaḥ*), "they who have come".

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वः (*vaḥ*)—you (O priests)
- वायुं (*vāyuṃ*)—Vāyu
- रथयुजं (*rathayujaṃ*)—so that he makes ready his chariot (for going to the sacrifice)
- कृणुध्वं (*kṛṇudhvaṃ*)—make
- देवं (*devaṃ*)—the shining
- विप्रं (*vipraṃ*)—the special fulfiller of desired ends
- पनितारं (*panitāraṃ*)—the praiseworthy Vāyu
- अर्कैः (*arkaiḥ*)—with hymns of praise
- प्र (*pra*)—praise
- इषुध्यवः (*iṣudhyavaḥ*)—the goers
- ऋतसापः (*ṛtasāpaḥ*)—those who accept the sacrificial rite
- पुरंधीः (*puraṃdhīḥ*)—those of woman's form
- वस्वीः (*vasvīḥ*)—the praiseworthy
- पत्निः (*patniḥ*)—the wives of the gods
- अत्र (*atra*)—in this sacrifice
- नः (*naḥ*)—our
- धिये (*dhiye*)—for the sake of the rite
- आ धुः (*ā dhuḥ*)—may they come

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O priests, cause Vāyu to make ready his chariot for going to the sacrifice. Praise with hymns Vāyu, the shining one, the special fulfiller of desired ends, the praiseworthy. May the goers, the acceptors of the sacrifice, the wives of the gods, of woman's form and praiseworthy, come to this sacrifice for the sake of our rite.

##### English translation
Prevail, (priests), by your prayers, on Vayu, the divine, the fulfiller of desires, the adorable, to harness his chariot: may the light-moving accepters of sacrifice, the lovely and excellent wives (of the gods), come hither to our rite.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

This Ṛk, प्र वो वायुं (*pra vo vāyuṃ*), is to be recited as the yājyā mantra when the oblation is offered in the animal sacrifice made for the deity Vāyu; it is prescribed by the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra, प्र वो वायुं रथयुजं कृणुध्वमुत त्वामदिते महि (*pra vo vāyuṃ rathayujaṃ kṛṇudhvamuta tvāmadite mahi*) (Ā. 3-8).
#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> उप॑ व॒ एषे॒ वंद्ये॑भिः शू॒ष्यैः प्र य॒ह्वी दि॒वश्चि॒तय॑द्भिर॒र्कॆः

### Printed p. 372

> उषासानक्ता विदुषीव विश्वमा हा वहतो मर्त्याय  
> यज्ञं ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> उप । वः । एषे । वंद्येभिः । शूषैः । प्र । यह्वी इति । दिवः । चितयत्भिः । अर्कैः ।  
> उषसानक्ता । विदुषी इवेति विदुषीऽइव । विश्वं । आ । ह । वहतः । मत्याय ।  
> यज्ञं ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे उषासानक्ता अहोरात्राभिमानिदेवते यह्वी महत्यौ वंद्येभिर्वंदना.
> र्हैरितरैर्देवैः सह दिवोऽंतरिक्षादविशेषेण सर्वप्रदेशाद्वो युष्मभ्यमुप प्रैषे।
> उपप्रापयामि हविः ॥ ईषिः प्रापणकर्मा सादुपप्राभिः समन्वितः ॥ शूषैः सुखकरैः
> चितयद्भिर्ज्ञापयद्धिरर्कैर्मंत्रैः सहोपप्रापयामि । हे उक्तदेवते विश्वं
> सर्वं कर्तव्यजातं विदुषी इव जानत्याविन मर्त्याय यजमानाय यज्ञमा
> वहतः । अभिमुखं प्रापयतः । हेति पूरणः । यद्वा । शूषैरित्यादीनि तृतीयाबहुवचनांतानि वंद्येभिरित्यस्य विशेषणानीति केचिदाहुः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Uṣāsānaktā, deities presiding over day and night, you who are great: together with the other gods who are worthy of salutation, from the sky, that is, the firmament, from every region without exception, I bring to you the oblation, together with mantras that give comfort and make known. O deities so named, as though knowing all that is to be done, the two carry the sacrifice to the mortal, the sacrificer; they bring it face to face. The word ह (*ha*) is a filler. Or else: some say that शूषैः (*śūṣaiḥ*) and the other words in the instrumental plural are epithets of वंद्येभिः (*vaṃdyebhiḥ*).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यह्वी (*yahvī*)—of great nature
- उषासानक्ता (*uṣāsānaktā*)—O deities of day and night
- दिवः (*divaḥ*)—dwelling in the firmament
- नंद्येभिः (*naṃdyebhiḥ*)—together with the other gods who are worthy of worship
- वः (*vaḥ*)—to you
- शोषैः (*śoṣaiḥ*)—comfort-giving
- चितयद्भिः (*citayadbhiḥ*)—full of knowledge
- अर्कैः (*arkaiḥ*)—accompanied by mantras
- उप प्र एषे (*upa pra eṣe*)—I offer (the oblations, O gods)
- विदुषी इव (*viduṣī iva*)—like wise and learned women
- विश्वं (*viśvaṃ*)—all matters of the rite (you being knowers of them)
- मर्त्याय (*martyāya*)—to the sacrificer, a man
- यज्ञं (*yajñaṃ*)—so that it becomes the means of his sacrifice
- आ वहतः (*ā vahataḥ*)—bring that knowledge

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O deities of day and night, of great nature: together with the other gods who dwell in the firmament and are worthy of worship, I offer to you oblations accompanied by comfort-giving and knowledge-filled mantras. O gods, like wise and learned women, knowing all matters of the rite, bring to the human sacrificer that knowledge, so that it becomes the means of his sacrifice.
